# Chapter 12 — Deep-review findings register

Twelve audit units, reviewed in parallel against a fixed rubric. **306 findings** with a file path, a line, and a concrete failure scenario each.

| Severity | Count | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| P0 | 58 | Blocks G1 — unsafe or dishonest for any human use |
| P1 | 121 | Blocks G2 — prevents taking money safely |
| P2 | 103 | Blocks G3 — prevents GxP/submission-grade use |
| P3 | 24 | Quality, maintainability, hygiene |

## Read this before the findings

**These findings are largely unverified, and that is a material caveat.** The adversarial verification stage covered **14** of the 179 P0/P1 findings before the run was cut short by a container restart. The rest are single-reviewer output.

That matters because this audit has a measured error rate on exactly this kind of claim. Ten headline findings went through full adversarial verification (Chapter 03): **four survived intact, six were overstated, three materially** — including one where the guard-coverage number was wrong by roughly 3× and one where the XSS taint path was traced through dead code.

**So treat an unverified finding below as a lead worth an hour, not a fact.** On the observed base rate, expect a meaningful fraction to be overstated, mis-severitied, or already mitigated by a compensating control the reviewer did not find. Every one carries a `file:line` so it can be checked in minutes.

The findings that decide the verdict are **not** in this chapter — they are in the executive summary and Chapter 03, and all of those were verified by execution.

## Findings

### multi-tenancy & isolation

This unit is a well-designed RLS rollout bolted onto an application that cannot use it. The primitives are genuinely good — a default-deny `/api` auth boundary, JWT-only org derivation with header-impersonation logging, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` in 0021, a fail-closed boot posture, and a runtime guard that refuses to admit a second tenant while RLS is off — but the policy layer is inert by construction: `requireTenantContext` is mounted on exactly one route file (`server/routes/ana-features.ts:1386`), so `req.dbClient` is null everywhere else, `requestDb(req)` silently falls back to the pool-bound Drizzle (`server/db/requestDb.ts:65-67`), and 78 of 97 DB-touching route files issue queries on a shared pool whose connections carry `app.rls_enforce` but never `app.current_tenant_id`. Flipping `RLS_ENFORCE=on` would therefore not harden the product, it would break it. With the only real boundary being per-handler discipline, that discipline fails repeatedly: I confirmed live cross-tenant read/write IDORs in the flagship project router (`concept2cure.ts` tasks and CMC data), an attacker-controlled `ON CONFLICT (study_id)` that overwrites another tenant's clinical protocol record, a self-provisioning `authoring_tokens` table whose unique key omits the tenant, and four more org-blind upsert arbiters beyond the one already known. Two whole subsystems (Conversation-OS, the stability module's child tables, the GraphRAG knowledge graph) have **no tenant column at all**, so RLS could never cover them even at full enforcement. The CI gate that is supposed to catch this covers 32 tables out of hundreds, treats any occurrence of the word `organization_id` anywhere in a SQL string as proof of scoping, blanket-exempts `server/workers/` and `server/jobs/`, and does not see Drizzle at all — it flags zero of the fourteen findings below. Dominant risk: this system is safe today only because it holds one tenant; the first real second tenant converts several of these into immediate, silent regulatory-data corruption and disclosure.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- Default-deny `/api` authentication boundary is real and enforces in production: `server/startup/middleware.ts:113-119` mounts it before any route registration, and `server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:124-128` resolves to 'enforce' whenever NODE_ENV=production regardless of config. Every finding below requires an authenticated account, not an anonymous one.
- Organization id is derived exclusively from the verified JWT and never from headers, with an explicit impersonation-attempt warning when `x-org-id` disagrees — `server/middleware/tenantContext.ts:94-114`. This closes the most common multi-tenant footgun outright.
- `strictPositiveInt` (`server/middleware/tenantContext.ts:32-39`) rejects the `parseInt('42abc') === 42` truncation class on JWT `userId`/`organizationId` claims. Rare, correct, and clearly written for a reason.
- The production boot matrix in `server/db/rlsEnforcement.ts:85-129` refuses to boot on an unset or typo'd `RLS_ENFORCE`, and `RLS_REQUIRE_ENFORCE=true` gives an operator a hard fail-closed switch. Silent-off is genuinely not reachable by accident.
- `assertTenantIsolationBeforeNewOrg` (`server/db/rlsEnforcement.ts:165-188`) plus `assertCanAdmitNewTenant` (`server/db/tenantAdmission.ts:42-61`) is a real runtime control, not a document: it refuses to create organization #2 while RLS is not filtering, and fails CLOSED if the org count cannot be determined. This is the single best control in the unit and it is the reason the posture is defensible at one tenant.
- 0021 gets the Postgres details right where most teams get them wrong: `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` (migrations/0021_enable_rls_everywhere.sql:122-123) closes the table-owner bypass that makes plain ENABLE silently ineffective on Neon, and the `table_type = 'BASE TABLE'` filter (lines 93-98) stops views from aborting the rollout.
- `server/services/tenant/governed-tenant-context.ts:25-29` stamps the tenant vars transaction-locally on the governed-CRUD path, so that whole family of routes is already RLS-ready ahead of the flip.
- `tests/schema-contract/cmc-module3-tenant-arbiter.contract.test.ts` is exactly the right acceptance-test shape for this bug class: it builds the real tables in PGlite, applies the REAL migration file, and runs the REAL upsert statement text, reproducing the org-blind case first to prove the mechanism. `migrations/20260728_cmc_module3_org_scoped_uniqueness.sql` is on the durable deploy path (`scripts/db/migration-set.mjs`), so that instance is properly fixed.
- Correct ownership-validated upsert code exists in the same repo and should be the template: `server/services/committees/committee-service.ts:120-210` (every member/meeting/protocol lookup org-scoped before the ON CONFLICT), `server/services/protocol-soa/protocol-soa-service.ts:41-52` (validates both assessment and visit belong to the org before `ON CONFLICT (assessment_id, visit_id)`), `server/routes/q-sub.ts:214-216` (tenant gate before the section upsert), and `server/routes/concept2cure.ts:16679-16690` (notification read/dismiss checks id + recipient + org).
- The measurement layer is honest and useful: `server/db/poolInstrumentation.ts` + `server/db/tenantStore.ts` count queries with no tenant scope, and `scripts/ci/audit-requestdb-coverage.mjs` reports the real numbers without spin (97 route files / 16 adopted / 78 on the shared pool). The seller is not hiding the size of the gap.

#### `P0` C2C-TASK-01 — Project tasks are updated and deleted by primary key with no tenant filter, and the update body is applied verbatim

`server/routes/concept2cure.ts:13240` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · cross-tenant-idor

**Evidence.** router.put('/projects/:projectId/tasks/:taskId', ...): `const updates = req.body; ... const [updated] = await db.update(projectTasks).set(updates).where(eq(projectTasks.id, taskId)).returning();` — no organizationId predicate, no projectId predicate, and `updates` is the raw request body. The DELETE twin at line 13264 is `db.delete(projectTasks).where(eq(projectTasks.id, taskId)).returning()`, also unscoped. `project_tasks` carries `organizationId: integer('organization_id').notNull()` (shared/schema.ts:6816-6821), and the router is mounted behind authentication only (server/bootstrap/register-concept2cure-routes.ts:11).

**Failure scenario.** A user authenticated to org 1 sends `PUT /api/concept2cure/projects/1/tasks/9876` with body `{"name":"x"}`, where task 9876 belongs to org 2. The row is updated and `.returning()` sends org 2's complete task row back in the response — name, description, assignee, due date, metadata. Sending `{"organizationId": 1}` instead moves org 2's task into org 1 permanently, because `organizationId` is a real column on the table and `.set()` applies it. `DELETE /api/concept2cure/projects/1/tasks/9876` destroys it outright. `projectTasks.id` is a serial, so the whole estate is enumerable by counting up.

**Fix.** Change both handlers to `.where(and(eq(projectTasks.id, taskId), eq(projectTasks.organizationId, getOrganizationId(req)), eq(projectTasks.projectId, projectId)))`. Separately, replace `.set(req.body)` with a zod-parsed allowlist of mutable columns — `organizationId`, `projectId`, `id` and `createdAt` must not be settable from a request body.

#### `P0` RLS-POOL-01 — RLS cannot be enabled without breaking the product: 78 of 97 route files query a pool that never carries the tenant session variable

`server/db/runtime.ts:120` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · defense-in-depth-inoperable

**Evidence.** `const _db = pool ? drizzle(pool, { schema }) : null;` — the default Drizzle handle is bound to the shared pool. `installRlsEnforcement` (server/db/rlsEnforcement.ts:209-217) sets only `app.rls_enforce` on new connections; `app.current_tenant_id` is set solely by the per-request lazy client (server/middleware/tenantContext.ts:275-283) and by `withTenantConnection`. `requireTenantContext` is imported and mounted in exactly one route file — `server/routes/ana-features.ts:1386` — so `req.dbClient` is undefined everywhere else and `requestDb(req)` falls back to the pool-bound `db` (server/db/requestDb.ts:65-67). `node scripts/ci/audit-requestdb-coverage.mjs` prints: route files touching the DB 97, using requestDb(req) 16, still on the shared pool 78.

**Failure scenario.** An operator follows the documented remediation and sets `RLS_ENFORCE=on` in production. On the next request to any of the 78 unmigrated route files, the 0021 policy evaluates `NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant_id', TRUE), '')::INT` on a connection where the variable was never set, yielding NULL; every OR branch is false. Reads return zero rows (the UI renders empty projects, empty documents, empty submissions with no error) and writes fail with 'new row violates row-level security policy'. The only safe production posture is therefore RLS off — which is the posture the entire policy layer exists to escape.

**Fix.** Take option (B) from the seller's own burndown: install a pool checkout/query hook next to `installRlsEnforcement` that reads `getTenantScope()` from the AsyncLocalStorage and applies `SET app.current_tenant_id` on the connection, so the shared `db` becomes RLS-correct wherever a scope exists. Then globally mount `requireTenantContext` on `/api` (with a public/health/webhook allowlist) so the scope always exists. Gate the flip on a staging run with `RLS_ENFORCE=on` and a zero-row-regression check across the golden journeys.

#### `P0` STUDY-DESIGN-01 — Study-design persist upserts on a globally unique study_id that the caller supplies, overwriting another tenant's clinical protocol record

`server/services/study-design/study-design-repository.ts:242` · blocks **G1** · ~days · on-conflict-tenant-blind-arbiter

**Evidence.** `INSERT INTO cdisc_prm_studies (tenant_id, study_id, ...) VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (study_id) DO UPDATE SET protocol_title = EXCLUDED.protocol_title, ... metadata = EXCLUDED.metadata` — the DO UPDATE SET list does not include tenant_id. The arbiter resolves against `CONSTRAINT "cdisc_prm_studies_study_id_unique" UNIQUE("study_id")` (migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:924), which omits the tenant column even though a tenant-scoped index also exists at line 7026. `studyId` is `(design.id && design.id.trim()) || 'sd_'+randomUUID()` (same file, line 154), and `design` comes from `designSchema.safeParse(req.body?.design ?? req.body)` where the schema is `.passthrough()` and does not declare `id` at all (server/routes/study-design.ts:70-79, 89-96). Reached via `POST /api/study-design/persist` (server/routes/study-design.ts:356).

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 POSTs /api/study-design/persist with `{"design":{"id":"sd_<org2-study-id>","title":"...","phase":"1",...},"reason":"benign text"}`. Because the unique key is study_id alone, the conflict lands on org 2's row: its protocol title, phase, indication, primary/secondary objectives, design, blinding schema, randomization, population description, planned subjects, protocol status and the full design JSON in `metadata` are replaced with org 1's values, while `tenant_id` stays 2 so the row still reads as org 2's. Lines 268-296 then `DELETE ... WHERE study_id = $1 AND tenant_id = $2` using org 1's tenant, so org 2's original arms and endpoints survive alongside org 1's newly inserted ones — org 2's protocol becomes a silent hybrid of two studies.

**Fix.** Drop `cdisc_prm_studies_study_id_unique` and make the arbiter `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, study_id)` against the existing `prm_tenant_study_idx`. Ship the constraint change on the durable deploy path (`scripts/db/migration-set.mjs`), not only in migrations/, exactly as was done for 20260728_cmc_module3_org_scoped_uniqueness.sql. Also reject a caller-supplied `design.id` that does not already resolve inside the caller's tenant.

#### `P0` CONVOS-01 — Conversation-OS stores ten tables with no tenant column and scopes every query on a projectId taken from the request body

`server/routes/conversation-os.ts:43` · blocks **G1** · ~days · missing-tenant-column

**Evidence.** `const projectId = req.body?.projectId ?? req.query?.projectId;` — the only scoping key, taken straight from user input; `organizationId` is computed on line 44 and then never used in any persistence call. Every read is `WHERE project_id = $1 AND conversation_id = $2` with no org and no user predicate (server/services/conversation-os/persistence.ts:47-49, 80-82, 131-133, 163-165, 202-204, 277-279). db/migrations/20260326_conversation_os_durability.sql creates ten tables and contains zero occurrences of organization_id, org_id or tenant_id, so migrations/0021_enable_rls_everywhere.sql — which only selects columns named organization_id/org_id/tenant_id (lines 86-87) — can never install a policy on any of them.

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 issues `GET /api/conversation-os/conversations/<cid>/scout?projectId=42` where project 42 belongs to org 2. `listScoutFindings` runs `SELECT id, summary, chunk_ids, promoted FROM conversation_os_scout_findings WHERE project_id = 42 AND conversation_id = <cid>` and returns org 2's AI research findings. The same substitution on `/tool-events`, `/plan-summary` and `/proposals` returns org 2's execution plans and draft artifact content. On the write side, `POST /api/conversation-os/conversations/<cid>/retrieval/ingest` with `projectId: 42` inserts attacker-controlled text into `conversation_os_retrieval_chunks` under org 2's project — content that is subsequently fed to org 2's model as retrieval context. No control anywhere in the stack — route, service, table, or RLS — can stop this.

**Fix.** Add `organization_id integer NOT NULL` to all ten tables, backfill from the owning project, add it to every WHERE clause and to the two UNIQUE keys (`project_id, conversation_id` at lines 13 and 84, and `project_id, conversation_id, source_id` at line 39), and validate that the supplied projectId belongs to `req.user.organizationId` at the top of `resolveContext`. Put the migration on the durable deploy path.

#### `P0` AUTH-TOKENS-01 — authoring_tokens is created at runtime with UNIQUE(section_id, cite_id) omitting the tenant, and the upsert returns the conflicting row

`server/routes/authoring.router.ts:787` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · on-conflict-tenant-blind-arbiter

**Evidence.** `INSERT INTO authoring_tokens (section_id, cite_id, token_key, payload, payload_sha256, source_refs, created_by, tenant_id) VALUES ($1..$8) ON CONFLICT (section_id, cite_id) DO UPDATE SET token_key = $3, payload = $4, payload_sha256 = $5, source_refs = $6, updated_at = NOW() RETURNING *`. The arbiter and the SET list both omit tenant_id. The table is created by the route itself on every call — `ensureTokenTableExists()` at line 573-590 — with `tenant_id INTEGER DEFAULT 1` and `UNIQUE(section_id, cite_id)` at line 587, so this defect is provisioned unconditionally on any database the route touches. `sectionId` is `req.params.sectionId` (line 771) and is never checked for ownership.

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 POSTs `/api/authoring/sections/<org2-section-id>/tokens` with `{"cite_id":"c-1","token_key":"k","payload":{...}}`. If org 2 already has a token for that (section_id, cite_id), the DO UPDATE replaces org 2's citation payload, payload_sha256 and source_refs — the evidence binding behind a regulatory sentence — while tenant_id stays 2, and `RETURNING *` hands the attacker org 2's full row including its tenant_id, created_by and prior source_refs. That is a cross-tenant read and a Part 11 record alteration in one request. If no token exists, the insert creates one that org 2's section will read.

**Fix.** Change the runtime DDL to `UNIQUE(tenant_id, section_id, cite_id)` plus a guarded ALTER for existing databases, change the arbiter to `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, section_id, cite_id)`, drop the `DEFAULT 1` on tenant_id, and add an ownership check that sectionId belongs to `getTenantId(req)` before the write. Note that lines 1032, 1111, 2444, 3477, 5459, 5866 and 5933 in the same file already do include tenant_id in the arbiter — this is the one that does not.

#### `P0` C2C-CMC-01 — Project CMC data, full project context and task rollups are readable and writable across tenants by project id

`server/routes/concept2cure.ts:13937` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · cross-tenant-idor

**Evidence.** `router.get('/projects/:projectId/cmc', ...)`: `const [project] = await db.select().from(projects).where(eq(projects.id, projectId)).limit(1); if (!project) return sendError(res, 404, ...)` then returns `metadata.cmcDrugSubstance`, `cmcDrugProduct`, `cmcSpecifications`, `cmcStabilityStudies`, `cmcImpurities`. No organizationId predicate. The PUT twin at line 13961 performs `db.update(projects).set({ metadata: updatedMetadata }).where(eq(projects.id, projectId))`, equally unscoped. Same pattern at line 13998 (`/context`, which also returns 50 rows from `projectTasks` by projectId alone) and line 14173 (`/tasks/assess`). Contrast line 16679 in the same file, where the notification route correctly checks id + recipientUserId + orgId.

**Failure scenario.** A user authenticated to org 1 requests `GET /api/concept2cure/projects/57/cmc` where project 57 belongs to org 2, and receives org 2's drug substance, drug product, specification table, stability studies and impurity profile — the most commercially sensitive content in the platform. `PUT /api/concept2cure/projects/57/cmc` then overwrites it. `projects.id` is a serial, so an attacker walks 1..N and the 404-versus-200 split enumerates every project in the estate.

**Fix.** Add `eq(projects.organizationId, getOrganizationId(req))` to the project lookup in all four handlers (and to the `db.update(projects)` where clause), returning 404 on miss so existence is not disclosed. `getOrganizationId` already exists at line 1073 and throws when absent, so there is no fail-open path.

#### `P0` BUDGET-01 — Protocol budget parameters upsert on protocol_document_id alone with no ownership check anywhere in the request path

`server/services/protocol-budget/protocol-budget-service.ts:44` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · on-conflict-tenant-blind-arbiter

**Evidence.** `INSERT INTO protocol_budget_params (organization_id, protocol_document_id, target_enrollment, sponsor_payment_per_subject, indirect_rate_pct, created_by) VALUES ($1..$6) ON CONFLICT (protocol_document_id) DO UPDATE SET target_enrollment = COALESCE($3, ...), sponsor_payment_per_subject = $4, indirect_rate_pct = $5, updated_at = now() RETURNING id` — organization_id is neither in the arbiter nor in the SET list. The backing index is `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_protocol_budget_params_doc ON protocol_budget_params(protocol_document_id)` (migrations/20260702_protocol_budget.sql:43). The caller is `PUT /api/protocol-budget/documents/:id/params` (server/routes/protocol-budget.ts:73-83), which parses `id` from the URL and passes it straight through — there is no query anywhere in that file or the service that verifies the document belongs to the caller's org.

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 sends `PUT /api/protocol-budget/documents/88/params` with `{"targetEnrollment":10,"sponsorPaymentPerSubject":1,"indirectRatePct":0,"reason":"routine update"}` where protocol document 88 belongs to org 2. Org 2's budget parameters row is overwritten; organization_id stays 2, so org 2's `GET /documents/88/summary` now computes and displays a feasibility verdict from org 1's numbers with no indication anything changed. The governed-action ledger records the change under org 1, so org 2's audit trail shows nothing.

**Fix.** Recreate the index as `UNIQUE (organization_id, protocol_document_id)` on the durable deploy path and change the arbiter to match. Independently, add an org-scoped existence check on `protocol_document_id` at the top of both `setBudgetParamsTx` and `addBudgetItemTx` and throw `ProtocolBudgetError('NOT_FOUND')` on miss — `protocol-soa-service.ts:41-46` in the same codebase is the template.

#### `P0` SOE-01 — Schedule-of-events regeneration upserts on project_id alone and proceeds after the org-scoped project lookup returns null

`server/services/projects/schedule-of-events/service.ts:390` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · on-conflict-tenant-blind-arbiter

**Evidence.** `INSERT INTO project_schedule_of_events (organization_id, project_id, ...) VALUES ($1,$2,...) ON CONFLICT (project_id) DO UPDATE SET status='active', project_type=EXCLUDED.project_type, goals=EXCLUDED.goals, basis_summary=EXCLUDED.basis_summary, version = project_schedule_of_events.version + 1, ... RETURNING id, version` — organization_id absent from both the arbiter and the SET list. The backing index is `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX unique_project_schedule ON project_schedule_of_events (project_id)` (db/migrations/20260617_project_schedule_of_events.sql:48). In server/routes/project-schedule-of-events.ts:105-120 the handler calls `resolveProjectType(orgId, projectId)`, which queries `WHERE id = $1 AND organization_id = $2` and returns null on a foreign project — and then calls `generateProjectSchedule` anyway. The migration header at lines 20-22 claims 'Not in the RLS allowlist: organization_id is NOT NULL, so the blanket tenant-isolation policy is the correct default', which is false: 0021 ran long before this table existed and never revisits new tables.

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 POSTs `/api/concept2cure/projects/42/schedule-of-events/generate` where project 42 belongs to org 2. The org-scoped project-type lookup returns null, the handler continues, and the upsert conflicts on org 2's plan row: status, project_type, regulatory_framework, goals, basis_summary, ana_summary, confidence, baseline_date and target_date are all replaced and version is incremented, while organization_id stays 2. `loadPlan` is org-scoped (line 166-172) so the attacker sees `plan: null` and nothing appears to have happened — a blind destructive write. Org 2's submission milestone schedule, which the scheduleOfEventsSweep job acts on, is now derived from a different company's inputs.

**Fix.** Recreate `unique_project_schedule` as `(organization_id, project_id)` on the durable deploy path, change the arbiter to match, and make the route return 404 when `resolveProjectType` cannot find the project in the caller's org rather than continuing with a null type.

#### `P0` STAB-01 — The stability module has no tenant column on any child table and reads/writes every record by id alone

`server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:1342` · blocks **G3** · ~weeks · missing-tenant-column

**Evidence.** `GET /studies/:id/inuse`: `pool.query('SELECT * FROM stab_inuse WHERE study_id = $1 LIMIT 1', [id])` — no tenant predicate and no set_config at all. The upsert below it (line 1367) is `ON CONFLICT (study_id) DO UPDATE SET multi_dose=$2, ..., hold_time_days=$4`. `PATCH /studies/results/:id` (line 970) is `UPDATE stab_results SET value=$2, unit=$3, pass=$4 WHERE result_id=$1` and `DELETE /studies/results/:id` (line 993) is `DELETE FROM stab_results WHERE result_id = $1`. `GET /studies/:id` (line 548) does set `app.tenant_id` — a variable no policy in the repo reads; the 0021 policy reads `app.current_tenant_id` (migrations/0021_enable_rls_everywhere.sql:136) — and then queries stab_studies, stab_conditions, stab_timepoints, stab_tests and stab_results by study_id alone. Only stab_studies ever got a tenant column, added nullable and as TEXT (db/migrations/023_stability_step2.sql:19); stab_conditions, stab_timepoints, stab_tests, stab_results, stab_inuse, stab_oot_rules and stab_audit (db/migrations/022_stability_v2.sql:23-60, 026_stability_step4.sql:2-35) have none, so 0021 cannot ever install a policy on them.

**Failure scenario.** With the stab_* schema provisioned (scripts/db_migrate.sh applies db/migrations), an authenticated user in org 1 issues `PATCH /api/stability/studies/results/<result-uuid>` with `{"value":98.5,"pass":true}` against a result belonging to org 2 and the assay value is rewritten — a GxP stability record altered with no tenant check, no attribution to the correct tenant, and no policy able to intervene. `GET /api/stability/studies/<uuid>` returns another tenant's complete stability study: conditions, timepoints, tests and every result value. `GET /api/stability/protocols` (line 2147, signature `(_req, res)`) selects all rows from stab_protocols with no tenant concept at all.

**Fix.** Add `organization_id integer NOT NULL` to every stab_* table, backfill from the parent study, add the predicate to all ~90 queries in this router, and correct the session variable name from `app.tenant_id` to `app.current_tenant_id`. If the module is not shipping, unmount it at server/bootstrap/register-inline-routes.ts:202 rather than leaving it reachable.

#### `P1` GATE-01 — The tenant-isolation CI gate cannot detect any of the defects in this report and reports a passing baseline anyway

`scripts/ci/check-tenant-isolation.mjs:77` · blocks **G2** · ~days · ineffective-ci-control

**Evidence.** `const TENANT_SCOPED_TABLES = new Set([...])` lists 32 tables. None of project_tasks, cdisc_prm_studies, protocol_budget_params, project_schedule_of_events, section_dependencies, authoring_tokens, conversation_os_*, stab_* or knowledge_graph_nodes is in it. The safety test is `if (TENANT_FILTER_RE.test(sql)) continue;` (line 291) where the regex is a bare word match for organization_id/tenant_id/org_id anywhere in the string (lines 112-127) — so an INSERT whose column list mentions organization_id passes even when its ON CONFLICT arbiter omits it, which is precisely the shape of five findings here. Drizzle query-builder calls are excluded by design (lines 13-16), which is where C2C-TASK-01 and C2C-CMC-01 live. `server/workers/` and `server/jobs/` are blanket-allowlisted (lines 205-207). Running the gate today yields 25 findings, all 25 present in docs/reports/tenant-isolation-baseline.json, so it is green.

**Failure scenario.** A developer adds a new route with `ON CONFLICT (project_id) DO UPDATE` on a tenant-keyed table. CI passes, the PR merges, and a second tenant's rows become writable — the exact sequence that produced SOE-01, BUDGET-01, SECDEP-01 and STUDY-DESIGN-01 after the cmc-module3 instance of the same bug had already been found and documented.

**Fix.** Generate TENANT_SCOPED_TABLES from the schema rather than hand-maintaining it. Add a distinct check that parses `ON CONFLICT (...)` arbiter column lists and fails when the target table has a tenant column absent from the arbiter — that single rule catches five of the findings here. Replace the substring tenant-filter test with a WHERE-clause-scoped check. Remove the workers/jobs blanket exemption in favour of per-file entries with stated rationale.

#### `P1` SECDEP-01 — Section dependencies upsert on a tenant-blind unique key and return the conflicting row to the caller

`server/routes/project-sections.ts:909` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · on-conflict-tenant-blind-arbiter

**Evidence.** `INSERT INTO section_dependencies (organization_id, project_id, section_code, depends_on_code, dependency_type) VALUES ($1..$5) ON CONFLICT (project_id, section_code, depends_on_code) DO UPDATE SET dependency_type = $5 RETURNING *`. The backing constraint is `UNIQUE(project_id, section_code, depends_on_code)` (db/migrations/20260220_ind_section_tracking.sql:126) on a table whose `organization_id INTEGER NOT NULL` is at line 119. `project_id` comes from `req.body` (line 885) and is never validated against `extractOrgId(req)` — contrast the same file's `/initialize` handler at line 136, which does scope its existence check by org.

**Failure scenario.** A user in org 1 POSTs `/api/project-sections/dependencies` with `{"project_id":42,"section_code":"2.7","depends_on_code":"2.5","dependency_type":"informs"}` where project 42 is org 2's and already has that dependency as 'blocks'. The DO UPDATE flips org 2's dependency type — changing which CTD sections its authoring workflow treats as gating — and `RETURNING *` hands org 1 the whole org 2 row including its organization_id and row id, confirming the target exists and identifying its tenant.

**Fix.** Recreate the constraint as `UNIQUE(organization_id, project_id, section_code, depends_on_code)`, change the arbiter to match, add an org-scoped existence check on project_id before the write, and stop returning `*` — return only the fields the caller supplied.

#### `P1` RLS-DEPLOY-01 — 0021 is absent from the production migration entrypoint, so no existing database receives the tenant-isolation policy and no post-rollout table is ever covered

`scripts/db/migration-set.mjs:55` · blocks **G2** · ~days · control-not-on-deploy-path

**Evidence.** `export const C2C_MIGRATION_FILES = [...]` — the ordered list applied by `scripts/db/deploy-migrate.mjs`, which its own header (line 3) calls 'THE production deploy-time schema migration entrypoint' — does not contain 0021_enable_rls_everywhere.sql. 0021 is applied only by `scripts/db/install-fresh.mjs` (RLS_MIGRATIONS, line 71-78), which by its own header (lines 6-11) is from-scratch provisioning only. deploy-migrate.mjs states the consequence at lines 41-43: '0021_enable_rls_everywhere has long since run and never revisits new tables'. Grepping the repo, only migrations/20260608_ai_placement_policies.sql, 20260612_rls_research_admin.sql, 20260617_ai_provider_preference.sql, 20260727_onboarding_proposal_runs.sql, db/migrations/20260725_authoring_document_loop_tables.sql and db/migrations/20260728_artifacts_table.sql create a tenant_isolation_policy of their own.

**Failure scenario.** An operator flips RLS_ENFORCE=on on a production database that was provisioned before install-fresh.mjs existed, or by drizzle push plus hand-applied SQL. `pg_policies` contains no tenant_isolation_policy rows, so nothing filters and the flip is a no-op — while the boot log prints 'RLS enforcement mode installed { mode: on }' and the readiness posture reports enforcement is active. Separately, on any database, every table added after the initial install (rbm_*, cre_*, cmc_module3_*, collab_document_state, protocol_budget_*, project_schedule_of_events, committee_*) has no policy regardless of the flag.

**Fix.** Add an idempotent re-run of 0021 to C2C_MIGRATION_FILES so every deploy re-sweeps for newly added tenant-keyed tables (0021 is already written to be re-runnable — it DROPs and recreates the policy per table). Add a verification step to deploy-migrate.mjs that counts tenant-keyed tables lacking tenant_isolation_policy and fails the deploy above zero, and surface that count on /readyz alongside the RLS mode so 'enforcement on' can never be reported while zero policies exist.

#### `P1` STAB-02 — Two stability endpoints report success without writing anything to the database

`server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:499` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · fabricated-success

**Evidence.** `router.put('/studies/:id', ...)`: the body is `const updatedStudy = { id, ...updateData, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() }; res.json({ message: 'Study updated successfully', study: updatedStudy });` with the inline comment `// Return updated study data (in production, would update database)`. No query is issued. `router.patch('/studies/:id/status', ...)` at line 522 is the same shape: it validates the status against a whitelist and then returns `{ message: 'Study status updated successfully', study: { id, status, updated_at } }` without touching the DB.

**Failure scenario.** A user edits a stability study and marks it COMPLETED. The API returns 200 with 'Study status updated successfully' and the client renders the new state from the echoed response. On the next page load the study is still ONGOING. In a GxP context this is worse than an error: the operator has a screenshot of a successful status transition that the record does not reflect, and no audit entry exists for the change that appeared to happen.

**Fix.** Either implement the UPDATE with an org predicate and an audit write, or return 501 Not Implemented. Echoing the request body as a persisted entity is the one option that must not ship.

#### `P2` GRAPHRAG-01 — GraphRAG queries the knowledge graph with no tenant predicate on tables that have no tenant column; currently dormant only because the pool handle is never wired

`server/routes/graphrag.ts:806` · blocks **G1** · ~days · latent-cross-tenant-read

**Evidence.** `GET /communities`: `const entityQuery = projectId ? "...WHERE metadata->>'projectId' = $1 LIMIT 1000" : "SELECT id, entity_type, name, description, metadata, confidence FROM knowledge_graph_nodes LIMIT 1000"` — the unfiltered branch has no tenant concept, and the filtered branch keys on a user-supplied projectId embedded in JSONB. `POST /query` at lines 570-578 runs a pgvector nearest-neighbour scan over `knowledge_graph_nodes` with only `WHERE embedding IS NOT NULL`. The ingest at line 696 is `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET metadata = EXCLUDED.metadata`. knowledge_graph_nodes has no organization_id/tenant_id column anywhere in the repo, so 0021 cannot cover it. The routes resolve their pool as `(req as any).pool || (req.app as any).pool` (lines 547, 675, 765); grepping server/ for any assignment to req.pool or app.pool outside tests returns nothing, so `pool` is undefined at runtime and the handlers throw into their catch blocks and return honest 500s.

**Failure scenario.** Today: every GraphRAG endpoint returns HTTP 500 'GraphRAG query failed' because `pool` is undefined — the module is non-functional, not leaky. The moment anyone attaches a pool (a one-line middleware, or a refactor to import the shared pool the way every neighbouring router does), `POST /api/graphrag/query` immediately returns entity names and descriptions extracted by the LLM from other tenants' uploaded regulatory documents, ranked by semantic similarity to the attacker's query string, and `GET /api/graphrag/communities` returns up to 1000 of them at once.

**Fix.** Add organization_id to knowledge_graph_nodes and knowledge_graph_edges, filter every query on it, and make the ingest arbiter `(organization_id, id)`. Until that lands, unmount the router at server/bootstrap/register-advanced-platform-routes.ts:178 rather than leaving a wired-and-leaking route one refactor away.

#### `P2` RLS-VAR-01 — The stability router sets a session variable no policy in the codebase reads, presenting as tenant scoping that does nothing

`server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:144` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · claim-code-mismatch

**Evidence.** `await client.query("SELECT set_config('app.tenant_id', $1, true)", [String(safeTenantId)])` — repeated at lines 165, 197, 222, 316, 561 and elsewhere, each preceded by the comment 'Set tenant context for RLS policies'. The policy installed by migrations/0021_enable_rls_everywhere.sql reads `app.current_tenant_id` and `app.current_org_id` (lines 136-137); `withTenantConnection` sets `app.current_tenant_id`/`app.current_org_id`/`app.current_user_role` (server/db/withTenantConnection.ts:87-89) and `requireTenantContext` sets the same three (server/middleware/tenantContext.ts:276-282). Nothing anywhere reads `app.tenant_id`.

**Failure scenario.** A reviewer performing a customer security assessment greps for tenant-context handling, finds seven set_config calls in the stability router each commented 'Set tenant context for RLS policies', and concludes the module is RLS-scoped. It is not: even if a policy existed on the stab_* tables, the variable it reads would be empty on these connections, so every row would be filtered out — or, in the current no-policy state, every row is returned. The comment asserts a control that does not exist.

**Fix.** Rename to `app.current_tenant_id` throughout this file, and add a CI grep asserting that the only set_config target names appearing in server/ are the three the policy actually reads.

### auth & identity

This unit is a well-instrumented perimeter wrapped around a soft core. The perimeter is genuinely good: a default-deny `/api` auth boundary mounted before any route registration, HS256-pinned JWT verification with a rotation window, fail-closed boot asserts for JWT/refresh/MFA/RLS/audit secrets, a DB-backed org-membership re-check with explicit invalidation, constant-time multi-tenant SCIM auth with a fail-closed IP allowlist, fail-closed SAML XML-DSig, bcrypt cost-12 with lockout and password history, and a correct deny-by-default RBAC service. The core is where it breaks. The `/api/auth` and `/api/auth/enterprise` routers are allowlisted out of the boundary and then hand-roll `verifyJwtWithRotation` with no token-class guard — so the MFA-partial and MFA-challenge tokens, which are signed with the same secret, are accepted as full credentials by `/api/auth/enterprise/refresh-token`, `/select-organization`, and both `/mfa/setup` endpoints. A password alone plus one extra HTTP request yields a 24h session; the repo's own `nonAccessTokenReason` control exists and is unit-tested but is never applied on these paths. Beyond that: logout does not revoke access tokens despite saying it does, SCIM deprovisioning writes a `users.status` that no auth path reads, the entire regulatory authoring surface (281 handlers) is gated on a role the product cannot assign so only org admins can reach it, and production executes a different, weaker auth middleware than dev and tests do (empirically confirmed esbuild vs tsx twin resolution). The dominant risk is that MFA — the control this product's Part 11 story rests on — is bypassable with the password alone, and that the surrounding compensating controls all sit outside the one prefix where the bypass lives.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- Default-deny /api authentication boundary mounted once, before every route registration and after the audit observer (server/index.ts:123 → server/startup/middleware.ts:113-119). It is the single control that saves the codebase from the ~34 route modules that would otherwise resolve to the stale auth.js twin without an org-membership check. Its allowlist matcher is correctly strict — `fullPath === entry.path || fullPath.startsWith(entry.path + '/')` (server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:111-117) — so /api/healthxyz cannot ride on /api/health, and each of the 19 entries carries a written justification and names its alternative control.
- Fail-closed boot posture in server/config/environment.ts, all firing at import time: JWT secret must exist and be ≥32 chars (:108-131), a dedicated REFRESH_TOKEN_SECRET distinct from the JWT secret is mandatory in staging and production (:203-241), a rotation slot identical to the current secret is rejected (:171-179), a dedicated MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEY ≥32 chars is required in production (:258-274), plus RLS-enforcement and audit-seal posture asserts (:284, :292). This is materially better than the industry norm of warning and continuing.
- JWT verification is algorithm-pinned to HS256 at both call sites with a documented previous-secret rotation window that retries only on genuine signature mismatch and rethrows the original error (server/utils/jwtVerify.ts:58-99), backed by a CI gate (`npm run ci:jwt-verify-pinned`) that checks the pin textually — which is why the pin is spelled inline rather than hoisted.
- Organization-membership re-check with a deliberately-designed cache: positive AND negative results cached for 60s, revoked membership fails closed with AUTH_009, DB failure fails open with a structured warning and is never cached so enforcement resumes the moment the DB recovers, size-capped, with explicit invalidation hooks called from the invite/remove/role-change paths (server/middleware/orgMembership.ts:37-208). It is housed in a module with no .js twin specifically so every resolver agrees on it (:1-30), and the transport-agnostic core (:229-258) deliberately gives the collaboration socket a stricter 'indeterminate' policy than HTTP, with the reasoning written down.
- SAML is genuinely fail-closed: real XML-DSig via node-saml with no 'proceed with caution' branch, audience and InResponseTo enforced (server/routes/sso.ts:265-271); signed LogoutResponse required on BOTH the redirect and POST bindings with the raw query string preserved for detached-signature verification (:467-505, :510-514); RelayState treated as untrusted and used only to select a trust anchor, with that reasoning documented (:31-38, :246-248); returnTo restricted to root-relative same-origin paths so the token cannot leak cross-origin (:221-227); and JIT provisioning refuses to fall back to a hardcoded org, throwing SamlOrgResolutionError and returning 403 instead (:57-83, :232-244).
- SCIM auth is the strongest single component in this unit: constant-time bearer comparison with constant work per tenant and no early break so timing does not reveal which tenant matched (server/routes/scim.ts:236-258), DB-backed tenants stored as SHA-256 hashes with a stale-cache-on-error policy (:121-141), an opt-in but fail-closed per-org source-IP allowlist where an unresolvable IP is denied (:191-197), its own rate limiter because it sits outside the /api limiters (:32-47), org pinned from the token and never from the request (:271-281), and GxP-flagged audit rows on provision and deprovision (:290-309).
- server/services/roleBasedAccess.ts is a correct RBAC core: DB-backed, org-scoped, explicit deny wins over grant (:183), deny-by-default when the schema or DB is unavailable (:135-136, :148-150), fail-closed on any query error, and TTL-cached with invalidation. It is what the JWT-claim-based guards in middleware/auth.ts should have been, and requirePlatformAdmin is already used in place of requireRole on the genuinely cross-tenant admin routers (server/routes/admin/scim-ip-allowlist.ts:25-27, server/routes/admin/scim-tenants.ts:24).
- Credential handling is solid: bcrypt cost 12 everywhere (server/routes/auth.ts:1754, :1892), a 12-character NIST-style policy with common-pattern rejection (server/services/auth-security-service.ts:45-88), 5-strike / 30-minute account lockout with the exact unlock time deliberately withheld from the response (:128-216, server/routes/auth.ts:406-414), 5-deep password history checked with bcrypt.compare (:1880-1889), reset tokens as 32 random bytes stored only as a SHA-256 hash with a 15-minute expiry and single use (:1655-1666, :1740-1765), and consistently non-enumerating responses on login, signup and forgot-password (:385-388, :810-816, :1638-1652).
- Email OTP is implemented carefully: crypto.randomInt over the full 6-digit range, SHA-256 at rest with the plaintext never persisted, 10-minute expiry, single-use clearing on success, and a constant-time hash comparison (server/services/emailOtpService.ts:53-135).
- API-key auth fails closed rather than open: a key-store outage returns 503 instead of falling through with authMethod already set (server/middleware/enterprise-security.ts:719-728), the format check was deliberately folded into the same SQL path to remove a timing oracle with the reasoning documented (server/services/api-key-service.ts:117-131), keys are 256-bit random stored as SHA-256, expiry transitions are audited, and requireScope enforces ALL declared scopes rather than any (enterprise-security.ts:759-784).
- scripts/ci/check-js-ts-shadows.mjs is an unusually honest engineering artifact: it contains an empirically derived resolver table for tsx / esbuild / vitest across four importer-specifier combinations, dated and attributed, and forces every shadow pair to be either deleted or allowlisted with a challengeable reason (:5-25, :44-67). The finding in AUTH-06 exists because this gate documented it rather than hid it.
- Authentication events are written to the Part 11 audit trail with a non-fatal wrapper so an audit outage cannot break login: unknown-email attempts (with the literal attacker-supplied address, deliberately), wrong-password, lockout, MFA-challenge-issued as a separate event from session creation, and logout attributed via an unverified decode so expired-session logouts are still recorded (server/routes/auth.ts:33-64, 377-384, 396-405, 428-437, 584-593, 999-1011).
- The token-class control itself is well built and unit-tested where it is applied — server/middleware/tokenType.ts:33-41 covers mfaPending, the pending_mfa role and all three type discriminators, and is exercised by server/__tests__/security/security-swarm-hardening.test.ts:36-49, tests/schema-contract/authoring-auth-parity.contract.test.ts:119-126 and the collaboration-socket contract test. The defect in AUTH-01/AUTH-02 is one of coverage, not of design.

#### `P0` AUTH-01 — MFA fully bypassable: the MFA-partial token is accepted as a credential by /api/auth/enterprise/refresh-token

`server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:826` · blocks **G1** · ~days · authentication-bypass

**Evidence.** POST /refresh-token: `const decoded = verifyJwtWithRotation(oldToken) as any;` (:826) then `const newToken = jwt.sign({ userId: decoded.userId, email: decoded.email, organizationId: decoded.organizationId, role: refreshRole }, config.jwt.secret, { expiresIn: '24h' });` (:834-843). There is no token-class check. The MFA-partial token is minted at :339-349 as `jwt.sign({ userId, email, organizationId, role: 'pending_mfa', mfaPending: true }, config.jwt.secret, { expiresIn: '5m' })` — same secret, and it carries every claim the new token copies. The guard that exists for exactly this (`nonAccessTokenReason`, server/middleware/tokenType.ts:33-41, applied in server/middleware/auth.ts:128) never runs because server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:62 allowlists `{ path: '/api/auth', match: 'prefix' }` and /api/auth/enterprise is mounted under it (server/bootstrap/register-platform-routes.ts:157). The same hole exists at /select-organization (server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:735 → :779) and /api/auth/mfa/verify's sibling paths.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker has victim@customer.com's password from a credential-stuffing list. (1) POST /api/auth/enterprise/verify-password {email, password} → 200 {partialToken}. The attacker never receives the email OTP and cannot complete MFA. (2) POST /api/auth/enterprise/refresh-token with header `Authorization: Bearer <partialToken>` and no body → 200 {token}. That token has role=<victim's real org role from lookupOrgRole>, organizationId=<victim's org>, no `type`, no `mfaPending`, 24h TTL. It passes nonAccessTokenReason, passes enforceOrgMembership, and authenticates every /api route. Mandatory MFA is defeated in two requests with only the password.

**Fix.** Call `nonAccessTokenReason(decoded)` (server/middleware/tokenType.ts) immediately after every hand-rolled `verifyJwtWithRotation` in server/routes/authEnterprise.ts and server/routes/auth.ts and 401 on a non-null result. Better: delete the hand-rolled verification and require `authenticateToken` on every /api/auth/* route that is not part of the pre-MFA handshake, and give the MFA-partial/challenge tokens their own dedicated signing secret so they cannot verify on the access path at all.

#### `P0` AUTH-02 — MFA enrolment take-over: a pre-MFA challenge token can re-key the victim's TOTP secret and then satisfy MFA with it

`server/routes/auth.ts:1461` · blocks **G1** · ~days · authentication-bypass

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/mfa/setup: `const decoded = verifyJwtWithRotation(token) as { userId: string; email: string };` (:1461) then `await mfaService.generateSecret(parseInt(decoded.userId), decoded.email)` (:1468) — no token-class check, no current-code re-authentication. generateSecret unconditionally overwrites the row: `db.update(users).set({ mfaSecret: encryptedSecret, mfaMethod: 'totp' })` (server/services/mfaService.ts:328-335) and returns the plaintext base32 secret to the caller. The login challenge token is `jwt.sign({..., type: 'mfa_challenge'}, requireJwtSecret(), {expiresIn:'5m'})` (mfaService.ts:539-550) — verifiable by that same `verifyJwtWithRotation`. /api/auth/mfa/verify then falls back to `mfaService.verifyToken(userId, code)` (server/routes/auth.ts:1300-1302), and verifyToken only requires a stored secret — `if (!user?.mfaSecret) return false;` (mfaService.ts:369) — it never checks `mfaEnabled`. The identical hole exists at server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:513-520 (/mfa/setup via extractJwtUser, :151-166).

**Failure scenario.** Attacker has victim@customer.com's password. (1) POST /api/auth/login {email,password} → {mfaRequired:true, challengeId}. (2) POST /api/auth/mfa/setup with `Authorization: Bearer <challengeId>` → 200 {secret: 'JBSWY3DP...'}; the victim's users.mfaSecret is now the attacker's secret and mfaMethod is flipped to 'totp'. (3) Attacker computes the current TOTP from that secret. (4) POST /api/auth/mfa/verify {challengeId, code: <TOTP>, method:'totp'} → 200 {accessToken, refreshToken}. Full session with the password only. As a side effect the victim's real authenticator (if any) is silently destroyed and the victim is locked out of their own second factor.

**Fix.** Reject non-access tokens on /mfa/setup and /mfa/enable (nonAccessTokenReason). Additionally: refuse generateSecret when users.mfaEnabled is already true unless the caller supplies a valid current TOTP or password re-auth, and make mfaService.verifyToken require `user.mfaEnabled === true` for the login path (keep the enable-flow exception explicit and separate).

#### `P1` AUTH-03 — Logout does not invalidate the access token, but tells the user it did

`server/routes/auth.ts:1013` · blocks **G2** · ~days · session-management

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/logout calls `await revokeToken(token)` (:991) and responds `{ success: true, message: 'Logged out successfully. Tokens invalidated.' }` (:1013). But no authenticator ever reads the revocation list: a repo-wide grep for isTokenRevoked/isTokenRevokedSync shows the only consumer is server/routes/auth.ts:1040 inside POST /api/auth/refresh. server/middleware/auth.ts authenticateToken (:111-163) and server/auth.ts authMiddleware (:74-159) both go verify → token-class → enforceOrgMembership with no revocation check. server/routes/auth.ts:958 exports isTokenBlacklisted, which nothing imports. server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:891-896 POST /logout is a pure no-op that returns {success:true} without touching any store.

**Failure scenario.** A user on a shared workstation clicks Log out at 09:00. The UI clears local storage and the server returns 'Tokens invalidated.' A colleague who captured the Bearer token from the browser devtools (or a stolen laptop with the token still in a proxy log) replays `Authorization: Bearer <token>` against GET /api/documents at 09:05 and every other /api route until 09:00 the next day — the access token's full 24h TTL. Identical result after an admin 'forces logout' from the enterprise router, which does nothing at all.

**Fix.** Add an `await isTokenRevoked(raw)` check to authenticateToken (server/middleware/auth.ts) and authMiddleware (server/auth.ts) before populating req.user, using the same Redis→DB→memory service. Make /api/auth/enterprise/logout revoke the presented Bearer token. Consider a per-user `tokens_valid_after` timestamp so an admin-initiated revocation kills all of a user's tokens without enumerating them.

#### `P1` AUTH-04 — 281 regulatory route handlers are gated on a role the product can never assign, so only org admins can use the core regulatory surface

`server/middleware/auth.ts:197` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · authorization

> **RESOLVED 2026-09-02.** Reproduced first, against a provisioned database with
> RLS enforcing: an invited colleague (role `member` — what every invitation
> mints) got `403 AUTH_004` from `GET /api/submissions` while the founder got
> 200. The count is 289 guards now, not 281.
>
> Fixed by the first option below, as a derived grant rather than a fifth
> assignable role: `ORG_ROLE_FUNCTIONAL_GRANTS` in `server/middleware/auth.ts`
> maps the org role a customer actually assigns onto the functional role the
> guards name — admin, owner, manager, member and the legacy editor carry
> `regulatory-author`; `viewer` does not. It is applied where `req.user.roles`
> is built, so it covers every token this platform mints (password login, dev
> login, MFA completion, refresh, SSO, SCIM) and tokens issued before the
> change, without editing ten sign sites and without asking existing customers
> to re-role their users.
>
> The blanket admin override is deliberately KEPT, but it is no longer
> load-bearing: it now covers only what it was written for, and the comment at
> its site says so. Verified after the change on the same three callers:
> member 200, admin 200, viewer 403 — and a member still fails a guard that
> asks for `admin` or any PLATFORM_SCOPED_ROLE.
> Proof: `server/middleware/__tests__/role-claims.test.ts`.

**Evidence.** requireRole: `if (!hasRole && !userRoles?.includes('admin')) { return 403 }` (:197). ~28 mounted route files gate on `requireRole('regulatory-author')` — server/routes/submission-readiness.ts:16, server/routes/authoring-pdf.routes.ts:29, server/routes/cybersecurity-524b.ts:16, server/routes/human-factors.ts:17, server/routes/spl-fhir.ts:17, server/routes/ind-forms.routes.ts:50, server/routes/etmf.routes.ts:26, server/routes/cdisc-validation.routes.ts:30, server/routes/submissions.ts:129, server/routes/global-ri/_shared.ts:19, server/routes/ind-lifecycle/shared.ts:19, server/routes/ind-master-data.routes.ts:37 and others (281 requireRole call sites in server/routes). No code path assigns that role: the user-management API enum is `z.enum(['admin','manager','member','viewer'])` (server/routes/tenant-users.ts:16 and :30), SCIM's VALID_ROLES is the same four (server/routes/scim.ts:60), SAML JIT provisions `role: 'member'` (server/routes/sso.ts:669), signup writes `role: 'admin'` (server/routes/auth.ts:875), and server/auth/README.md documents exactly admin|manager|member|viewer. The blanket admin override at :197 is therefore the only way any request ever passes these gates.

**Failure scenario.** A pilot customer's regulatory writer is invited as `manager` (the natural role for a non-admin author). Their JWT carries role='manager', so roles=['manager']. They open the IND authoring workspace and POST /api/submission-readiness/assess: requireRole('regulatory-author') → allowedRoles.some(...) is false and userRoles does not include 'admin' → 403 {code:'AUTH_004', message:'Insufficient permissions'}. The same 403 blocks eTMF, CDISC validation, IND forms, SPL/FHIR, human factors, 524B cybersecurity, submission PDF generation and global-RI. The only workaround is to promote every author to org admin, which simultaneously grants them the requireOrgAccess and requirePermission admin bypasses (AUTH-14) and the audit/SIEM router (server/routes/admin/audit-siem.ts:42).

**Fix.** Either add 'regulatory-author' (and any other capability roles the routes assume) to the assignable role set in tenant-users.ts, SCIM VALID_ROLES and the RBAC hierarchy, or replace requireRole('regulatory-author') with rbacService.requirePermission('<resource>','write') (server/services/roleBasedAccess.ts:263), which is DB-backed and org-scoped. Then remove the blanket `userRoles.includes('admin')` override so authority checks are explicit.

#### `P1` AUTH-05 — SCIM deprovisioning does not deprovision — a deactivated user can still log in

`server/routes/scim.ts:672` · blocks **G2** · ~days · lifecycle-management

**Evidence.** DELETE /scim/v2/Users/:id (the documented offboarding path — the module header at :5-6 says users are 'critically — DEPROVISIONED automatically when an employee is offboarded') executes only `UPDATE users SET status = 'inactive', updated_at = now() WHERE id = $1` (:672). The organization_users membership row is left in place. `users.status` exists in shared/schema.ts:2555 (`status: text('status').default('active').notNull()`), but no authentication path reads it: POST /api/auth/login (server/routes/auth.ts:355-486) checks lockout, password hash and org membership only; /api/auth/mfa/verify (:1266-1390) does not check it; server/middleware/orgMembership.ts:116-126 only asserts a membership row exists. PATCH .../Users/:id with active=false (:626-632) has the same effect.

**Failure scenario.** Okta offboards jane@customer.com on her last day; the IdP issues DELETE /scim/v2/Users/41. The row becomes status='inactive' and the customer's Okta console shows the deprovision succeeded. The next morning Jane opens the app, enters her unchanged password, receives the email OTP at her still-active personal-forward mailbox, and gets a full 24h session with her original org role and full access to the tenant's regulatory documents. Nothing in the product blocked her; the SCIM audit row says she was deactivated.

**Fix.** In the SCIM deactivate paths, delete (or mark revoked) the organization_users row and call invalidateOrgMembershipCache(userId, orgId). Independently, add a `status === 'active'` check to POST /api/auth/login, /api/auth/mfa/verify, /api/auth/enterprise/verify-password and to enforceOrgMembership so existing tokens die within the cache TTL.

#### `P1` AUTH-06 — Production executes a different, weaker auth middleware than dev and tests do

`scripts/ci/check-js-ts-shadows.mjs:60` · blocks **G2** · ~days · build-runtime-divergence

**Evidence.** The repo's own gate documents the hazard and allowlists it: `'server/middleware/auth.js', // why: diverged legacy middleware; prod bundles it for ~26 .ts routes importing '../middleware/auth.js' (ledger M-5).` (:60). I verified the resolver behaviour empirically with this repo's own toolchain: a .ts entry importing './mod.js' where both mod.ts and mod.js exist resolves to the .js twin under esbuild (the production build, scripts/build-server.mjs) and to the .ts twin under tsx (npm run dev). 34 non-test modules import '../middleware/auth.js' (server/routes/billing.ts:32, ind-submissions.routes.ts:13, global-compliance.ts:21, qc.routes.ts:18, evidence-fabric.ts:18, pyramid.routes.ts:43, plus 8 server/bootstrap/register-*.ts files). server/middleware/auth.js has no nonAccessTokenReason check (compare server/middleware/auth.ts:128), no enforceOrgMembership call (compare auth.ts:157), and an isPublicRoute() bypass at :29 covering '/health', '/auth/login', '/api/public' etc.

**Failure scenario.** pyramid.routes.ts is mounted at /api/v1, which server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:85 allowlists as a prefix, so the boundary never runs and the route's own authenticateToken is the only gate. In production that import resolves to server/middleware/auth.js, which does not reject non-access tokens. A holder of a refresh token or an MFA-partial token sends `Authorization: Bearer <refresh token>` to GET /api/v1/pyramids/types and is authenticated — the exact control the boundary was built to enforce is absent on this prefix. More broadly, every route-level authorization test in server/routes/__tests__ runs auth.ts (or the vitest-resolved twin) while production runs auth.js, so the test suite does not exercise the code that ships.

**Fix.** Delete server/middleware/auth.js and repoint all 34 importers at '../middleware/auth' (extensionless) or, if the file must stay, reduce it to `export * from './auth.ts'` like the other allowlisted shims (server/utils/jwtVerify.js). Until then, apply the boundary's authenticateToken to /api/v1 sub-routers that are not API-key surfaces.

#### `P1` AUTH-07 — trust proxy is never configured, collapsing every auth rate limiter into one global bucket and breaking the SCIM IP allowlist

`server/index.ts:89` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · deployment-configuration

**Evidence.** `const app = express();` (:89) with no `app.set('trust proxy', ...)` anywhere in server/ or scripts/ — the only occurrence in the repo is a test harness, server/__tests__/security/scim-ip-allowlist-enforcement.test.ts:46 (`app.set('trust proxy', true); // req.ip = leftmost X-Forwarded-For`), i.e. the test asserts behaviour production does not have. server/routes/charters.ts:77-80 explicitly states 'The bootstrap MUST set trust proxy for the deployment topology' — it does not. Every auth limiter is keyed on req.ip and passes `validate: { xForwardedForHeader: false }`, which suppresses express-rate-limit's warning about exactly this: loginLimiter (server/routes/auth.ts:111-121, 10/15min), signupLimiter (:124-134), mfaLimiter (:153-163), passwordResetLimiter (:137-150), enterpriseAuthLimiter (server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:135-148), scimRateLimiter (server/routes/scim.ts:35-46). server/routes/scim.ts:182-184 clientIpOf() and :265 feed req.ip into isIpAllowedForOrg (:191-197).

**Failure scenario.** Deployed behind any ALB/nginx/Replit proxy, req.ip is the proxy's address for every request. At 09:00 on Monday, the 11th user across the entire platform to attempt a login inside a 15-minute window receives 429 'Too many login attempts' — a self-inflicted platform-wide login outage that gets worse with customer count, and a trivially triggerable DoS (11 bogus POSTs to /api/auth/login locks out every real user for 15 minutes). Simultaneously, a tenant that configures a SCIM IP allowlist of their Okta egress range gets 403 'Source IP not permitted' on every sync because the compared IP is the load balancer's, and every Part 11 audit row written by auditAuthEvent (server/routes/auth.ts:382) records the proxy IP instead of the actor's.

**Fix.** Set `app.set('trust proxy', <exact hop count for the deployment>)` in server/index.ts before applyCoreMiddleware, and re-enable express-rate-limit's xForwardedForHeader validator so a misconfiguration is loud. Do not use `true` (blindly trusting the leftmost XFF lets a client spoof its own rate-limit key).

#### `P1` AUTH-08 — Electronic-signature identity is taken from the request body and is not tenant-scoped

`server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:651` · blocks **G3** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/enterprise/electronic-signature runs authMiddleware, then ignores the authenticated principal: `signerId: req.body.signerId,` with the comment '// signerId is identity — sourced from the request body exactly as before ... It is intentionally NOT part of the validated schema as a trusted field' (:649-652). signerName/signerTitle/signerEmail are likewise caller-supplied strings from electronicSignatureSchema (:95-112) and are stored verbatim as the Part 11 signature manifest's printed name (server/services/auth-security-service.ts:503-514). createElectronicSignature (auth-security-service.ts:434-543) re-authenticates against that arbitrary signerId's password hash and inserts into electronicSignatures with `documentId: params.documentId` — there is no organizationId column read, written or compared anywhere in the function, and no check that documentId belongs to the caller's tenant.

**Failure scenario.** User A (authenticated, org 1) POSTs /api/auth/enterprise/electronic-signature with {documentId: 8842, versionId: 3, signerId: <A's own id>, signerName: 'Dr. Rachel Kim, VP Regulatory', signatureType: 'approval', signatureMeaning: 'Approved for submission', password: <A's own password>}. Document 8842 belongs to org 2. The insert succeeds: org 2's document now carries an approval signature attributed to a printed name A invented, created by a user with no relationship to that tenant. GET /api/auth/enterprise/electronic-signature/:id/verify returns valid:true because the integrity hash is recomputed from the same row.

**Fix.** Derive signerId, signerName, signerTitle and signerEmail from req.user / the users row, never from the body. Reject the request if req.body.signerId is present and differs from the session user. Resolve documentId's organization and 403 unless it matches authedOrgId(req). Route all signing through the canonical part11ComplianceService path already used by server/routes/submission-sign-release.ts:283 and retire this parallel endpoint.

#### `P2` AUTH-09 — Any configured SAML tenant's IdP can assert another tenant's user and get a session in that user's org

`server/routes/sso.ts:617` · blocks **G2** · ~days · federated-identity

**Evidence.** The ACS handler selects the trust anchor from the untrusted RelayState org slug (`const { org: orgSlug, returnTo } = decodeRelayState(relayState);` :246, then `getSamlConfig(orgSlug)` :248) and validates the assertion against that org's IdP certificate. findOrCreateSamlUser then matches on email alone and hands back the matched user's OWN organization: `const organizationId = orgAssoc.length > 0 ? orgAssoc[0].organizationId : configOrgId;` and `const orgRole = orgAssoc.length > 0 ? orgAssoc[0].role : 'member';` (:617-618). The issued JWT uses that org and role (:295-307). There is no check that the asserted email's domain is claimed by the org whose IdP signed the assertion.

**Failure scenario.** The deployment serves two enterprise tenants via SAML_TENANTS: 'acme' and 'globex'. A rogue (or compromised) Okta administrator at globex mints an assertion for NameID/email cfo@acme.com and POSTs it to /api/auth/sso/saml/callback with RelayState encoding {org:'globex'}. node-saml validates the signature against globex's certificate — it is genuinely signed. findOrCreateSamlUser finds the existing acme user cfo@acme.com and returns organizationId=<acme's org id>, orgRole=<the CFO's real role, e.g. admin>. The response is a 24h JWT scoped to acme with the CFO's privileges. Globex's IdP admin now has admin access to acme's tenant.

**Fix.** Bind verified email domains to organizations (a saml_domains table or an allowed_domains column on the SAML tenant config) and reject any assertion whose email domain is not claimed by the org that owns the validating certificate. Additionally, refuse to issue a session for an existing user whose org association differs from configOrgId — a cross-org match should be an error, not a silent org switch.

#### `P2` AUTH-10 — Role is read from the JWT claim and never re-checked, so a demoted admin keeps admin authority for up to 24 hours

`server/middleware/auth.ts:149` · blocks **G2** · ~days · authorization

**Evidence.** authenticateToken populates `role: decoded.role || 'user'` and `roles: decoded.roles || [decoded.role || 'user']` straight from the token (:149-150), then calls enforceOrgMembership, which only asserts that an organization_users row exists — it selects the role but discards it (server/middleware/orgMembership.ts:116-126, :190-192). requireRole (:194-197), requireOrgAccess (:221) and requirePermission (:267) all read req.user.role/roles. Access tokens are minted with `expiresIn: JWT_EXPIRES_IN` = '24h' (server/routes/auth.ts:96, :1325-1335). The sibling authenticator server/auth.ts:133 does re-derive the role from the membership row (`const resolvedRole = membership[0].role;`), so the two authenticators disagree about whether the token's role is authoritative.

**Failure scenario.** A customer discovers an admin has gone rogue and at 14:00 uses PUT /api/tenants/:id/users/:userId to change their role from 'admin' to 'viewer'. invalidateOrgMembershipCache drops the membership cache, but the membership row still exists so enforceOrgMembership passes. The rogue admin's existing token still carries role='admin', so requireRole, requireOrgAccess (:221 admin bypass) and requirePermission (:267 admin bypass) all continue to treat them as an admin until the token expires the next day. The customer believes access was revoked.

**Fix.** Have enforceOrgMembership return the live role and overwrite req.user.role/req.user.roles with it (the query already selects it), matching server/auth.ts:133. Cache the role alongside the membership boolean and keep the existing invalidation hooks.

#### `P2` AUTH-11 — The entire enterprise RBAC admin API is dead — every endpoint calls service methods that do not exist

`server/api/enterprise/rbac-routes.js:25` · blocks **G1** · ~days · broken-surface

**Evidence.** rbac-routes.js (mounted at /api/enterprise/rbac, server/bootstrap/register-core-routes.ts:63) calls rbacService.getAllRoles (:25), rbacService.pool.query (:56, :369, :387), rbacService.assignRole (x2), rbacService.revokeRole, rbacService.createCustomRole, rbacService.getUserPermissions and rbacService.hasPermission. The RBACService class it imports (server/services/roleBasedAccess.ts, re-exported by the .js shim at server/services/roleBasedAccess.js) defines only checkPermission (:124), getUserRoles (:210), hasRole (:248), requirePermission (:263), requireRole (:292) and invalidateUser (:325). There is no `pool` property and none of the other seven methods exist.

**Failure scenario.** An admin opens the RBAC administration screen and it calls GET /api/enterprise/rbac/roles. `rbacService.getAllRoles()` throws TypeError: rbacService.getAllRoles is not a function, the handler's catch returns 500 {error:'Failed to retrieve roles'}. The same happens for role assignment, revocation, custom-role creation and per-user permission views. Every role-management journey through this router is a 500; the only working role management is the separate PUT in server/routes/tenant-users.ts.

**Fix.** Either implement the missing methods on RBACService (backed by organization_users and client_user_permissions) or delete the router and the mount at register-core-routes.ts:63 so the surface is honestly absent rather than silently broken. Add a smoke test that exercises each mounted admin endpoint for a non-5xx status.

#### `P2` AUTH-12 — The default-deny auth boundary runs in log-and-continue mode in every environment except NODE_ENV=production

`server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:127` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · configuration-posture

**Evidence.** `return env.NODE_ENV === 'production' ? 'enforce' : 'warn';` (:127). In warn mode the boundary runs authenticateToken against a fake response object that swallows the 401 and calls next() (:174-193), logging once per method+path per process. The boundary is the only auth on families that never applied their own middleware — its module header states it exists because 'anything mounted BEFORE that gate (diagnostic endpoints, /api/users, admin routers) silently bypassed it' (:6-8). server/config/environment.ts treats 'staging' as a first-class environment (ENV_MAP, isStaging at :298, and getRefreshTokenSecret enforces dedicated secrets for `ENV === 'production' || ENV === 'staging'` at :210), so staging is expected to hold real data.

**Failure scenario.** A pilot customer is onboarded onto the staging deployment (NODE_ENV=staging) with real, non-public trial documents. AUTH_BOUNDARY_MODE is not set because production doesn't need it. An unauthenticated GET /api/users or GET /api/documents from the public internet is logged as 'Unauthenticated request passed through auth boundary (mode=warn)' and then handed to a handler that has no auth of its own — the response body is returned to the anonymous caller. The warning is logged once per path per process, so a scan of 500 endpoints produces 500 log lines and 500 responses.

**Fix.** Invert the default: enforce unless AUTH_BOUNDARY_MODE is explicitly set to 'warn', and refuse to boot in warn mode when ENV is production or staging (same fail-closed posture as getRefreshTokenSecret). At minimum default to enforce for staging.

#### `P2` AUTH-13 — SSO dev callback mints a real 24h token for a hardcoded user, outside the dev-auth policy and invisible to its CI gate

`server/routes/sso.ts:540` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · dev-bypass

**Evidence.** `const isDev = process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development';` (:19). GET /api/auth/sso/:provider/initiate redirects to the callback with a mock code when isDev (:527-531), and GET /api/auth/sso/:provider/callback then signs a real access token with no credential of any kind: `jwt.sign({ userId: '1', email: 'sso-user@example.com', organizationId: '2', role: 'client_user', provider }, config.jwt.secret, { expiresIn: '24h' })` (:540-552), returned in a redirect query string. server/auth/dev-auth-policy.ts:17-20 states the hard rule 'Any new dev-auth shortcut added to the codebase MUST gate behind isDevAuthAllowed()' (NODE_ENV=development AND ALLOW_DEV_AUTH=1); this one is not. The enforcing gate cannot see it: scripts/ci/check-no-dev-auth-in-prod.mjs scans only paths matching AUTH_FILE_PATTERNS (:45-55), which include server/routes/auth*.ts but not server/routes/sso.ts, and it greps for `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`, not `=== 'development'`. Running the gate reports 'OK — no leaky dev-auth gates found'.

**Failure scenario.** A sales engineer runs a demo instance with NODE_ENV=development (ALLOW_DEV_AUTH unset, which the team believes disables all dev auth) and exposes it for a prospect walkthrough. Anyone who reaches GET /api/auth/sso/google/initiate is redirected to the callback and handed a valid 24h JWT for userId 1 in organizationId 2 — full access with zero credentials, and the token is visible in the URL, browser history and any proxy log.

**Fix.** Gate both handlers behind isDevAuthAllowed() and return 404 otherwise, matching /api/auth/dev-login (server/routes/auth.ts:646-651). Extend AUTH_FILE_PATTERNS in check-no-dev-auth-in-prod.mjs to cover server/routes/sso.ts and add `NODE_ENV === 'development'` to the forbidden pattern set alongside any jwt.sign not inside an isDevAuthAllowed() block.

#### `P2` AUTH-14 — requireOrgAccess grants org admins unconditional cross-org access and is a no-op on every route that actually uses it

`server/middleware/auth.ts:221` · blocks **G2** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** `if (req.user.role === 'admin' || req.user.roles?.includes('admin')) { return next(); }` (:220-222) — comment reads 'Admin users have access to all organizations', but 'admin' here is an ORGANIZATION role assigned to every customer's own admin (server/routes/auth.ts:875 on signup), not a platform role. For non-admins the check only fires when the request happens to carry an org id: `req.params.orgId || req.params.organizationId || req.body?.organizationId || req.query?.organizationId` (:226-231); when none is present it calls next() unconditionally. All 11 uses in server/routes/cortexRoutes.ts (:172, 200, 215, 296, 326, 374, 406, 434, 455, 482, 503) are on routes with no such parameter — e.g. PATCH /api/cortex/atoms/:id (:199-207) passes `String(req.params.id)` to cortex.updateAtom with no org argument at all. The repo already knows about the admin bypass: server/routes/admin/scim-ip-allowlist.ts:25-26 says 'requireRole was unusable here: its legacy org-admin bypass let every customer org admin reach this cross-tenant router.'

**Failure scenario.** A customer's own org admin (role='admin', org 7) calls any endpoint that composes requireOrgAccess with an orgId parameter, e.g. GET /api/<resource>/organizations/9/... — the guard returns next() at :221 without comparing 9 to 7, and the handler proceeds against org 9. Separately, on PATCH /api/cortex/atoms/:id the guard passes for any authenticated user because no org id is present in params, body or query, so tenant isolation for that route depends entirely on the cortex service, which is not given an org id.

**Fix.** Remove the 'admin' short-circuit (use a distinct platform_admin claim verified against a DB grant, as requirePlatformAdmin already does). Make requireOrgAccess fail closed when it cannot determine the target org, and pass authedOrgId(req) into the cortex service calls so the guard is not the only isolation.

#### `P2` AUTH-15 — Two divergent MFA implementations: the e-signature path reads TOTP secrets as plaintext that the live enrolment path stores encrypted

`server/services/auth-security-service.ts:271` · blocks **G3** · ~days · gxp-compliance

**Evidence.** auth-security-service.generateMFASecret writes the secret in the clear: `.set({ mfaSecret: secret.base32, mfaBackupCodes: hashedBackupCodes, mfaMethod: 'totp' })` (:268-275), and verifyMFACode reads it as such: `speakeasy.totp.verify({ secret: mfaSecret!, encoding: 'base32', token: code, window: 1 })` (:319-324). The live enrolment path is the other implementation: server/services/mfaService.ts:326 `const encryptedSecret = encrypt(secretBase32);` producing an `iv:authTag:ciphertext` hex string (mfaService.ts:187-198). Backup codes also disagree: mfaService hashes `code.replace(/-/g,'').toUpperCase()` (mfaService.ts:298) while auth-security-service hashes `code.toUpperCase()` with dashes intact (:338). verifyMFACode is the only MFA check used by createElectronicSignature (:476-484).

**Failure scenario.** A user enrols TOTP through the live path (POST /api/auth/mfa/setup → mfaService.generateSecret), so users.mfa_secret holds '9f3a...:1b2c...:d4e5...' and mfaEnabled becomes true. They then attempt a 21 CFR Part 11 electronic signature: createElectronicSignature sees user.mfaEnabled === true, requires an mfaCode, and calls verifyMFACode, which base32-decodes the AES ciphertext as if it were a secret. No code will ever match, and the dash-formatted backup codes hash differently too, so the signature is rejected with 'MFA verification failed' every time — the user can never e-sign. For every other user (mfaEnabled=false, the default because email OTP does not set that flag) the signature is accepted on password alone and the manifest records `authenticationMethod: 'password'`, `secondFactorVerified: false` (:510-511, :528-530), so the two-factor e-signature path is unreachable in practice.

**Fix.** Delete the duplicate MFA implementation in auth-security-service.ts and have createElectronicSignature call mfaService.verifyToken. Require a second factor for every e-signature regardless of the mfaEnabled flag (email OTP is available to all users), and stop recording secondFactorVerified:false as a compliant Part 11 signature.

#### `P3` AUTH-16 — The header-impersonation guard in validateTenantContext is dead code because it is mounted before the auth boundary

`server/middleware/enterprise-security.ts:525` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · audit-trail

**Evidence.** validateTenantContext gates everything on `const user = (req as any).user;` then `if (user?.organizationId) { ... }` (:522-525).
