It is mounted inside applySecurityMiddleware (`app.use(validateTenantContext);` :976), which runs from applyCoreMiddleware — server/index.ts:113 — while the auth boundary that populates req.user is mounted ten lines later at server/index.ts:123 (applyAuthBoundary → startup/middleware.ts:114). No middleware before :113 sets req.user (validateApiKey at :989 sets authMethod/tenantId/apiScopes but never req.user, enterprise-security.ts:714-718). So for every ordinary JWT request the block never executes: the 403 TENANT_MISMATCH (:557-560), the `tenant_impersonation_attempt` audit write (:545-552) and the reportSecurityAlert (:551-556) never fire.

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated user in org 1 sends `X-Organization-Id: 2` on every request to probe for tenant leakage. The advertised control returns 403 and writes an audit event; in fact nothing happens — req.user is undefined at that point, the block is skipped, and the probe proceeds silently. A Part 11 inspector or SOC reviewing the audit trail for impersonation attempts sees zero events regardless of how many were attempted. (Data access itself is still safe: authedOrgId (server/utils/authedOrgId.ts:39-47) and getSecureOrgId (server/utils/tenantContext.ts:64-85) source the org from the JWT, so this is a lost detection and audit control, not a lost read control.)

**Fix.** Move `app.use(validateTenantContext)` out of applySecurityMiddleware and mount it in server/index.ts immediately AFTER applyAuthBoundary, or re-mount a second instance there. Add an assertion test on middleware ordering so the dependency cannot silently regress again.

#### `P3` AUTH-17 — Electronic-signature integrity verification is self-referential and detects no tampering

`server/services/auth-security-service.ts:581` · blocks **G3** · ~days · gxp-compliance

**Evidence.** verifySignatureIntegrity rebuilds the payload entirely from the row it is checking — documentId, versionId, signerId, signerEmail, signatureType, signatureMeaning, signedAt (:581-590) — SHA-256s it with no key or server-held secret (:591-594), and compares to sig.signatureHash stored in the same row (:596). createElectronicSignature computes the hash the same way (:487-500). There is no HMAC key, no chaining to a prior record, and no signature over the hash.

**Failure scenario.** An operator (or an attacker with a DB connection or a SQL-injection foothold elsewhere) updates electronic_signatures SET signature_meaning='Approved for submission', signature_type='approval' for a row that originally said 'Reviewed', recomputes SHA-256 over the seven documented fields with a two-line script, and writes the new value into signature_hash. GET /api/auth/enterprise/electronic-signature/:id/verify then returns valid:true, hashIntegrity:'VERIFIED'. The 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(c)/(e) tamper-evidence claim, and the stored complianceStatement 'legally binding equivalent of a handwritten signature', are unsupported for this table.

**Fix.** Replace the bare SHA-256 with an HMAC keyed by a secret the application layer holds and the database does not (the repo already provisions AUDIT_HMAC_KEY — see assertAuditSealPostureForProduction in server/config/environment.ts:292), and chain each signature to the previous row's hash so deletion is also detectable.

#### `P3` AUTH-18 — Signup issues a full 24h admin session with no email verification and no MFA

`server/routes/auth.ts:906` · blocks **G2** · ~days · identity-proofing

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/signup creates the org and the user with `role: 'admin'` (:875) and immediately returns `const token = jwt.sign({ userId, email, organizationId, organizationUuid, role: 'admin' }, config.jwt.secret, { expiresIn: JWT_EXPIRES_IN })` (:906-918) in a 201 response. There is no email-ownership challenge anywhere in the handler, and the mandatory-MFA path that /api/auth/login always takes (:572-622, 'Always require 2FA') is not applied. The only throttle is signupLimiter, 5 per hour per IP (:124-134) — which is per-proxy-IP given AUTH-07.

**Failure scenario.** An attacker signs up as legal@bigpharma.com with a company name matching a real customer. They receive an immediate admin session and a live tenant, and the platform sends a welcome email to the real owner of that address (:922-930). The tenant now exists in the customer's namespace with an attacker-controlled admin, and any later SAML/SCIM or invite flow keyed on that email collides with it (see AUTH-09's email-matching provisioning).

**Fix.** Require an emailed verification link before issuing a session token at signup (or issue only a limited 'unverified' token that cannot reach tenant data), and record the verification in the audit trail as the account-creation authority check.

#### `P3` AUTH-19 — The per-user email-OTP attempt cap is resettable on demand via /mfa/resend

`server/routes/auth.ts:1423` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · brute-force

**Evidence.** verifyEmailOtp enforces MAX_ATTEMPTS=5 by reading and incrementing users.email_otp_attempts (server/services/emailOtpService.ts:40, :113-122). POST /api/auth/mfa/resend calls `const otp = await emailOtpService.createEmailOtp(userId);` (server/routes/auth.ts:1423), and createEmailOtp unconditionally writes `emailOtpAttempts: 0` (emailOtpService.ts:70-77). /mfa/resend requires only the 5-minute challenge token, no proof of email possession. The shared mfaLimiter is 10 requests/15min keyed on req.ip (server/routes/auth.ts:153-163), which AUTH-07 collapses to a single global bucket behind a proxy.

**Failure scenario.** An attacker with the victim's password obtains a challengeId, then loops: 5x POST /api/auth/mfa/verify with guessed 6-digit codes, 1x POST /api/auth/mfa/resend to zero the counter, repeat. The per-OTP brute-force cap the service documents ('Max 5 verification attempts per OTP (prevents brute force)', emailOtpService.ts:19) never binds; only the IP-keyed limiter does, and a distributed source or a shared-proxy deployment blunts that. Each resend also mails the victim a fresh code, which doubles as a harassment/notification-fatigue vector.

**Fix.** Track resends and total verification attempts per challenge (or per user per window) in the DB and refuse to reset emailOtpAttempts on resend — carry the counter forward and invalidate the login attempt entirely after N cumulative failures. Add a per-user (not per-IP) limiter on /mfa/verify and /mfa/resend.

### 21 CFR Part 11: audit chain & e-signature

The cryptographic core is genuinely good and I verified it independently: server/services/audit/chain.ts + audit-hmac-seal.ts implement a SHA-256 hash chain sealed with a keyed HMAC using a shared canonical serialization (writer and verifier cannot drift), constant-time seal comparison, a fail-closed integrity verdict (audit-integrity-service.ts refuses to report ok:true when seals are unverifiable), a real production boot matrix (auditSealPosture.ts, fired from environment.ts:292), and a DB-backed PGlite integration test that actually tampers a row and proves detection. 53/53 unit tests in this area pass. That is better than most vendors in this category. The problem is everything around it. Three independent classes of defect: (1) the audit_events chain — the SIEM/export-facing chain that four separate "integrity" surfaces report on — is populated exclusively by a DB trigger in db/migrations/20260222_audit_events_hash_chain.sql that is on NO production apply path (I enumerated C2C_MIGRATION_FILES by executing the module; it is not there, not in the root migrations/ overlay, and not matched by the CI *_gcc_* psql loop), so every hash is NULL and every verifier affirmatively reports "intact"/"healthy"/"verified" over an unchained table; the DB immutability triggers for audit_logs and audit_events are missing for the same reason. (2) The audit_logs chain has a proven ordering defect — the writer picks its predecessor by (occurred_at DESC, id DESC) while the verifier walks (occurred_at ASC, id ASC), so two audit writes in the same millisecond whose UUIDs sort the wrong way permanently break the chain; I reproduced ok:false against PGlite. (3) The e-signature surface is split across three mutually inconsistent implementations, one of which (/api/part11/signatures) cannot execute at all because its INSERT targets columns that do not exist, while artifact promotion to "approved" binds no signature yet writes a DOCUMENT_SIGNED event into the audit trail. Dominant risk for a buyer: the platform does not merely lack Part 11 controls in places — it affirmatively asserts that controls are working when they are not, which is the single worst posture in front of an FDA inspector or a GxP vendor qualification.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- The hash-chain + keyed-seal design is genuinely sound and I verified it independently. server/services/audit/chain.ts:142-154 and :65-74 use the SAME canonical JSON serialization for the writer and the verifier via a shared deriveChainHash, so they cannot drift on key order or whitespace — the single most common way homegrown audit chains silently stop detecting tampering.
- server/services/audit/audit-hmac-seal.ts:87-93 pins an explicit newline-delimited byte layout rather than JSON.stringify of an object, with an explicit comment about field-boundary ambiguity ('ab'+'c' vs 'a'+'bc'); :120-138 uses crypto.timingSafeEqual with a correct length pre-check and returns false rather than throwing on malformed input; :70-78 refuses to compute a seal under an empty key. This is careful, correct crypto engineering.
- server/services/audit/audit-integrity-service.ts:60-63 is properly fail-closed: `ok = chain.ok && seals.checked && seals.valid`, with an explicit `unverifiable` flag so 'we could not check' can never be read as 'verified'. Many vendors report the SHA-256 chain alone as proof of integrity; this code explicitly refuses to.
- There is a real DB-backed integration test, not a mock. server/services/audit/__tests__/audit-integrity-pglite.integration.test.ts seeds a chain through the actual production writer against in-process PGlite, then at :60-66 runs `UPDATE audit_logs SET target='tampered'` and asserts detection, and at :71-79 asserts ok:false when the key is absent. I ran the suite: 53/53 tests pass across 6 files in 4.65s.
- server/services/audit/auditSealPosture.ts implements a real production boot matrix — refuse to boot on a short key regardless of the escape hatch (:90-100), refuse to boot on a missing key unless AUDIT_SEAL_ACCEPT_UNSEALED=true (:113-119), and log a structured accepted-risk warning naming controlledBy/acceptedVia/acceptedRisk/remediation when the operator opts in (:124-131). It is wired fire-on-import from server/config/environment.ts:292. The fail-closed boot claim for AUDIT_HMAC_KEY holds.
- server/routes/c2c/actions.ts::recordGovernedAction (:275-340) is the correct Part 11 write pattern in this codebase: it takes the CALLER's transaction client and writes the chained, sealed audit_logs row and the c2c_ana_actions ledger row inside the same transaction as the business mutation, with tenant context stamped transaction-locally (writeMutation :419) so it survives RLS_ENFORCE=on.
- server/routes/ectd-documents.ts:328-360 (and coauthor.ts) delete the regulated record and insert the audit_events row in ONE transaction with the delete's RETURNING gating the audit write — genuinely atomic and fail-closed, exactly as the comment claims.
- The CI gate scripts/ci/check-regulated-delete-audit.mjs is real, runs on every change (.github/workflows/ci.yml:140), has an EMPTY allowlist, and deliberately requires a call/statement rather than a bare word so a comment cannot mask a gap (:44-46). I executed it: '[regulated-delete-audit] OK — every regulated-table delete has an audit call'.
- server/services/audit/audit-archive.service.ts does verify-after-write end to end rather than trusting an in-memory variable: the filesystem sink re-reads and re-hashes the file (:216-220) and the S3 sink re-GETs the object and recomputes the rows hash (:305-313), with the delete gated on a checksum match (:114-119). The canonical JSON (:185-194) sorts keys so an external auditor needs only one canonicalizer.
- server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:1103-1160 is honest where it would be easy not to be: it reports every Part 11 section as 'not_assessed' with an explicit disclaimer that the platform does not self-certify, and returns readinessScore: null — the comment records that this previously returned every section as 'compliant' with a fabricated 0.92 SOC 2 score. That reversal is exactly the behaviour a buyer wants to see.
- server/startup/audit-enforcement.ts makes a default-off posture VISIBLE rather than silent: a loud production boot warning naming the exact variable and remediation, with an opt-in hard fail via AUDIT_REQUIRE_ENFORCE. Mirrors the RLS posture pattern.
- server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:382-406 binds signer identity to the authenticated session and rejects a body-supplied signerId that does not match, citing §11.200(a)(2) — the right control, correctly implemented (even though the route's INSERT is broken downstream).
- server/routes/esignature.ts implements §11.70 content binding properly: it loads the version's bytes server-side through a tenant-scoped join on documents.organization_id (:310-317) so a foreign versionId cannot be signed, and buildVersionBindingDigest (server/services/part11/version-binding.ts:44-48) throws on empty content so an unbindable signature is refused rather than silently applied.
- server/services/audit/signedAuditExport.ts:207-214 prefixes CSV formula-triggering characters, closing CSV injection on an export inspectors will open in Excel.
- server/services/ana-ri/governed-action-signoff.ts is a clean, dependency-injected, fail-closed re-authentication verifier — password required, MFA state failure fails closed rather than skipping the factor (:76-80), and it never trusts a client 'already verified' flag.

#### `P0` AUDIT-01 — audit_events hash chain is never populated in any deployable database, and four separate integrity surfaces report it "intact"

`db/migrations/20260222_audit_events_hash_chain.sql:99` · blocks **G1** · ~days · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** The ONLY thing that writes audit_events.record_hash / previous_hash / sequence_number is the DB trigger created here: `CREATE TRIGGER trg_audit_events_hash_chain ... EXECUTE FUNCTION audit_events_hash_chain();`. No application code sets those columns — every INSERT INTO audit_events in server/ (chainIntegrityMonitor.ts:163, signedAuditExport.ts:299, part11-compliance.ts:286, ectd-documents.ts:342, coauthor.ts:251, ind.ts:287, scim.ts:300, audit-trail-routes.ts:259, ivdr-pack-worker.ts:108, project-hierarchy.ts:303) omits them. That trigger file is on NO apply path: I enumerated the production set by executing the module — `node -e "import('./scripts/db/migration-set.mjs')..."` returns 23 files and this is not one of them (scripts/db/migration-set.mjs:54); install-fresh.mjs:121-135 overlays only MIGRATIONS_DIR = `migrations/` (root tree) plus the four 20260725_authoring_* files; the CI psql loop matches only `db/migrations/*_gcc_*.sql` (.github/workflows/ci.yml:296) and this file has no _gcc_ infix; deploy-aws.yml runs only `npm run db:migrate:deploy` (deploy-migrate.mjs). shared/schema.ts:9851-9853 declares the three columns so drizzle-kit push creates them — nullable and permanently NULL. Consequence in code: server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:900 `const isIntact = brokenLinks.length === 0;` — the loop above it guards both checks (`row.previous_hash !== null` at :927 and `if (row.record_hash && ...)` at :951), so with all-NULL hashes zero checks run and the endpoint returns `chainStatus: 'intact', integrityValid: true` plus the literal string `tamperEvident: 'Each entry is cryptographically linked to its predecessor via SHA-256 hash chain'`. chainIntegrityMonitor.ts:76-84 has the same null-guard and returns status 'healthy'. signedAuditExport.ts:135 same, and stamps `chainIntegrity: {status:'intact'}` into a signed regulatory export manifest. scripts/run-chain-verify.mjs:88-92 same, exits 0 with verdict INTACT. chain.ts:296 `WHERE record_hash IS NOT NULL` returns zero rows so verifyAuditEventsChainSeals returns {valid:true}.

**Failure scenario.** An operator provisions production per the documented procedure (install-fresh.mjs, then deploy-migrate.mjs on every deploy). A user deletes an eCTD document; ectd-documents.ts:342 writes an audit_events row with record_hash NULL. A DBA with write access then UPDATEs that row's `reason` and `user_name` to attribute the deletion to someone else (no immutability trigger either — see AUDIT-04). An inspector opens GET /api/part11/audit-trail/chain-integrity and receives {chainStatus:'intact', integrityValid:true, tamperEvident:'Each entry is cryptographically linked to its predecessor via SHA-256 hash chain'}, and the signed audit export manifest carries chainIntegrity.status='intact' under an HMAC signature. The platform has cryptographically attested to the integrity of a record it never hashed.

**Fix.** Add db/migrations/20260222_audit_events_hash_chain.sql (and 20260617_audit_events_hmac_seal.sql) to C2C_MIGRATION_FILES in scripts/db/migration-set.mjs so deploy-migrate applies them. Independently, make every verifier fail-closed on absence: if any row in scope has record_hash IS NULL, report status 'unverifiable'/integrityValid:null — never 'intact'. Add a boot invariant (server/lib/startup-invariants.ts) that checks pg_trigger for trg_audit_events_hash_chain and marks it critical.

#### `P0` AUDIT-02 — Two audit writes in the same millisecond permanently break the audit_logs chain (writer and verifier disagree on ordering) — reproduced

`server/services/audit/chain.ts:118` · blocks **G1** · ~days · correctness

**Evidence.** Writer (computeAuditChainSealed) selects its predecessor with `ORDER BY occurred_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE` (chain.ts:117-118). Verifier (verifyAuditChain) walks `ORDER BY occurred_at ASC, id ASC` (chain.ts:189). audit_logs.id is a random UUID (shared/schema.ts:1958 `uuid('id').default(gen_random_uuid())`; server/services/auditService.ts:266 passes `randomUUID()`), and occurred_at is `new Date().toISOString()` — millisecond precision (auditService.ts:245). So when two rows share occurred_at, the writer's predecessor (max id) and the verifier's predecessor (previous row in ascending id order) are different rows ~50% of the time. I reproduced this against in-process PGlite using the real chain.ts writer and verifier: two rows at occurred_at '2026-07-28T12:00:00.000Z' with ids 'ffffffff-...-0001' then '00000000-...-0002' produce `verifyAuditChain => {ok:false, rowsChecked:1, brokenAt:{id:'00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000002', expected:'fd9e9782...', stored:'a50648d5...'}}` and the verifier walk order is ['second.write','first.write']. The same `ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE` pattern also does not prevent concurrent forks: in READ COMMITTED the second transaction blocks on the row it already selected and, on unblocking, re-returns that same row rather than re-evaluating LIMIT 1 against the row the first transaction inserted. The correct pattern is used elsewhere in this repo — db/migrations/20260222_audit_events_hash_chain.sql:64 uses `pg_advisory_xact_lock`.

**Failure scenario.** A user logs in; server/services/audit/auditLogger.ts:98 forwards the event to auditService.logAction, and a second request (e.g. a document view) is audited in the same millisecond. Both rows get occurred_at '2026-...T12:00:00.000Z'. The second row's random UUID happens to sort below the first's. From that moment on, verifyAuditChain returns ok:false forever: the nightly sweep (server/jobs/auditChainIntegritySweep.ts:43) logs 'AUDIT CHAIN BROKEN at row <id> — investigate immediately' every night, GET /api/c2c/actions/verify-chain returns HTTP 409, and GET /api/part11/audit-trail/seal-integrity returns ok:false. Because audit_logs is append-only there is no remediation — the false break is permanent, and it trains operators to ignore genuine tamper alarms.

**Fix.** Serialize the chain on a fixed lock rather than on the last row: `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(<constant>)` before the predecessor SELECT (matching the audit_events trigger). Give audit_logs a monotonic BIGSERIAL sequence column and make BOTH writer and verifier order by that single column, so the traversal order is total and identical. Bind the real sequence number into the HMAC seal instead of the fixed AUDIT_SEAL_SEQ = 0 (chain.ts:32), which currently makes reordering undetectable by the seal.

#### `P0` AUDIT-03 — The Part 11 per-entity audit-trail read endpoint always returns an empty trail with HTTP 200

`server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:701` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · dishonest-empty-state

**Evidence.** `SELECT id, entity_type, entity_id, action, user_id, user_name, user_role, previous_value, new_value, change_reason, created_at, ip_address, session_id, record_hash FROM audit_trail WHERE entity_id = $1 ...`. The audit_trail table (shared/schema.ts:628-655, migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:188) has columns id, trail_id, entity_type, entity_id, leaf_id, action, user_id, user_name, signature, ip_address, user_agent, reason, metadata, timestamp, created_at, updated_at. It has NO user_role, previous_value, new_value, change_reason, session_id or record_hash. The query therefore always raises 42703, and the handler's bare catch at line 730 swallows it: `catch { res.json({ success: true, data: { entries: [], total: 0, entityId } }); }`. Compounding: the chain-integrity block at :709 tests `result.rows[0].hash_signature` and `row.previous_hash`, neither of which is ever SELECTed, so chainIntegrity is hardcoded-by-accident to 'unverifiable' even on the success path.

**Failure scenario.** An FDA investigator (or a customer's QA auditor) opens the audit trail for approved document 4471 via GET /api/part11/audit-trail/4471. The server returns HTTP 200 with {success:true, data:{entries:[], total:0}}. The UI renders 'No audit entries'. The auditor concludes the record has no change history — when in reality the query never ran. There is no error, no warning, no non-200. The same catch also converts a genuine DB outage into 'no audit trail exists'.

**Fix.** Correct the column list to the real audit_trail schema (or point the route at audit_events, which is what the writer POST /audit-trail actually populates via appendAuditEntry). Remove the swallow-all catch: a failed audit-trail read on a regulated surface must return 5xx with a machine code, never a synthetic empty success. Delete the dead chainIntegrity block or SELECT the columns it needs.

#### `P1` AUDIT-04 — The DB-level immutability triggers for audit_logs and audit_events are on no production apply path — audit rows are UPDATE/DELETE-able

`scripts/db/migration-set.mjs:54` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · missing-control

**Evidence.** db/migrations/20260617_audit_logs_immutability.sql creates trg_audit_logs_no_update / _no_delete / _no_truncate, and db/migrations/20260222_audit_events_immutability.sql creates the audit_events equivalents. Neither appears in C2C_MIGRATION_FILES (verified by executing the module: 23 entries, none of them these). Neither is in the root `migrations/` tree that install-fresh.mjs:121-135 overlays. Neither matches the CI `db/migrations/*_gcc_*.sql` loop (.github/workflows/ci.yml:296) — no _gcc_ infix. They appear only in db/migrations/migrations_manifest.json:511 / :444, and the repo's own migration-set.mjs:42 states 'NOTHING consumes that manifest'. The immutability migration's header even says it 'REQUIRES REVIEW BEFORE DEPLOY ... Do NOT auto-apply', and nothing ever did. Meanwhile server/services/audit/audit-archive.service.ts:124-128 and docs/validation/TM-CORTEX-001-PART11-TRACEABILITY.md:31 both describe these triggers as an active control.

**Failure scenario.** An operator or an attacker with DB credentials runs `UPDATE audit_logs SET action='data_access' WHERE id='<uuid of a delete event>'` on the production database. The statement succeeds — there is no trigger. Detection depends entirely on the sha256 chain, which (a) excludes NULL-chain rows entirely (AUDIT-12), and (b) already reports ok:false for benign reasons (AUDIT-02), so the tamper is indistinguishable from the standing false alarm. On audit_events the same UPDATE succeeds and is completely undetectable because record_hash is NULL there (AUDIT-01).

**Fix.** Add both files to C2C_MIGRATION_FILES so deploy-migrate applies them (they are idempotent and guarded on to_regclass). Add a boot invariant that queries pg_trigger for trg_audit_logs_no_update/_no_delete/_no_truncate and trg_audit_events_no_* and fails closed in production. Also fix audit-archive.service.ts:145 first (AUDIT-08) or the archive path will start aborting once the DELETE trigger exists.

#### `P1` AUDIT-05 — Promoting an AI artifact to "approved" binds no electronic signature, yet writes a DOCUMENT_SIGNED event into the Part 11 audit trail

`server/services/ai-actions/handlers/promote-artifact.ts:245` · blocks **G1** · ~days · missing-esignature

**Evidence.** The handler's own marker: `// TODO(compliance): require electronic_signatures record (server/routes/esignature.ts) // for promotion to approved — bind signatureId/manifestHash here.` (also at :428). The gate that actually runs is requireHumanApproval (:434-481): authenticated user, sourceSurface !== 'workflow_trigger', role in APPROVAL_ROLES, and `payload.confirmApproval === true` plus a non-empty `approvalReason` string. There is NO password re-verification, NO MFA, NO isSigningAuthorized() call, and no row written to electronic_signatures or concept2cure_signatures. The transaction at :216-296 sets concept2cureArtifacts.status = 'approved' (:281) and creates a unifiedDocuments row. Then at :306 the audit write is `action: 'signature_apply'`, which server/services/auditService.ts:104 maps to the tamper-proof event type `'DOCUMENT_SIGNED'`. Contrast the real signing path, server/routes/esignature.ts:197-243, which re-verifies bcrypt password + TOTP server-side at the moment of signing.

**Failure scenario.** A user with role 'approver' whose session was authenticated 6 hours ago POSTs {actionType:'promote_artifact', targetId:'art_991', projectId:12, payload:{confirmApproval:true, approvalReason:'ready for submission'}}. Artifact 991 flips to status 'approved', a governed submission-candidate document is created, and audit_logs records action 'signature_apply' while audit.tamper_proof_log records event_type 'DOCUMENT_SIGNED'. No signature exists in electronic_signatures for that document. An inspector reading the audit trail sees a signing event; asked to produce the signature manifestation (§11.50) and the signature/record link (§11.70), the sponsor has nothing. The audit trail itself is now inaccurate, which is a §11.10(e) finding independent of the missing signature.

**Fix.** Either (a) require a real §11.200 signing ceremony before status can reach 'approved' — reuse server/services/ana-ri/governed-action-signoff.ts::verifySignerCredentials plus isSigningAuthorized(resolveSignerOrgRole(...)) — and persist an electronic_signatures row inside the same transaction, storing its id and bound_payload_digest in the approval metadata; or (b) stop calling it a signature: change action to 'artifact.promoted' and remove the signature_apply→DOCUMENT_SIGNED mapping for this path.

#### `P1` AUDIT-06 — POST /api/part11/signatures cannot persist a signature at all (column mismatch), never verifies MFA, and its revoke endpoint does not revoke

`server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:488` · blocks **G1** · ~days · broken-route

**Evidence.** The INSERT at :487-495 targets `electronic_signatures (id, document_id, document_version, signer_id, signer_name, signer_title, signer_organization, meaning, custom_meaning, signature_hash, password_verified, mfa_verified, ip_address, user_agent, timestamp)`. The real table (shared/schema.ts:1955-2015; migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:3182-3207) has none of document_version, signer_organization, meaning, custom_meaning, password_verified, mfa_verified, user_agent or timestamp, and its `id` is `serial` while the route supplies uuidv4(). I searched every migration for those columns — only compliance.electronic_signatures (db/migrations/080_gcc_*, a different schema and shape) and unrelated grdhe/gcc tables have them. The INSERT therefore always raises 42703 and the handler returns 500 ESIGNATURE_PERSIST_FAILED (:526). Second defect at :480: `mfaVerified: !!req.body.mfaToken` — the second §11.200(a)(1) component is recorded as verified purely because a client-supplied string was present; verifyMfaToken is never called on this route. Third defect at :642-676: POST /signatures/:signatureId/revoke writes an audit entry and returns `{revoked:true}` at :673 but issues no UPDATE — the signature row's is_valid stays true forever. Fourth: GET /signatures/:documentId at :580 selects the same nonexistent columns and its catch at :591 returns `{success:true, data:[], message:'Signature table not yet initialized'}` — a 200 with a false explanation. docs/validation/TM-CORTEX-001-PART11-TRACEABILITY.md:34 cites this exact file as the implementing code for P11-08 (§11.50/§11.70).

**Failure scenario.** A pilot customer wires the documented Part 11 signature API. Every POST /api/part11/signatures returns 500. They fall back to reading GET /api/part11/signatures/4471, which returns HTTP 200 with an empty array and 'Signature table not yet initialized' — so the UI shows 'no signatures' for a document that may in fact have signatures written by /api/esignature/sign into the same table. If the INSERT column list is ever repaired without also fixing :480, every signature is written with mfa_verified=true on the strength of a client-supplied string, and every revoke silently no-ops.

**Fix.** Delete this route or rewrite it against the real electronic_signatures shape and delegate to the same code path as server/routes/esignature.ts (which is correct). Replace `!!req.body.mfaToken` with a server-side verifyMfaToken call gated on isMfaEnabled. Make revoke perform the append-only supersession the schema already models (supersededBy at shared/schema.ts:2014) instead of returning a cosmetic {revoked:true}. Remove the catch-to-empty-array in GET.

#### `P1` AUDIT-07 — esignature /sign's "no signature without an audit row" fail-closed guard is unreachable dead code

`server/routes/esignature.ts:413` · blocks **G3** · ~days · false-fail-closed

**Evidence.** esignature.ts:390-392 states 'The audit write is AWAITED before we report success: a signing action whose audit record fails must NOT be reported as signed... If it throws we fail the request', and :413-419 catches auditErr and returns 500 ESIGNATURE_AUDIT_FAILED. But auditService.logAction (server/services/auditService.ts:186-327) cannot reject: the audit_logs transaction is wrapped in try/catch at :239-299 ending in `logger.error('Failed to write chained audit_logs row', error)`, and the tamper-proof write is wrapped at :304-326 ending in `logger.error('Failed to write tamper-proof audit entry', error)`. Every await in the function body is inside one of those two try blocks. The promise always resolves.

**Failure scenario.** The database rejects the chained audit INSERT (e.g. the tenant_id RLS WITH CHECK fails after RLS_ENFORCE is flipped on, or the connection pool is exhausted). auditService.logAction logs an error and resolves. esignature.ts:421 returns HTTP 201 with a signatureId. The electronic_signatures row exists; there is no corresponding audit_logs row and no chain link. The system has recorded a Part 11 signature that the audit trail does not know happened — precisely the state the code comment claims is impossible. The same false guarantee is inherited by every other caller that awaits logAction expecting fail-closed behaviour (promote-artifact.ts:305 comments 'Awaited (not fire-and-forget) because this is a 21 CFR Part 11 governed action').

**Fix.** Give auditService a strict variant (logActionOrThrow) that propagates persistence failures, and use it on all Part 11 signing/approval paths. Better: write the signature row and its audit row in ONE transaction, the way server/routes/c2c/actions.ts::recordGovernedAction already does (it takes the caller's client and INSERTs inside their transaction).

#### `P1` AUDIT-08 — The audit-log archive job's DELETE casts a UUID primary key to int[] — the retention/archival path can never complete a batch

`server/services/audit/audit-archive.service.ts:145` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · correctness

**Evidence.** `await txClient.query('DELETE FROM audit_logs WHERE id = ANY($1::int[])', [ids]);` where `const ids = rows.map(r => (r as any).id)` (:137). audit_logs.id is uuid — shared/schema.ts:1958 `id: uuid('id').primaryKey().default(sql\`gen_random_uuid()\`)`, migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:175 `"id" uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`, and server/services/auditService.ts:266 inserts `randomUUID()`. Postgres rejects `uuid = integer` (42883) / rejects the cast of a uuid text to int (22P02). The unit test cannot catch this: server/services/audit/__tests__/audit-archive.test.ts:13 declares `type Row = { id: number; ... }` and its mock filters on numeric ids, so the test models a schema the product does not have.

**Failure scenario.** An operator runs `npm run audit:archive` against production. The first batch of 1000 rows older than 24 months is read, hashed, and written to S3 (or the filesystem) successfully. The DELETE then raises 'operator does not exist: uuid = integer'; the catch at :147 pushes 'Delete-from-hot failed' into result.errors and `break`s out of the loop. Nothing is ever removed from the hot table. Every subsequent run re-reads the same 1000 rows and writes a NEW cold-storage object with a fresh batchId — unbounded duplicate archives in S3 with no dedup, and audit_logs grows without bound. The documented 24-month hot-retention control (docs/operations/audit-log-retention-policy.md) has never worked.

**Fix.** Change the cast to `$1::uuid[]`. Fix the test fixture to use UUID ids so the type contract is actually asserted. Add a run-once idempotency marker (e.g. an archived_batches table keyed on the min/max created_at of the batch) so a failed delete does not produce duplicate cold objects on retry.

#### `P1` AUDIT-09 — Every scheduled Part 11 retention and chain-verification job exists only as an npm script with no scheduler anywhere in the repo

`package.json:116` · blocks **G3** · ~days · missing-scheduler

**Evidence.** package.json:115-118 define `audit:archive`, `retention:run`, `audit:verify:24h`, `audit:verify:full`. I grepped .github/, helm/, terraform/, infra/, docker-compose*.yml, .replit, Makefile and scripts/ for each name: the only hits are the definitions themselves and doc comments. server/jobs/retentionCron.ts:19-21 states outright 'it is not auto-registered at boot. Invoke runRetentionJob() from server/bin/run-retention.ts or an external scheduler', and server/bin/run-retention.ts has no caller. scripts/run-chain-verify.mjs:12-15 documents the cron entries an operator is expected to create by hand; scripts/run-audit-archive.mjs:9-10 likewise. Nothing in this repository creates them. Note the one job that IS wired — server/jobs/auditChainIntegritySweep.ts, scheduled from server/index.ts:233 with a default-ON production gate — covers audit_logs only, not audit_events.

**Failure scenario.** The buyer deploys via .github/workflows/deploy-aws.yml onto ECS. No cron, no ECS Scheduled Task, no k8s CronJob is created for any of these. Documents whose vault.documents.retention_until passed two years ago are never archived or deleted, so the retention schedule the customer contracted for is silently not enforced; audit_logs never trims; the weekly full audit_events chain verification never runs. During a GxP vendor audit the sponsor is asked to produce evidence of periodic audit-trail review (§11.10(e)) and retention enforcement and has none, because the jobs have never executed.

**Fix.** Ship the schedules as infrastructure: add ECS Scheduled Tasks (or k8s CronJobs in helm/) for retention:run, audit:verify:24h, audit:verify:full and audit:archive, pinned to the same image the migrate task uses. Alternatively register them in-process behind explicit env gates the way auditChainIntegritySweep.ts already is, and log a boot-posture line naming the controlling variable so absence is visible.

#### `P1` AUDIT-10 — TamperProofAuditLog's production fail-closed constructor throw is swallowed — the tamper-proof log silently disables itself

`server/services/auditService.ts:154` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · false-fail-closed

**Evidence.** server/lib/tamper-proof-audit.ts:125-130 throws `[FATAL] AUDIT_HMAC_SECRET is required in production... Refusing to start.` The only construction site is getTamperProofAuditLog(pool) called from auditService.ensureInitialized at :145, and the whole body is wrapped: `} catch (error) { logger.warn('Tamper-proof audit unavailable — fallback to console', error); return null; }` (:154-157). A null return makes the entire step-2 write at :304-323 a no-op. .env.example:521 ships AUDIT_HMAC_SECRET empty. Separately, even when it does initialize, TamperProofAuditLog.log() writes user_id into a UUID column (tamper-proof-audit.ts:156 `user_id UUID`) while auditService.ts:316 passes `entry.userId?.toString()` for an integer user id — every authenticated write raises 22P02 and is swallowed by the catch at :324.

**Failure scenario.** Production boots with AUDIT_HMAC_SECRET unset. The constructor throws exactly as designed; ensureInitialized catches it and logs a warn line among thousands. The service reports healthy. Every audit event thereafter writes only to audit_logs; nothing reaches audit.tamper_proof_log, and auditService.verifyChain() (:415-425) returns {valid:false, entriesVerified:0} which no caller distinguishes from 'tampered'. The 'dual-write audit pipeline' promised in auditService.ts:4-6 is a single write, and the second, independently-signed ledger a GxP assessor would ask for does not exist. Even with the secret set, user_id='42' into a UUID column fails on every authenticated action, so the tamper-proof log contains only anonymous/system events.

**Fix.** Let the production throw escape: re-throw from ensureInitialized when NODE_ENV=production so boot fails, or assert AUDIT_HMAC_SECRET in server/config/environment.ts alongside assertAuditSealPostureForProduction (which does this correctly for AUDIT_HMAC_KEY). Change audit.tamper_proof_log.user_id to TEXT, or coerce/lookup a UUID before writing. Surface tamper-proof availability in /readyz.

#### `P1` AUDIT-11 — The tamper-proof audit interceptor and the continuous chain monitor are default-OFF, and their controlling variable is absent from .env.example

`server/startup/services.ts:252` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · insecure-default

**Evidence.** `if (process.env.AUDIT_TRAIL_ENABLED === 'true') { chainMon.value.startChainMonitor(pool, 5*60*1000); } else { console.log('ℹ️ Audit chain integrity monitor skipped...') }`. server/startup/audit-enforcement.ts:29-30 confirms the same flag gates the global audit interceptor and states it is 'default-off'. AUDIT_TRAIL_ENABLED appears nowhere in .env.example (I grepped the whole repo: only server/startup/*.ts, its tests, and scripts/audits reference it). assertAuditTrailForProduction (audit-enforcement.ts:44-66) only warns unless the operator additionally sets AUDIT_REQUIRE_ENFORCE=true.

**Failure scenario.** A customer follows .env.example to configure production. AUDIT_TRAIL_ENABLED is not in the file, so it is not set. The server boots, prints one warning line, and runs with the global tamper-proof audit interceptor NOT recording mutations and the 5-minute chain-integrity monitor NOT running. The customer believes they have continuous audit-trail integrity monitoring (it is stated as a platform control in part11-compliance.ts:1119 §11.10(e) 'Hash-chained, tamper-evident audit trail'), and no monitoring is running at all.

**Fix.** Add AUDIT_TRAIL_ENABLED to .env.example with the production value and a comment naming what turns off without it. Make AUDIT_REQUIRE_ENFORCE default to true in production once the schema prerequisites are on the migration path (AUDIT-01, AUDIT-04). Expose the resolved posture on /readyz so it is observable rather than a single boot log line.

#### `P2` AUDIT-12 — Many audit_logs writers bypass the hash chain entirely, and the verifier silently excludes every unchained row

`server/services/audit/chain.ts:188` · blocks **G3** · ~days · incomplete-coverage

**Evidence.** verifyAuditChain scopes itself with `WHERE sha256_chain IS NOT NULL` (:188), as does verifyAuditChainSeals (:241). Only three writers compute a chain value: server/services/auditService.ts:252, server/routes/c2c/actions.ts:297 and server/routes/c2c/commitments.ts. These write to audit_logs WITHOUT it: server/prisma/client.js:67, server/services/templates/templateStore.ts:67 (which additionally leaves sha256_chain unset while filling actor_id/target/occurred_at), server/services/licensing/eula-service.ts:292, server/services/license-manager.ts:364, server/services/documentExportService.ts:916, server/services/sentenceTraceabilityService.ts:797, server/services/keywordExtractionService.ts:512, server/services/figureGenerationService.ts:618, server/services/autoExtractionPipeline.ts:909 and :932, and db/migrations/20260209_create_pm_settings.sql:42. This directly contradicts auditService.ts:230-238 ('Every audit row is committed into the append-only chain').

**Failure scenario.** A template is approved through templateStore.auditTemplate, writing an audit_logs row with sha256_chain NULL. An attacker with DB write access modifies that row's action from 'template.approve' to 'template.view' and adds a fabricated row, both with sha256_chain NULL. verifyAuditChain skips both, reports ok:true over the remaining rows, and /api/part11/audit-trail/seal-integrity returns verified integrity. The chain provides zero coverage over an arbitrary and growing subset of the audit trail, and nothing reports what fraction is covered.

**Fix.** Route every audit_logs write through auditService.logAction / recordGovernedAction so the chain is computed, or make sha256_chain NOT NULL with a DB trigger that computes it. At minimum, have verifyAuditChain COUNT the unchained rows and include `unchainedRows` in the result, and make audit-integrity-service.ts refuse ok:true when that count is non-zero.

#### `P2` AUDIT-13 — POST /api/part11/audit-trail returns HTTP 201 even when the audit_events insert fails

`server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:308` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · lost-audit-record

**Evidence.** appendAuditEntry issues the INSERT fire-and-forget: `_part11Pool.query(...).catch((err: Error) => { console.error('[Part11] CRITICAL: Failed to persist audit entry to DB:', err.message); ... })` (:308-311), and the function returns the in-memory entry synchronously (:316). The POST handler at :739 calls it and responds 201 regardless. Note audit_events.entity_id is INTEGER (shared/schema.ts:9819) but the route accepts any client-supplied entityId, so a non-numeric entityId guarantees the failure path. Also at :312 `console.warn('[Part11] WARNING: No DB pool available -- audit entry stored in-memory only')` — same 201.

**Failure scenario.** A client posts a configuration-change audit entry with entityId 'doc-4471-a'. The INSERT raises 22P02 (invalid input syntax for integer). The route returns 201 with an audit entry id and a hash. The caller records success. Nothing is in the database. The 'chain' the response references (entry.hash / entry.previousHash, seeded from a module-level `lastAuditHash` at :261 that resets on every process restart and is never persisted) exists only in that process's memory.

**Fix.** Await the INSERT and return 5xx on failure. Persist entry.hash/previousHash into record_hash/previous_hash (or drop them from the response, since they are not stored). Validate entityId as an integer before accepting.

#### `P2` AUDIT-14 — A frozen or signed authoring document is immutable only at the API layer; the freeze itself is not transactional

`server/routes/authoring.router.ts:242` · blocks **G3** · ~days · api-only-immutability

**Evidence.** Immutability is enforced solely in canEditSection: `if (LOCKED_DOCUMENT_STATUSES.has(String(parent.status ?? '').toUpperCase())) return false;` (:242-246, set defined at :154), plus an equivalent check at :1437 for section adds and :4663 for change-request apply. I grepped every db/migrations/20260725_authoring_*.sql for CREATE TRIGGER / RAISE EXCEPTION — there are none. There is no DB constraint or trigger preventing UPDATE of authoring_sections or authoring_documents once status='FROZEN'. Separately, the freeze handler runs three independent pool.query calls with no transaction: INSERT INTO frozen_documents (:3335), UPDATE authoring_documents SET status='FROZEN' (:3343), then createAuditTrail (:3348). And server/services/ana-ri/command-executor.ts:2826 issues `UPDATE authoring_documents SET status='FROZEN'` on a separate path.

**Failure scenario.** Document D is frozen and e-signed; authoring_signatures.covered_content_hash binds the signature to snapshot v1.0.frozen. Any code path that reaches authoring_sections without going through canEditSection — a migration, an admin script, a future route, or the template-apply handler at :4107 which has no lock check — UPDATEs a section's content. The signature still verifies (it covers the snapshot, not live state), the frozen snapshot still verifies, but the live document an author sees no longer matches what was signed, and nothing in the database prevented it. Separately, if the process dies between :3335 and :3343, frozen_documents holds a snapshot while authoring_documents.status remains editable — the document is 'frozen' in the evidence table and open for editing in the live table.

**Fix.** Add BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers on authoring_sections and authoring_documents that raise when the owning document's status is FROZEN/APPROVED, with a narrowly-scoped GUC bypass for a documented unfreeze path (mirror the pattern in db/migrations/20260617_audit_logs_immutability.sql). Wrap the freeze handler's three statements in one transaction.

#### `P2` AUDIT-15 — §11.50 signature manifestation is not reachable from any document render, export, or print path

`server/services/compliance/signature-manifestation.ts:68` · blocks **G3** · ~days · missing-control

**Evidence.** renderSignatureManifestation is well-written and correct (printed name, UTC date/time, meaning). Its only non-test caller in the entire repo is server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:678 — an AnA chat tool handler whose output is a JSON string returned to the model, with the message 'Embed the block in any human-readable (PDF/Word) form of the signed record' left as an instruction to a human. It reads concept2cureSignatures (AnaToolExecutor.ts:700), a third signature table distinct from both electronic_signatures (written by esignature.ts) and authoring_signatures (written by authoring.router.ts). No PDF/DOCX/eCTD renderer imports it; I grepped the whole repo for renderSignatureManifestation and requiredManifestFields. part11-compliance.ts:1121 nonetheless states the platform control for §11.50 is 'Name, date/time, and meaning displayed on signature'.

**Failure scenario.** A sponsor exports an approved Module 2.5 to PDF for submission. The rendered PDF contains no signature block — no printed name, no signing date/time, no meaning — because nothing in the export pipeline calls the manifestation renderer, and the signature it would render lives in a table (concept2cure_signatures) that the two working signing paths do not write to. §11.50 requires the signed electronic record to display these three elements in any human-readable form. The submission-grade output is non-conformant on its face.

**Fix.** Call renderSignatureManifestation from the eCTD leaf renderer and the DOCX/PDF export path, resolving signatures from whichever table actually holds them for that document class. Consolidate the three signature tables (electronic_signatures, authoring_signatures, concept2cure_signatures) behind one resolver so 'what signatures cover this record' has a single answer.

#### `P2` AUDIT-16 — The Part 11 traceability matrix cites a module that does not exist and rests on mocked-only verification; the generated evidence pack greps for a string that is not in the code

`docs/validation/TM-CORTEX-001-PART11-TRACEABILITY.md:31` · blocks **G3** · ~days · validation-evidence-integrity

**Evidence.** Row P11-05 names `server/services/audit/auditLoggerV2.ts` as implementing code for §11.10(e). That file does not exist — `find . -name 'auditLoggerV2*' -not -path '*/node_modules/*'` returns nothing, and server/services/audit/ contains only audit-archive.service.ts, audit-hmac-seal.ts, audit-integrity-service.ts, auditLogger.ts, auditSealPosture.ts, chain.ts, chainIntegrityMonitor.ts, signedAuditExport.ts. Row P11-05 also cites 'audit_events (trigger-immutable)' — see AUDIT-01/AUDIT-04, that trigger is not applied. Row P11-08 cites part11-compliance.ts for §11.50/§11.70 — see AUDIT-06, that route cannot execute. Open item 2 (:66-68) is accurate: I confirmed server/__tests__/security/coauthor-document-delete-audit.contract.test.ts:30 mocks the entire data layer (`transaction: async (cb) => cb({ query: clientQuery })`), so the test named 'fails closed — an audit failure rolls the delete back' proves only that a 500 is returned; no rollback is exercised because the mock has no transaction semantics. Separately, scripts/audits/generate-evidence-pack.mjs:133 asserts the immutability control by `fileContains('server/startup/middleware.ts', 'IMMUTABILITY_ROUTE_PATTERNS')` — `grep -c IMMUTABILITY_ROUTE_PATTERNS server/startup/middleware.ts` returns 0; the constant is named IMMUTABLE_ROUTE_PATTERNS (middleware.ts:144).

**Failure scenario.** A GxP vendor-qualification assessor works the traceability matrix row by row, as it invites them to ('an assessor can move from a Part 11 clause to running evidence without a code tour'). P11-05 points at a nonexistent file. P11-08 points at a route that returns 500 on every call. The generated evidence pack reports immutabilityMiddleware:false for a control that does exist. The assessor's finding is not 'control missing' but 'the supplier's validation documentation does not reflect the system as built' — which invalidates the whole validation package and typically triggers a full re-qualification.

**Fix.** Regenerate the matrix from the code rather than by hand: for each row, assert the cited path exists and the cited test/gate is in a CI workflow, and fail CI when it is not. Fix the evidence-pack needle to IMMUTABLE_ROUTE_PATTERNS and replace substring-grep 'proofs' with executed assertions. Land the DB-backed delete→audit integration test rather than deferring it to the operator.

#### `P2` AUDIT-17 — Every chain verifier loads the entire audit table into Node memory with no bound; the continuous monitor does it every five minutes

`server/services/audit/chain.ts:185` · blocks **G2** · ~days · efficiency

**Evidence.** verifyAuditChain issues `SELECT id, action, actor_id, target, payload_hash, occurred_at, sha256_chain FROM audit_logs WHERE sha256_chain IS NOT NULL ORDER BY occurred_at ASC, id ASC` with no LIMIT and materializes all rows (:185-195). verifyAuditChainSeals (:239) and verifyAuditEventsChainSeals (:293) do the same. server/services/audit/chainIntegrityMonitor.ts:128-132 runs an unbounded `SELECT ... FROM audit_events ORDER BY organization_id, sequence_number ASC` on a setInterval of 5 minutes (services.ts:254). signedAuditExport.ts:115-122 repeats it on every export. Only the export's row query is capped (LIMIT 50001 at :191); the integrity snapshot beside it is not.

**Failure scenario.** A production tenant reaches 20 million audit_events rows after a year (the platform audits reads as well as writes — auditLogger.logDocumentAccess at :280). Every 5 minutes the monitor pulls all 20M rows into the API process. The Node heap exceeds its limit and the process is OOM-killed, or the query saturates the database and user requests time out. The audit-integrity control becomes the platform's primary availability incident, and because the monitor is the thing that would report a break, the outage also blinds the control.

**Fix.** Verify incrementally: persist a watermark (last verified id/sequence and its hash) and check only rows after it, with a periodic full pass run off-peak against a replica. Stream with a server-side cursor instead of materializing. Cap and paginate snapshotChainIntegrity.

#### `P2` AUDIT-18 — The §11.10(g) signing-authority gate on /api/esignature/sign reads a stale JWT role instead of the authoritative membership role every other signing route uses

`server/routes/esignature.ts:47` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · stale-authorization

**Evidence.** resolveUserRole reads `r.userRole ?? r.user?.role ?? r.tenantContext?.role` (:45-49) and the gate at :160-166 calls isSigningAuthorized on it. The global auth boundary (server/startup/middleware.ts:35 → middleware/authBoundary.ts:145) uses authenticateToken from server/middleware/auth.ts, which sets `role: decoded.role || 'user'` from the JWT claim (:149) — minted at login and never re-resolved. Its companion enforceOrgMembership (server/middleware/orgMembership.ts:116-126) SELECTs organizationUsers.role but discards it, returning only 'member' | 'revoked'. Every other signing surface does it correctly: part11-compliance.ts:433 `await resolveSignerOrgRole(Number(signerId), signerOrgId)`, submission-sign-release.ts:176, ana-ri/seal-verified.ts:121. server/services/part11/resolve-signer-role.ts:16-19 explicitly warns against the pattern esignature.ts uses.

**Failure scenario.** User 88 logs in while holding organization_users.role = 'approver' in org 12; the access token carries role='approver'. QA demotes them to 'viewer' in org 12 (revoking signing authority). Their token is still valid. They POST /api/esignature/sign for document 4471: enforceOrgMembership finds a membership row and passes; resolveUserRole returns the stale 'approver' from the token; isSigningAuthorized returns true; the signature is applied and persisted with signer_id 88. The identical request to /api/part11/signatures or /api/submissions/:id/sign-release would be refused with ESIGNATURE_NO_AUTHORITY. Authority revocation does not take effect on the primary signing route until token expiry.

**Fix.** Replace resolveUserRole in esignature.ts with `await resolveSignerOrgRole(userId, orgId)` so all four signing surfaces share one authoritative source. Optionally have enforceOrgMembership overwrite req.user.role with the membership role it already fetches.

#### `P2` AUDIT-19 — Regulated eCTD document deletes are hard deletes that record no snapshot of the destroyed record

`server/routes/ectd-documents.ts:356` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · record-retention

**Evidence.** The delete is atomic with its audit row (a genuine strength — `DELETE FROM coauthor_documents ... RETURNING id, organization_id` then `INSERT INTO audit_events ...` in one transaction, :329-357), but the audit row's metadata parameter is literally `JSON.stringify({})` (:356) and no old_values / new_values / changed_fields column is populated, even though audit_events has all three (shared/schema.ts:9830-9832). The reason is the fixed string 'eCTD coauthor document deleted' (:355). server/routes/coauthor.ts:251 follows the same shape. The record row itself is gone — there is no soft-delete flag on coauthor_documents in this path.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory author deletes eCTD document 4471 (a Module 2.7 leaf) by mistake. The row is destroyed. audit_events records that user X deleted coauthor_document 4471 at time T with metadata {}. Neither the title, the content, the CTD section, nor any prior state is recoverable from the audit trail. §11.10(c) requires protection of records to enable accurate and ready retrieval throughout the retention period, and §11.10(e) requires the audit trail not to obscure previously recorded information. Neither is satisfied: the previously recorded information is simply gone.

**Fix.** Capture the deleted row into the audit event's old_values (the RETURNING clause already runs — return * and serialize it), or convert the operation to a soft delete with a retention-governed purge, matching the vault model in server/jobs/retentionCron.ts which archives before deleting.

#### `P2` AUDIT-20 — Signed audit exports fall back to the application JWT secret as their integrity key

`server/services/audit/signedAuditExport.ts:80` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · key-reuse

**Evidence.** `const key = process.env.AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY || process.env.JWT_SECRET_PROD || process.env.JWT_SECRET;` — the function throws only when all three are absent, and its own error message says 'Refusing to sign with a default key per 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(e)'. .env.example:520 ships AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY empty, so the JWT secret is the operative default. That secret is held by every process that mints or verifies access tokens.

**Failure scenario.** AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY is left unset (the .env.example default). An audit export is produced for an FDA inspection and signed with JWT_SECRET. Anyone who can read JWT_SECRET — the same secret used for session tokens, and one that must be rotated on any auth incident — can forge a valid manifest signature over an altered export, and verifySignedAuditExport (:338) will accept it. Rotating JWT_SECRET after an auth incident also invalidates every previously issued audit-export signature, so historical exports can no longer be verified.

**Fix.** Require a dedicated AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY and remove the JWT fallbacks; assert its presence at boot alongside assertAuditSealPostureForProduction. Support key ids and a previous-key slot so rotation does not invalidate historical exports (the attestation subsystem already models this per .env.example:527-529).

#### `P3` AUDIT-21 — The chain-integrity monitor writes its integrity-failure event to a hardcoded organization_id of 1

`server/services/audit/chainIntegrityMonitor.ts:166` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · cross-tenant-attribution

**Evidence.** `VALUES (1, 'audit.chain_integrity_failure', 'audit_chain.monitor', 0, 0, 'system', 'system', '127.0.0.1', NOW(), $1, $2, true, true)` — organization_id is the literal 1. scripts/run-chain-verify.mjs:139 does the same. The monitor scans all organizations (:130-131) but files every failure under org 1.

**Failure scenario.** Org 57's chain breaks. The monitor detects it and files a regulatory_significant, gxp_relevant audit_events row under organization_id = 1 — a different tenant. Org 57's audit trail contains no record that its own chain was found broken, while org 1's trail contains a GxP-relevant event about data it does not own. If org 1 does not exist (or the FK to organizations rejects), the INSERT fails and the catch at :173 reduces the detection to a console line that vanishes on restart.

**Fix.** Insert one failure event per affected organization_id derived from brokenDetails[].orgId, or route system-scope events to a dedicated system organization resolved at boot rather than a literal.

#### `P3` AUDIT-22 — The audit-integrity and archive ops scripts disable TLS certificate verification against the production database

`scripts/run-chain-verify.mjs:56` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · tls

**Evidence.** `ssl: isNeon ? { rejectUnauthorized: false } : false,` — identical at scripts/run-audit-archive.mjs:56. `isNeon` is true for any URL containing 'neon.tech' OR 'sslmode=require' (:53), which covers most managed Postgres URLs. By contrast scripts/db/deploy-migrate.mjs:51-53 documents the correct approach ('The connection verifies the server certificate... supply the Amazon RDS CA bundle via NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS rather than disabling verification').

**Failure scenario.** The nightly chain-verify job runs from a CI runner or a bastion. An attacker who can intercept the connection (compromised DNS, a rogue egress proxy) presents any certificate; the client accepts it and streams the entire audit_events table — every user name, IP, entity id and reason across all tenants — to the attacker, and can return a forged INTACT result set. This is also a direct finding in any SOC 2 / customer security review.

**Fix.** Use the same verified-TLS connection helper as scripts/db/connection.mjs (which deploy-migrate.mjs uses) in both ops scripts, with NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS for the provider CA.

#### `P3` AUDIT-23 — The 24-hour chain-verify window cannot check the link across the window boundary

`scripts/run-chain-verify.mjs:62` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · incomplete-coverage

**Evidence.** With `--window=24h` the query is filtered `WHERE timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '24 hours'` (:62) and the walk seeds `prevByOrg` empty (:85), so the first in-window row for each org has expectedPrev === null and its previous_hash is never compared against its true predecessor outside the window (:87-92). The daily job is the 24h variant; the full walk is documented as weekly (:12-15) and, per AUDIT-09, neither is scheduled.

**Failure scenario.** An attacker alters the last audit_events row of yesterday and the first row of today so the pair is internally consistent within each window. The daily 24h scan seeds from null and never compares across the seam, so it reports INTACT for six consecutive days until the weekly full scan (which is not scheduled) would have run.

**Fix.** Before walking a window, fetch the immediately preceding row per organization_id and seed prevByOrg with its record_hash, so the boundary link is verified.

### AI gateway, safety & grounding (server/services/ai-gateway/**, ai-governance/**, the 3 baselined gateway bypasses, citation verification, claim-grounding / evidence-validation, grounding evals)

This is an unusually thoughtful gateway design wrapped around several load-bearing controls that are not actually wired up. The architecture is real: a single `route()` chokepoint with a placement/residency registry that is never relaxed during fallback, an approved-model lockfile with a CI drift gate, a redaction-verification pass that fails closed, a concurrency semaphore, stream watchdogs, and a deterministic offline grounding eval that runs as a blocking CI test. But three of the controls a regulated buyer would pay for are inert. (1) The Part 11 AI audit trail is never persisted — `getGateway()` is called without a `dbPool` at all 81 call sites, so `ai.gateway_audit_log` has never been written in any deployment and the record lives in a 100-entry in-memory ring buffer; the seller's own evidence-pack generator asserts the opposite. (2) "Evidence validation" measures whether the model wrote a `[KNOWN]` tag within 500 characters of its own claim, and the UI renders that count as "N sources · M grounded claims" behind a shield-check icon — a fabricated CFR citation with a self-applied `[KNOWN]` label reads as verified. (3) The groundedness→human-review gate takes its score from the client request body, and on the legacy accept path the section content is written to the database before the gate can throw. Prompt-injection defence is ~89 lines of English regex that I defeated on paper in six ways (unicode homoglyph, a >40-char gap, non-English, and — most importantly — a poisoned RAG chunk that asserts a false regulatory fact without any override verb), while it false-positives and hard-blocks on ordinary protocol-amendment phrasing; uploaded PDF bodies, the primary indirect vector, are never scanned at all. The dominant risk for a buyer is that the AI-governance story is documentation-grade rather than evidence-grade: the schemas, lockfiles and verdict objects exist, but the writes, the enforcement and the honest user-facing signals do not.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- Placement/residency is treated as a hard constraint, not a preference, and is correctly never relaxed on the degraded path: gateway.ts:1605-1614 relaxes the health filter but keeps `meetsPlacementRequirements`, and gateway.ts:1676-1683 keeps it in the fallback chain — with the intent documented inline ("Residency / ZDR are hard constraints — never fall back across them"). providers/placement.ts:153-173 is a clean, testable compliance predicate, and the shared frontier providers correctly default to zeroDataRetention:false (placement.ts:76/83) rather than assuming a ZDR agreement exists.
- The redaction path verifies itself and fails closed rather than trusting that the detector and the scrubber agree: policy.ts:336-353 re-classifies the redacted copy and returns `action:'block'` if any redact-disposition span survived. The redaction is applied to a copy (policy.ts:187-213), never mutating the caller's messages. This is genuinely careful engineering.
- Approved-model lockfile with a real drift gate: server/services/ai-governance/approved-models.ts:41-182 pins id→version with rationale, eval reference and review date, and server/services/ai-governance/__tests__/approved-models.test.ts:14-20 fails CI when `DEFAULT_MODELS` diverges — including tests for version bumps, removals and unapproved additions. Within the limits noted in AIGW-13, this is more model governance than most products of this type have.
- The grounding check is deliberately run against exactly what the model saw, not the fuller raw tool output, with the reasoning written down: routes/ana-ri/stream.ts:990-999 pushes the *budgeted* tool strings into `toolEvidenceCorpus` specifically so a claim sitting in truncated-away text cannot be scored as grounded — "a false pass in the one direction that lets a fabrication through".
- Fail-closed production guard against serving demo content: gateway.ts:531-536 throws rather than returning the `[KNOWN]`-tagged placeholder regulatory text when no provider is configured, and NODE_ENV=production is genuinely set on the real deploy paths (docker-compose.yml:69, docker-compose.beta.yml:64, .github/workflows/deploy-aws.yml:633, package.json:34), so the guard actually engages.
- Outbound concurrency is bounded at a single chokepoint: the FIFO Semaphore at gateway.ts:340-375 wraps every provider invocation via `executeProvider` (gateway.ts:760-773), so primary and fallback paths are both covered without disturbing retry/circuit-breaker/timeout logic.
- Retry policy is correct where it matters: retry-policy.ts:26-28 never retries hard 4xx, :31-33 separates self-healing overload (429/503/529) with a larger budget and longer backoff, and :42-44/:52-54 refuse to retry streaming requests because tokens have already been delivered to the caller's callback. Both streaming paths also carry a 30s per-chunk stall watchdog (gateway.ts:1284-1299, 1509-1523) and return accumulated partial content rather than losing the turn.
- The audit *schema* is the right one for a regulated buyer even though it is never written: gateway.ts:1882-1921 records prompt SHA-256 (`hashPrompt`, gateway.ts:2019-2022), prompt version, temperature, seed, the full tried-models fallback chain, and the resolved substrate/region/retention policy — and content-policy findings carry only detector names and classifier-redacted excerpts, never raw content. Policy refusals are audited as first-class compliance events (gateway.ts:1935-1976). Fixing AIGW-01 turns this from a design into a control.
- Citation verification does real external lookups rather than heuristics: PubMed ESummary and CrossRef with bounded concurrency (citation-verification-service.ts:344-358), honest `unverifiable`/`error` statuses that never fabricate a verdict (:336, :340), retraction detection from PubMed pubtype (:161), and identifier-vs-title mismatch detection (:252-259) that catches a DOI resolving to a different article than the one cited.
- Anthropic's reasoning-only model surface is handled correctly and is version-detected rather than hardcoded: gateway.ts:930-933 `isReasoningOnlyModel`, gateway.ts:944-971 omits sampling params for Opus 4.7+, and clamps legacy `budget_tokens` below `max_tokens` with 1024 tokens of answer headroom — all covered by tests at __tests__/gateway.test.ts:233-284.
- A blocking, fully offline CI gate for the anti-hallucination property exists at all: tests/ai-safety/grounding-eval.test.ts asserts `failed === 0 && fabrication.recall === 1` over the fixture set with no model call, no network and no API keys, so it can never be flaky or silently skipped. The gate's own docstring explains why it mirrors the advisory script exactly.
- The gateway-bypass ratchet (scripts/ci/check-gateway-bypass.mjs, wired at .github/workflows/ci.yml:148-149 and repeated in deploy-aws.yml) is the right idea and even reports when a baselined file no longer needs its exemption (:65-70) — the problem is only that its pattern is too narrow (AIGW-04).

#### `P0` AIGW-01 — AI gateway audit trail is never persisted — ai.gateway_audit_log has never been written

`server/services/ai-gateway/gateway.ts:411` · blocks **G3** · ~days · audit-trail

**Evidence.** gateway.ts:411 `this.auditLogger = new GatewayAuditLogger(this.config.dbPool);`. `dbPool?: unknown` (types.ts:453) is optional and NOTHING ever supplies it: `grep -rn "getGateway({" server/` returns zero hits, and all 81 `getGateway(...)` call sites in server/ pass no argument (the only `new AIGateway(config)` outside tests is the singleton factory at gateway.ts:2235). audit.ts:17 `this.pool = dbPool || null;` and audit.ts:49-51 `if (this.pool) { await this.persistToDb(entry); }`. The only other retention is a bounded RAM buffer: audit.ts:13 `private maxBufferSize = 100;` audit.ts:26-28 `if (this.buffer.length > this.maxBufferSize) this.buffer.shift();`. The seller's evidence pack states the opposite: scripts/ai-governance/generate-evidence-pack.ts:82-87 — "its audit log records: provider, model, model/prompt version, prompt SHA-256 hash, temperature, seed, token usage, cost, latency, and the fallback chain (`ai.gateway_audit_log`)".

**Failure scenario.** An FDA inspector asks for the AI call record behind a specific Module 2.5 paragraph generated three months ago: which model version, which prompt hash, which temperature/seed, which fallback rung, which substrate/region/retention posture. `SELECT * FROM ai.gateway_audit_log` returns "relation does not exist" — the table is only created inside `ensureTable()`, which is only reachable from `persistToDb()`, which is only reachable when `this.pool` is non-null, which never happens. Even the in-memory buffer is gone: it holds 100 entries and is cleared on every process restart/deploy. The content-policy findings (PII blocks, injection flags) written at gateway.ts:1918-1920 and the refusal records at gateway.ts:1947-1972 are lost identically.

**Fix.** Pass the app pool at the single construction site — e.g. in the bootstrap that first touches the gateway, call `getGateway({ dbPool: pool })` before any other module imports it, or import the pool directly inside GatewayAuditLogger instead of accepting it as optional config. Make it fail-closed: if `auditEnabled` is true and no pool resolves at construction, throw at boot rather than silently degrading to RAM. Replace `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` inside the request path with a real migration so the table exists independent of first-write.

#### `P0` AIGW-02 — "Evidence validation" is label-proximity over the model's own output, but is rendered to users as verified sources

`server/services/ana-ri/evidence-validation.ts:378` · blocks **G1** · ~days · honesty

**Evidence.** evidence-validation.ts:121 `const pattern = /\[(KNOWN|INFERRED|MISSING)[^\]]*\]/gi;` extracts labels **from the assistant's own response text**. evidence-validation.ts:180 `if (distance < minDistance && distance <= EVIDENCE_PROXIMITY_CHARS)` with `EVIDENCE_PROXIMITY_CHARS = 500` (line 43). evidence-validation.ts:316 `grounded: nearestLabel !== null`. evidence-validation.ts:378 `source_count: labels.length,` and :380 `grounded_claim_count: groundedCount,`. Nothing in this file reads tool output, retrieved documents, or any corpus. The client renders it as provenance: client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/Message.tsx:401 ``${evidence.sourceCount} source${...}`` and :404 ``${evidence.sourceCount} source${...} · ${evidence.groundedClaims} grounded claim${...}`` with :405 `const icon = weak ? <I.alert size={11}/> : <I.shieldCheck size={11}/>`. It is also the value persisted with the assistant message (routes/ana-ri/post-processing.ts:261-263) and returned on /chat (routes/ana-ri/chat.ts:755).

**Failure scenario.** AnA answers a Module 3 question with: "[KNOWN] Per 21 CFR 211.166(a)(3), stability testing must include three primary batches at the proposed commercial scale." The section number and requirement are invented. `extractLabels` finds one `[KNOWN]`, `extractClaims` matches the `must include` pattern, `findNearestLabel` finds the label 4 characters away, so `grounded = true`, `ungroundedCount = 0`, `overclaims = []`, `validated = true`, `source_count = 1`. Message.tsx renders a green shield reading "1 source · 1 grounded claim". A regulatory writer treats that badge as "the platform checked this against a source" and pastes the fabricated citation into a submission.

**Fix.** Stop calling the label count `source_count`. Either (a) rename the whole verdict to what it measures (`evidence_label_count`, `label_discipline_ok`) and change the UI copy to "N evidence labels" with a neutral icon, or (b) make the count real by populating it from `verifyAnswerGrounding`'s checked/grounded corpus counts (already computed alongside it at post-processing.ts:214-217) and from the actual tool/citation provenance list. Do not show a shield-check for a check that never touched a source.

#### `P0` AIGW-03 — Groundedness→human-review gate is client-scored and fires after the content is already written

`server/routes/c2c/actions.ts:632` · blocks **G1** · ~days · fail-open-gate

**Evidence.** actions.ts:610-615 destructures `{ refinedContent, status, groundednessScore, confidence, groundednessReviewAck } = req.body ?? {}` and :624-626 copies them straight into the gate payload. review-policy.ts:122 `const resolved = resolveGroundedness(p);` → groundedness.ts:120-125 `explicitScore(p)` reads `p.groundednessScore ?? p.groundedness ?? p.confidence ?? p.confidenceScore` and review-policy.ts:141 returns `{ score: explicit, source: 'explicit', enforce: true }`. Separately, the section write happens BEFORE the gate: actions.ts:630-640 `UPDATE cerv2_510k_sections SET content = $1, draft_source = NULL, status = $2, accepted_by = $3, accepted_at = now() ... WHERE id = $4` executes at line 632, and only then does line 656 `await writeMutation('accept-ai-suggestion', ...)` run the gate, whose `GroundednessReviewError` is caught at :670 and returned as `422 GROUNDEDNESS_REVIEW_REQUIRED` at :672.

**Failure scenario.** A user POSTs /api/cerv2-sections/4711/accept-ana-draft with `{refinedContent: <AI draft>, groundednessScore: 0}`. Line 632 writes the draft into `cerv2_510k_sections.content`, sets `status='ready_for_review'`, and stamps `accepted_by`/`accepted_at`. Line 656 then throws and the caller receives HTTP 422 "human review required" — but the ungrounded content is already in the section, already marked accepted, and the 422 rolls nothing back. Alternatively the same user POSTs `{groundednessScore: 1}` (or `{confidence: 100}`) and the gate returns `blocked:false` regardless of the actual content, because nothing server-side recomputes or cross-checks the score against the submitted `refinedContent`.

**Fix.** Compute the score server-side from `refinedContent` (call `citationCoverage`/`computeEvidenceConfidence` on the payload) and treat any client-supplied `groundednessScore`/`confidence` as advisory metadata that can only lower, never raise, the effective score. Move the `UPDATE cerv2_510k_sections` inside the same transaction as `writeMutation`, after the gate, so a 422 leaves the section untouched.

#### `P1` AIGW-04 — Gateway-bypass CI gate only greps for `new OpenAI(`, so the real bypass surface (shared client + embeddings) is invisible and unbounded

`scripts/ci/check-gateway-bypass.mjs:29` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · governance-bypass

**Evidence.** check-gateway-bypass.mjs:29 `const PATTERN = 'new (OpenAI|Anthropic)[[:space:]]*\\(';` and :55 excludes anything under `server/services/ai-gateway/`. The baseline (scripts/ci/gateway-bypass-baseline.json:3-7) therefore lists only 3 files. But `getOpenAIClient()` — itself baselined — is re-exported and used to make raw chat completions in 12 places: server/services/csr-extractor-service.ts:373 `const response = await getOpenAIClient().chat.completions.create({ model: 'gpt-4-turbo-preview', ... })` sending `${text.slice(0, 8000)}` of a customer clinical study report; server/services/EvidenceManagementService.ts:102; server/services/csr-extractor-service.ts:485; server/services/submission-twin-service.ts:43; server/workers/vectorization-worker.ts:57; server/services/innovation/{regulatory-negotiation-logbook,auto-traceability,regulatory-delta-radar}-service.ts. Separately server/services/ai-gateway/embeddings/embedding-provider.ts:81 `new OpenAI({...})` is exempt purely because it sits under GATEWAY_DIR, yet performs no PII classification, no audit write, no cost metering and no placement check.

**Failure scenario.** A customer signs a contract stating all AI processing of their documents is audited and subject to their data-residency policy. They upload a CSR; csr-extractor-service.ts:373 ships 8,000 characters of it to `gpt-4-turbo-preview` on the shared OpenAI API. No PII/PHI classification runs (policy.ts:261 never invoked), no audit row is produced, `api_usage_logs` records nothing so the call is unbilled and invisible to the weekly limit, and the org's `zeroDataRetention`/`dataResidency` policy is never consulted. CI stays green because the file contains no `new OpenAI(`. The same is true of every chunk embedded by vectorization-worker.ts.

**Fix.** Extend the CI pattern to also flag `getOpenAIClient(`, `getAnthropicClient(`, and `.chat.completions.create(` / `.messages.create(` outside the gateway executors, and re-baseline honestly (the list will be ~15 files, not 3). Route csr-extractor, EvidenceManagementService, submission-twin and the innovation services through `getGateway().route()` with organizationId. Put the embedding provider behind the same PII/placement/audit path as chat, or document in the contract that embeddings are exempt.

#### `P1` AIGW-05 — Org residency/ZDR policy, tenant metering and rate isolation are all skipped when a caller omits organizationId — including FDA IND drafting

`server/services/ai-gateway/gateway.ts:1719` · blocks **G2** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** gateway.ts:1718-1719 `private async applyOrgPlacementDefaults(request) { if (request.organizationId === undefined || request.organizationId === null) return request;` — the org's residency/zero-retention policy is not applied at all. gateway.ts:1991-1993 `const orgRaw = request.organizationId; ... if (orgId == null || !Number.isFinite(orgId) || orgId <= 0) return;` — the call is dropped from `api_usage_logs` entirely. policy.ts:520 `const orgKey = request.organizationId?.toString() || '__global__';` — all such calls share one 100 req/min bucket across every tenant.
