PATCH lets the same caller silently overwrite `value`/`pass` on another tenant's out-of-specification result, which is the record class 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(e) requires to be attributable and tamper-evident.

**Fix.** Add `and study_id in (select study_id from stab_studies where tenant_id = $n)` (or a join on the parent study) to both statements, sourcing the tenant from the verified token via the existing getSecureOrgId/authedOrgId helpers; write a stab_audit row using the module's own audit() helper on both paths; and fix the session-variable name so RLS is a real second layer (`app.current_tenant_id`, set on every checked-out client, not `app.tenant_id`).

#### `P2` API-11 — /api/ai-assistance is not covered by the /api/ai auth gate that its own comment claims covers it; the LLM endpoint has no per-mount auth and no usage limit

`server/bootstrap/register-core-routes.ts:90` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · authorization

**Evidence.** ```
45-48:  // Gate the whole /api/ai namespace once: covers aiRoutes below, every
        // phase3Routes endpoint ... and the /api/ai AI-assistance alias mounted later in this file.
48:     app.use('/api/ai', authenticateToken);
...
90:     app.use('/api/ai-assistance', aiCircuitBreaker, aiAssistanceRoutes);
91:     app.use('/api/ai', aiCircuitBreaker, aiAssistanceRoutes);
```
Express `app.use('/api/ai', ...)` requires the next character to be '/' or end-of-string, so it does not match /api/ai-assistance. I confirmed this against the repo's own express@5.2.1:
  GET /api/ai/foo              -> {"hit":"ai","gated":true}
  GET /api/ai-assistance/assist -> {"hit":"ai-assistance","gated":false}
server/routes/ai-assistance.ts exposes POST /assist, POST /verify, GET /health — so POST /api/ai-assistance/assist reaches the LLM path with authenticateToken never having run at the mount. It is also absent from the weekly-limit list (register-ai-routes.ts applies enforceWeeklyLimit only to /api/ana, /api/ana-ri, /api/chat, /api/claude) and, per API-05, is categorised into the 100/min 'api' rate bucket, not the 30/min 'ai' bucket.
The global boundary does authenticate it today, so this is defence-in-depth loss rather than an open door — but register-ai-routes.ts:9-16 states the design intent explicitly: "authenticateToken before the rate limiters ... gives a layered defense" against "a direct cost vector".

**Failure scenario.** Combined with API-07: with AUTH_BOUNDARY_MODE=warn, POST /api/ai-assistance/assist is fully unauthenticated and calls the AI gateway on every request, at 100/min per source IP, with no weekly cap. Even with the boundary enforcing, any authenticated pilot user gets 3.3x the intended AI budget on this endpoint with no per-org ceiling.

**Fix.** Change line 90 to `app.use('/api/ai-assistance', authenticateToken, aiCircuitBreaker, weeklyRequestLimit, aiAssistanceRoutes)`. Add a CI assertion that every mount prefix appearing in the AI family list carries authenticateToken, so a prefix rename cannot silently drop the gate again.

#### `P2` API-12 — authenticateToken mounted on the bare /api prefix in register-clinical-intel-routes — the anti-pattern the repo documents as having previously broken the X-API-Key public API

`server/bootstrap/register-clinical-intel-routes.ts:82` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · defense-in-depth

**Evidence.** ```
82: app.use('/api', authenticateToken, rtmExportRoutes.default);
```
The RTM export router only owns /api/programs/:programId/rtm*, but mounting the middleware on '/api' makes it run for EVERY /api request that reaches this layer. server/bootstrap/register-core-routes.ts:56-60 documents exactly why that is wrong:
"phase3Routes registers everything under /ai/* but is mounted at the bare '/api' prefix, so an auth middleware placed directly on THIS mount would intercept EVERY /api request — including intentionally-public families mounted later (e.g. the X-API-Key-authed /api/v1)."
Today this is survivable only by registration order: registerClinicalIntelRoutes runs in registerPostStartRoutes (server/startup/routes.ts:154), after /api/v1 (register-document-routes.ts:469), /api/billing (register-inline-routes.ts, slot 3) and /api/csp-report (register-platform-routes.ts:11) are already mounted, so those families match earlier layers and never reach line 82. Everything registered AFTER line 82 — the remainder of registerClinicalIntelRoutes and all of registerAdvancedPlatformRoutes — is force-authenticated. It also re-runs full JWT verification plus the DB-backed enforceOrgMembership check (server/middleware/auth.ts:157) on every such request, duplicating work the boundary already did.

**Failure scenario.** A future Stripe/Firecrawl/partner webhook or a second X-API-Key family is added to registerPostStartRoutes or to registerAdvancedPlatformRoutes (both run after line 82). The provider POSTs with a signature header and no JWT; line 82's authenticateToken returns 401 before the handler is reached. The webhook silently fails retries, and the cause is a middleware three files away that has nothing to do with the new route — the same failure shape the register-core-routes comment describes.

**Fix.** Change to the router's real prefix: `app.use('/api/programs', authenticateToken, rtmExportRoutes.default)` (or apply authenticateToken inside rtm-export.ts with `router.use`). Add a lint/CI rule forbidding `app.use('/api', <auth middleware>, ...)` — the repo already has the reasoning written down; it just isn't enforced.

#### `P2` API-13 — Unvalidated writes across 205 of 363 write-bearing route files; the GxP stability create path persists 15 unvalidated body fields and derives a date from them

`server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:283` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · input-validation

**Evidence.** Measured across server/routes/**, server/api/** and server/src/routes/**: 363 files declare at least one router.post/put/patch/delete; 205 of them contain no reference to zod at all. The heaviest: server/src/routes/stability.router.ts (49 writes), server/routes/authoring.router.ts (48), server/routes/ivd-lifecycle.ts (47), server/routes/biostatPlatform.ts (32), server/routes/resolution.ts (25), server/routes/innovation-routes.ts (25), server/routes/ind-lifecycle/documents.routes.ts (22), server/routes/operating-system.ts (19), server/routes/authoring-actions.ts (19).
Concrete instance — POST /api/stability/studies (server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:283-347):
```
287-301: const { productName, batchNumber, dosageForm, strength, scope, climaticZone, storageConditions, duration, testParameters, startDate, notes, status } = req.body;
318-320: const plannedEndDate = new Date(startDate);
         plannedEndDate.setMonth(plannedEndDate.getMonth() + duration);
325-347: INSERT INTO stab_studies (product_id, name, code, dosage_form, strength, duration_months, scope, climatic_zone, start_date, status, tenant_id, ...) VALUES ($1..$11)
```
No schema, no type checks, no enum check on `scope`/`climaticZone`/`status`, no numeric check on `duration`, no date validity check on `startDate`. Contrast the disciplined pattern the repo does have: server/routes/api-keys.ts:42-52 and server/api/cmc/projectRoutes.ts:495-512 both parse with zod and derive organizationId from the authenticated request rather than the body.

**Failure scenario.** A client (or an integration) POSTs {productName:'X', batchNumber:'B1', duration:'24 months', startDate:'Q1 2027', ...}. `new Date('Q1 2027')` is Invalid Date; `setMonth(NaN + '24 months')` leaves it Invalid; the INSERT writes start_date from the raw string (Postgres rejects it → 500) or, if startDate happens to be parseable and duration is a string, plannedEndDate silently becomes Invalid Date and any downstream shelf-life/timepoint scheduling built on it is wrong. Because there is no field-level rejection, the caller cannot tell which field was bad.

**Fix.** Adopt one shared pattern: a zod schema per write route parsed before any DB call, with the 400 body shape already used at server/api/cmc/projectRoutes.ts:534-539. Prioritise by blast radius — the GxP/Part 11 writers first (stability, authoring, ind-lifecycle/documents, biostat). Add a CI ratchet counting write-bearing route files without a validator, seeded at the current 205 and only allowed to fall (the repo already runs this ratchet pattern for orphan endpoints and typecheck).

#### `P3` API-14 — Dead entries on the public allowlist: /api/claude/health and /api/claude/models are documented public but the mount applies authenticateToken to the whole prefix

`server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:78` · ~hours · consistency

**Evidence.** ```
authBoundary.ts:78  { path: '/api/claude/health', match: 'exact' },
authBoundary.ts:80  { path: '/api/claude/models', match: 'exact' }, // static model catalog, no tenant data
```
and the same two paths in the legacy gate at register-platform-routes.ts:240-241. But the router is mounted with a prefix-level gate:
```
register-ai-routes.ts:109  app.use('/api/claude', authenticateToken, weeklyRequestLimit, anaIntelligenceRoutes.default);
```
The boundary lets the request through, then authenticateToken at the mount rejects it. Both allowlist entries are therefore unreachable-as-documented.
Conversely /api/ai-gateway/health IS genuinely public (mounted with no guard at server/startup/inline-endpoints.ts:535) and its error path returns `message: err?.message` (line 557) — an unauthenticated exception-message echo, the same leak that was deliberately closed for /api/health/full at lines 136-140.

**Failure scenario.** An operator or a monitoring vendor configures an uptime probe on GET /api/claude/health because the allowlist and the legacy gate both document it as public. Every probe returns 401 and the check flaps or is silently ignored. Separately, an unauthenticated GET /api/ai-gateway/health during a provider outage returns the raw exception text from the gateway module.

**Fix.** Either delete the two /api/claude allowlist entries (both files) or split the health/models routes out of anaIntelligenceRoutes onto an ungated sub-mount. Change server/startup/inline-endpoints.ts:556-558 to log the cause and return `{status:'error'}` only, matching the /api/health/full treatment. Add a test asserting every PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST entry actually resolves to a handler reachable without credentials — that single test would have caught this.

#### `P3` API-15 — Unmounted route modules are mostly harmless dead weight — but the class is not benign, and only cognitive-ecosystem.ts is confirmed deliberate

`server/routes/cognitive-ecosystem.ts:1` · ~hours · dead-code

**Evidence.** I checked the unmounted-module claim directly rather than inheriting it. cognitive-ecosystem.ts (16 route declarations) is confirmed unreachable: the only non-self reference in server/ is the comment at server/bootstrap/register-document-routes.ts:219-222 — "Retired (#844, Phase 0.2): cognitive-ecosystem was a placeholder — every endpoint returned hardcoded mock data and its services were never implemented". It is not imported anywhere, so it is neither bundled nor routable: dead weight, not hidden surface. Its own in-file guard (lines 47-56, extractUserContext) would in any case have required a verified tenant.
Most other modules that look unmounted to a naive import scan are in fact mounted through dynamic-import config arrays (e.g. billing-dashboard, device-cockpit, global-markets, workspace-config at register-inline-routes.ts:180-187; ivd-lifecycle, design-risk, qms via register-document-routes.ts) — I verified fourteen of the largest and all were reachable.
The exception is API-03: server/api/cmc/portfolio.ts is genuinely unmounted AND the client depends on it, so "unmounted" is not automatically "inert".

**Failure scenario.** No security exposure from cognitive-ecosystem.ts — it cannot be reached. The cost is review surface: 16 endpoint declarations and their service imports still ship in the repo, they inflate every endpoint count a buyer or a customer security review is given (docs/audit-2026-07/02-architecture-map.md cites 9 modules / 48 endpoints as unmounted), and a future contributor can re-mount a placeholder that returns fabricated regulatory data with a one-line change and no gate objecting.

**Fix.** Delete cognitive-ecosystem.ts and its services/cognitive-ecosystem tree (the retirement decision is already recorded in docs/AI_CONSOLIDATION_PLAN.md:17). For the general class, replace the naive unmounted-module heuristic with one that resolves dynamic-import config arrays, and pair it with the inverse check from API-03 (client call with no endpoint), which is the direction that actually causes user-visible breakage.

### client shell, navigation & UX honesty (client/src/main.tsx, App.jsx, ZenRouter.tsx, concept2cure/v2/**, shared/constants/ui-surface-registry*.ts)

This is a single-shell React/wouter SPA: main.tsx → App.jsx → ZenRouter (auth boundary) → V2App, which resolves /concept2cure/:surfaceId against a 96-entry surface registry and a 100-entry SURFACE_VIEWS map. The engineering is uneven in a specific and diagnosable way: a newer "fixture-free" data layer (liveGetOrNull/useLiveData/useLiveRows/EmptyState) produces genuinely honest live/empty/error states on the surfaces migrated to it, while an older `live ?? fixture` layer still ships on ~10 surfaces and is actively deceptive — `useLiveList` treats a legitimately EMPTY backend response as a failure and renders hardcoded pharma pipeline records (BX-204, CardioFlow CX) instead, so a brand-new customer org's first impression is fabricated regulatory data. On top of that sit ~30 controls that render as functional and do nothing: the AnA composer discards every attachment, a "Track changes" toggle and an "Autosaved" indicator sit above a contentEditable that never persists, "Accept FDA text" on the labeling-negotiation panel is inert, and 17 of the 19 modules in the Apps catalog navigate to ids that do not exist and land on an internal install-scaffold page titled "Home". Navigation is the second systemic problem: 91 of 96 registered surfaces have no global-nav entry, the only comprehensive module catalog (Home) has no nav entry at all, and the brand logo — the universal "go home" affordance — opens the document editor. Accessibility is aspirational rather than implemented: the repo's only a11y test exercises components that are unreachable dead code, every field in every governed data-entry drawer is programmatically unlabeled, and the streaming-AI live-region behavior directly contradicts the repo's own Human Factors spec. The dominant risk for a buyer is honesty: a regulated-industry user cannot currently tell, from the screen, which numbers are real, which buttons did something, and whether their work was saved.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- The fixture-free data layer is genuinely well designed and is the right target state: liveGetOrNull / liveMutateOrNull / useLiveData / useLiveRows / EmptyState (dataConnect.tsx:213-370) separate loading, honest-empty and honest-error as three distinct states, never throw, and never substitute a stand-in. Surfaces migrated onto it are exemplary — Registrations.tsx:225-241 renders three genuinely different panels for loading / error / zero-rows with copy that explicitly says 'nothing is shown from a cached sample'; GlobalRiBrowser (Surfaces.tsx:317-350) and Vault.tsx:140 follow the same pattern.
- ProjectHome's 'Add sources' upload (ProjectHome.tsx:340-410) is a complete, accessible, correct implementation: role + tabIndex + Enter/Space keyboard activation + aria-label on the drop zone, a real onChange that uploads, a visually-hidden aria-live="polite" status region (:402), per-file error state, and a deliberately conservative 'sections drafted from a source that has since changed' notice that reports drift without silently regenerating regulated text (:363-399). This proves the capability exists and makes the composer/attachment defects omissions rather than skill gaps.
- The Apps entitlement toggle is a model optimistic write: it PUTs to /api/module-subscriptions/:moduleId/toggle, reverts local state on any non-OK or thrown response, and surfaces the server's reason — 'Revert -- never report a write that did not happen' (AdminSurfaces.tsx:1346-1380). It also mirrors the server gate client-side so an out-of-plan module shows an upgrade path instead of a button that would 403 (:1416-1423).
- Honest-copy discipline appears in real places, not just comments: the CollabLauncher task toast reads 'Captured in this session -- <id> for <person>. Not saved to the org board yet.' (CollabLauncher.tsx:521-524), and ClinicalOps.tsx:334,347, CmcModule.tsx:141 and Risk.tsx:269 all carry governed-form copy that explicitly states the write is not yet persisted or audit-logged. Inconsistency.tsx:234 fires a toast saying 'cross-dossier propagation is not yet wired'.
- navTargets.test.tsx is a real, unusual and valuable CI gate — it statically extracts every literal onNav/nav/open/setSurface target across all v2 surfaces and fails the build if any does not resolve to a SURFACE_VIEWS key, a DEEP_LINK_ALIAS, 'home', or a CollabLauncher modal mode. Its own header names the exact failure mode it prevents ('a soft dead-end a mount test never catches but a human hits on their first click'), and it honestly documents its blind spot for computed targets.
- Icon hygiene is correct throughout: every glyph in the shared vocabulary renders as `<Icon size="1em" strokeWidth={1.75} aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" />` (icons.tsx:123), so icons never pollute the accessibility tree or the tab order.
- Baseline a11y infrastructure that many products get wrong is present: a visible :focus-visible ring on every shell button with outline-offset (app-v2.css:60), a global prefers-reduced-motion block neutralising animation and transition durations (index.css:81-87), and correct landmark structure with accessible names — nav aria-label="Primary" (Shell.tsx:136), header (:288), main (V2App.tsx:252), aside aria-label="AnA assistant" (Shell.tsx:470) — plus aria-current on active nav items (Shell.tsx:124).
- SurfaceBoundary (SurfaceScaffold.tsx:26-49) is a per-surface error boundary keyed on the active surface id, so a crash in one surface degrades only that pane, auto-clears on navigation, and shows honest, blame-free recovery copy ('A module script dropped on the way in — usually a transient network hiccup, not your work').
- The routing model is clean and testable: routing.ts is a 22-line pure module mapping URL segment ↔ surface id with an alias table, so every registry id is a working deep link and nav state lives in the URL rather than component state (V2App.tsx:121, 138-143). The shell is deliberately rendered outside the location-keyed transition Switch so navigation does not remount and drop palette/AnA/scroll state (ZenRouter.tsx:150-169).
- The authentication boundary is unconditional and correct: every non-auth route funnels through ProtectedRoute, which waits for bootstrap before deciding and redirects to login with an encoded returnTo (ZenRouter.tsx:71-92); AuthRoute mirrors it for signed-in users on auth pages.
- main.tsx orders its imports deliberately and documents why — CSP nonce plumbing first so runtime-injected <style> elements from Tiptap/Radix are nonced before anything can create one, then Sentry, then i18n before first paint so <html lang> is correct on the initial render (main.tsx:1-22).
- Security-conscious AnA framing in the shell: the rail's comment block (Shell.tsx:433-447) documents that a previously-shipped fetch to a non-existent /api/coauthor endpoint was 404-ing on every render and that its result was being discarded into the same fixture, and replaces the derived 'sample' flag with an honestly-obtained one — a real instance of the team finding and removing a fake-provenance path rather than papering over it.

#### `P0` HONESTY-01 — useLiveList treats a genuinely empty backend as a failure and renders fabricated regulatory records instead of an empty state

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/dataConnect.tsx:115` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · data-honesty

**Evidence.** matchesShape: `if (fixture.length === 0) return live.length > 0;` then `if (live.length === 0) return false;` (dataConnect.tsx:114-115). useLiveList then falls back: `return { data: fixture, loading: false, sample: true, error: raw.error };` (dataConnect.tsx:143). The module header even states the intent: 'Empty live list with a non-empty fixture is also rejected (nothing to show → keep the sample so the surface isn't blank)' (dataConnect.tsx:91). Consumers with hardcoded record arrays: BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:238,241,407,410,412,599,605,611 (PED_PLANS/PED_PREA/ORPH_DES/ORPH_RPD/ORPH_ADV/LCM_SUPP/LCM_REN/LCM_CMC), LabelingPi.tsx:121 (LP_ROWS), ChangeAssessment.tsx:147, DesignControls.tsx:84, BiopharmaProject.tsx:84.

**Failure scenario.** A newly provisioned customer org signs in and opens /concept2cure/pediatric. GET /api/biopharma/pediatric returns 200 with `{"data":[]}` — the org genuinely has no pediatric plans. matchesShape returns false because live.length===0, so the surface renders PED_PLANS: three fabricated FDA iPSP / EMA PIP records for products BX-204, BX-301 and BX-115, with deferral counts, waiver counts and PREA due dates. The regulatory lead reads 'PSP-204 · FDA iPSP · 12–17 · 1 deferral · PREA post-approval Y2' as their own portfolio. Identical behavior on /concept2cure/orphan (fabricated orphan designations and RPD vouchers), /concept2cure/lifecycle-mgmt (fabricated supplements/variations) and /concept2cure/labeling-pi (a fabricated 17-section BX-204 USPI including a boxed warning).

**Fix.** Delete the empty-list rejection: an HTTP 200 with zero rows is a successful empty result and must render EmptyState, not the fixture. Migrate the remaining useLive/useLiveList consumers onto useLiveRows/useLiveData + EmptyState (the pattern already proven in Registrations.tsx:168 and Vault.tsx:140) and delete the record-array fixtures. Until migration completes, at minimum distinguish `sample-because-empty` from `sample-because-error` and render EmptyState for the former.

#### `P0` HONESTY-02 — AnA composer silently discards every attachment; in Agent mode it discards the message entirely

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:428` · blocks **G1** · ~days · dead-control

**Evidence.** `const addFiles = (fl) => { if (!fl) return; setFiles((f) => [...f, ...Array.from(fl).map((x) => x.name)]); };` (Shell.tsx:428-431) — only File.name is retained; the File objects are dropped. `const send = () => { const t = draft.trim(); if (!t && !files.length) return; onSend(agent ? \`[Agent] ${t}\` : t || \`Attached ${files.length} file(s)\`); setDraft(''); setFiles([]); };` (Shell.tsx:451-457). There is no upload call anywhere in Shell.tsx. V2App.tsx:191-196: `const ask = (text) => { const clean = text.replace(/^\[Agent\]\s*/i, '').trim(); if (!clean) return; ... }`.

**Failure scenario.** Three distinct losses. (a) A user attaches CSR.pdf and types 'summarise the primary endpoint' — the message sent to /api/ana-ri/stream is only the text; the PDF is never mentioned or uploaded, and AnA answers about a document it was never given. (b) A user attaches CSR.pdf with no text — the literal string 'Attached 1 file(s)' is streamed to the model, which answers about nothing. (c) A user toggles Agent mode, attaches CSR.pdf, types nothing and presses send: onSend receives '[Agent] ', V2App's ask() strips the prefix, trims to '', and returns early — no request is made at all, yet the composer has already cleared setFiles([]). The attachment chips vanish, no message appears, no error is shown.

**Fix.** Store File objects, not names; POST them to the existing document/vault ingest endpoint before sending the turn and pass the resulting document ids in the stream request; block send (or show an error) until upload resolves. The correct implementation already exists in this codebase — ProjectHome.tsx:354-360 does real upload with an aria-live status region and per-file error state.

#### `P0` HONESTY-03 — Governed data-entry drawers tell the user an audit entry was written; nothing is persisted anywhere

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:249` · blocks **G3** · ~weeks · regulatory-honesty

**Evidence.** BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:249 `governed: 'Pediatric plans are governed -- saving writes an audit entry; AnA drafts the rationale for review.'`; onSubmit at :261-269 calls `addPlan({...}); setForm(false); fireToast('Pediatric plan added · ' + v.product + ' ' + v.kind);`. `useRows` (BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:163-179) is pure React state. grep for apiRequest/liveMutateOrNull/fetch in BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx returns zero hits. Same pattern at :420 ('a governed submission -- prevalence evidence and rationale are captured for the application package'), :619 ('Supplements are governed submissions -- classification and pathway are recorded'), :824 ('Signals are governed safety records -- logging creates a §11 audit entry and routes causality assessment'). Nonclinical.tsx:306 `governed: 'Study records are governed -- the report is classified, SEND is queued, and an audit entry is written.'` with onSubmit at :319-330 firing `fireToast('Study added -- ' + v.id + ' -- SEND queued')` and no network call. C2CForm renders the claim behind a padlock icon (C2CForm.tsx:179-188).

**Failure scenario.** A safety lead opens /concept2cure/pharmacovigilance, clicks 'Log signal', fills in an immune-mediated pneumonitis signal, reads the padlocked notice 'logging creates a §11 audit entry and routes causality assessment', and clicks the padlocked 'Log signal' button. A toast confirms success and the row appears in the table. No POST is issued, no audit row exists, no causality assessment is routed, and the row disappears on the next page load or navigation. In a Part 11 context the user has been affirmatively told a regulated record and its audit entry were created when neither exists.

**Fix.** Either wire these five forms to real POST endpoints with server-side audit writes, or change the governed copy to the honest form this codebase already uses elsewhere — ClinicalOps.tsx:334 says 'This adds the site to the board for review — it does not yet write to the governed store or write a Part 11 audit entry', CmcModule.tsx:141 and Risk.tsx:269 do the same. Do not ship a padlock icon and a §11 claim above an unwired write.

#### `P0` HONESTY-04 — Doc journey editor shows an 'Autosaved · v1.0' indicator over a contentEditable that has no persistence at all

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/DocJourney.tsx:262` · blocks **G1** · ~days · data-loss

**Evidence.** `<span className="dj-tb-save">{I.check} Autosaved · {s.seal ? 'v1.0' : s.wm ? s.wm.replace('DRAFT ', '') : 'v0.x'}</span>` (DocJourney.tsx:262), directly above `<div className={'dj-page' + ...} contentEditable suppressContentEditableWarning={true} spellCheck={false}>` (DocJourney.tsx:264). grep for apiRequest / liveMutateOrNull / fetch( / localStorage in DocJourney.tsx returns zero hits — the only network call in the file is a read (`liveGet<...>('/api/doc-journey', ...)` at :318). The toolbar's 'Track changes' toggle (DocJourney.tsx:261), 'Insert citation' (:258), 'Insert table' (:259) and the stage 'PDF' export (:406) have no onClick handler.

**Failure scenario.** A medical writer opens /concept2cure/doc-journey, sees the §2.5 Clinical Overview page, clicks 'Track changes' (nothing happens — the toggle has no handler and no state), types two paragraphs of revised endpoint language into the contentEditable, sees the persistent green check reading 'Autosaved · v1.0', and navigates to another surface. React unmounts the component; the edits are gone. There is no draft, no version, no recovery, and the user was explicitly told the work was saved.

**Fix.** Remove the contentEditable and the fabricated 'Autosaved' chip from this surface until it is wired, or route it to the real editor (document-authoring, which does have working persistence). If the surface is kept read-only, drop contentEditable and the toolbar entirely rather than leaving inert formatting controls above a fake save indicator.

#### `P0` NAV-01 — 17 of the 19 modules in the Apps catalog navigate to surface ids that do not exist, landing the user on an internal install-scaffold page titled 'Home'

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/AdminSurfaces.tsx:1461` · blocks **G1** · ~days · navigation

**Evidence.** Apps launch card: `<button className="btn ghost" ... onClick={() => open(a.id)}>Open</button>` (AdminSurfaces.tsx:1459-1465) where `const open = (id) => onNav(id)` (AdminSurfaces.tsx:1219) and `a.id = m.moduleId` from the live catalog (mapLiveCatalog, AdminSurfaces.tsx:1181). The catalog module_ids are seeded in db/migrations/20260220_user_intelligence_platform.sql:79-108: ectd-coauthor, ind-filing, 510k-submission, pma-submission, nda-bla, ce-marking, cmc-wizard, doc-canvas, protocol-designer, csr-author, cer-generator, evidence-engine, insight-synthesis, strategic-advisor, safety-signal, analytics-hub, compliance-monitor, vault, team-workspace. Only 'ectd-coauthor' and 'vault' exist as keys in SURFACE_VIEWS (surfaceViews.ts:163,229). V2App.tsx:166-220: an unknown id yields `surface = undefined`, `ctxSurface` falls back to a hardcoded object with `label: 'Home'`, `view = undefined`, and the body renders `<KitSurfaceScaffold surface={ctxSurface} .../>`. The repo's own nav gate, navTargets.test.tsx:20-22, documents this blind spot: 'Targets built from computed expressions (variables, props) can't be checked statically and are intentionally out of scope'.

**Failure scenario.** An admin opens Apps (a top-level rail destination), sees '510(k) Navigator — Predicate device analysis, substantial equivalence, FDA eSTAR integration' marked as enabled on their plan, and clicks Open. The URL becomes /concept2cure/510k-submission. The page that renders is titled 'Home', with the eyebrow 'Biotech & Pharma · workspace', a readiness chip reading 'Routes-ready', a card headed 'Mounted routes' listing '✓ /api/projects', a 'Bindings' card showing 'Layout mode: projects', and a numbered panel headed 'Install path · kit → live (5 layers)' ending with 'Hook — useProjects via apiQueryOptions — ~5 lines'. Sixteen more catalog entries behave identically.

**Fix.** Map available_modules.module_id (or the existing available_modules.path column, which is already populated) to real surface ids before calling onNav, and render an honest 'not yet available in this workspace' state for modules with no surface. Extend navTargets.test.tsx with a runtime assertion that every module_id in the seeded catalog resolves to a SURFACE_VIEWS key.

#### `P1` NAV-02 — No 404 handling: any unknown or stale deep link renders the internal engineering install scaffold under the title 'Home'

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:166` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · error-states

**Evidence.** `const surface = activeId === 'home' ? undefined : getSurface(activeId); const ctxSurface = surface ?? ({ id: 'home', label: 'Home', ... notes: 'Home opens with the segment pathway chips and the composer. Ports in the surface phase.' } as UiSurface);` (V2App.tsx:166-184) and `else { body = <KitSurfaceScaffold surface={ctxSurface} onAsk={ask} />; }` (V2App.tsx:219). routing.ts:11-17 returns any URL segment verbatim as the surface id. KitSurfaceScaffold (Surfaces.tsx:623-712) renders 'Mounted routes', 'Bindings', 'Layout mode', 'UI kit', 'Contract', 'Install path · kit → live (5 layers)', per-step engineering instructions ('~5 lines'), and 'Compliance rails gating this surface'.

**Failure scenario.** A user follows a bookmarked or emailed link to a surface that was renamed (e.g. /concept2cure/rbm-operations, retired per ui-surface-registry.ts:600-604). Instead of a 404 with a route back into the product, they get a page headed 'Home' asserting 'Mounted routes ✓ /api/projects' and a five-step internal install checklist. The user has no way to tell they mistyped, and a prospect on a pilot sees the vendor's internal build tracker.

**Fix.** Add an explicit unknown-id branch in V2App before the scaffold fallback that renders a real not-found state with a link to Home and the command palette. Restrict KitSurfaceScaffold to registered surfaces with no view (currently zero), or delete it — every one of the 96 registered surfaces already has a SURFACE_VIEWS entry, so it now serves only as the 404 handler.

#### `P1` NAV-03 — 91 of 96 registered surfaces have no global-nav entry; the only comprehensive module catalog (Home) is itself unreachable from the rail, and the brand logo opens the document editor

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/registryModel.ts:116` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · navigation

**Evidence.** RAIL_PRIMARY has exactly 5 entries — conversation-thread, projects, communication-center, apps, setup (registryModel.ts:116-122). UI_SURFACES totals 96 (44 in ui-surface-registry.ts + 52 in ui-surface-registry.ui-v2.ts), so 91 have no rail entry. The Home landing renders the only broad catalog — `getSegmentModules(segment)` covering 75 unique ids under 'Everything in your {category}' (Surfaces.tsx:65, 216-247) — but 'home' appears in no rail entry, is not in UI_SURFACES so it never appears in the ⌘K 'Jump to' list, and the brand logo navigates elsewhere: `onClick={() => onNav('document-authoring')} title="Document workspace"` (Shell.tsx:141-144). The only in-app path back to Home is clicking a Client Category, which mutates the user's segment as a side effect (`const selectSegment = (id) => { set('segment', id); nav('home'); }`, V2App.tsx:144-147). Eighteen registered surfaces (project-home, regulatory-workspace, investigator-brochure, report-engine, billing, ana-memory, ana-command, etmf, labeling-smpc, licensing, training, coverage, admin-console, onboarding, onboarding-ingest, usage, design-controls, human-factors) appear in neither the rail nor any SEGMENT_MODULES group.

**Failure scenario.** A first-time device RA lead lands on Home, browses the module grid, and opens 'Risk management'. They now want the 510(k) workbench. The rail offers only Chats, Projects, Communication Center, Apps and Settings. Clicking the Concept2Cure logo — the universal go-home gesture — drops them into a full-screen document editor with the AnA rail collapsed (surfaceViews.ts:160 sets full:true, hideAna:true). To get the catalog back they must click a Client Category, which silently changes their client segment, or hand-edit the URL to /concept2cure. Nothing in the UI tells them the catalog exists on Home.

**Fix.** Add Home as the first RAIL_PRIMARY entry (or make it the logo's target, which is the conventional affordance) and decouple 'return to Home' from 'change my client segment'. Surface the module catalog as a persistent destination rather than a landing-page-only section.

#### `P1` HONESTY-05 — AnswerLead renders confident regulatory narrative computed from fixture rows with no provenance marker

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:286` · blocks **G1** · ~days · data-honesty

**Evidence.** `const [plans, addPlan] = useRows<PedPlan>(livePlans.data);` (BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:239) — livePlans.data is the PED_PLANS fixture whenever sample===true. The AnswerLead headline is computed from it: `{drafts.length} pediatric {…} still in draft -- the closest gate is <b>{topDraft.kind} for {topDraft.product}</b> ({topDraft.due})` (BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:286-300). AnswerLead.tsx:40-72 renders only eyebrow/headline/body/reassure/action — it has no sample or provenance slot of any kind. The 'sample' chip that does exist is scoped to the cards below (`<SpCard ... sample={livePlans.sample}>`, BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:320) and to a hardcoded chip on the queue block (BiopharmaSpecialty.tsx:70), neither of which covers the lead. The same pattern repeats at :453 (Orphan), :653 (Lifecycle) and :859 (Pharmacovigilance).

**Failure scenario.** With the backend empty or 500-ing, /concept2cure/pediatric opens with an un-badged, top-of-page statement in the product's most authoritative voice: '3 pediatric plans are still in draft -- the closest gate is FDA iPSP for BX-204 (PREA · post-approval Y2)', followed by 'A pediatric plan usually gates the parent submission, so finishing the FDA iPSP rationale keeps your main program on track.' The design intent is stated in AnswerLead.tsx:2-7 — 'Every surface OPENS by speaking to the person's real question... the honest answer... before any table, chart, or metric'. The reader takes a program-timing statement about a product that does not exist as their own status.

**Fix.** Add a required provenance prop to AnswerLead and suppress or explicitly mark the lead whenever its inputs are sample-backed; better, do not compute a lead at all from fixture data — render EmptyState instead.

#### `P1` DEAD-01 — Roughly 25 controls across live surfaces render as functional and have no handler, including the primary 'Accept FDA text' action on the labeling-negotiation panel and five buttons in the persistent top bar

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/LabelingPi.tsx:205` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · dead-control

**Evidence.** LabelingPi.tsx:205 `<button className="reg-btn pri">{I.check} Accept FDA text</button>` and :207 `<button className="reg-btn ghost">View full redline</button>` — no onClick; the middle sibling at :206 does have one. LabelingPi.tsx:178-179: 'PDF' and 'Word' export buttons, no onClick. Shell.tsx:327 (org switcher), :337 (Task), :345 (Collaborate), :348 (Notifications), :351 (Help) — five inert buttons in the top bar rendered on every screen; the Task/Collaborate titles claim they 'land with the tasks surface port' / 'collaboration port', both of which have since shipped (surfaceViews.ts:142,224). ConversationThread.tsx:363 `<button className="ct-comp-attach" title="Attach a document for AnA to use">{I.paperclip}</button>` — no onClick, on the first rail destination. Surfaces.tsx:122-127: the Home composer's `<input type="file">` has ref, multiple and accept but no onChange, and Surfaces.tsx:143-149 clicks it. UsageBilling.tsx:376 'Buy more / Up to 30% off', :406 auto-reload 'Manage', :484 referral 'Copy link' (beside 'you both get $10 in usage credits. Terms apply'). DocJourney.tsx:258,259,261,406. CroPortfolio.tsx:198 'Onboard sponsor', :303 'Open programs'. Orchestration.tsx:422 'New run'. AgencyMeetings.tsx:440 'PDF'. ChangeAssessment.tsx:208 'Open change record'. AdminSurfaces.tsx:1565 'Export all' (flagged in a code comment the user never sees). Vault.tsx:224-232: the primary 'Upload' button calls onAsk with a sentence instead of uploading.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory affairs lead on /concept2cure/labeling-pi reviews FDA's proposed §1 Indications and Usage text against the sponsor text, decides to concede, and clicks the primary-styled 'Accept FDA text'. Nothing happens — no state change, no toast, no error. The lead reasonably concludes the decision is recorded, tells the team §1 is agreed, and the position is never captured anywhere. Separately, a user needing to attach a document on the Chats surface clicks the paperclip and gets no file dialog at all.

**Fix.** Sweep every <button> in the live surface set for a missing handler. For each: wire it, remove it, or render it visibly disabled with an explanatory tooltip. Extend the existing navTargets.test.tsx gate with a companion static check that fails the build on a rendered <button> that has neither onClick, type="submit", nor disabled.

#### `P1` A11Y-01 — Every field in every governed data-entry drawer is programmatically unlabeled, and segmented controls expose no selection state

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/C2CForm.tsx:169` · blocks **G2** · ~days · accessibility

**Evidence.** `<div key={f.key} className={'de-field' + ...}><label className="de-label">{f.label}{f.required && <span className="req">*</span>}</label>{f.desc && <div className="de-desc">{f.desc}</div>}{renderField(f)}</div>` (C2CForm.tsx:169-178). The <label> is a sibling, not a wrapper, and carries no htmlFor; renderField (C2CForm.tsx:96-153) emits inputs/selects/textareas with no id, no aria-label and no aria-labelledby. Required fields get a visual '*' but no aria-required. The 'seg' control renders plain <button>s whose selection is conveyed only by `data-on` (C2CForm.tsx:126-145), styled solely by border/background/text colour (journey-v2.css:420-422) — no role="radiogroup"/"radio", no aria-checked, no aria-pressed. C2CForm is mounted by 24 surface files (Nonclinical, Risk, CmcModule, HaqManager, ClinicalOps, IdentityConsole, GatewayTransmittals, ReportGovernance, BiopharmaSpecialty, DesignControls, HumanFactors, NdaCockpit, QmpWorkspace, ProtocolRegisterForms, AgencyMeetings, Labeling, CommunicationCenter, DeepResearch, Inconsistency and others). The registry declares 'accessibility-enforcement' as a gating compliance rail on essentially every surface (ui-surface-registry.ts:105,128 et seq.).

**Failure scenario.** A screen-reader user tabs into the 'Add a GLP study' drawer at /concept2cure/nonclinical. NVDA announces 'edit, blank' for Study ID, 'combo box' for Study type and Species, and for the finding-classification segmented control it announces four unrelated buttons — 'clean button', 'non-adverse button', 'adverse button', 'pending button' — with no indication that they are one group or which is selected. The user cannot complete a governed regulatory record. The same failure occurs in the SCIM token-issue and IP-allowlist drawers (IdentityConsole.tsx:61,71) and the gateway transmit drawer (GatewayTransmittals.tsx:84).

**Fix.** Generate a stable id per field, set htmlFor on the label and id on the control, add aria-required for required fields and aria-describedby for f.desc. Convert 'seg' to a radiogroup with role="radio" + aria-checked (or native radios) so state is programmatic, not colour.

#### `P1` A11Y-02 — Streaming AI output floods assistive tech token-by-token in the AnA rail and is entirely silent on the primary Chats surface — both contradict the repo's own Human Factors spec

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:490` · blocks **G2** · ~days · accessibility

**Evidence.** `<div className="ana-body" aria-live="polite">` (Shell.tsx:490) wraps the whole message list including `<div className="bd">{m.body}</div>` (Shell.tsx:618). useAnaChat rewrites that text on every SSE chunk: `streamedText += chunk; const next = streamedText; setMessages(prev => prev.map(m => m.id === assistantId ? { ...m, text: next, statusPhase: undefined } : m));` (useAnaChat.ts:924-935). Conversely ConversationThread — the first RAIL_PRIMARY destination — has no aria-live anywhere: the scroll container is `<div className="ct-scroll" ref={scrollRef}>` (ConversationThread.tsx:322) and the streaming indicator is three empty spans with no text or label (`<div className="ct-typing"><span /><span /><span /></div>`, ConversationThread.tsx:352). HUMAN_FACTORS_AND_USABILITY_SPEC_2026-06-15.md:123 requires exactly the opposite: 'Streaming AnA responses announce start and completion politely (not per-token, to avoid speech flooding); an injected error uses aria-live="assertive". A spinner alone is never the only completion signal.'

**Failure scenario.** A screen-reader user asks AnA a question in the rail. Each SSE token mutates the live region, so the reader restarts and re-speaks the growing answer dozens of times per second — the answer is effectively unusable and the user cannot interrupt it. The same user on /concept2cure/conversation-thread gets the opposite failure: they send a message, three unlabelled dots animate, and nothing is announced at all — they have no way to know the reply started, streamed, or finished.

**Fix.** Take the streaming message out of the live region; add a separate visually-hidden polite region that announces only 'AnA is responding' and 'Response complete', plus an assertive region for errors. Add the same region to ConversationThread and give ct-typing an accessible label. The correct pattern already exists at ProjectHome.tsx:402 (`<div aria-live="polite" className="sr-only">{statusMessage}</div>`).

#### `P1` HONESTY-06 — Collaboration 'send' silently discards the message with no toast, no error and no persistence

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/CollabLauncher.tsx:360` · blocks **G1** · ~days · dead-control

**Evidence.** `const send = () => { if (!body.trim()) return; /* MOCK ACTION (flagged): does NOT call POST /api/collaboration/messages; at most it captures an optimistic in-session task below. */ if (makeTask) { C2C.addTask({...}); onCreated?.(); } onClose(); };` (CollabLauncher.tsx:360-379). When makeTask is false — the default — the panel simply closes: no network call, no toast, no state change. The panel header advertises the opposite: `<span className="cl-ctx-meta">thread on <code>/api/collaboration</code></span>` (CollabLauncher.tsx:385). The recipient picker is fixture-backed (`team: TB_TEAM as Record<string, TeamMember>`, CollabLauncher.tsx:135-141) with a hardcoded default `const [to, setTo] = useState('sm')` (:357). The launcher renders on every surface (V2App.tsx:293 `<CollabLayer onNav={nav} />`) and is also bound to ⌘⇧T (CollabLauncher.tsx:444-447).

**Failure scenario.** A reviewer presses ⌘⇧T on the eCTD compile surface, switches to Discuss, picks a colleague from the dropdown, writes 'Module 3 stability data does not match the spec — please confirm before we freeze', and clicks send. The panel closes cleanly. No message is delivered, nothing is stored, no confirmation or error appears, and the named colleague may not even be a real user in the org (the picker lists fixture people). The reviewer believes the blocker has been raised.

**Fix.** Wire send() to POST /api/collaboration/messages and source the recipient list from GET /api/collaboration/team, or disable the Discuss tab. At minimum apply the honest-toast pattern already used one screen over for tasks — CollabLauncher.tsx:522 renders 'Captured in this session -- not saved to the org board yet.'

#### `P1` BILL-01 — Every plan-management and upgrade link points at a route that does not exist and silently redirects the user to the home greeting

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/AdminSurfaces.tsx:1364` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · billing

**Evidence.** Three anchors target /settings/subscription: the license band's 'Manage plan' (AdminSurfaces.tsx:1361-1366), the degraded-license fallback's 'Manage plan' (:1379-1384) and, critically, the entitlement-gated 'Upgrade plan' CTA on every locked add-on card (:1466-1473). grep across client/src finds no route registration for /settings — App.jsx:55-76 has no matching Route, so the request falls through to `<Route>{() => <Suspense><ZenRouter /></Suspense>}</Route>` (App.jsx:69-75); ZenRouter's uiV2Owns test fails for that path (ZenRouter.tsx:159-162) and the Switch's catch-all fires: `<Route>{() => <Redirect to="/concept2cure" />}</Route>` (ZenRouter.tsx:238).

**Failure scenario.** A customer on the Standard tier opens Apps, sees 'NDA / BLA Suite — Upgrade your plan to unlock', and clicks the primary 'Upgrade plan' button. The browser performs a full navigation to /settings/subscription, the app boots, and the user is redirected to the 'Good afternoon' greeting page with no explanation. The single monetisation path out of an entitlement gate dead-ends, and the same happens for 'Manage plan' — a paying customer cannot reach billing self-service from the license band at all.

**Fix.** Point these at the real in-app billing surface (SURFACE_VIEWS has both 'billing' and 'usage' mapped to UsageBilling, surfaceViews.ts:227-228) via onNav, or register /settings/subscription. Add a route-existence assertion for every literal href in the v2 surfaces alongside the existing navTargets gate, which today only covers onNav/nav/open call sites.

#### `P1` I18N-01 — 18 shipped locales cover 144 auth-only keys; zero of the 127 shipping surfaces are translated and the language switcher is unreachable after login — while <html lang> is set to the chosen language

`client/src/i18n/index.ts:33` · blocks **G2** · ~months · i18n

**Evidence.** `applyDocumentLanguage` sets `document.documentElement.lang = def.code` and `dir` on every language change (i18n/index.ts:34-40, 68-70). client/public/locales ships 18 languages × 4 namespaces totalling 144 keys each (auth 107, common 27, home 6, settings 4). grep for useTranslation across client/src returns 14 files; zero are under client/src/concept2cure/v2 — the entire shipping shell (Shell.tsx, V2App.tsx and all 127 surface files) is hardcoded English. Every LanguageSwitcher mount point is either an auth page (Concept2CureLogin.tsx:384, ZenSignup.tsx:1006) or a file unreachable from main.tsx (Concept2CureHome.tsx:314 and the cmc/risk/biopharma/intelligence TopBar.tsx shells). GLOBAL_LANGUAGE_STRATEGY_2026-06-29.md:14-17 claims 'Product/UI internationalization (~85% built) — i18next, 19 languages with real translation bundles, a language switcher, account-persisted preference'.

**Failure scenario.** A PMDA-facing user selects 日本語 on the login page. The login form translates. After authenticating there is no language control anywhere in the shell, and every subsequent screen — rail, top bar, all 127 surfaces, all governed form copy, all error and empty states — is English. Meanwhile document.documentElement.lang is 'ja', so a screen reader switches to a Japanese speech synthesiser and reads English regulatory text with Japanese phonology (WCAG 3.1.1). A buyer told the product is '~85% internationalised' and selling into Japan discovers post-close that only the sign-in page is localised.

**Fix.** Either scope the claim honestly (auth-only localisation) or begin extracting shell and surface strings. Independently and immediately: only set <html lang> to a locale for which the rendered content is actually translated, and expose the language control inside the shell (Settings) rather than only on auth pages.

#### `P1` HONESTY-07 — Hardcoded regulatory counts render beside a 'Live' provenance pill on the labeling surface

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/LabelingPi.tsx:144` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · data-honesty

**Evidence.** `<div className="reg-kpi" data-tone="err"><div className="reg-kpi-v">1</div><div className="reg-kpi-l">Boxed warning proposed</div></div>` (LabelingPi.tsx:144) and `<div className="reg-kpi-v">17</div><div className="reg-kpi-l">Full PI sections</div>` (:142) are literals, unconnected to `rows`. The adjacent 'Open agency edits' KPI at :143 IS derived (`rows.filter(s => s.flag === 'agency').length`). The provenance pill at :137 is `<SampleTag sample={sample} />`, which renders 'Live' whenever useLiveList adopted the backend rows (dataConnect.tsx:147-159).

**Failure scenario.** An org's real label worklist loads successfully from /api/labeling-pi with the full section shape, so SampleTag renders 'Live'. Under that 'Live' badge the surface displays a red-toned KPI reading '1 — Boxed warning proposed'. The org may have zero proposed boxed warnings, or three. The single most consequential fact on a US prescribing-information screen is a constant, presented as live data.

**Fix.** Derive both KPIs from rows (boxed-warning count from the 'BW' section's state; section count from rows.length) or remove them. Add a lint/test rule forbidding numeric literals inside .reg-kpi-v on surfaces that render SampleTag.

#### `P1` A11Y-03 — Twelve icon-only controls in live surfaces have no accessible name, including the send button on the primary chat surface and five modal close buttons

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/ConversationThread.tsx:367` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · accessibility

**Evidence.** Icons are correctly hidden from AT — `return <Icon size="1em" strokeWidth={1.75} aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" />;` (icons.tsx:123) — which means an icon-only button with no title and no aria-label has no accessible name at all. In the live (import-reachable) surface set these are: ConversationThread.tsx:367 (send), ProjectHome.tsx:597 (send), PrecedentEngine.tsx:622 (submit claim), TemplateLibrary.tsx:579 (apply edit), and close buttons at AnaCommand.tsx:755, AnaVerbs.tsx:450, GovernedActionModal.tsx:39, FilingsCatalog.tsx:71, Projects.tsx:198, TaskBoard.tsx:454, :612, :704. By contrast the AnA rail's send button does it correctly (`aria-label="Send"`, Shell.tsx:756).

**Failure scenario.** A screen-reader user on /concept2cure/conversation-thread — the first rail destination and the product's declared primary operating surface — types a question, tabs to the send control, and hears only 'button'. They cannot distinguish it from the inert paperclip beside it (which is also unnamed and does nothing). Opening any task detail panel, they tab to the close control and hear 'button' again with no way to know it dismisses the dialog.

**Fix.** Add aria-label to each. Add the missing controls to the existing a11y-names test pattern — that test currently exercises only Composer.tsx and Message.tsx, both of which are unreachable dead code.

#### `P2` A11Y-04 — Status is encoded by colour alone on the prescribing-information section tree, which the repo's own Human Factors spec explicitly forbids

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/styles/app-v2.css:2130` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · accessibility

**Evidence.** .lp-sec-dot is a 7×7px circle differentiated across five states solely by background colour: approved→--success, review→--warning, negotiation→--accent-100, draft→--text-400, na→--bg-300 (app-v2.css:2130-2135). The row that contains it (LabelingPi.tsx:164-171) renders the section number, the section label, an optional 'agency' flag icon, and the dot — no status text, no title, no aria-label, no shape variation. HUMAN_FACTORS_AND_USABILITY_SPEC_2026-06-15.md:124 states: 'Color is never the only channel. Confidence bands, status pills (Drafting / In review / Approved / Blocked / Locked / Ready)... carry a text label and/or icon and shape, not just the earthy color. A user with color-vision deficiency must be able to tell...' and :187 lists it as an acceptance checkbox.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory reviewer with deuteranopia opens /concept2cure/labeling-pi to triage the 17-section USPI before an end-of-cycle labeling meeting. --success green and --warning amber are indistinguishable to them at 7px, so 'Approved' and 'In FDA labeling review' sections look identical. They present a section as agreed that is still open with the agency.

**Fix.** Add a text or shaped indicator to each section row (a short status word, or distinct glyphs per state) and a title/aria-label on the dot. Audit the same pattern across .kdot, .pj-goal-dot, .pj-find-dot, .pt-col-dot and .tb-path-dot.

#### `P2` A11Y-05 — No focus management on either modal primitive: no focus trap, no focus restore, no aria-modal on the data-entry drawer

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:942` · blocks **G2** · ~days · accessibility

**Evidence.** CmdK focuses its input on open (`setTimeout(() => inputRef.current?.focus(), 0)`, Shell.tsx:942-948) but never restores focus on close (the run/Escape paths at Shell.tsx:1025-1055 just call onClose), and nothing constrains Tab — the shell behind the overlay is neither inert nor aria-hidden, so Tab walks straight into the rail and top bar underneath. The list is a plain div of buttons with no role="listbox"/"option" and no aria-activedescendant, so the arrow-key selection tracked in `sel` (Shell.tsx:1042-1047) is purely visual (`className={...sel === si ? ' on' : ''}`, Shell.tsx:1102) and is never announced. C2CForm has role="dialog" but no aria-modal, no initial focus and no focus trap (C2CForm.tsx:156-158); it handles only Escape (:88-94).

**Failure scenario.** A keyboard user presses ⌘K, types 'audit', and presses ArrowDown three times. Nothing is announced — the screen reader still reports the text field and never mentions that 'Audit trail' is now highlighted. They press Tab instead; focus silently leaves the dialog and lands on a rail button behind the scrim, where Enter navigates the app underneath the still-open palette. On Escape, focus is lost to <body> and the next Tab restarts from the top of the page.

**Fix.** Add a focus trap and focus restore to both primitives; mark the palette input/list as a combobox+listbox with aria-activedescendant so arrow selection is announced; add aria-modal="true" to C2CForm and focus its first field on open.

#### `P2` NAV-04 — The organization switcher is inert and TenantContext.setCurrentOrganization has zero consumers — multi-org users cannot change organization from the UI

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:327` · blocks **G2** · ~days · multi-tenancy

**Evidence.** `<button type="button" className="tb-org" title="Organization (switcher lands with the auth flow phase)"><span className="tb-org-mark">{orgMark}</span><span className="tb-org-name">{orgName}</span><span className="tb-org-chev">{I.down}</span></button>` (Shell.tsx:327-331) — a chevron-bearing menu affordance with no onClick. TenantContext exposes `setCurrentOrganization: handleSetCurrentOrganization` (TenantContext.tsx:398) and loads the full organization list (:151-162), but grep across client/src for switchOrganization/setCurrentOrganization outside TenantContext.tsx returns zero call sites.

**Failure scenario.** The product markets a CRO segment with 'Multi-sponsor portfolio... org-isolated across sponsors' (registryModel.ts:1013-1016) and a CRO home program card reading 'Portfolio — 7 sponsors · 12 studies'. A CRO user belonging to three organizations signs in, is placed in whichever org the bootstrap selected, and finds a top-bar control showing their org name with a dropdown chevron that does nothing when clicked. There is no other org-switch affordance in the shell, so they cannot reach their other sponsors' workspaces at all.

**Fix.** Wire the button to a menu backed by TenantContext.organizations + setCurrentOrganization, or remove the chevron and render the org name as static text so it does not read as a control.

#### `P2` DEADCODE-01 — Half the client tree (479 of 783 files, ~90,000 LOC) is unreachable from the entry point, the repo's only accessibility test exercises dead components, and dead-surface CSS still ships

`client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/__tests__/a11y-names.test.tsx:16` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · maintainability

**Evidence.** A module-graph walk from client/src/main.tsx (resolving relative + @/ + @shared/ specifiers) reaches 304 of 783 source files; 479 files totalling 90,028 LOC are unreachable. Largest clusters: concept2cure/components/ana 11,230 LOC (47 of 53 files dead, including Message.tsx, ChatView.tsx, Part11SignModal.tsx, renderSafeMarkdown.ts, SealBadge.tsx), v2/surfaces 8,351 LOC across 25 files (TranslationWorkspace.tsx, the entire Editor* family, ReviewersEye.tsx, VaultSources.tsx), v2/fixtures 7,926 LOC across 32 files. The single accessibility test in the repo imports two of those dead files: `import { Composer } from '../Composer'; import { Message } from '../Message';` (a11y-names.test.tsx:11-12) — so the shipping UI has no a11y coverage at all. client/src/high-contrast.css exists with `* { color: black !important; background-color: white !important; }` and is imported by nothing. Rollup tree-shakes the dead JS (the string 'txw-act' is absent from dist/public/assets/*.js) but its stylesheet still ships, because live AdminSurfaces.tsx:36 imports '../styles/translation-v2.css' for a surface (TranslationWorkspace) that is registered nowhere.

**Failure scenario.** A buyer's security reviewer runs SCA/SAST across client/src and must triage findings in 90k LOC of code that cannot execute, with no manifest distinguishing live from dead. A new engineer fixes an accessibility bug in Composer.tsx, watches a11y-names.test.tsx pass, and ships nothing — the user-facing composer is Shell.tsx's AnaRail, which the test never touches. The 'zero reachable UI' translation offering is the same story: TranslationWorkspace.tsx and translation-workspace-data.ts exist and compile, but no registry entry, no SURFACE_VIEWS key and no import reference them.

**Fix.** Delete the unreachable trees (knip.json already exists in the repo and can enumerate them) or move them to an explicitly excluded directory. Rewrite a11y-names.test.tsx against the live Shell AnaRail, ConversationThread and C2CForm. Remove the orphaned translation-v2.css import from AdminSurfaces.tsx.

#### `P2` PERF-01 — No route-level code splitting below the shell: all 100 surfaces are statically imported into one 1.56 MB chunk, ~900 KB gzipped total first load

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaceViews.ts:18` · blocks **G2** · ~days · performance

**Evidence.** surfaceViews.ts:18-102 statically imports all 100 surface components at module scope; SURFACE_VIEWS (surfaceViews.ts:119-230) holds direct component references with no React.lazy. V2App imports it eagerly (V2App.tsx:31). Built output (dist/public/assets, 2026-07-28): V2App-B-lJY4T-.js 1,558,490 bytes raw / 432,832 gzip; V2App-D4XI4l9g.css 694,554 / 97,488; ZenRouter-DTJeGLxe.js 530,497 / 144,339; ZenRouter CSS 152,595 / 22,665; index.js 141,412 / 44,701; index.css 205,150 / 36,447. Total 3,697,024 raw / 902,029 gzip. The only lazy boundaries in the app are App.jsx:38 (ZenRouter) and ZenRouter.tsx:44 (V2App).

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory affairs user on a hospital or pharma VPN signs in to check one task on the Chats surface. Before the greeting paints they download ~900 KB gzipped / 3.7 MB decompressed, including the parse and execute cost of all 100 surfaces — the CMC module, the RBM analytics, the eCTD compiler, the IVD completeness workbench — none of which they open. On a throttled 3G-equivalent link this is a multi-second blank screen behind ZenLoadingScreen, which renders `<div className="min-h-screen bg-transparent" aria-hidden="true" />` (ZenRouter.tsx:59-61) — a completely empty, AT-hidden element with no loading text.

**Fix.** Convert SURFACE_VIEWS entries to React.lazy so each surface is its own chunk, keeping only Home, ConversationThread and Projects eager. Split the 695 KB CSS bundle alongside. Give ZenLoadingScreen visible, announced loading copy.

#### `P2` DISCLOSE-01 — The internal surface-registry install tracker is exposed to customers from the account menu

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:104` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · information-disclosure

**Evidence.** `{ label: 'Codebase coverage', ic: 'grid', to: 'coverage' }` sits in ACCT_ITEMS, the bottom-left account menu shown to every authenticated user with no role gate (Shell.tsx:93-108; only the 'Admin' entry above it is gated, at :98). It resolves to CodebaseCoverage (surfaceViews.ts:144), which renders the entire UI_SURFACES registry cross-referenced against SURFACE_VIEWS: 'every canonical surface, its real mounted API prefixes, AnA tool families, typed contract and honest readiness... Doubles as the Phase-3 install/coverage tracker' (Coverage.tsx:1-10), including readiness chips labelled 'Kit-only' and 'Planned' (Coverage.tsx:22-27).

**Failure scenario.** A prospect in a paid pilot clicks their avatar, sees 'Codebase coverage', and opens the vendor's internal build tracker. They learn that Risk management, Labeling, PDEV, Biopharma and the FDA CRL library are 'Kit-only' — the registry's own definition is 'Design prototype exists; backend binding map being assembled' (registryModel.ts:280-284) — and they get a complete enumeration of the platform's internal REST prefixes on one screen. This surfaces during the evaluation as a credibility problem and as free reconnaissance.

**Fix.** Remove 'Codebase coverage' from the customer account menu; gate the surface behind an internal/staff flag or delete it from SURFACE_VIEWS for production builds.

#### `P2` DESTR-01 — Revoking an enterprise SCIM provisioning tenant is a single unconfirmed click

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/IdentityConsole.tsx:136` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · destructive-action

**Evidence.** `const revoke = useCallback(async (id: number) => { const { ok, status } = await rawJson('DELETE', \`/api/admin/scim-tenants/${id}\`); if (!ok) { fireToast(...); return; } fireToast('SCIM tenant #' + id + ' revoked.'); ... }, [...])` (IdentityConsole.tsx:136-142), invoked directly from `<button className="nda-open" ... onClick={() => revoke(t.id)}>{I.alertTriangle} Revoke</button>` (IdentityConsole.tsx:205). No confirmation, no reason prompt, no typed-name gate. The repo does the right thing one file over — AdminSurfaces.tsx:1870-1881 prompts for a ≥3-character reason before revoking an access grant and records it in the audit trail.

**Failure scenario.** An admin on the Enterprise identity console misclicks 'Revoke' on the wrong row while comparing two SCIM tenants. The DELETE fires immediately, the toast confirms, and that identity provider's provisioning integration stops — new joiners are no longer created, leavers are no longer deprovisioned, and the token cannot be recovered (the console stores hash-only per §11.10(d), per the registry note at ui-surface-registry.ts:797). There is no undo and no recorded reason for the change.

**Fix.** Apply the reason-prompt pattern already used for access-grant revocation, ideally with a typed-confirmation for irreversible credential destruction, and record the reason server-side.

#### `P3` NAV-05 — NAV_HIDDEN — the mechanism the design-constitution comment relies on to justify collapsing the rail to five destinations — is dead code with duplicate and unregistered entries

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/registryModel.ts:125` · ~hours · dead-code

**Evidence.** registryModel.ts:111-114 asserts 'DEMOTED, NOT DELETED. Every id removed from the rail moves into NAV_HIDDEN below, which is the established "reachable via ⌘K and deep-link, just not a rail entry" mechanism.' NAV_HIDDEN's only consumer is surfacesByTier (registryModel.ts:1078-1087), and grep across client/src finds zero call sites for surfacesByTier outside its own definition and the registryModel unit test — so the set gates nothing. The ⌘K palette does not consult it: it filters UI_SURFACES directly (Shell.tsx:960, 982-984). The set itself has 42 entries but only 40 unique — 'tasks' (lines 136, 155) and 'vault' (129, 158) are duplicated — and 'review-approve' (line 152) is not a registered surface id. Shell.tsx:114-115's railVisible flag gate for 'crl-library' is likewise unreachable, since crl-library is not in RAIL_PRIMARY.

**Failure scenario.** An engineer adds a surface to NAV_HIDDEN expecting the documented demotion semantics and observes no behavioural change whatsoever, because nothing reads the set. A buyer auditing the seller's claim of design-constitution §4 compliance finds the compliance argument rests on a code path that does not run.

**Fix.** Either delete NAV_HIDDEN, surfacesByTier and railVisible as dead code, or make the palette actually consult NAV_HIDDEN (e.g. to de-rank demoted surfaces) so the comment is true. Deduplicate and drop the unregistered id either way.

#### `P3` UX-01 — Dark-mode preference is defined, styled and persisted with no control anywhere to set it

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:239` · ~hours · ux

**Evidence.** `dark: boolean` is part of Prefs and persisted to localStorage under 'c2c-v2-prefs' (V2App.tsx:46, 56, 110-119); it drives `className={\`c2c-v2 shell${prefs.dark ? ' dark' : ''}\`}` (V2App.tsx:239) and app-v2.css carries .dark rules. grep across client/src/concept2cure/v2 finds no `set('dark'` call and no theme toggle in Shell.tsx, the account menu (Shell.tsx:93-108) or the Setup surface. The shell also has no prefers-color-scheme handling of its own — the only occurrence in client/src is in the unimported high-contrast.css.

**Failure scenario.** A user working late, or one who has set their OS to dark mode for photosensitivity reasons, has no way to switch the application theme. The capability is built, styled and persisted, and is simply unreachable.

**Fix.** Add a theme control to the account menu or Setup, and honour prefers-color-scheme as the initial default.

### external integrations (server/integrations, server/services/connectors, FDA/EUDAMED clients, FDA ESG + eCTD external validator, email, billing/Stripe, storage providers, virusScan, ssrfGuard)

This unit is bimodal. The deliberately-hardened parts are genuinely good: FDA ESG — the most dangerous surface in the product — is honest and fail-closed (simulation is opt-in and gated to NODE_ENV development/test only; the simulated response is status 'simulated_not_transmitted' and the ack stub literally prints "THIS IS NOT AN AGENCY ACKNOWLEDGEMENT"), the AS2/SFTP gateway is real protocol code that persists FDA's verbatim MDN and refuses to call an SFTP PUT an acknowledgement, and ssrfGuard is correctly applied at both credential-storage time and every outbound sink on exactly the four connectors with tenant-supplied base URLs. The lead alleging bare-fetch SSRF in AnaToolExecutor / citation-verification-service / guidance-ingestion-service does NOT hold — every one of those fetches targets a fixed host. The parts nobody hardened are badly broken: the Stripe webhook can never verify a signature because the global /api JSON parser consumes the body first (reproduced: HTTP 400 on a correctly-signed event), so no paid subscription ever activates; annual checkout charges ~1/12 of the intended price; storage get() resolves a version id across every tenant with no org check; the FAERS/MAUDE safety tools report page size as the true adverse-event total and report an openFDA outage as "no adverse events found"; and an AnA tool advertised as "search FDA guidance documents" actually queries openFDA's chemical-substance registry and relabels substance names as guidance. The dominant risk is not the FDA transmission path the seller's own docs worry about — it is that commerce and the evidence-retrieval integrations were never held to the same honesty bar as the submission gateway.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- FDA ESG simulation is opt-IN and fail-closed: server/services/ESGSubmissionService.ts:59-62 allows the non-transmitting path only for an exact NODE_ENV of 'development' or 'test'; unset/blank/'staging'/'production' all refuse. The simulated response (line 361-378) carries status 'simulated_not_transmitted', transactionId 'SIMULATED-NOT-SENT-*', no acknowledgment number, and simulated:true. downloadAcknowledgment (line 509-525) returns a document headed 'THIS IS NOT AN AGENCY ACKNOWLEDGEMENT'. Production transmit/status/ack all throw a structured not-implemented error rather than fabricating an FDA transaction (lines 350-356, 407-412, 547-552). Audit lead (d) is refuted: there is no path where the platform claims a submission was transmitted when it was not.
- server/services/submission-gateways/fda-esg.ts is real transport code, not a stub: mTLS AS2 POST with rejectUnauthorized:true (line 245-256), SFTP fallback via dynamic import, and — importantly — after an SFTP PUT it sets status 'in_transit' with NO ackReceivedAt and an explicit comment refusing to fabricate an FDA acknowledgment (lines 374-391). The agency's raw MDN body is persisted verbatim (line 426-431) and downloadAcknowledgment returns it with provenance:'agency', falling back to a clearly-labelled platform record otherwise (lines 505-531).
- rollbackTransmittal (server/services/submission-gateways/fda-esg.ts:632-730) is transactional, tenant-scoped, SELECT FOR UPDATE-locked, writes the governed-action audit row BEFORE mutating state, and returns agencyRetractionRequired:true plus a note telling the operator the agency still holds the bytes. This is the correct model for an irreversible external action.
- server/utils/ssrfGuard.ts is a single audited implementation covering https-only, loopback, RFC1918, CGNAT, link-local/metadata (169.254), IPv6 ULA/link-local and .internal/.local suffixes, and it is applied twice — at credential storage (connector-registry.ts:168-190) and immediately before every outbound fetch. server/services/connectors/fhir-r4.ts:320 even re-validates a SMART token endpoint that was DISCOVERED from the tenant's own /.well-known/smart-configuration, which is the exact case a naive implementation misses.
- server/integrations/veeva-vault/client.ts states and keeps an explicit honesty contract: VaultCredentialError names the missing env vars, every Vault responseStatus FAILURE becomes a VaultApiError carrying Vault's own error list (assertVaultSuccess, line 147-159), and there is no mock mode anywhere in the module.
- server/services/connectors/veeva-vault.ts escapes every user value into VQL via escapeVqlLiteral and exposes buildDocumentVql as a pure, unit-testable function (lines 24-56) — real injection defence, not a comment. server/fda_maude_client.js does the same for openFDA Lucene via luceneField/safeDate.
- Stripe webhook handling is otherwise correct: signature verification with a generic client-facing error so the endpoint is not fingerprinted (server/routes/billing.ts:287-295), and idempotency keyed on event.id in stripe_events before processing (server/services/billing.ts:630-646). organizationId is never read from the request body on any billing route (billing.ts:41-45, 88-96, 208-219).
- server/eudamed_client.js:122-141 defaults to an explicit dataSource:'unavailable', isLiveData:false, zero-incident response rather than fabricating EU vigilance data; illustrative records are opt-in behind EUDAMED_ALLOW_SIMULATED and every record carries simulated:true.
- server/services/citation-verification-service.ts verifies citations against live PubMed ESummary and CrossRef, distinguishes verified / not_found / unverifiable / error rather than asserting existence, flags retracted publications, and detects an identifier that resolves to a different article than the cited title (computeDiscrepancies, lines 245-261).
- The eCTD external-validator seam is designed fail-closed: NoopExternalValidator returns passed:false and ran:false for an un-run validation (noop-validator.ts:21-32), and evaluateExternalValidationGate treats 'required but did not run' as a hard blocker (gate.ts:52-59). lorenz-adapter.ts throws rather than returning a fabricated pass.
- server/middleware/uploadSafety.ts:151-166 fails CLOSED in production when the AV scanner did not actually run, and correctly refuses to echo the matched signature name back to the client.
- server/services/integrations/connector-search.ts reports per-connector failures in a `skipped` array with reasons ('not connected for this organization', 'credentials invalid or connector unhealthy', or the raw error) instead of collapsing them into an empty result set.

#### `P0` BILL-01 — Stripe webhook signature verification is structurally impossible — every webhook returns 400, so no paid subscription is ever activated

`server/startup/middleware.ts:74` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · billing-correctness

**Evidence.** server/startup/middleware.ts:70-74 mounts the Firecrawl webhook raw-body-safe BEFORE the parser (`// 5. Firecrawl webhooks — raw-body-safe, must come BEFORE the global JSON parser.`) then `app.use('/api', express.json({ limit: '2mb' }));`. The Stripe webhook gets no such exemption: server/routes/billing.ts:269 declares `router.post('/webhooks/stripe', raw({ type: 'application/json' }), ...)` and the router is mounted at /api/billing in server/bootstrap/register-inline-routes.ts:159, i.e. after applyCoreMiddleware (server/index.ts:113). billing.ts:266-267 even documents the requirement: 'IMPORTANT: This must use raw body parsing... It should be mounted BEFORE the JSON body parser in index.ts.' body-parser 2.3.0 raw() is a no-op once the stream is consumed, so req.body reaches stripe.webhooks.constructEvent as a parsed object.

**Failure scenario.** Reproduced end-to-end against express 5.2.1 / body-parser 2.3.0 / stripe 18.5.0 with the exact mount order: a correctly HMAC-signed customer.subscription.updated event POSTed to /api/billing/webhooks/stripe returns HTTP 400 {"error":"Invalid signature","detail":"Webhook payload must be provided as a string or a Buffer ... Payload was provided"}. Concretely: a customer completes Stripe Checkout and is charged. Stripe delivers checkout.session.completed and customer.subscription.created; both 400. handleSubscriptionUpdated never runs, so organizations.tier, payment_status, seats_purchased, max_projects, max_storage and next_billing_date are never set and provisionModulesForTier never fires. getSubscriptionStatus (billing.ts:583-618) then reports payment_status 'incomplete' and active:false. The customer has paid and has no entitlements. Symmetrically, invoice.payment_failed and customer.subscription.deleted also 400, so a churned or delinquent customer is never downgraded.

**Fix.** Mount the Stripe webhook path before the global JSON parser exactly as Firecrawl is, e.g. `app.use('/api/billing/webhooks/stripe', express.raw({type:'application/json'}), stripeWebhookRouter)` at step 5 of applyCoreMiddleware; or attach a `verify` callback to the /api express.json that stashes the raw Buffer on req.rawBody and pass that to constructEvent. Add an integration test that signs a payload with STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET and asserts 200 through the real app pipeline, not a bare router.

#### `P0` BILL-02 — Annual subscriptions are priced at roughly one twelfth of the intended amount

`server/services/billing.ts:356` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · billing-correctness

**Evidence.** billing.ts:342-357: `let perUserAmount = tierPricing.perUserMonthly; ... let unitAmount = perUserAmount * seatCount; if (billingCycle === 'annual') { unitAmount = Math.round(unitAmount * (1 - tierPricing.annualDiscountPct / 100)); }`, then billing.ts:379-385 creates the Stripe price with `unit_amount: unitAmount` and `recurring: { interval: billingCycle === 'annual' ? 'year' : 'month' }`. There is no multiplication by 12 anywhere in the file (grep for `12` returns only minCommitmentMonths). The identical bug is in createDTCCheckoutSession at billing.ts:491-501.

**Failure scenario.** A pharma org with 1 seat picks Standard ($459/user/month, PRICING.pharma at billing.ts:164) and billingCycle 'annual' on POST /api/billing/checkout (route enum permits it, server/routes/billing.ts:53). unitAmount = 45900 cents, then 45900 * 0.87 = 39933 cents. Stripe price is created as $399.33 per YEAR. Correct annual price is 459 * 12 * 0.87 = $4,791.96. The customer is billed $399.33/year for a $5,508/year product — a 92% revenue loss, contractually locked in at the price the customer agreed to on the Stripe page. Same for DTC Startup Biotech: $499/mo becomes $424.15/year instead of $5,089.80.

**Fix.** Multiply by 12 before applying the annual discount when interval is 'year' (or create a monthly-interval price with interval_count and let Stripe do the arithmetic). Add a unit test asserting the created price's unit_amount for each tier x cycle combination against an explicit expected table.

#### `P1` STOR-01 — Storage providers resolve a vaultVersionId across every tenant; two AI-action handlers pass a caller-supplied id with no organization check

`server/services/storage/local-provider.ts:166` · blocks **G1** · ~days · cross-tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** IStorageProvider.get/delete/getSignedUrl take only a vaultVersionId — there is no orgId parameter (server/services/storage/storage-provider.ts:53-70). LocalStorageProvider.findVersionDir (local-provider.ts:166-179) iterates every directory under storage/vault (i.e. every organization) looking for the version id. S3StorageProvider.getMeta (s3-provider.ts:275-303) does ListObjectsV2 with `Prefix: ''` and matches any key containing `/versions/{id}/_meta.json`, again across all orgs.
