Callers pass the id straight from request input: server/services/ai-actions/handlers/ocr-extract-text.ts:47-68 (`const vaultVersionId = String(request.payload.vaultVersionId); ... const file = await storage.get(vaultVersionId)`) and extract-template-from-upload.ts:44-48. The payload schema on POST /api/ai-actions/execute is `payload: z.record(z.unknown()).optional()` (server/routes/ai-actions.ts:77), so nothing constrains it.

**Failure scenario.** Reproduced against the exact LocalStorageProvider logic: org 2 stores 'confidential-IND.txt' under storage/vault/2/secret-program/versions/<uuid>. A user authenticated to org 1 POSTs /api/ai-actions/execute with actionType 'ocr_extract_text' and payload.vaultVersionId = that uuid. findVersionDir walks storage/vault/1 then storage/vault/2, finds the directory, and the handler returns the OCR'd contents of org 2's file to the org-1 caller ('ORG 2 CONFIDENTIAL SUBMISSION DATA' in the reproduction). The version id is a UUIDv4 so it is not guessable, but it is not a secret either — it appears in action provenance records, logs and API responses, and there is no tenant check anywhere in the path to stop it once known. Separately, because vaultVersionId is joined with path.join and never validated, a value containing ../ segments escapes the vault root; LocalStorageProvider.delete would then rmSync that directory recursively (local-provider.ts:98-104), though no route currently reaches delete.

**Fix.** Add orgId (and ideally projectId) to the IStorageProvider get/delete/getSignedUrl signatures and scope the lookup to that tenant's prefix; keep a DB index of vaultVersionId -> (orgId, projectId) instead of scanning. Reject any vaultVersionId that is not a bare UUID before it reaches path.join. In both AI-action handlers, verify the resolved file's metadata.orgId equals ctx.user.organizationId before returning bytes.

#### `P1` SAFETY-01 — FAERS/MAUDE adverse-event tools report page size as the true report total, and report an openFDA outage as 'no adverse events found'

`server/fda_faers_client.js:274` · blocks **G1** · ~days · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** fda_faers_client.js:274 sets `total_reports: apiResponse.results.length` — the number of records returned, capped by the request limit — while the genuine total from `apiResponse.meta.results.total` is stored on meta (line 202) and never read by analyzeFaersData. analyzeFaersData:329 builds `Analysis of ${results.total_reports} FDA FAERS reports found ${results.serious_reports} serious reports (${seriousPercentage}%)`. server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:1466-1493 caps max_results at 100 (default 50), passes it as `limit`, and emits provenance titled `FAERS adverse-event signal for ${subject} (${total} reports)`. On the failure side, fda_maude_client.js:203-207 catches every API error and `return []`, and analyzeMaudeData:286-294 turns an empty array into `summary: 'No FDA MAUDE data found for this device.'`; fda_faers_client.js:172-181 returns an object whose `error` key analyzeFaersData never inspects.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory user asks AnA for the FAERS safety signal on a widely-used drug with ~40,000 FAERS reports. search_drug_adverse_events requests limit 50, openFDA returns 50 records, and the tool tells the model 'Analysis of 50 FDA FAERS reports found 12 serious reports (24.0%)' with provenance 'FAERS adverse-event signal for <drug> (50 reports)'. The user is given a report count three orders of magnitude too low and a seriousness percentage computed over an arbitrary 50-record page, and there is nothing in the payload indicating truncation. Second scenario: openFDA is rate-limiting or down. searchDeviceReports catches, returns [], and the tool answers 'No FDA MAUDE data found for this device' with total_reports 0 — an affirmative clean-safety claim manufactured from a network failure. Either output can land verbatim in a CER or PSUR narrative.

**Fix.** Return meta.results.total as the authoritative total and label the returned records as a sample of size N; propagate a distinct status ('unavailable' with the upstream error) instead of [] / 0 on failure, and make the AnA handlers surface that status rather than a zero count. openFDA also caps limit at 1000 and offers skip-based paging if a true tally is needed.

#### `P1` INTEG-01 — 'Live FDA guidance' AnA tool queries openFDA's chemical-substance registry and relabels substance names as guidance documents with a fabricated status

`server/services/regulatory-currency/guidance-ingestion-service.ts:128` · blocks **G1** · ~days · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** `const DEFAULT_FDA_URL = 'https://api.fda.gov/other/substance.json';` (line 128) — the openFDA GSRS substance registry, not a guidance-document endpoint. The mapping at lines 174-180 is `title: r.substance_name ?? r.title ?? 'Unknown'`, `issueDate: r.issue_date ?? r.create_date ?? ''`, `url: r.url ?? 'https://www.fda.gov/search?q=' + encodeURIComponent(r.substance_name ?? '')`, and `status: opts.status ?? r.status ?? 'unknown'` — the returned status is the caller's own filter echoed back. The result is returned as `{ status: 'fetched', guidances, source: 'fda.gov' }` (line 182-187). The tool description handed to the model reads 'Search FDA guidance documents from the live FDA guidance API. Returns matching guidance documents with their title, issue date, topic, URL, and status. Use to check current FDA guidance on a topic before advising.' (server/services/ana/guidanceIngestionTools.ts:18-19), and the tool is registered in ALL_ANA_TOOLS via AnaToolDefinitions.ts:2525 and handled at AnaToolExecutor.ts:15827-15836.

**Failure scenario.** A user asks AnA 'what is the current final FDA guidance on companion diagnostics?'. The model calls fetch_fda_guidance_list with topic 'companion diagnostic', status 'final'. The service queries api.fda.gov/other/substance.json, maps each returned substance record to a 'guidance' whose title is a chemical substance name, whose issueDate is empty, whose URL is a generic fda.gov search link, and whose status is the literal string 'final' because that is what the caller asked for. The model receives status:'fetched', source:'fda.gov' with no indication of degradation and presents these as current final FDA guidance documents. The user acts on, or cites, non-existent guidance.

**Fix.** Either wire the real FDA guidance source (the guidance-documents JSON/CSV export at fda.gov) or delete the tool. Never echo the requested filter into a returned record's status field. Also note `params.set('search', searchTerms.join('+AND+'))` (line 157) URL-encodes the '+' so even the intended openFDA search syntax is malformed.

#### `P1` AV-01 — Virus scanning is fail-open on the chat upload path and fail-closed everywhere else, and a production deploy without CLAMAV_HOST kills all governed uploads with no boot warning

`server/routes/chat/upload.ts:136` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · security-control-consistency

**Evidence.** server/utils/virusScan.ts:80-87 returns `{scanned:false, clean:true, reason:'clamav_not_configured'}` when CLAMAV_HOST is unset, and the file explicitly warns 'Callers must NOT treat scanned:false as a clean signal'. server/middleware/uploadSafety.ts:151-166 honours that: in production it throws 503 FILE_SCAN_UNAVAILABLE. server/routes/chat/upload.ts:136-143 does not: `if (!scan.scanned) { logger.warn('Virus scan bypassed', ...) }` and the request proceeds to write the bytes to disk (lines 163-170), in every environment including production. server/services/securityHealth.ts:217-225 marks the clamav check `status:'skipped', critical:false` when CLAMAV_HOST is unset, so runBootSecuritySelfTest raises nothing.

**Failure scenario.** Production deploy (npm run start, NODE_ENV=production) without CLAMAV_HOST — a realistic first deployment since nothing at boot demands it. Outcome A: every governed upload path (server/routes/document-routes.ts:197, ctd-onboarding.ts:179, evidence-management.routes.ts:166, protocol_routes.ts:707/766, analytics-routes.ts:75, api/templates/routes.ts:444) returns 503 'File scanning is temporarily unavailable' — document ingestion, the core journey of the product, is entirely dead and the boot self-test reported a clean posture. Outcome B: the same operator's users can still attach files through the chat paperclip endpoint, which stores an unscanned EICAR/malware file under uploads/org-N/ and later re-reads it for parsing. A customer security review that tests both paths finds an inconsistent AV control and an unscanned ingress in production.

**Fix.** Make chat/upload.ts:136 use the same production fail-closed branch as uploadSafety (ideally call assertUploadSafe there instead of duplicating the logic), and make the clamav boot check critical (not 'skipped') when NODE_ENV is production so a missing CLAMAV_HOST halts or loudly warns at boot rather than silently disabling uploads.

#### `P1` STOR-02 — S3 storage provider cannot find any file once the bucket exceeds 1000 objects — get, getSignedUrl and delete silently fail

`server/services/storage/s3-provider.ts:279` · blocks **G1** · ~days · data-availability

**Evidence.** getMeta (s3-provider.ts:275-303) is the single resolution path for get(), delete() and getSignedUrl(): `ListObjectsV2Command({ Bucket, Prefix: '', MaxKeys: 1000 })` then `.find(obj => obj.Key?.includes('/versions/' + vaultVersionId + '/_meta.json'))`. There is no ContinuationToken loop. The comment at line 276 concedes 'In production, use DB index. For now, scan is acceptable for this provider.' list() (lines 186-238) is likewise a single un-paginated ListObjectsV2 (default 1000 keys).

**Failure scenario.** put() writes two objects per file (the payload plus _meta.json), so a bucket holds 2N objects for N files. Once N exceeds ~500, ListObjectsV2 returns only the lexicographically first 1000 keys. Any file whose meta key sorts after that window makes getMeta return null: get() returns null (the AI-action handlers report FILE_NOT_FOUND for a file that exists), getSignedUrl throws 'S3: version X not found' so the download link cannot be produced, and delete() returns false while leaving the objects in place. Because keys are prefixed with the org id as a string, org '10' sorts before org '2', so which tenants lose access is arbitrary. The bytes are intact in S3 but unreachable through the product — indistinguishable from data loss to the user, on the exact provider intended for production.

**Fix.** Persist vaultVersionId -> (orgId, projectId, key) in Postgres at put() time and read the key from there; failing that, paginate with ContinuationToken and scope the Prefix to the caller's org. Paginate list() as well.

#### `P1` ENT-01 — IVDR entitlement check queries columns that exist in no schema, so /api/ivdr fails in production and is silently open in development

`server/bootstrap/register-regulatory-routes.ts:103` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · entitlements

**Evidence.** The query at lines 103-110 is `SELECT 1 FROM module_subscriptions ms JOIN available_modules am ON ms.module_id = am.id WHERE ms.organization_id = $1 AND am.module_key = 'ivdr_module' AND ms.status = 'active'`. module_subscriptions has no `status` column in any definition — shared/schema.ts:731-757, migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:4072-4085, or server/db/bootstrap/auth-schema.ts:138-146. available_modules has no `module_key` column (shared/schema.ts:714-728; migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:206-221); grep shows 'module_key' appears in this one file and nowhere else in the repo. The join is also text = serial (module_subscriptions.module_id is TEXT and the real FK targets available_modules.module_id, per migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:6711). The catch at lines 125-142 only special-cases 42P01 (undefined table) and a timeout; an undefined-column error falls to `if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'development') throw err;`.

**Failure scenario.** Any request to /api/ivdr/* in production: Postgres raises 42703 (column ms.status does not exist) or 42883 (operator does not exist: text = integer), the middleware rethrows, and the request 500s. The entire IVDR module — a licensed, tier-gated product surface — is unreachable for every tenant regardless of entitlement. In development the same error is swallowed at line 138-141 and the request proceeds, so the failure is invisible to the team that wrote it and only appears for paying customers.

**Fix.** Fix the query to `JOIN available_modules am ON ms.module_id = am.module_id ... AND am.module_id = 'ivdr_module' AND ms.enabled = true`, and make the catch treat any unexpected DB error as a deny in every environment rather than allowing dev-mode access.

#### `P1` EMAIL-01 — POST /api/report-os/deliveries records a regulatory correspondence as 'sent' when no email is ever transmitted

`server/routes/report-os.ts:1814` · blocks **G3** · ~days · honesty

**Evidence.** The handler (report-os.ts:1744-1846) accepts a recipients array, persists an outbound correspondence row into c2c_correspondence with `direction:'outbound'` and status 'responded' (persistCorrespondenceToPlatform, lines ~690-720), then builds the delivery record with `status: payload.channel === 'platform_send' ? 'sent' : 'exported'` (line 1814), persists it and returns 201. Nothing in the file imports or calls any mail transport. server/services/emailService.ts:489-529 defines sendReportDeliveryEmail — including a return shape `{delivered, transport:'smtp'|'log'}` designed exactly for this — and a repo-wide grep shows zero callers of sendReportDeliveryEmail, sendGenericEmail or sendInvitationEmail.

**Failure scenario.** An integrator (or a future UI) POSTs /api/report-os/deliveries with channel 'platform_send' and recipients ['pm@fda.hhs.gov']. The API returns 201 with status 'sent'; the platform writes an auditable outbound-correspondence record asserting a transmittal to that recipient with direction 'outbound' and status 'responded'. No mail is generated. Under Part 11 this is a falsified record of a regulatory communication in the system of record, and the org's correspondence timeline will show a submission-related transmittal that never occurred. Mitigating fact I verified: no client code in this repo calls the endpoint today, so it is API-only exposure — but the route is live-mounted at /api/report-os and the record it writes is the audit-relevant one. Note also that organizationId is taken from the request body (createDeliverySchema, report-os.ts:109), not the JWT, so the falsified record can be written into another tenant's correspondence store.

**Fix.** Call sendReportDeliveryEmail and derive status from its `delivered` result ('sent' only on transport 'smtp', otherwise 'failed' or 'queued'); refuse channel 'platform_send' outright when isEmailConfigured() is false; and source organizationId from the authenticated tenant context rather than the body.

#### `P1` BILL-03 — Any authenticated user can self-provision the Enterprise tier for $0 through the industry checkout route

`server/services/billing.ts:352` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · entitlements

**Evidence.** PRICING.pharma Enterprise has `perUserMonthly: 0` with the comment '// Custom pricing' (billing.ts:186-196; same for medtech:232 and academic:273). createCheckoutSession does not guard it: `let unitAmount = perUserAmount * seatCount;` (line 352) then creates a Stripe recurring price with unit_amount 0 (line 379-392) and subscription_data.metadata tier 'enterprise', maxProjects '-1', maxStorageGB '1000' (lines 405-419). server/routes/billing.ts:52 permits tier 'enterprise' on POST /api/billing/checkout behind authenticateToken only, with no role check. createDTCCheckoutSession does guard the same case — `if (dtcTier.baseMonthly === 0 && tier !== 'free') throw new Error('Enterprise tier requires custom pricing')` (billing.ts:460) — proving the omission on the industry route is an oversight, not a policy. On the receiving side handleSubscriptionUpdated maps maxProjects -1 to 99999 (line 753) and calls provisionModulesForTier(orgId,'enterprise') which, at orgLevel 3, enables every module (lines 849-876).

**Failure scenario.** Any authenticated user in any org POSTs /api/billing/checkout {tier:'enterprise', billingCycle:'monthly', seats:1}. Stripe creates a $0/month recurring price and a Checkout session; on completion the subscription carries metadata tier 'enterprise'. handleSubscriptionUpdated then sets organizations.tier='enterprise', max_projects=99999, max_storage=1000 and enables every row in available_modules for that org — Enterprise entitlements, unlimited projects, all modules, $0 charged. This is latent only because BILL-01 currently prevents any webhook from being processed; fixing BILL-01 without fixing this makes it immediately exploitable, which is the dangerous fix ordering.

**Fix.** Reject a tier whose perUserMonthly is 0 in createCheckoutSession exactly as the DTC path does, and additionally require an admin/owner role on POST /api/billing/checkout. Ignore tier metadata from a subscription whose price unit_amount is 0.

#### `P1` ECTD-01 — Agency-grade eCTD external validation never runs in any code path, and configuring EVALIDATOR_ENDPOINT makes validation strictly worse while reporting 'configured'

`server/services/ectd/external-validator/lorenz-adapter.ts:29` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · regulatory-validation

**Evidence.** LorenzEValidatorAdapter.isConfigured returns true for any http(s) value of EVALIDATOR_BINARY/EVALIDATOR_ENDPOINT without probing it (`if (/^https?:\/\//i.test(bin)) return true;`, line 29), but validate() unconditionally throws 'LORENZ eValidator report parsing is not yet implemented' (lines 51-54). resolveExternalValidator (index.ts:32-38) returns the LORENZ adapter as soon as isConfigured() is true, so the working, license-free FdaCriteriaValidatorAdapter fallback is never reached. Separately, assess-dispatch-readiness.ts:178-185 never calls validate() at all — it only folds in `params.externalValidationReport ?? null` — and grep shows no caller anywhere (AnaToolExecutor.ts:9358, submission-service.ts:299 and :463, routes/submissions.ts:944 and :1268) ever supplies that field. The surfaced flag is `configured: externalValidator.name !== 'noop'` (line 179, 205).

**Failure scenario.** An operator sets EVALIDATOR_ENDPOINT=https://evalidator.internal/api believing they have wired the agency validator, and also had EVALIDATOR_USE_FDA_CRITERIA_FALLBACK=true. resolveExternalValidator now picks LORENZ, so the FDA-criteria structural checks (missing backbone, MD5 index, empty leaves, naming violations) stop running entirely. assess-dispatch-readiness reports externalValidation {configured:true, ran:false, errorCount:0, cleared:true} — the readiness surface says an agency-grade validator is configured and the gate is clear, while zero external validation of any kind occurred. With ECTD_REQUIRE_EVALIDATOR unset (the default) an eCTD sequence is cleared for dispatch to FDA having never been validated by anything beyond the in-house structural checks.

**Fix.** Make isConfigured() reflect the ability to actually produce a report (return false while validate() is unimplemented, or probe the endpoint), so the FDA-criteria fallback is reachable. Have assessSequenceDispatchReadiness actually invoke resolveExternalValidator().validate() against the built package rather than relying on a parameter no caller passes, and stop reporting configured:true for an adapter that can only throw.

#### `P2` EMAIL-02 — Weekly-digest and comparison-notification endpoints report success for mail that was only logged, with an env-var escape hatch that re-enables the fabrication in production

`server/routes/notification_routes.ts:18` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · honesty

**Evidence.** sendEmail (notification_routes.ts:12-30) refuses only when `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' && process.env.ENABLE_MOCK_NOTIFICATIONS !== 'true'`; otherwise it logs '[MOCK EMAIL]' and returns true. Callers treat that as delivery: POST /api/notify/send-weekly-digest responds `{success:true, message:'Weekly digest sent successfully'}` and writes an export-log row of actionType 'send_digest' (lines 61-79); POST /api/notify/send-comparison-notification does the same (lines 228-248). Compare ESGSubmissionService.simulationAllowed (server/services/ESGSubmissionService.ts:59-62), which in the same repo requires an exact 'development' or 'test' and explicitly refuses unset/blank/'staging'.

**Failure scenario.** A pilot or staging deployment run with `tsx server/index.ts` or any process where NODE_ENV is unset or 'staging' (the canonical npm start does set production, which is the mitigating control): a user requests their weekly digest, the server prints '[MOCK EMAIL] Sending email to user@example.com', returns 'Weekly digest sent successfully', and writes an audit row asserting the digest was delivered. No mail exists. Separately, setting ENABLE_MOCK_NOTIFICATIONS=true in a genuine production environment restores the fabricated success by design. getProtocolDetails (lines 329-353) likewise returns a hardcoded fake protocol titled 'Phase 3 Study of Drug XYZ for Treatment of Condition ABC' outside production, which becomes the email subject line.

**Fix.** Route these through server/services/emailService.ts (sendGenericEmail already returns a real boolean) and return the transport result; delete the ENABLE_MOCK_NOTIFICATIONS bypass or make it impossible when NODE_ENV is anything other than 'development'/'test', matching the ESGSubmissionService convention already established in this repo.

#### `P2` FAERS-02 — FAERS query builder does not quote or escape drug/manufacturer names, so multi-word names silently return wrong results

`server/fda_faers_client.js:123` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** fda_faers_client.js:120-129 interpolates raw input: `searchQuery += 'patient.drug.medicinalproduct:' + productName;` and `searchQuery += 'patient.drug.openfda.manufacturer_name:' + manufacturer;`. Only productNdc is quoted (line 117). The sibling MAUDE client does this correctly via luceneField/escapeLucenePhrase (fda_maude_client.js:40-42, used at lines 148-160), and grep shows no escapeLucene/luceneField in the FAERS client at all.

**Failure scenario.** A user asks for the FAERS profile of 'Humira Pen'. The Lucene query becomes `patient.drug.medicinalproduct:Humira Pen`, which openFDA parses as the field term 'Humira' plus a bare term 'Pen' against the default field — returning a different, larger and unrelated result set than requested, with no error. The adverse-event counts and top-reaction list presented to the user are for the wrong query. The same input path also lets a crafted value inject Lucene operators (e.g. `x OR _exists_:patient`) to attribute an arbitrary result set to a product.

**Fix.** Reuse the luceneField/escapeLucenePhrase helpers already implemented in fda_maude_client.js for productName and manufacturer, and validate date inputs with the existing safeDate helper instead of interpolating them raw at lines 136-140.

#### `P2` CONN-01 — Connector catalog reports 'healthy' for connectors that were never configured or whose credentials were never tested

`server/services/connectors/connector-registry.ts:150` · blocks **G1** · ~days · honesty

**Evidence.** getConnectorCatalog derives `healthy: credMap.get(entry.id) !== false` (line 150). For a connector with no row in connector_credentials, credMap.get returns undefined and `undefined !== false` is true, so healthy is true. storeCredentials (lines 183-201) writes `is_valid = true, last_validated_at = NOW()` unconditionally, without ever calling connector.authenticate() or status(). No health probe is performed anywhere in getConnectorCatalog. server/services/integrations/integration-status.ts:146 consumes exactly this (`catalog.filter(c => c.configured && c.healthy)`) to tell AnA which repositories are live.

**Failure scenario.** An admin pastes an expired Veeva Vault password into Settings. storeCredentials marks is_valid=true, the connectors catalog shows Veeva Vault as configured and healthy, and AnA's self-report says 'Connected document repositories (veeva_vault)'. The first actual search fails authentication — surfaced only inside connector-search's `skipped` array, not in the settings surface the admin was looking at. Equally, every credential-free connector (ClinicalTrials.gov, PubMed, EUDAMED, EMA, PMDA, NMPA, Grants.gov) is reported healthy even in a deployment with no outbound egress, so the platform asserts availability it has never verified.

**Fix.** Call connector.authenticate()/status() during storeCredentials and set is_valid from the result; derive healthy from a recent status() probe (cached), and report 'unknown' rather than true when no probe has run.

#### `P2` PROV-01 — Document intake provenance records the Tika metadata step as successful even when the Tika sidecar was unreachable

`server/services/documentIntelligence/intakePipeline.ts:42` · blocks **G3** · ~hours · audit-trail-accuracy

**Evidence.** intakePipeline.ts:36-44 pushes `{step:'tika_metadata', provider:'tika', success: true, ...}` with the literal true, unconditionally. TikaClient.detectMetadata (server/integrations/tika/client.ts:47-58) swallows every failure and returns a fallback object `{contentType:'application/octet-stream', isScannedPdf:false, metadata:{fallback:true, reason:...}}`. Every other step in the same pipeline derives success from the result (docling at line 68, unstructured at line 83, ocrmypdf at line 56).

**Failure scenario.** The Tika sidecar at TIKA_BASE_URL is down. detectMetadata returns the fallback with isScannedPdf:false, so the OCR branch at line 47 never fires and a scanned PDF is silently processed as if it had a text layer. The provenance chain persisted for that document asserts 'tika_metadata ... success: true, details: contentType=application/octet-stream'. An auditor reconstructing how a regulatory document was ingested is shown a successful metadata-extraction step that never happened, and the recorded content type is wrong.

**Fix.** Return an explicit degraded flag from TikaClient (it already sets metadata.fallback) and set provenance success from it, matching how docling/unstructured/ocrmypdf are handled three lines below.

#### `P2` LINT-01 — Document quality report cannot distinguish 'no issues found' from 'two of three checkers never ran'

`server/services/documentQuality/qualityLintService.ts:67` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · honesty

**Evidence.** runVale swallows every failure with `catch { return []; }` (qualityLintService.ts:67-68) and LanguageToolClient.check does the same internally (server/integrations/languagetool/client.ts:58-62, `logger.warn(...); return []`). The emitted DocumentQualityReport (server/services/documentIntelligence/contracts.ts:77-83) carries only artifactId, versionId, advisoryMode, generatedAt and issues — there is no field recording which checkers executed. POST /api/conversation-os/conversations/:id/quality/lint returns it verbatim (server/routes/conversation-os.ts:184-192).

**Failure scenario.** The `vale` binary is absent and LANGUAGETOOL_BASE_URL points nowhere — the default in any deployment that has not stood up the sidecars. A user runs quality lint on a CSR section. Both external checkers return empty, only the in-house Concept2Cure rules contribute, and the response is a report with a short issues array and no indication of degradation. The user reads it as 'this section passed style and grammar QC'. Severity is limited by advisoryMode defaulting to true and the whole surface being behind the qualityChecks feature flag.

**Fix.** Add a `checkers: Array<{name, ran, reason?}>` field to DocumentQualityReport and populate it from each client's actual outcome; render 'not run' distinctly from 'no findings'.

#### `P2` EUDAMED-01 — EUDAMED simulated-record tagging is stripped by the CER aggregation layer, which folds illustrative incidents into real totals and risk level

`server/data_integration.js:181` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** eudamed_client.js tags illustrative data thoroughly — meta.dataSource 'illustrative', meta.isLiveData false, meta.simulated true, and simulated:true on every incident (lines 195-239). analyzeEudamedData (lines 273-347) reads only `eudamedData.incidents` and returns totals plus `summary: 'Analysis of N EUDAMED incidents found M serious incidents with F Field Safety Corrective Actions.'` with no simulated marker. data_integration.js:181-195 consumes that analysis, adds eudamedEvents/eudamedSerious into `totalEvents`/`seriousEvents`, pushes the literal string 'EU EUDAMED' into `sourcesUsed` whenever incidents.length > 0 (line 132-134), and calculateRiskLevel (lines 318-337) derives High/Medium/Low from those combined counts.

**Failure scenario.** An operator sets EUDAMED_ALLOW_SIMULATED=true to 'preview the workflow'. gatherIntegratedData then reports sources: ['FDA MAUDE','EU EUDAMED'], a totalEvents figure that includes 5-50 fabricated EU incidents (including entries typed 'Death'), a seriousEvents count, and a calculateRiskLevel verdict computed over them — with the simulated flag surviving only in the untouched sourceData.eudamed blob that the summary consumer does not read. Mitigating fact I verified: data_integration.js has no importers anywhere in the repo, so this aggregation path is currently dead code — but it ships, and the client it depends on is live.

**Fix.** Propagate meta.simulated into analyzeEudamedData's return and into the aggregated summary/sources (e.g. label the source 'EU EUDAMED (SIMULATED)' and refuse to compute a risk level when any contributing source is simulated), or delete data_integration.js if it is genuinely dead.

#### `P2` VEEVA-01 — The Veeva Vault coexistence integration is complete, unwired, and single-tenant despite an org-scoped API

`server/integrations/veeva-vault/vault-sync-service.ts:143` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · integration-completeness

**Evidence.** VaultSyncService.fromDefaults() builds the client with `VeevaVaultClient.fromEnv()` (line 143), and resolveVaultConfig reads process-global VEEVA_VAULT_DNS/USERNAME/PASSWORD (client.ts:71-95). pushDocument/pullDocument take organizationId but use it only for the veeva_vault_links row and the audit entry (lines 147-210) — the Vault connection itself is identical for every tenant. A repo-wide grep for VaultSyncService, pushDocument and pullDocument outside server/integrations/veeva-vault returns nothing: no route, service or AnA tool imports it. Meanwhile the signup welcome email promises 'Connect with Veeva, Medidata, SharePoint, and more' (server/services/emailService.ts:232) and the connector catalog advertises Veeva Vault as a professional-tier connector (connector-interface.ts:320-357).

**Failure scenario.** A CRO buyer evaluating 'Veeva Vault coexistence' finds a well-written protocol client and sync service that no user journey can reach — the only live Veeva path is the read-only federated-search connector in server/services/connectors/veeva-vault.ts, which does not push documents back. If the sync service is later wired as written, a multi-tenant deployment would have every organization reading from and writing to whichever single Vault the operator configured in env, because the org id never reaches the connection.

**Fix.** Either wire VaultSyncService to a route and switch it to per-org credentials loaded from connector_credentials (the encrypted per-org store already exists in connector-registry.ts), or remove it and stop advertising push-back coexistence until it exists.

#### `P2` CONN-02 — Tenant connector credentials are encrypted with a hardcoded key unless NODE_ENV is exactly 'production'

`server/services/connectors/connector-registry.ts:60` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · secrets-management

**Evidence.** connector-registry.ts:52-60: the boot refusal fires only `if (!ENCRYPTION_KEY_FROM_ENV && process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production')`; otherwise `const ENCRYPTION_KEY = ENCRYPTION_KEY_FROM_ENV || 'default-dev-key-change-in-prod';`, which is then scrypt-derived and used for AES-256-GCM over the full credential JSON (lines 78-98, 192).

**Failure scenario.** A staging or pilot environment brought up with NODE_ENV unset or 'staging' and no CONNECTOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY stores every tenant's Veeva Vault password, Box client secret, Azure client secret, Google service-account private key and FHIR bearer token encrypted under a key string that is committed to this repository. Anyone with a copy of the database (a backup, a snapshot, a support export) and the source recovers all of them. The canonical npm start sets NODE_ENV=production, which is the mitigating control; the repo's own ESGSubmissionService (server/services/ESGSubmissionService.ts:59-62) documents why that check should not be an equality test against 'production'.

**Fix.** Require CONNECTOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY whenever NODE_ENV is not exactly 'development' or 'test' — i.e. invert the gate to opt-in-to-insecure, matching the fail-closed convention already used for ESG simulation.

#### `P2` FIRE-01 — Firecrawl webhook persists the full body of every unauthenticated, signature-failing request into the audit log

`server/routes/firecrawl-webhooks.ts:15` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · resource-abuse

**Evidence.** The route accepts up to 2MB of any content type (`express.text({ type: '*/*', limit: '2mb' })`, line 9), computes isValid, then INSERTs into external_tool_audit_log with `JSON.stringify({ rawPayload, signaturePresent })` — before the 401 is returned (lines 15-28). tenant_id is coerced to 0 for unauthenticated callers (line 19). The path is on the auth-boundary public allowlist (server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:91).

**Failure scenario.** An unauthenticated attacker POSTs 2MB bodies with no signature to /api/firecrawl-webhooks. Each request writes a ~2MB row into external_tool_audit_log under tenant_id 0 and returns 401. The Redis /api rate limiter bounds the rate, but sustained traffic within that budget grows the audit table without limit and pollutes the same table used for genuine external-tool audit evidence. Secondary observation: even a validly signed webhook is only logged — the handler returns {success:true} without processing the crawl result, so Firecrawl completion events are recorded and discarded.

**Fix.** Verify the signature first and store only a hash plus size for failures; cap the persisted payload length; attribute failures to a dedicated table or a bounded counter rather than the tenant audit log.

#### `P2` GUID-01 — Guidance freshness check reports unknown citations as current, and the ICH registry is six hardcoded entries with no refresh path

`server/services/regulatory-currency/guidance-ingestion-service.ts:292` · blocks **G2** · ~days · silently-wrong-regulatory-output

**Evidence.** checkGuidanceFreshness returns, for any citation matching neither registry, `{ title, current: true, warning: 'Not found in the curated registry — freshness could not be verified.' }` with the inline comment '// Conservatively assume current if not found' (lines 291-296). The ICH registry it checks against is a six-element hardcoded array (ICH_GUIDELINES, lines 79-122) with no ingestion or expiry mechanism. The tool description presented to the model asserts the check is 'DETERMINISTIC — pure cross-reference' (server/services/ana/guidanceIngestionTools.ts:69).

**Failure scenario.** A reviewer runs check_guidance_freshness over a document citing 'FDA Guidance for Industry: Bioanalytical Method Validation (2018)' and 'ICH Q3D(R1)'. Neither is in the six-entry ICH list or the currency registry, so both come back current:true. Any UI or model summary keying off the boolean renders a green 'current' for citations whose freshness was never checked — the opposite of conservative for a regulatory currency review. Because the ICH table is hardcoded, it also goes stale silently: the moment ICH publishes a new step date, every deployment reports the old one as authoritative with no signal.

**Fix.** Return current:null (or a distinct 'unverified' state) when neither registry matches, and drive ICH step dates from a dated, refreshable data source with an explicit as-of timestamp surfaced in the result.

### billing, entitlements & licensing

This unit is a well-factored, well-tested *entitlement calculator* bolted onto a Stripe integration that has never worked end-to-end. The pure layers are genuinely good: `mdx-entitlements.ts` is monotonic, fail-closed on unknown tier/feature, and covered by 45 passing tests (I ran them); `seat-licensing`, `credit-ledger` and `weekly-usage-limits` all implement governed mutations with admin role + reason-for-change + Part 11 audit; `atomicQuotaService` uses a correct `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` consumption pattern; the default-deny `authBoundary` with its narrow, documented `/api/billing/webhooks` exemption is real. But the money path underneath is broken in three independent, provable ways at once: annual subscriptions are priced at ONE MONTH's amount per year (a ~92% revenue loss on the UI's default billing cycle), the Stripe webhook can never verify a signature because `express.json()` consumes the body before the route's `raw()` parser (I reproduced this against express 5.2.1 and stripe 18.5.0), and even if it verified, the handlers read Stripe-v18-only fields under a pinned `2023-10-16` API version and would throw on every subscription event. The consequence is that `organizations.tier` is never written by billing at all — and its DB default is `'standard'`, the $499/mo paid tier, so every self-serve signup silently receives the paid Standard entitlement set for free while the Business Center reports $499/mo of "recognized revenue" for each of them. The dominant risk for a buyer is that this unit cannot take money correctly today and its own financial reporting (MRR, gross margin, loss-making clients) is systematically wrong in both directions — revenue inflated by unpaid default-standard orgs, cost understated because 62 of 64 LLM call sites never attribute usage to a tenant.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- Pure, correct, and genuinely tested entitlement math. server/services/entitlements/mdx-entitlements.ts:160-164 (isEntitled) is monotonic across tiers and returns false — never throws and never guesses — for an unknown feature OR an unknown tier; featureTier returns null rather than a fabricated tier. I ran the suites: 45 tests pass across mdx-entitlements, licensing/entitlements, report-os/entitlement-map and seat-licensing. The MDX strategy doc's claim that this module invents no tier is accurate.
- A real default-deny authentication boundary with a narrow, justified webhook exemption. server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:58-103 enumerates every public /api path with a written reason, and the Stripe carve-out at l.87 is prefix-matched precisely ('/api/billing/webhooks') so /api/billing/webhooksXYZ cannot ride on it (isPublicApiPath, l.111-117). /api/billing/dtc-pricing is exact-matched (l.98) rather than opening the whole pricing surface.
- organizationId is never accepted from request input on any financial mutation. server/routes/billing.ts:41-45 documents the rule and every handler sources the org from tenantContext/JWT (l.90-91, 137-138, 166-167, 213-214); l.210-212 carries an explicit note that a body-supplied organizationId 'would have let a caller start a DTC checkout that bills another tenant's payment method'. The DTC checkout schema likewise refuses an organizationId field entirely.
- atomicQuotaService.js implements quota consumption the correct way: SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the license row plus the INSERT inside one transaction (l.23-88 for projects, l.125-288 for users), so concurrent creates serialise. atomicAcceptInvitation (l.329-455) is better still — it moved seat consumption from invite time to accept time, re-checks the quota under the same lock, and refuses to add a cross-org user without their consent (l.196-255).
- Governed mutations with reason-for-change and Part 11 audit are implemented, not just claimed, on the newer surfaces: setSeatsPurchased (server/services/seat-licensing.ts:132-178) validates, refuses to reduce below consumed seats, and writes previousValue/newValue; setWeeklyLimit and setAutoReload do the same; the routes enforce requireRole('admin','owner') (billing-dashboard.ts:764, 800, 894) and reject a missing reason with a 400.
- credit-ledger.ts is a properly designed money-adjacent ledger: signed append-only entries with CHECK constraints, balance_after_cents materialised per row, a per-org advisory lock so concurrent writes cannot interleave balance computation, and overdraft rejection with INSUFFICIENT_CREDITS unless a platform admin explicitly allows negative (l.11-19, 178-250). Credit grants are requirePlatformAdmin-only with the target org explicitly separated from the caller's tenant (billing-dashboard.ts:931-953).
- GET /api/admin/business/metering-coverage (server/routes/admin/business-center.ts:262-328) is an unusually honest self-diagnostic: it deliberately surfaces the accuracy boundary of its own cost model, reporting features that bill usage with no explicit rate ('pricedAt: default') and configured rates with no usage. Very few codebases ship a control that publicly grades the trustworthiness of their own margin numbers.
- Tenant-scoping bugs in this unit have demonstrably been found and fixed rather than papered over: billing-dashboard.ts:638-642 documents replacing an unscoped `WHERE customer_id = $1` stripe_events query (against columns that did not exist) with a correct `WHERE organization_id = $1`; entitlements/resolver.ts:126-137 filters feature_toggles so one tenant never sees another's pilot flags. The RLS allowlist entry for stripe_events (server/db/rlsAllowlist.ts:23-27, 63) is documented with the exact reason (nullable organization_id) and the policy shape a fix would need.
- Webhook signature failures do not leak implementation detail: server/routes/billing.ts:288-294 logs the SDK message server-side and returns a bare 'Invalid signature', with a comment explaining that echoing it would fingerprint the Stripe library version for whoever is probing. Stripe webhooks are also correctly exempted from CSRF (server/middleware/csrf.ts:29) on the grounds that signature verification covers them.
- The redirect surfaces on checkout are constrained rather than free-form: redirectUrlSchema requires an absolute http(s) URL (server/routes/billing.ts:47-49) applied to successUrl/cancelUrl/returnUrl, with seats bounded to 1..10000 and currency shape-validated — the validation posture is right even where the currency allowlist is missing (BILL-06).

#### `P0` BILL-01 — Annual subscriptions are charged one month's price per year (~92% under-billing)

`server/services/billing.ts:352` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · billing-correctness

**Evidence.** createCheckoutSession: `let unitAmount = perUserAmount * seatCount;` (l.352) then `if (billingCycle === 'annual') { unitAmount = Math.round(unitAmount * (1 - tierPricing.annualDiscountPct / 100)); }` (l.355-357), then `stripe.prices.create({ unit_amount: unitAmount, ... recurring: { interval: billingCycle === 'annual' ? 'year' : 'month' } })` (l.379-385). There is no ×12 anywhere. Identical defect in createDTCCheckoutSession: `let unitAmount = dtcTier.baseMonthly;` (l.491) → `* (1 - annualDiscountPct/100)` (l.493) → `recurring: { interval: ... 'year' : 'month' }` (l.501). The UI defaults to annual: client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/LicensingSurface.tsx:92 `useState<'monthly'|'annual'>('annual')`.

**Failure scenario.** A pharma customer selects Professional, 25 seats, Annual (the pre-selected cycle) and clicks 'Choose Growth'. Server computes perUserAmount = 39900 × (1−0.20 bundle) = 31920¢; unitAmount = 31920 × 25 = 798,000¢ ($7,980); annual branch → round(798000 × 0.87) = 694,260¢. Stripe creates a price of $6,942.60 with interval='year'. The customer is billed $6,942.60 ONCE PER YEAR for a plan the same page advertises as '~$6,942/mo · 25 seats'. Intended annual revenue $83,311.20; actual $6,942.60 — a $76,368.60/yr loss per account. DTC Standard: $424.15/yr charged instead of $5,089.80/yr.

**Fix.** Multiply by 12 before applying the annual discount when interval='year' (unitAmount = round(monthlyTotal * 12 * (1 - annualDiscountPct/100))), and add a unit test asserting the Stripe price amount for each (tier, cycle) pair against an expected table.

#### `P0` BILL-02 — Stripe webhook signature verification is structurally impossible — the body is already parsed by the time raw() runs

`server/routes/billing.ts:269` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · correctness

**Evidence.** `router.post('/webhooks/stripe', raw({ type: 'application/json' }), ...)` (l.269) then `event = stripe.webhooks.constructEvent(req.body, sig, webhookSecret)` (l.286). But server/startup/middleware.ts:74 mounts `app.use('/api', express.json({ limit: '2mb' }))` in applyCoreMiddleware, and the billing router is mounted much later at server/bootstrap/register-inline-routes.ts:159 (`{ path: '/api/billing', mod: '../routes/billing.js' }`). Firecrawl webhooks are explicitly mounted BEFORE the parser (middleware.ts:70) — billing is not. I reproduced the exact mount order against the repo's express 5.2.1: the handler receives `{isBuffer:false, type:'object', ctor:'Object'}`. Feeding that object to the repo's stripe@18.5.0 constructEvent throws: 'Webhook payload must be provided as a string or a Buffer … Payload was provided as a parsed JavaScript object instead.'

**Failure scenario.** Stripe POSTs customer.subscription.created for a customer who just paid $1,499/mo. express.json() has already read the stream, so body-parser's raw() short-circuits and req.body is a plain object. constructEvent throws, the route returns 400 'Invalid signature' (l.294), and Stripe retries for 3 days and gives up. organizations.tier, payment_status, next_billing_date, seats_purchased and module_subscriptions are NEVER updated for any customer, ever. The paying customer's /api/billing/status keeps returning payment_status 'incomplete'.

**Fix.** Mount a raw-body carve-out for '/api/billing/webhooks' in applyCoreMiddleware before the express.json() line (mirroring the firecrawl pattern at middleware.ts:70), or use express.json({verify: (req,_res,buf)=>{req.rawBody=buf}}) globally and pass req.rawBody to constructEvent.

#### `P0` BILL-03 — Every new organization defaults to the paid 'standard' tier — the free tier is unreachable and unentitled tenants get paid capabilities

`migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:4197` · blocks **G2** · ~days · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** `"tier" text DEFAULT 'standard' NOT NULL` on organizations (also shared/schema.ts: `tier: text('tier').default('standard').notNull()`). server/routes/auth.ts:857 signup inserts `.values({ name: companyName, slug, industryMode, stripeCustomerId })` — no tier, no payment_status. Nothing else sets tier: the only writers are handleSubscriptionUpdated (dead, see BILL-02), handleSubscriptionDeleted, and createDTCCheckoutSession's free branch (billing.ts:463-469) — which is unreachable because dtcCheckoutBodySchema's enum is `['standard','professional','enterprise']` (server/routes/billing.ts:64), so tier='free' can never be requested.

**Failure scenario.** An attacker (or any curious visitor) POSTs /api/auth/signup with a throwaway email. The org row is created with tier='standard', payment_status='incomplete', max_users=5, max_projects=10. They then call GET /api/billing/capabilities and receive report_families, device_assembly_readiness, global_market_planner and ana_advisory as enabled='tier'; POST /api/deep-research/jobs passes requireTier('standard') (deep-research.ts:39) and checkQuota grants 50 deep_research credits; csr_builder gets 10; the public API grants 50 api_csr_search + 25 each of four other metered endpoints. All of it is the $499/mo Startup Biotech plan, delivered for $0 with no card on file.

**Fix.** Change the column default to 'free' (with a data migration that only touches orgs with no stripe_subscription_id), set tier explicitly at signup, and add a boot assertion that no org has tier != 'free' while payment_status is 'incomplete' and stripe_subscription_id IS NULL.

#### `P1` BILL-04 — Webhook handlers read Stripe API v18 fields while the client is pinned to apiVersion 2023-10-16 — subscription handlers throw, invoice handlers silently no-op

`server/services/billing.ts:694` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · correctness

**Evidence.** `function getSubscriptionPeriodEnd(subscription) { return subscription.items.data[0].current_period_end; }` (l.694-696) with the comment 'Stripe API v18 moved current_period_end … onto each SubscriptionItem'. But every Stripe client in this unit is constructed as `new Stripe(key, { apiVersion: '2023-10-16' as any })` (billing.ts:25, routes/billing.ts:285, billing-dashboard.ts:72, stripe-revenue.ts:37) — under 2023-10-16 the API returns current_period_end on the Subscription, not on items. Same defect in `getInvoiceSubscriptionId` (l.702-706), which reads `invoice.parent?.subscription_details?.subscription` — `Invoice.parent` does not exist in the 2023-10-16 response shape.

**Failure scenario.** A verified customer.subscription.updated arrives (after BILL-02 is fixed). getSubscriptionPeriodEnd returns undefined → `new Date(undefined * 1000)` is Invalid Date → node-postgres serializes it as '0NaN-NaN-NaNTNaN:NaN:NaN.NaN+NaN:NaN' (I verified this with the repo's pg utils) → Postgres rejects it with 'invalid input syntax for type timestamp' → handleSubscriptionUpdated (l.734-758) throws before ever reaching provisionModulesForTier(l.761). Separately, invoice.payment_failed hits getInvoiceSubscriptionId, gets null, and returns at l.803 without touching payment_status — so a customer whose card declines is never marked past_due.

**Fix.** Either pin apiVersion to the version whose shapes the code reads (2025-04-30+) or read the 2023-10-16 fields (`subscription.current_period_end`, `invoice.subscription`). Add a defensive guard that rejects a non-finite period end rather than writing Invalid Date.

#### `P1` BILL-05 — A webhook the app fails to process returns HTTP 200, so Stripe never retries — and the promised replay mechanism does not exist

`server/routes/billing.ts:305` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · reliability

**Evidence.** Catch block: '// Return 200 to prevent Stripe retries for processing errors — the error is logged and the event was already stored in stripe_events for replay.' then `res.json({ received: true, error: 'Processing error logged' })` (l.300-309). I grepped the whole repo for any consumer of `stripe_events WHERE processed = false` (the migration even creates idx_stripe_events_unprocessed for it, migrations/0003_billing_stripe.sql:27) — there is no worker, script, cron or admin endpoint that reprocesses them. There is also zero test coverage of processWebhookEvent anywhere in server/ or tests/.

**Failure scenario.** handleSubscriptionUpdated throws on a transient DB deadlock while processing customer.subscription.deleted. processWebhookEvent writes the error to stripe_events and rethrows; the route logs it and answers 200. Stripe marks the event delivered and never retries. The org keeps tier='professional' and every provisioned module forever, with no subscription and no invoice. Nothing in the product ever notices, because the only artifact is a row in stripe_events that nothing reads.

**Fix.** Return 500 for processing failures so Stripe's built-in retry applies (the stripe_events UNIQUE(event_id) constraint already makes reprocessing safe), OR build the replay worker the comment promises and alert on processed=false rows older than N minutes.

#### `P1` BILL-06 — Client-controlled `currency` relabels the same integer amount, letting a tenant subscribe to a $499 plan for ~$2

`server/routes/billing.ts:66` · blocks **G2** · ~days · billing-correctness

**Evidence.** dtcCheckoutBodySchema accepts any three ASCII letters: `currency: z.string().regex(/^[A-Za-z]{3}$/, 'currency must be a 3-letter ISO code').optional()` (l.66-69). It is passed straight through: createDTCCheckoutSession does `unitAmount = dtcTier.baseMonthly` (billing.ts:491) and `stripe.prices.create({ unit_amount: unitAmount, currency: currency || 'usd', ... })` (billing.ts:497-500). There is no allowlist and no FX conversion — only the currency label changes. The interface comment itself assumes parity: `currency?: string; // usd, eur, jpy, cny` (billing.ts:447).

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated tenant POSTs /api/billing/dtc-checkout {"tier":"professional","billingCycle":"monthly","currency":"idr"}. Stripe creates a recurring price of unit_amount 149900 in IDR — a zero-decimal currency — i.e. Rp 149,900 ≈ US$9.20/month instead of $1,499/month. The subscription's metadata still says tier='professional', so the (once fixed) webhook provisions the full Growth plan. Even the 'intended' currencies are wrong: currency='jpy' yields ¥49,900 ≈ $320 for the $499 Standard plan.

**Fix.** Reject any currency not on an explicit allowlist and hold a real per-currency price table (or Stripe multi-currency Prices with published amounts); never reuse a USD-cent integer as an amount in another currency, and handle zero-decimal currencies explicitly.

#### `P1` BILL-07 — Requesting tier='enterprise' on /api/billing/checkout mints a $0/month Enterprise subscription

`server/services/billing.ts:188` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** Every archetype's Enterprise tier carries `perUserMonthly: 0` (pharma l.188, medtech l.233, academic l.273 — '// Custom pricing'). createCheckoutSession looks the tier up unconditionally (`const tierPricing = archetypePricing.find(p => p.tier === tier)`, l.335) and computes `unitAmount = perUserAmount * seatCount` = 0 (l.342-352), then creates the price and a subscription-mode Checkout Session with `subscription_data.metadata.tier = 'enterprise'` (l.406-418). The route schema explicitly permits it: `tier: z.enum(['standard','professional','enterprise'])` (server/routes/billing.ts:52). The DTC path DOES guard this — `if (dtcTier.baseMonthly === 0 && tier !== 'free') throw new Error('Enterprise tier requires custom pricing')` (l.460) — the B2B path has no equivalent.

**Failure scenario.** Any authenticated user POSTs /api/billing/checkout {"tier":"enterprise","billingCycle":"monthly","seats":1}. Stripe returns a Checkout URL for a $0.00/month recurring price. On completion the subscription carries metadata tier='enterprise', maxProjects='-1', maxStorageGB='1000'. Once BILL-02/BILL-04 are fixed, handleSubscriptionUpdated sets organizations.tier='enterprise' and provisionModulesForTier enables every module including nda-bla, insight-synthesis and strategic-advisor — the top plan, billed $0 forever.

**Fix.** Mirror the DTC guard: reject any tier whose computed unitAmount is 0 in createCheckoutSession, and drop 'enterprise' from the self-serve checkout enum (route it to sales as the UI already does).

#### `P1` BILL-08 — Cancellation and downgrade never revoke module access — provisioning only ever grants

`server/services/billing.ts:849` · blocks **G2** · ~days · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** provisionModulesForTier only ever upserts enabled=true: `INSERT INTO module_subscriptions (...) VALUES ($1,$2,true,NOW()) ON CONFLICT (organization_id, module_id) DO UPDATE SET enabled = true, enabled_at = NOW()` (l.867-873) — the non-qualifying branch is a no-op. handleSubscriptionDeleted (l.766-781) sets payment_status='canceled' and tier='free' but touches no module rows. I grepped every writer of module_subscriptions across server/, migrations/ and db/: the only paths that can set enabled=false are the admin toggle (routes/module-subscriptions.ts:186) and master-admin (routes/admin/master-admin.ts:586). Meanwhile canAccessModule short-circuits on the stale grant: `if (license.enabledModules.includes(moduleId)) { return { allowed: true }; }` (server/services/license-manager.ts:172-175) — before any tier check.

**Failure scenario.** Org 42 subscribes to Professional; provisioning enables ectd-coauthor, cmc-wizard, protocol-designer, csr-author, evidence-engine, compliance-monitor. Three months later they cancel. handleSubscriptionDeleted sets tier='free'. Every module_subscriptions row stays enabled=true, so canAccessModule returns allowed:true for cmc-wizard on the free tier, requireModule('cmc-wizard') passes, GET /api/module-subscriptions/enabled still lists all six, and the client renders the full paid product. The org has cancelled and lost nothing.

**Fix.** On downgrade/cancel, disable module_subscriptions rows the new tier does not qualify for (in the same transaction as the tier write), and reorder canAccessModule to evaluate the tier gate before the enabledModules short-circuit.

#### `P1` BILL-09 — 62 of 64 LLM call sites never attribute usage to a tenant, so most AI spend is never metered and the usage dashboard reads near-zero

`server/services/usage-recorder.ts:70` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · usage-metering

**Evidence.** normalizeUsageEntry drops any entry with no org: `const orgId = Number(entry.organizationId); if (!Number.isFinite(orgId) || orgId <= 0) return null;` (l.70-72). The gateway guards identically before recording: `if (orgId == null || !Number.isFinite(orgId) || orgId <= 0) return;` (server/services/ai-gateway/gateway.ts:1991). recordApiUsageSafe has exactly ONE caller in the whole server tree (gateway.ts:1996). But organizationId is optional on the gateway request (ai-gateway/types.ts:300,488) and on the unified client's options (server/lib/unified-ai-client.ts:65). I counted 64 `ai.complete(` / `ai.chat(` call sites under server/ (excluding tests); grepping 12 lines of context after each, only 2 mention organizationId. Concrete example: server/services/safety-narrative-service.ts:321 calls `ai.chat([...], { taskType:'regulatory_review', jsonMode:true, temperature:0.2, maxTokens:8000, callerModule:'safety-narrative-service' })` — no organizationId.

**Failure scenario.** A tenant generates 200 ICH E3 safety narratives via /api/safety-narratives. Each is an 8,000-max-token Claude call. Every one is dropped by normalizeUsageEntry, so api_usage_logs gets zero rows. GET /api/billing/usage/summary returns totalCost 0 and totalTokens 0; the customer-facing Usage panel shows 0% used; the weekly cost_cents and tokens limits (weekly-usage-limits.ts:242, which sums api_usage_logs) never trip; and the Business Center reports 100% gross margin for that client. The tenant has consumed thousands of dollars of Claude tokens that the platform can neither see, cap, nor bill.

**Fix.** Make organizationId REQUIRED on GatewayRequest/AICompletionOptions (type-level), thread tenant context through the service layer, and add a CI check that fails any ai.chat/ai.complete call site lacking organizationId. Emit a metric for dropped (unattributable) usage entries so the gap is visible.

#### `P1` BILL-10 — Annual subscribers bypass every credit quota — the billing-period window starts in the future

`server/services/usage-metering.ts:210` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · quota-enforcement

**Evidence.** `function getBillingPeriodStart(nextBillingDate) { if (nextBillingDate) { const start = new Date(nextBillingDate); start.setMonth(start.getMonth() - 1); return start; } ... }` (l.210-219). checkQuota then counts usage with `WHERE ... created_at >= $3` using that value (l.145-150), and next_billing_date is written from the subscription period end (billing.ts:755) — one YEAR out for annual plans.

**Failure scenario.** An org on the annual Standard plan renews 2027-01-01, so organizations.next_billing_date = 2027-01-01. On 2026-07-28, getBillingPeriodStart returns 2026-12-01 — five months in the future. The usage_records query `created_at >= 2026-12-01` matches zero rows, so `used = 0` and `remaining = limit` on every single call, forever. The 50 deep_research and 10 csr_builder credits the annual customer paid for become unlimited, and every subsequent Claude-backed run is uncapped.

**Fix.** Derive the period start from the subscription's actual current_period_start (or billing_cycle-aware arithmetic: subtract 1 year for annual), never a blind minus-one-month from the next billing date.

#### `P1` BILL-11 — Any org member — not just admins — can start checkout and open the Stripe Customer Portal for the whole organization

`server/routes/billing.ts:135` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · authorization

**Evidence.** `router.post('/portal', authenticateToken, async (req, res) => {...})` (l.135) — authenticateToken only, no role guard; same for POST /checkout (l.88), POST /dtc-checkout (l.208), and POST /budgets in billing-dashboard.ts:367. createPortalSession simply resolves the org's stripe_customer_id and returns a portal URL (server/services/billing.ts:545-563). The same file's other financial mutations DO gate on role — billing-dashboard.ts:764 `router.put('/seats', authenticateToken, requireRole('admin','owner'), ...)`, l.800 weekly-limits, l.894 auto-reload — so this is an inconsistency inside one unit, not a design decision.

**Failure scenario.** A junior member of a 25-seat Enterprise org (role 'member') POSTs /api/billing/portal with an empty body, receives portalUrl, opens it, and lands in the org's Stripe Billing Portal — where, per default Stripe portal configuration, they can view every historical invoice and billing address, update or remove the payment method, switch the plan, and cancel the subscription. They can equally POST /api/billing/checkout {tier:'enterprise'} or POST /api/billing/budgets to set the org's monthly budget to $0.

**Fix.** Apply requireRole('admin','owner') to /checkout, /dtc-checkout, /portal and POST /budgets, matching the existing guard on /seats, /weekly-limits and /credits/auto-reload.

#### `P1` BILL-12 — The `licenses` table has no DDL anywhere in the repo, yet quota enforcement depends on it — one project path fails closed, another fails open

`server/services/atomicQuotaService.js:26` · blocks **G1** · ~days · quota-enforcement

**Evidence.** atomicCreateProject opens with `SELECT * FROM licenses WHERE organization_id = $1 AND status = 'active' FOR UPDATE` and returns `{success:false, error:'NO_LICENSE'}` when the result is empty (l.26-40); atomicCreateUser (l.129) and atomicAcceptInvitation (l.372) do the same, atomicCreateSubmission joins it (l.473). quotaEnforcementService.js:21 and server/routes/license-routes.js query it too. `grep -rn -i 'licenses' --include=*.sql .` across migrations/, db/migrations/, init-sql/, sql/ and scripts/ returns exactly one hit — an unrelated `regulatory_licenses` json column — so no migration creates `licenses` or `license_users`. Rows can only ever be produced by the admin route at server/routes/license-routes.js:384. Billing never writes it: billing.ts only updates `organizations` and `module_subscriptions`. The parallel path fails the other way: server/routes/project-hierarchy.ts:230-251 runs the same license lookup and, on `rows.length === 0`, skips the quota check entirely.

**Failure scenario.** A customer signs up, pays, and clicks 'New project'. POST /api/projects-management/projects reaches atomicCreateProject, the `licenses` relation does not exist (or has no row for the org), and the route returns 400 {error:'NO_LICENSE'} (server/routes/projects-management.ts:255-263) — the core journey is unreachable. Conversely, the same customer POSTs a child project via /api/project-hierarchy: the license lookup returns zero rows, the quota block at l.238 is skipped, and they can create unlimited projects, defeating the max_projects the plan sold them.

**Fix.** Decide on one quota source of truth. Either add the `licenses`/`license_users` migration and have the Stripe webhook maintain those rows, or (simpler) delete the `licenses` dependency and enforce against organizations.max_projects/max_users, which is what billing actually writes. Make project-hierarchy.ts share the enforcement helper rather than duplicating it with inverted failure semantics.

#### `P2` BILL-13 — checkQuota and recordUsage are not atomic — concurrent requests overshoot the credit limit

`server/services/usage-metering.ts:145` · blocks **G2** · ~days · quota-enforcement

**Evidence.** checkQuota reads `SELECT COALESCE(SUM(credits_used),0) ... FROM usage_records WHERE organization_id=$1 AND feature_id=$2 AND created_at >= $3` and returns `allowed: remaining > 0` (l.145-159) with no lock and no transaction; recordUsage is a separate unguarded INSERT (l.97-101). Every caller uses the read-then-write shape: deep-research-orchestrator.ts:77 `const quota = await checkQuota(...)` … l.101 `await recordUsage(...)`; csr-builder.ts:162/170 in reserveCSRBuilderQuota; public-api.ts requireQuota (l.137) then per-handler recordUsage (l.343 etc.). Note the contrast with the correct pattern in the same unit — atomicQuotaService.js uses SELECT … FOR UPDATE inside a transaction.

**Failure scenario.** A Standard org has consumed 49 of 50 deep_research credits. A script fires 10 concurrent POST /api/deep-research/jobs. All 10 execute checkQuota before any recordUsage commits, all read used=49, all see remaining=1 and allowed=true, and all 10 proceed. 10 comprehensive jobs × 3 credits are recorded = 79 credits against a 50 limit, and 10 real multi-connector Claude research runs are executed and paid for by the vendor.

**Fix.** Collapse check+record into one statement that inserts conditionally on the aggregate (INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (SELECT COALESCE(SUM(credits_used),0) …) + $credits <= $limit RETURNING id) or take a per-(org,feature) advisory lock, as credit-ledger.ts already does for balances.

#### `P2` BILL-14 — Business Center P&L is wrong in both directions: revenue models $499/mo for every unpaid default-standard org, cost ignores actual AI spend

`server/routes/admin/business/cost-model.ts:94` · blocks **G2** · ~days · financial-reporting

**Evidence.** buildCostAccounting reads `SELECT id, name, slug, COALESCE(tier,'standard') AS tier, status, COALESCE(seats_purchased,0) AS seats FROM organizations` for ALL orgs (l.93-97) and computes `modeledRevenueCents = price.monthly + price.perSeat * seats` with DEFAULT_TIER_PRICES.standard = 49900 (l.35, l.133); Stripe actuals are only used when the org has a stripe_customer_id AND paid invoices (l.136-138). Cost comes solely from `usage_records.credits_used × unit rate` (l.98-103, l.140-145) at 5–25¢/credit (DEFAULT_COST_RATES l.23-26) — it never reads api_usage_logs.cost_cents, the table that holds real LLM spend. These rows drive /pnl and /executive-summary ('mrrCents', 'grossMarginPct', 'lossMakingClients' — business-center.ts:237-255).

**Failure scenario.** 200 people sign up for the free product. Because of BILL-03 every org row is tier='standard' with no Stripe customer, so buildCostAccounting models $499 × 200 = $99,800 of revenue and reports portfolio.mrrCents = 9,980,000. Meanwhile the tenants' Claude spend runs through safety-narrative/cerGenerator/ai.chat and never lands in usage_records, so costCents = 0 and the executive summary reports 100% gross margin and zero loss-making clients. A buyer reading this dashboard sees ~$1.2M ARR at 100% margin against a real figure of $0 ARR and a real Claude bill.

**Fix.** Only model revenue for orgs with an active paid subscription (payment_status IN ('active','trialing') AND stripe_subscription_id IS NOT NULL); attribute cost from api_usage_logs.cost_cents (real spend) with usage_records credits as a secondary view; label modeled vs actual per row in the P&L, which the code already tracks via revenueSource.

#### `P2` BILL-15 — 'Hard limit' on the monthly budget is stored and displayed but never enforced anywhere

`server/routes/billing-dashboard.ts:400` · blocks **G1** · ~days · honest-states

**Evidence.** POST /budgets persists it — `INSERT INTO billing_budgets (organization_id, monthly_budget_cents, hard_limit_enabled, alert_thresholds, updated_at) …` (l.400-416) — and GET /budgets returns `hardLimitEnabled: row.hard_limit_enabled || false` (l.350). Grepping hard_limit_enabled / hardLimitEnabled across all of server/ and client/src returns only these read/write sites in billing-dashboard.ts (l.318, 338, 350, 374, 401, 406, 409, 413, 422) — there is no consumer. No middleware, no AI-gateway pre-check, no route guard reads it.

**Failure scenario.** A customer worried about runaway AI cost opens Usage & billing, sets monthlyBudget = $500 and toggles 'hard limit' on. The setting round-trips and displays as enabled. They then run a large batch job. Nothing consults billing_budgets on any request path, so spend continues past $500 with no block — the only effect of the toggle is a row in a table. The customer believes they have a spend cap and does not.

**Fix.** Either enforce it (check current-period spend against monthly_budget_cents in the AI-gateway/weekly-limit middleware and 402/429 when hard_limit_enabled and exceeded) or remove the control and its label until it is enforced.

#### `P2` BILL-16 — The credit ledger has no debit caller — the balance only ever goes up, and the UI's 'Buy more' / 'Manage' controls do nothing

`server/services/credit-ledger.ts:239` · blocks **G1** · ~days · honest-states

**Evidence.** `export async function debitCredits(...)` (l.239-250) has zero callers — grepping debitCredits across server/ (excluding tests) returns only its definition. The only write path reaching the ledger from HTTP is POST /credits/adjust, which is requirePlatformAdmin and restricted to entryType 'grant'|'adjustment' (billing-dashboard.ts:931-953). The module's own header is candid: 'purchase/auto_reload entries record the ledger movement only — charging the payment method (Stripe) is deliberately out of scope here' (l.26-29). The client renders it as a live product: `<button className="ub-buy">Buy more <span className="ub-buy-tag">Up to 30% off</span></button>` with no onClick (client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/UsageBilling.tsx:376-381) and `<button className="sp-ask">Manage</button>` for auto-reload with no onClick (l.406).

**Failure scenario.** A pilot user opens Usage & billing → Billing and sees 'Current balance $0.00', a 'Buy more — up to 30% off' button and an auto-reload rule. Clicking 'Buy more' does nothing (no handler); clicking 'Manage' does nothing. Even if a platform admin granted them $500 of credits, no product action would ever debit the balance, because nothing calls debitCredits. The entire credit economy is inert while presenting as functional.

**Fix.** Wire debitCredits into the metered paths (or the AI gateway's recordTenantUsage), wire 'Buy more' to a Stripe checkout for a credit top-up and 'Manage' to the existing PUT /credits/auto-reload, or replace both buttons with an honest 'coming soon' state.

#### `P2` BILL-17 — Plan changes made in the Stripe Customer Portal never change the tier — tier is read only from subscription metadata, never from the price

`server/services/billing.ts:721` · blocks **G2** · ~days · billing-correctness

**Evidence.** handleSubscriptionUpdated derives everything from metadata written once at checkout: `const tier = subscription.metadata?.tier || 'standard'; const seats = parseInt(subscription.metadata?.seats || '5', 10); … const maxProjects = parseInt(subscription.metadata?.maxProjects || '10', 10);` (l.725-729), and bails entirely when metadata is absent: `const organizationId = subscription.metadata?.organizationId; if (!organizationId) return;` (l.722-723). Nothing reads subscription.items.data[0].price to re-derive the plan. createPortalSession (l.545-563) applies no feature restrictions to the portal.

**Failure scenario.** A Professional customer opens the Customer Portal (which any member can do, BILL-11) and switches to the cheaper Standard price. Stripe fires customer.subscription.updated; the subscription's metadata still says tier='professional', seats='25', maxProjects='100' because Stripe does not rewrite metadata on a plan change. The handler re-writes exactly those old values. The customer now pays Standard and keeps Professional entitlements indefinitely. A subscription created directly in the Stripe Dashboard (no metadata at all) is silently ignored — the org is never provisioned.

**Fix.** Resolve the tier from the subscription's actual price/product (via a price-id → tier lookup table or product metadata) and use subscription metadata only as a fallback; reconcile the org row from the price on every subscription event.

#### `P2` BILL-18 — Invoiced-revenue loader sums amount_paid across currencies as if all were USD and ignores refunds and credit notes

`server/services/billing/stripe-revenue.ts:75` · blocks **G2** · ~days · financial-reporting

**Evidence.** `const invoices = await stripe.invoices.list({ customer, created:{gte}, status:'paid', limit:100 }); const sumCents = invoices.data.reduce((sum, inv) => sum + (inv.amount_paid ?? 0), 0);` (l.75-84) — no inv.currency check, no subtraction of inv.post_payment_credit_notes_amount or refunds. The consumer reports the result as dollars: business-center.ts:69 `currency: 'usd'` on /cost-accounting, and the CSV export formats it as `fmt(r.revenueCents)` = cents/100 (business-center.ts:93-107). The module also documents that it deep-paginates no further than 100 invoices (l.48-52).

**Failure scenario.** A customer subscribed with currency='jpy' (reachable per BILL-06) pays a ¥49,900 invoice. amount_paid = 49900 (JPY is zero-decimal), so the loader records 49,900 'cents' and the Business Center reports $499.00 of revenue for a payment worth about $320. Separately, a $1,499 invoice that was fully refunded still has status 'paid' with amount_paid 1499_00, so refunded revenue is counted at full value in MRR and gross margin.

**Fix.** Group by inv.currency and either reject/convert non-USD at a recorded FX rate, and net out refunds/credit notes (inv.post_payment_credit_notes_amount, and charge-level refunds) before summing.

#### `P2` BILL-19 — Entitlement resolution fails OPEN to 'standard' — a DB error grants the entry paid tier's features

`server/services/entitlements/resolver.ts:105` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** resolveCapabilities initialises `let tier: Tier = 'standard';` (l.105) and on lookup failure only logs: 'tier lookup failed; defaulting to standard' (l.112-117). report-os/entitlement-map.ts repeats it: `let tier: Tier = 'standard'; try { … } catch { tier = 'standard'; }` (l.94-100), with a header comment claiming this is 'fail-closed on the paid tiers, never a silent unlock' (l.24-25) — but 'standard' IS a paid tier ($499/mo) and it unlocks report_families, device_assembly_readiness, global_market_planner and ana_advisory. Same pattern at report-os/canvas/suggestion-service.ts:186 and canvas/definition-service.ts:115 (`.catch(() => ({ tier: 'standard' as const }))`).

**Failure scenario.** The organizations table is briefly unavailable (connection-pool exhaustion, failover). A genuinely free-tier org calls POST /api/report-os/reports for a governed report family. resolveCapabilities throws, tier silently becomes 'standard', isEntitled('report_families','standard') returns true, and the report is generated and billed to the vendor's Claude account. Every free tenant hitting the platform during that window receives the Standard feature set.

**Fix.** Default to 'free' on resolution failure (fail closed), or surface a 503 rather than a fabricated tier; correct the comment that calls 'standard' a fail-closed default.

#### `P2` BILL-20 — license-manager's tier gates exist but are wired to exactly one route file, and unknown features default to allowed

`server/services/license-manager.ts:273` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** `export function isFeatureAvailable(feature, orgTier) { const requiredTier = FEATURE_TIER_MAP[feature]; if (!requiredTier) return true; // Unknown features default to available …}` (l.273-279) — fail-open by design, and requireFeature (l.298-317) is built on it. Grepping requireFeature/requireModule/requireTier from license-manager across all of server/ (excluding tests) returns exactly one consumer: server/routes/deep-research.ts:30 `router.use(loadLicense())`, l.39 `requireTier('standard')`, l.186 `requireTier('professional')`, l.239 `requireTier('standard')`. Nothing gates ctd_builder, biostatistics, document_builder, multi_agency_export, cmc_blueprint, api_access, sso or electronic_signatures — all of which FEATURE_TIER_MAP (l.221-268) declares as professional/enterprise features.

**Failure scenario.** A free/standard tenant calls POST /api/statistical-defensibility, /api/biostat-design-stats or the CTD builder routes directly. FEATURE_TIER_MAP says biostatistics, statistical_defensibility, ctd_builder and cmc_blueprint require 'professional', but no middleware on those routers consults it, so the request executes normally. A typo'd gate would also silently pass: requireFeature('biostatistcs') returns true for every tier because the key is not in FEATURE_TIER_MAP.

**Fix.** Invert the default (unknown feature ⇒ denied, with a boot-time assertion that every requireFeature key exists in FEATURE_TIER_MAP), and apply loadLicense()+requireTier/requireFeature to the routers that back each professional/enterprise feature key.

#### `P2` BILL-21 — Stripe-webhook module provisioning ignores the industry restriction the rest of the system enforces

`server/services/billing.ts:862` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · entitlement-enforcement

**Evidence.** provisionModulesForTier reads only the tiers key: `const requiredTiers: string[] = meta.tiers || []; const qualifies = requiredTiers.length === 0 || requiredTiers.some(t => orgLevel >= (tierLevel[t] ?? 99));` (l.860-864) — meta.industries is never consulted. Both other implementations do enforce it: the SQL function provision_org_modules checks `v_industry = ANY(... metadata->'industries')` (db/migrations/20260220_user_intelligence_platform.sql:145-149) and canAccessModule checks `allowedIndustries.includes(license.industryMode)` (license-manager.ts:205-210). The seed data restricts by industry, e.g. 510k-submission and ce-marking are `"industries": ["medtech"]`. Note also the empty-tiers branch grants any module lacking a tiers key to every tier including free.

**Failure scenario.** A pharma org upgrades to Professional. provisionModulesForTier enables 510k-submission, pma-submission, ce-marking and cer-generator — all medtech-only — writing enabled=true rows. From then on canAccessModule short-circuits at `license.enabledModules.includes(moduleId)` (l.172-175) and returns allowed:true regardless of industry, so the industry restriction is permanently defeated for that org and the medtech surfaces appear in a pharma tenant.

**Fix.** Have provisionModulesForTier call the same predicate as canAccessModule/provision_org_modules (or simply delegate to the SQL function), and treat an absent tiers key as 'no tier qualifies' rather than 'all tiers qualify'.

#### `P3` BILL-22 — GET /api/billing/activity returns the full raw Stripe event payload to any authenticated org member

`server/routes/billing-dashboard.ts:643` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · data-exposure

**Evidence.** `SELECT id, event_type, payload, created_at FROM stripe_events WHERE organization_id = $1 ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT $2` (l.643-649) and the response maps `data: r.payload` (l.656) — payload is the entire event as stored by processWebhookEvent (`JSON.stringify(event)`, server/services/billing.ts:645).
