The eyebrow at `:104` reads 'Submission · GET /api/submissions' and the page description at `:109` tells a regulatory user the workspaces are 'scaffolded from the submission contract' — implementation status as product copy, against §3.5.

**Failure scenario.** An RA lead on a frozen sequence clicks the primary rocket button 'Send via ESG gateway'. The row does not change, no confirmation appears, and the surface looks exactly as it did. She clicks it twice more before noticing the AnA composer in the other pane has accumulated three copies of a request she never sent. In the worst reading she believes the dispatch is queued and stops chasing it.

**Fix.** Label buttons for what they do at click time: 'Ask AnA to dispatch this sequence' with the AnA sparkles icon, not a rocket and the agency's name. Give every hand-off immediate feedback — focus the composer, or show 'Sent to AnA — review the request before it runs'. Remove route paths and the word 'scaffolded' from user-facing copy; put provenance in an expandable detail per §3.1 progressive disclosure, not in the page's identity line.

### Information architecture and navigation — client/src/concept2cure/v2 shell (Shell.tsx, registryModel.ts, routing.ts, surfaceViews.ts, V2App.tsx) and shared/constants/ui-surface-registry*.ts

Ground truth at HEAD, verified by executing the modules rather than reading them: `UI_SURFACES` resolves to **96** registered surfaces (44 base + 52 ui-v2), not ~101; `SURFACE_VIEWS` has 100 keys; `RAIL_PRIMARY` has 5 destinations; `NAV_HIDDEN` has 42 literal entries that dedupe to **40** (`tasks` and `vault` are each listed twice, and `review-approve` is not a registered surface).

The 5-item rail is a truncation, not an IA — and provably so from the spec it cites. Commit 576ec5dd5 (yesterday) collapsed a 13-entry, four-section rail to 5 to satisfy `ana-ui-design-constitution` §4. But §4 is one clause of a *ChatGPT-parity* spec whose §7 defines four sidebar zones: A (New Chat, Search), B (the five), C (recency — pinned/recent projects, recent chats, active project subtree), D (footer). The implementation shipped **B and D only**. Zone A does not exist; Zone C does not exist, and Shell.tsx:183-184 asserts in a code comment that recency "is rendered elsewhere in this component" when it is not. ChatGPT's five-item sidebar is survivable because ~90% of that sidebar is Zone C. Strip Zone C and you have five items and nothing else. Simultaneously the rail still renders the 6-item `CLIENT_CATEGORIES` block *above* the five — exactly the "category pileup / role-specific alternate nav tree" §7 forbids. The change implemented the number without the system, and kept the thing the same spec prohibits.

Can a new user form a correct mental model? No, for a compounding reason: the product's most complete capability map (the `SEGMENT_MODULES` grid, 75 surfaces across 6 labelled groups) lives on `home`, and `home` is not a registered surface, is not in the rail, and is not findable in ⌘K. The only click path to it is a client-category chip that also mutates global segment state. The brand logo — the most prominent rail affordance — navigates to `document-authoring`, which the same file lists in `NAV_HIDDEN` and whose test asserts is "not a shell sibling." So the one thing that acts like a sixth top-level destination is a surface the codebase formally forbids at top level.

The command palette cannot carry ~85 surfaces. It is a case-insensitive substring match over `label` + `notes` only, hard-capped at 8 (Shell.tsx:982-984). Executed against the real registry: "510k" returns 1 result and it is **not** the 510(k) workbench; "capa" returns 5 results, none about CAPA (it substring-matches "capability"); "sign" returns 18 truncated to 8, and the e-signature surface is not among them; "clinical study report", "adverse", "labelling", "deficiency", "complete response", "information request" and "translation" return **zero** — the last despite `/api/translation` being mounted and a `client/src/concept2cure/translation/` tree existing. There is no fuzzy match, no synonym/acronym map, no recency, and no project/document/program search at all, which is what a regulatory professional actually types.

Two findings are worse than IA problems. First, the persistent AnA rail derives its "Live" provenance pill from `connected()`, which returns `Boolean(getAuthToken())` — *authenticated*, not *live* — and stamps it onto `ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG`, which registryModel.ts:712 labels FIXTURE and registryModel.ts:15-18 says must never render as live data. Every signed-in user on every surface sees "Program BX-204 · oncology BLA · Readiness 72% · ORR contradiction detected 1h ago" under a pill reading **Live** with the tooltip "Live data from the concept2cure-v2 backend". Second, "Apps" — one of only five destinations — routes by entitlement `module_id`, and 17 of the 19 seeded module ids are not surface ids, so clicking Open lands the user on an engineering install-tracker page titled **"Home"**.

The codebase has real strengths and the fix is not a rewrite: the registry already carries the taxonomy for a correct IA (14 `group` values, 4 `navTier` values), every surface already has a canonical URL, and the project-workspace module grid is the best discovery affordance in the product. The IA needs 7 top-level destinations derived from `group`, plus the Zone A and Zone C the spec already mandates.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- The surface registry is genuinely excellent and is the asset that makes a real IA cheap. shared/constants/ui-surface-registry.ts types every one of 96 surfaces with layoutMode, real mounted apiPrefixes, sharedContract, discoveryCatalog, readiness tier, compliance rails, icon, navTier and a free-form `group`. Executed at HEAD the `group` axis already yields a coherent 14-bucket domain taxonomy — submission:16, admin:14, authoring:12, workspace:11, device:8, lifecycle:8, clinical:6, evidence:5, intelligence:5, safety-pv:3, review:2, quality-cmc:2, collaboration:2, explore:2. The information architecture this product needs is already in the data; the shell simply never reads it. Recommendation below is a re-render of existing fields, not new modelling.
- Every surface has a canonical, stable, deep-linkable URL. routing.ts:11-22 is 12 lines: `/concept2cure/:surfaceId` with DEEP_LINK_ALIASES for historical ids, and V2App.tsx:121 derives nav state from the URL rather than component state. This means the IA can be restructured entirely in registryModel.ts and Shell.tsx without touching a single surface, and it means every finding below is fixable without re-plumbing. For a regulated product, per-surface URLs are also what make an audit trail entry ('user opened X at T') linkable.
- Honest-state discipline is real and unusually consistent. Projects.tsx:151-176 surfaces the server's actual create error instead of a fake success toast; Projects.tsx:260-266 renders an explicit 'Team assignment isn't available yet' EmptyState rather than a fabricated roster; ProjectHome.tsx:645-652 and :687-690 name the specific backend gap (UUID-vs-numeric key mismatch) instead of inventing rows; AdminSurfaces.tsx:1278-1291 reverts an optimistic module toggle and reports the real reason when the write fails. This is the discipline a Part 11 product needs.
- Shell.tsx:436-447 is the best comment in the codebase. It documents that a previous author had labelled a `GET /api/coauthor` fetch a 'HARD RULE' binding AnA to 'the real co-author endpoint', discovered the endpoint does not exist (server/routes/coauthor.ts mounts only /sessions, /documents, /documents/:id, /templates), that the request 404'd on every render forever, and deleted the fetch rather than keep a fake one. Deleting a load-bearing lie and explaining why is exactly right — the residual defect (F-01) is only that the replacement provenance signal is the wrong boolean.
- The first-run onboarding model is thoughtfully built and correctly sourced. V2App.tsx:126-137 explicitly refuses to derive the welcome from `prefs.segment` because that is a browser-local toggle defaulting to 'biotech', and instead reads the tenant's real organizations.client_type — with a written rationale that a device/diagnostics/CRO client would otherwise be greeted with biotech prompts and a tenant-switcher would inherit the previous tenant's segment. onboardingWelcome.ts:1-18 is equally careful about the scripted-copy / live-response boundary. The content is right; only the default prefs hide it (F-10).
- ProjectHome.tsx:620-642 (AuthorWorkspace) is the single best discovery affordance in the product and should be the model for the fix. It renders the full segment capability grid scoped to the open project, grouped under real regulatory labels ('Author & assemble', 'Evidence & data', 'Applications & filing', 'Review & govern', 'Science & intelligence', 'Lifecycle & access'), with each card carrying the registry `notes` first sentence as a subtitle and full notes as a tooltip. It defaults open (stage state initialises to 'author' at :833), so it is visible without a click. This is what 'where is everything' should look like.
- Governed-action modelling is honest about what is and is not real. AI_ACTIONS (registryModel.ts:311-368) carries an explicit `governed` flag per action; Shell.tsx:628-632 renders a lock affordance on governed chips; Shell.tsx:363-388 routes pending sign-offs to the real GovernedActionSignoff → POST /api/ana-ri/governed-action and renders the server's confirmation rather than a fabricated audit hash; registryModel.ts:428-433 documents why the six read-only graph tools are deliberately NOT e-sign gated ('adding an e-signature gate to reading evidence would train users to click through signoffs that carry no meaning'). That reasoning is correct and rare.
- The registryModel ↔ registry parity tests (client/src/concept2cure/v2/__tests__/registryModel.test.ts:68-107) do real work: every NAV_GROUP_OF id, every NAV_HIDDEN id, every SEGMENT_MODULES item, every SURFACE_ACTIONS key, every DEEP_LINK_ALIASES target and every segment defaultSurface must resolve to a registered surface. This is why there are no dangling grid tiles anywhere in the product. The gap is only that the suite tests id-resolution, never reachability (see F-05).

#### `P0` F-01 — AnA rail stamps a "Live" provenance pill on hard-coded fixture regulatory status (readiness %, contradiction alerts) for every signed-in user

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:449` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** Shell.tsx:449 `const anaSample = !connected();` and dataConnect.tsx:31-33 `export function connected(): boolean { return Boolean(getAuthToken()); }` — this is an AUTH check, not a data-provenance check. The shell is behind ProtectedZenApp, so every user who can see the rail has a token, so anaSample is always false. Shell.tsx:529 renders `<SampleTag sample={anaSample} />`, and dataConnect.tsx:147-158 makes sample=false render the string 'Live' with title='Live data from the concept2cure-v2 backend'. The values that pill labels come from getAnaContext (registryModel.ts:1151-1164), which unconditionally returns `program: co.program, stage: co.stage, readiness: co.readiness, evidence: co.evidence, activity: co.activity` from getCoauthor(segment) → ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG, declared at registryModel.ts:712 as `/** FIXTURE — per-segment co-author context */` and covered by the file header rule at registryModel.ts:15-18: 'Surfaces render them only behind the SampleTag pill ... never as live data (GAP RULE)'. Shell.tsx:532-587 renders those fixture values as Program, Stage, a Readiness progress bar with a numeric percentage, Linked evidence chips, and Recent activity rows with an alert icon. ANA_SURFACE_CTX can override here/focus/actions/suggestions but NOT program/stage/readiness/evidence/activity — those five are hard-wired from the fixture at :1156-1161.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory affairs director at a Class II device company signs in, opens the 510(k) workbench for CardioFlow CX, and expands the AnA rail. The rail shows: 'Working in 510(k) workbench [Live]', 'Program: BX-204 · oncology BLA', 'Stage: Draft', 'Readiness: 72%', 'Linked evidence: 3 studies · 1 CSR · 2 precedents', 'Recent activity: ⚠ ORR contradiction detected — 1h ago'. None of it relates to her device, her org, or any row in her database; all six values are literals in registryModel.ts:713-756. She hovers the pill and reads 'Live data from the concept2cure-v2 backend'. She either (a) reports a 72% dossier-readiness figure and an open ORR contradiction into a submission-readiness review, where it is contradicted by the actual dossier, or (b) discovers the fabrication during a diligence demo. Readiness percentages and contradiction alerts are decision inputs on a filing timeline; a false one labelled Live is a defect with regulatory consequence, and it also breaches the file's own written GAP RULE.

**Fix.** Stop deriving provenance from auth. Either (a) do not render program/stage/readiness/evidence/activity at all until a real endpoint supplies them — render the surface-scoped `here`/`focus`/`actions`, which ARE legitimately deterministic UI copy — or (b) keep the block but force `sample={true}` for any field sourced from ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG, since no endpoint feeds them. Longer term, split the pill: provenance must be a per-field property carried with the data (`{value, source: 'live'|'fixture'|'absent'}`), never a single boolean computed from session state. Add a test that asserts no ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG value can reach a `sample={false}` SampleTag.

#### `P0` F-02 — "Apps" — 1 of 5 top-level destinations — routes by entitlement module_id; 17 of 19 seeded modules dead-end on an engineering install page titled "Home"

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/AdminSurfaces.tsx:1462` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** AdminSurfaces.tsx:1220 `const open = (id: string) => onNav(id);` and :1462 `onClick={() => open(a.id)}`, where `a.id` is `m.moduleId` from GET /api/module-subscriptions/catalog (mapLiveCatalog at :1180). Those ids come from `available_modules.module_id` (server/services/license-manager.ts:112-142), seeded at db/migrations/20260220_user_intelligence_platform.sql:78-107 with 19 rows. Cross-checked against UI_SURFACES ∪ SURFACE_VIEWS keys, only `ectd-coauthor` and `vault` are real surface ids. The other 17 — ind-filing, 510k-submission, pma-submission, nda-bla, ce-marking, cmc-wizard, doc-canvas, protocol-designer, csr-author, cer-generator, evidence-engine, insight-synthesis, strategic-advisor, safety-signal, analytics-hub, compliance-monitor, team-workspace — resolve to nothing. V2App.tsx:166-184 then falls back: `getSurface('510k-submission')` is undefined, so `ctxSurface` becomes the hard-coded HOME object `{id:'home', label:'Home', notes:'Home opens with the segment pathway chips and the composer. Ports in the surface phase.'}`, and V2App.tsx:219 renders KitSurfaceScaffold. Surfaces.tsx:623-713 shows what that is: an install tracker with 'Mounted routes', 'Layout mode', 'UI kit', 'Contract', and a five-step 'Install path · kit → live (5 layers)' checklist. This is a third id vocabulary in the product — shared/regulatory/app-registry.ts uses a FOURTH (study_protocol_design, clinical_csr, substantial_equiv, human_factors) and its own header documents the same class of drift ('cmc vs cmc-wizard, study_protocol_design vs protocol-designer').

**Failure scenario.** An evaluating VP of Regulatory clicks 'Apps' — one of only five things the product offers — sees the 510(k) Navigator card described as 'Predicate device analysis, substantial equivalence, performance testing matrix, FDA eSTAR integration', and clicks Open. The URL becomes /concept2cure/510k-submission. The page header reads 'Biotech & Pharma · workspace / **Home** / Home opens with the segment pathway chips and the composer. Ports in the surface phase.' followed by a developer install checklist showing 'Mounted routes: /api/projects' and 'Install path · kit → live (5 layers)'. The real 510(k) workbench exists at /concept2cure/device-510k and is fully built. Sixteen more cards behave identically. The evaluator concludes the platform is a prototype.

**Fix.** Add a `surfaceId` column to available_modules (or a moduleId→surfaceId map in shared/) and route through it; refuse to render an Open button for a module with no resolvable surface. Independently, KitSurfaceScaffold must never be reachable in a production build — gate it behind import.meta.env.DEV and render an honest 'This module isn't available in your workspace' state otherwise. Also fix V2App.tsx:167-184: an unknown surface id must not silently inherit the identity of Home; it should produce an explicit not-found state carrying the requested id.

#### `P1` F-03 — The active destination in the 5-item rail has no selected state; the client-category filter does — the CSS matches aria-current="true" and the destinations emit aria-current="page"

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/styles/app-v2.css:869` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** app-v2.css:869-871 is the only selected treatment in the rail: `.nav-item[aria-current="true"]{background:var(--accent-000);color:var(--accent-200);font-weight:500;}` plus an accent left-bar at :871. Shell.tsx:124 emits `aria-current={activeId === target ? 'page' : undefined}` for all five RAIL_PRIMARY destinations. Shell.tsx:169 emits `aria-current={segment === c.id ? 'true' : undefined}` for the six CLIENT_CATEGORIES filter chips. There is no `[aria-current="page"]` rule anywhere in app-v2.css (grepped) and no `.nav-item[data-on]` rule either, although both element types also set `data-on` (Shell.tsx:123, :168). The only other candidate rule, app-v2.css:322 `.nav-item[data-focus]:not([aria-current])`, is dead — no rail item sets data-focus (the sole data-focus in the codebase is EctdCoauthor.tsx:317). Net effect: the selected-looking item in the rail is always a client-category chip, and never the page you are on.

**Failure scenario.** A CRO project manager clicks Projects, opens a sponsor program, then clicks Communication Center to check an FDA correspondence thread. At every step the rail's only highlighted row is 'Biotech' (a segment filter), and Projects / Communication Center look identical to the three destinations she is not on. With the rail collapsed — the shipped default (F-10) — the rail is eleven unlabelled 16px icons in a 56px strip with exactly one highlighted, and the highlighted one is the filter. She cannot tell where she is, and every wrong click costs a full surface load. The a11y story is also wrong in both directions: screen readers get aria-current="page" (correct) on items with no visual state, and aria-current="true" (semantically 'current item in a set') on what is functionally a toggle that should be aria-pressed.

**Fix.** One-line CSS fix for the symptom: change the three rules at app-v2.css:869-871 (and :887) to `.nav-item[aria-current]`, which then matches both 'page' and 'true'. The correct fix is to stop styling two different controls with one class: give the destinations a distinct selected treatment keyed on aria-current="page", and re-express the category chips as a segmented control with aria-pressed — they are a filter, not navigation, and giving them the strongest visual weight in the rail is what makes the IA read as backwards.

#### `P1` F-04 — Home — the only complete capability map outside a project — has no click path back; the brand logo instead navigates to a surface NAV_HIDDEN declares forbidden at top level

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:141` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Surfaces.tsx:216-247 renders the segment module grid on Home ('Everything in your {category}' / 'All modules built for this client category'), covering 36 surfaces per segment and 75 unique across all six. Home is reached only via V2App.tsx:213 `activeId === 'home'`; there is no `id: 'home'` entry in either registry file (grepped both), so getSurface('home') is undefined and Home cannot appear in ⌘K, which searches UI_SURFACES only (Shell.tsx:982). RAIL_PRIMARY (registryModel.ts:116-122) contains no home entry. The only in-product path to Home is V2App.tsx:144-147 `selectSegment` — clicking a client-category chip, which also mutates global segment state as a side effect. Meanwhile Shell.tsx:138-150 wires the brand logo — top-left, always visible, the universally-learned 'go home' affordance — to `onClick={() => onNav('document-authoring')}` with `title="Document workspace"` and a data-on active state backed by app-v2.css:912. `document-authoring` is listed in NAV_HIDDEN at registryModel.ts:132, and registryModel.test.ts:50-55 asserts it 'is not a shell sibling — §4 forbids it at top level'.

**Failure scenario.** A new medical-device regulatory associate is shown the module grid on first load, spots 'Design controls (DHF)' and 'Human factors (62366)', then clicks Projects to look at the portfolio. He now wants to get back to that grid. The rail has five items, none of them Home. He clicks the Concept2Cure logo — the standard gesture — and is dropped into a full-screen document editor with hideAna, no document open, and no way back except the browser Back button. He tries ⌘K and types 'home': zero surface matches, only 'Ask AnA: "home"'. The capability grid is now unreachable except by clicking a client-category chip, which silently re-scopes his whole workspace. The logo has effectively become an undeclared sixth top-level destination pointing at the one surface the codebase formally forbids there.

**Fix.** Register `home` as a real surface (it already has a component, a URL and a scaffold fallback), make it RAIL_PRIMARY[0] or make the brand logo route to it, and remove `document-authoring` from the logo. If chat must remain the first destination per Law 1, put Home on the logo and Chats first in the list — that is exactly how the spec's Zone A (brand + New Chat + Search) is meant to work. Either way the module grid needs a stable, non-side-effecting entry point.

#### `P1` F-05 — The 5-item rail is Zone B of a four-zone spec shipped without Zones A and C; a code comment asserts Zone C exists when it does not, and the rail still carries the category block §7 forbids

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:183` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks

**Evidence.** Shell.tsx:178-184 comment: '§7 keeps this rail to Zone B alone; recency (Zone C) and the account footer (Zone D) are rendered elsewhere in this component.' Zone D is real (Shell.tsx:187-239, rail-foot). Zone C is not: the Rail render is rail-top (logo + collapse) → rail-scroll (CLIENT_CATEGORIES at :161-177, RAIL_PRIMARY at :185) → rail-foot. No pinned projects, no recent projects, no recent chats, no active-project subtree; grepping 'recent' in Shell.tsx returns only :576 (the AnA fixture activity list). Zone A (New Chat, Search) is also absent — there is no New Chat button in the rail, and search exists only as a top-bar button (:332-336). The source spec at /root/.claude/skills/ana-ui-design-constitution/SKILL.md:133-145 defines all four zones and adds the rule 'no duplicate workspace nav sections, no role-specific alternate nav trees, no category pileup' — yet the shipped rail renders a 'Client categories' header (Shell.tsx:161) over six segment chips, above the five destinations, with no header of their own. Commit 576ec5dd5 (2026-07-28) removed 13 rail entries across four labelled sections (RAIL_CORE, RAIL_SPECIALIST, RAIL_EXPLORE, RAIL_QUICK) to reach five, including `tasks`/'My Tasks' which carried a count badge.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory reviewer whose entire job is working a queue signs in on Monday. In ChatGPT the five-item sidebar works because the other 90% of it is Zone C — the twenty things you touched recently. Here Zone C does not exist, so her sidebar is: six client-type filter chips, then Chats / Projects / Communication Center / Apps / Settings. Nothing she worked on Friday is visible. 'My Tasks' — which was a rail entry with a count badge one commit ago — is now in NAV_HIDDEN and only findable by typing 'tasks' into ⌘K or scrolling a project's module grid. Expanded, the rail reads as one eleven-item list under a single 'Client categories' heading, because the five destinations have no section label of their own; a first-time user reasonably reads 'Projects' as another client category. The number 5 was implemented; the system that makes 5 workable was not.

**Fix.** Do not ship Zone B without Zone A and Zone C — they are load-bearing, not decoration. Add Zone A (New chat, Search) at the top of the rail, and Zone C beneath the destinations backed by real data: pinned projects and the last ~10 projects/chats from /api/c2c/projects and the conversation store. Give the five destinations their own section label. Move the six CLIENT_CATEGORIES chips out of the rail entirely — client type is a tenant attribute (organizations.client_type, already read at V2App.tsx:131-137), not a navigation control; it belongs in Settings and the top-bar domain switcher, of which one already exists. Correct or delete the false Zone C comment at Shell.tsx:183-184 either way.

#### `P1` F-06 — Two client-segment switchers write one state field from two disjoint vocabularies; three values silently render the wrong client type's entire workspace

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/registryModel.ts:83` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** Executed at HEAD: CLIENT_CATEGORIES (registryModel.ts:83-90, the rail chips) = [medtech, biotech, diagnostics, pharma, cro, health]. SEGMENTS (registryModel.ts:173-267, the top-bar domain menu) = [medtech, biotech, pharma, cro, academic, regulatory, medical_writing]. `diagnostics` and `health` exist only in the first; `academic`, `regulatory`, `medical_writing` only in the second. Both controls write the same `prefs.segment` — rail via V2App.tsx:144-147 (which also nav('home')), top bar via V2App.tsx:257-261 (which instead navigates to s.defaultSurface). Verified by execution: getSegment('diagnostics') → undefined, so Shell.tsx:266 `getSegment(segment) ?? SEGMENTS[0]` falls back to medtech and the top bar displays 'Medical Device & Diagnostics'. getSegment('academic') resolves, but getSegmentContext('academic') → SEGMENT_CONTEXT.biotech (registryModel.ts:1038-1039), getSegmentModules('academic') → SEGMENT_MODULES.biotech (:1040-1041), and getCoauthor('academic') → ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG.biotech (:952-953), program 'BX-204 · oncology BLA'. registryModel.test.ts:152-156 only checks that client categories have icons; nothing tests that a CLIENT_CATEGORIES id resolves in SEGMENTS, or that a SEGMENTS id resolves in SEGMENT_CONTEXT/SEGMENT_MODULES.

**Failure scenario.** An academic medical centre's IIT coordinator opens the top-bar domain switcher and selects 'Academic / IIT' — a first-class option showing pathways 'IIT · IRB'. The app navigates to protocol-dev. She returns to Home and the page reads 'Biotech / IND · BLA · MAA · J-NDA / BX-301 — BLA · 351(a)' with the heading 'Everything in your Biotech' over a 36-module biotech grid; the AnA rail says she is working on BX-204 oncology BLA. Nothing in the UI indicates her selection was not understood — no fallback notice, no warning. Separately, a diagnostics client clicks 'Diagnostics' in the rail: Home correctly renders the IVDR grid, but the top bar simultaneously reads 'Medical Device & Diagnostics' (SEGMENTS[0], medtech) and its check mark sits on the wrong row. Two controls, one state, two vocabularies, silent divergence — on a product where client type drives which regulatory pathway the workspace assumes.

**Fix.** Collapse to one segment vocabulary. Delete CLIENT_CATEGORIES and derive the top-bar menu and any segment-scoped lookup from a single SEGMENTS array whose ids are exactly the keys of SEGMENT_CONTEXT, SEGMENT_MODULES and ANA_COAUTHOR_BY_SEG. Add a test asserting that set equality in all four directions. Make the three `?? biotech` fallbacks at registryModel.ts:952-953, :1038-1039, :1040-1041 throw in dev and render an explicit 'no workspace configured for this client type' state in prod — a silent fallback to another company's regulatory pathway must never be the default.

#### `P1` F-07 — The New Project wizard reads a window global the shell never sets, so device, IVD, CRO and health clients get biotech defaults persisted into regulatory_programs

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/Projects.tsx:125` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Projects.tsx:125 `const uiSeg = (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__C2C_SEGMENT) || 'biotech';`. Grepping the whole client tree, `window.__C2C_SEGMENT` is WRITTEN in exactly one place: BiopharmaJourney.tsx:260, inside that surface's own biotech/pharma toggle. V2App.tsx never sets it — it holds the segment in `prefs.segment` and passes it down as the `segment` prop, which Projects.tsx:361 destructures and then never uses. So uiSeg is 'biotech' for every user who has not first visited the Program Journey surface and flipped its toggle. uiSeg then drives four things in the create path: SEG2REG (:40-44) → the RegistryPicker's initial filing-type tab; SEG_LABELS (:59-62) → the 'Tailored for {segLabel}' banner at :392; SEG2PRODUCT (:53-56) → `productType` in the POST body at :144; and programTypeFor(sel, uiSeg) (:94-109) whose fallback at :108 returns 'ind' for anything non-medtech/diagnostics. The body is POSTed to /api/c2c/projects at :152 and persisted to regulatory_programs.

**Failure scenario.** A medical-device client selects 'Medical Device & IVD' in the rail, lands on Home, and clicks 'Start a new Medical Device & IVD project'. The wizard opens with a highlighted banner reading 'Tailored for Biotech — Showing the filing types that fit your client type', and the template picker opens on the pharma_biotech tab; 510(k), De Novo, PMA and IVDR are behind a tab switch the banner has just told her is not for her. If she does not notice and picks a template whose id lacks a device keyword, programTypeFor falls through to 'ind' and productType is written as 'biologic'. The program is now stored in regulatory_programs as an IND for a biologic, and Projects.tsx:47-50 stamps its portfolio chip as 'Biotech'. ProjectHome.tsx:852-854 then maps ws='Biotech'→seg='biotech' and renders the biotech module grid inside her device project — so the 510(k) workbench, design controls and human factors are absent from her own project workspace.

**Fix.** Delete the window global. Projects.tsx already receives the correct value as the `segment` prop (SurfaceViewProps, surfaceViews.ts:104-109) — pass it into NewProjectWizard and use it. Add a test that no surface reads a segment from `window`. Then reconcile SEG2WS: it maps diagnostics→'MDX' and health→'Biotech', which is the root of F-11; workstream should be derived from the chosen filing template's pathway, not from a six-value UI toggle.

#### `P1` F-08 — The command palette is the declared discovery mechanism for ~85 surfaces but is substring-only over label+notes, capped at 8, with no synonyms, no acronyms, no recency, and no project/document search

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:982` · blocks **G1** · ~weeks

**Evidence.** Shell.tsx:982-984: `UI_SURFACES.filter(s => s.label.toLowerCase().includes(term) || (s.notes ?? '').toLowerCase().includes(term)).slice(0, 8)`. Executed against the real registry, this is what a regulatory professional gets: 'clinical study report' → 0 results; 'adverse' → 0; 'labelling' (the EMA spelling) → 0; 'deficiency' → 0; 'complete response' → 0; 'information request' → 0; 'translation' → 0 (despite /api/translation being mounted at server/bootstrap/register-inline-routes.ts:403 and a client/src/concept2cure/translation/ tree existing). False positives are as bad: 'capa' → 5 results (global-ri, program-journey, protocol-dev, quality, clinical-ops) none of which are about CAPA — it substring-matches 'capability'/'capabilities'. 'sign' → 18 hits (matching 'design', 'assign') truncated to 8, and esign-modal is not among the 8. '510k' as typed → 1 result, and it is filings-catalog, NOT device-510k (whose label is '510(k) workbench'). The empty state (Shell.tsx:958-970) offers `UI_SURFACES.slice(0, 8)` — registry declaration order — which resolves to Projects, Apps catalog, Artifacts Center, Project home, Document editor & authoring, Regulatory workspace, Vault (DMS), Review & approval: no recency, no personalisation, no rationale. The truncation is silent — no 'showing 8 of 18', no way to see more. Shell.tsx:954-956 documents the missing corpora ('filings, documents, conversations, pathways, people and templates'), so today nothing but surfaces and 8 AI actions is searchable. The results also offer non-destinations as destinations: auth-session, tenant-org, feature-flags, ana-rail and esign-modal are in UI_SURFACES with no SURFACE_VIEWS entry, so selecting 'E-signature modal' navigates to an install scaffold.

**Failure scenario.** A CMC lead needs the eCTD compiler on a Friday filing deadline. He hits ⌘K, types 'module 3' — 1 result (cmc). He types 'CTD' — 8 results truncated from more, ordered by registry declaration. He types 'compile' — the label match works, but he did not know the surface is called that. A device colleague types '510k' and gets a single result that is the taxonomy catalog, not the 510(k) workbench sitting one letter away. A safety lead types 'adverse' and gets nothing at all, then types 'CAPA' and gets five results about 'capabilities'. Because Shell.tsx:1010-1018 always appends an 'Ask AnA: "..."' row, the palette never says 'no results' (the cmdk-empty branch at :1112 is unreachable whenever a query is present) — so a zero-match query looks like a successful one. This is the mechanism registryModel.ts:124 and the test suite designate as the reachability guarantee for the ~85 demoted surfaces.

**Fix.** Palette work is cheap and high-leverage. (1) Index more than label+notes: add a `keywords: string[]` field to UiSurface and seed it with the vocabulary users actually type — 510k, eSTAR, predicate, IND, NDA, BLA, MAA, CSR, SAP, ICSR, PSUR, CAPA, deviation, PLLR, SmPC, labelling, deficiency, IR, CRL, Module 1-5, 2.7.x. (2) Replace `includes` with token-prefix + fuzzy matching so 'capa' cannot match 'capability' and '510k' matches '510(k)'. (3) Raise the cap and show 'n more' rather than silently truncating. (4) Rank by recency (last-visited, localStorage) and by the tenant's client type before registry order — and make the empty state show recents, not slice(0,8). (5) Index projects, programs and documents; a filing-name query is the single most common thing a user will type. (6) Exclude the five non-destination registry entries from nav results.

#### `P1` F-09 — 57 of 96 surfaces have no NAV_GROUP_OF entry, so the breadcrumb and every palette result label them "Biotech & Pharma" — including every device and IVD surface

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:264` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Shell.tsx:264 `const tier = NAV_TIERS_V2.find(t => t.id === (NAV_GROUP_OF[surface.id] ?? 'biopharma'));` — the breadcrumb's middle segment. The identical fallback appears twice more in the palette, at Shell.tsx:964 and :992, as the `hint` on every nav result, and once more on the install scaffold at Surfaces.tsx:628. NAV_GROUP_OF (registryModel.ts:33-81) has 44 keys against 96 registered surfaces, so 57 surfaces fall through to 'biopharma' → 'Biotech & Pharma'. The list of surfaces that default includes device-510k, device-cer, device-diagnostics and ivd-completeness — the entire device/IVD spine except device-workstream — plus regulatory-workspace, vault, review, submission-center, gateway-transmittals, protocol-dev, etmf, conversation-thread and admin-console. registryModel.test.ts:68-71 asserts every NAV_GROUP_OF key resolves to a surface, but nothing asserts the reverse, so the 57 gaps are invisible to CI.

**Failure scenario.** An IVD manufacturer's regulatory manager opens the IVD completeness workbench. The breadcrumb reads 'Concept2Cure.RI › Biotech & Pharma › IVD completeness'. She opens ⌘K, types 'ivd', and all four results are hinted 'Biotech & Pharma'. Her company does not do biotech or pharma; the product is telling her, in its only persistent orientation cue and in every search result, that she is in someone else's product. In a segmented multi-tenant tool where the vendor's pitch is client-type-aware workspaces, this is the exact opposite signal — and it is a lookup-table gap, not a design decision.

**Fix.** Either complete NAV_GROUP_OF for all 96 surfaces, or stop using it for orientation. The registry already carries a better field: `group` (14 real domain values — submission, device, authoring, clinical, safety-pv, quality-cmc, evidence, intelligence, lifecycle, review, workspace, collaboration, explore, admin). Render the breadcrumb and palette hint from `surface.group`, which is populated on every surface and is a more useful orientation cue than a client-type tier. Add a CI assertion that every UI_SURFACES id has whichever key the breadcrumb reads.

#### `P2` F-10 — Shipped defaults hide the entire first-run experience: the rail starts collapsed to unlabelled icons and the AnA welcome renders only inside a rail that starts closed

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:55` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** V2App.tsx:55-62 DEFAULT_PREFS: `railCollapsed: true, anaOpen: false`. With railCollapsed=true, app-v2.css:834 narrows the rail to --rail-collapsed (56px, :29), :872 sets `.nav-item .lbl{display:none}` so all eleven rail rows become bare 16px icons, :848 hides the wordmark, and :862 collapses the 'Client categories' header to a 1px transparent divider — so the only text left in the rail is gone and the sole grouping cue with it. With anaOpen=false, Shell.tsx:459-468 returns the 32px vertical `ana-seam` stub; the `welcome` prop passed from V2App.tsx:278 is only rendered in the open branch at Shell.tsx:491-519, so the client-type-aware onboarding built in onboardingWelcome.ts and correctly sourced from the tenant's real client_type (V2App.tsx:126-137) is never displayed. Nothing auto-opens either panel on first run — there is no first-run check anywhere in V2App.

**Failure scenario.** A newly provisioned diagnostics client signs in for the first time. She sees: a 56px strip of eleven unlabelled icons (six of which change a filter and five of which navigate, with no visual distinction — see F-03), a centred 'Good morning' composer, and a 32px vertical bar reading 'AnA' on the right edge. The onboarding the team actually built — 'Welcome, Sarah — let's get your diagnostics program set up' with the starters 'Set up an IVD performance study', 'Plan my regulatory pathway' and 'Upload a document to start' (which opens the real onboarding-ingest flow) — is behind that 32px bar and she has no reason to click it. She scrolls past the composer and finds the module grid, but the product has told her nothing about what it is or where to begin. The single best first-run asset in the codebase ships switched off.

**Fix.** Make first run explicit rather than inheriting a power-user default. When no `c2c-v2-prefs` key exists, ship railCollapsed=false and anaOpen=true so the labelled rail and the welcome are both visible; persist the user's own choice from then on. Alternatively render the welcome in the center canvas on Home when the conversation is empty, so it does not depend on rail state at all.

#### `P2` F-11 — The diagnostics/IVD module group is unreachable from any project workspace — every device project maps to the medtech grid, which omits both IVD surfaces

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/ProjectHome.tsx:852` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** ProjectHome.tsx:852-854 derives the grid segment from the portfolio workstream: `{ MDX:'medtech', Biotech:'biotech', Pharma:'pharma', CRO:'cro' }[sel.ws ?? ''] ?? 'biotech'` — four values, no 'diagnostics' and no 'health'. Projects.tsx:47-50 SEG2WS maps `diagnostics: 'MDX'` and `health: 'Biotech'`, so an IVD project is stored and chipped as MDX and comes back as medtech. Executed diff of the two grids: SEGMENT_MODULES.medtech and .diagnostics both have 36 items, and the only two present in diagnostics but absent from medtech are `device-diagnostics` and `ivd-completeness`. ProjectHome.tsx:625 renders getSegmentModules(seg), so those two surfaces can never appear inside an IVD project's workspace.

**Failure scenario.** An IVD company creates its RT-PCR assay project. Because SEG2WS collapsed diagnostics into MDX, the project card reads 'MDX' and the project workspace renders the medtech grid: 510(k) workbench, CER, design controls, human factors — but not the IVD completeness workbench and not the IVD performance surface, the two things built specifically for this customer. Both exist, are registered, and have working views (surfaceViews.ts:155, :177). The user can only reach them from Home while the rail segment happens to be set to 'diagnostics', or by typing 'ivd' into ⌘K. From inside her own project — the place the product tells her is her workspace — the IVD capability is invisible.

**Fix.** Stop deriving the capability grid from a four-value workstream label. regulatory_programs already stores productType (SEG2PRODUCT writes 'ivd' for diagnostics) and the chosen submissionTypeId; derive the grid segment from those. Add 'diagnostics' and 'health' to the ProjectHome map as an immediate stopgap, and add a test asserting every SEGMENT_MODULES key is reachable from at least one project workstream value.

#### `P2` F-12 — The command palette — the product's designated discovery mechanism — is not operable by screen reader or by keyboard past the fold

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:1061` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Shell.tsx:1061 declares `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` but the list beneath it has no listbox semantics: results are plain `<button className="cmdk-item">` (:1099-1109) with no role="option" and no aria-selected; the input (:1064-1072) has no role="combobox", no aria-expanded, no aria-controls and no aria-activedescendant. Arrow keys move a purely visual `sel` index (:1042-1047, styled by `.cmdk-item.on` at app-v2.css:1434), so a screen-reader user hears nothing as the selection moves and Enter fires an item they were never told about. There is no scrollIntoView anywhere in the component (grepped) while app-v2.css:1432 caps `.cmdk-list` at max-height:340px with overflow-y:auto — the default 8 results plus 2 headers plus 2 quick actions exceed that at ~38px per row, so arrowing to the last item moves the highlight below the fold with no scroll. There is no focus trap: Tab escapes the dialog to the page behind. The footer advertises '/ jump · > action' (:1118) but Shell.tsx:950 merely strips a leading '/' — it behaves identically to no prefix, so one of the two advertised modes does not exist. The cmdk-empty branch (:1112) is unreachable while a query is present because :1010-1018 always appends an 'Ask AnA' row, so a zero-match search never says so. Every affected surface declares 'accessibility-enforcement' in its registry `compliance` array.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory affairs specialist who uses a screen reader is told the palette is how she reaches the ~85 surfaces that are not in the five-item rail. She opens it, types 'submission', and hears only her own typed characters — the results are announced neither on render nor on arrow-key movement, because there is no live region and no aria-activedescendant. She presses Enter and the app navigates somewhere she was never told about. A sighted keyboard user arrows to the eighth result, watches the highlight disappear below a list that does not scroll, and presses Enter blind. On a product whose own registry declares an accessibility compliance rail per surface, the single mechanism that carries the majority of the IA is the least accessible component in it.

**Fix.** Standard combobox pattern, roughly 30 lines: input gets role="combobox" + aria-expanded + aria-controls + aria-activedescendant pointing at the selected row id; rows get role="option" + aria-selected + stable ids inside a role="listbox"; add a ref-based scrollIntoView({block:'nearest'}) on sel change; trap Tab within the dialog and restore focus to the trigger on close. Delete the '/ jump' footer hint or implement it. Make the empty state reachable by rendering 'No surfaces match "x"' above the Ask-AnA row rather than instead of it.

#### `P2` F-13 — Settings — 1 of only 5 top-level destinations — persists security and governance toggles to browser localStorage, including MFA required, SSO and the translation two-person rule

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/AdminSurfaces.tsx:134` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks

**Evidence.** AdminSurfaces.tsx:134-174: Setup seeds from a hard-coded default object including `orgName: 'Acme Bio'`, `mfaRequired: true`, `ssoEnabled: false`, `txwRequireBackTranslation: true`, `txwTwoPersonRule: true`, `txwBlockMachineApproval: true`, merges whatever is in `localStorage.getItem('c2c_admin_settings')`, and its setter writes straight back to localStorage at :171. Only client type is governed — it routes through useIndustryProfile().save to the org industry profile (:191, :212). To the team's credit the surface says so plainly in its own header sub-copy: 'Client type is governed (reads and writes the org industry profile); the remaining settings are saved in this browser only.' The defect is structural, not deceptive: this is one of five things the product offers at top level, and the majority of what it contains is per-browser and unaudited.

**Failure scenario.** A quality manager preparing for an inspection opens Settings, confirms 'MFA required: on', 'Require back-translation: on' and 'Two-person rule: on', and records the configuration in a controlled document. Those three values live in her Chrome profile's localStorage. Her colleague on the same tenant sees whatever his browser holds, or the hard-coded defaults; a cleared cache reverts hers to the shipped defaults with no audit entry and no notification. Under 21 CFR Part 11 a security control that appears set, is per-browser, and produces no audit record is worse than an absent one — it manufactures false assurance. The surface's honest label mitigates the deception but not the exposure, because a top-level destination named 'Settings' carries an implicit promise of tenant scope.

**Fix.** Either promote these to real org-scoped, audited settings behind the endpoints that already exist for client type, or remove the security and governance toggles from this surface entirely and leave only genuine per-browser preferences (theme, rail collapsed, AnA open). A Part 11 product must not render an MFA or two-person-rule switch that writes to localStorage, however clearly it is labelled.

#### `P3` F-14 — The nav model carries dead and self-contradicting entries: duplicated NAV_HIDDEN ids, a non-surface id, a dead rail-visibility gate, an unused entitlement fixture, and a tier function now used only by its own test

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/registryModel.ts:125` · ~days

**Evidence.** NAV_HIDDEN (registryModel.ts:125-171) has 42 literal entries that dedupe to 40 — `tasks` appears at :146 and :155, `vault` at :129 and :158 — and `review-approve` (:152) is not a registered surface, which registryModel.test.ts:73-75 has to special-case with a `continue`. Since the rail is now a fixed five-item array (Shell.tsx:185 maps RAIL_PRIMARY directly), NAV_HIDDEN no longer suppresses anything: its only consumer is surfacesByTier (registryModel.ts:1078-1087), which after commit 576ec5dd5 is imported by nothing but registryModel.test.ts:23 — dead production code retained by a test. Shell.tsx:114-115 `railVisible` gates `crl-library` behind a feature flag and is applied at :185 to RAIL_PRIMARY, which does not contain crl-library, so the gate can never fire. LICENSE_UNLICENSED (registryModel.ts:291-292) hard-codes labeling/risk/pdev/biopharma as unlicensed with a comment promising a rail lock + upgrade CTA; grepping the client tree, `isLicensed` has zero callers. Five registry entries — auth-session, tenant-org, feature-flags, ana-rail, esign-modal — are components rather than destinations, have no SURFACE_VIEWS entry, and are therefore offered as navigable results in ⌘K that land on the install scaffold.

**Failure scenario.** A developer asked to add a sixth destination reads registryModel.ts and finds four mutually inconsistent nav models layered on top of each other: RAIL_PRIMARY (live), NAV_HIDDEN (inert but 40 entries long and asserted by tests), NAV_GROUP_OF + surfacesByTier (dead except in tests), and SEGMENT_MODULES (the model that actually drives discovery). She reasonably concludes NAV_HIDDEN still suppresses rail entries — the comment at :124 says exactly that — and removes an id from it expecting the surface to appear in the rail. Nothing happens. Meanwhile the test suite passes throughout, because it asserts id-resolution, never behaviour: registryModel.test.ts:57-66 asserts ten demoted ids are 'still reachable via ⌘K/deep-link' by checking Set membership, and never checks that ⌘K can surface them (F-08 shows it often cannot). The test reads as a usability guarantee and is a compliance checkbox.

**Fix.** Delete surfacesByTier and its test, or restore it to production use. Dedupe NAV_HIDDEN and drop review-approve so the test's special case can go. Delete railVisible or apply it where crl-library actually renders. Delete LICENSE_UNLICENSED/isLicensed or wire them to /api/module-subscriptions, which already returns real entitlement state. Add an `isDestination: boolean` field to UiSurface, set it false on the five component-only entries, and filter ⌘K nav results by it. Then replace the Set-membership reachability test with a real one: for every non-destination-excluded surface, assert that at least one of {rail, segment grid, ⌘K search on its own label} returns it.

### Accessibility and visual coherence — client/src/concept2cure/v2/ surfaces + styles, design-system/

The repo contains genuinely excellent accessibility work — GovernedActionSignoff.tsx is a textbook WCAG 2.2 AA modal, icons.tsx centrally hides all ~200 glyphs, and components/ana/Composer.tsx has a correct sr-only live region backed by a real accessible-name regression test. None of that reached the shell. The 862 aria-* attributes are concentrated in a handful of hand-crafted components; the primitives that carry the most traffic were ported from a design kit with the semantics stripped. Three defects are purchase-blocking. (1) C2CForm.tsx — the governed data-entry drawer, referenced 113 times across 20+ surfaces — has zero programmatic label association (bare <label> with no htmlFor, control as sibling) and renders validation failures into a plain <div> with no role="alert", so a screen-reader user pressing Save on a 21 CFR §11 governed record gets total silence and cannot complete the action. (2) Shell.tsx:490 puts aria-live="polite" on the entire .ana-body container — the whole transcript plus the context card — so every streamed token re-announces the full accumulated reply and routine navigation triggers assistant chatter. (3) app-v2.css:61 sets outline:0 on every input/textarea/select while :60 restores focus-visible for buttons only, and the sole focus color (--accent-100 #d97757) measures 2.69–3.12:1 against shell surfaces, below the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 floor — including on the Part 11 e-signature field. On coherence: there is not one design system, there are at least five styling mechanisms and the same problem is solved repeatedly and inconsistently — five separate sr-only reimplementations, two command palettes (the older mdx one has correct listbox/option/aria-selected semantics the newer canonical shell dropped), two AnA composers (one accessible and tested, one not), and 124 hardcoded hexes in v2 stylesheets despite design-system/CLAUDE.md:22 declaring any non-token hex a bug. The root cause is structural: eslint.config.js:38 excludes client/src/** entirely, and eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y is not even a dependency — so no lint rule has ever inspected any of it, and none could have.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- GovernedActionSignoff.tsx is the internal gold standard and proves the team knows the pattern: focus trap (:83-105), initial focus into the dialog (:62-67), Escape to cancel, real <label htmlFor> via useId, aria-invalid + aria-describedby pointing at an error span with role="alert" (:139-142), a proper role="radiogroup" with aria-checked for the §11.50 signature meaning (:156-162), and role="dialog"/aria-modal/aria-labelledby/aria-describedby. Every other dialog in the shell should simply be rewritten against this file.
- icons.tsx:123 — `el(Icon)` applies `aria-hidden="true" focusable="false"` to every one of the ~200 lucide glyphs at a single choke point. This is exactly the right architecture for decorative iconography and it means no icon in the entire v2 shell pollutes the accessibility tree. Rare to see done this cleanly.
- components/ana/Composer.tsx:169 uses the correct streaming pattern — a dedicated `<div className={styles.srOnly} aria-live="polite" role="status">` carrying only the status string, not the transcript — and :217 gives the textarea a real `aria-label="Message AnA"`. It is backed by components/ana/__tests__/a11y-names.test.tsx, a genuine accessible-name regression test asserting names via role queries for the composer, attach, tools, send and stop controls.
- ProjectHome.tsx:402 independently reaches the same correct answer: `<div aria-live="polite" className="sr-only">{statusMessage}</div>` for upload progress, and ProjectHome.tsx:366-397 pairs a role="status" panel with the honest microcopy "Nothing has been rewritten" rather than silently regenerating regulated text.
- index.css:81-88 ships a global `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` reset with `!important` on animation-duration, iteration-count, transition-duration and scroll-behavior. It is at the app root so it actually covers the 57 transitions and 12 animations in app-v2.css.
- dataConnect.tsx:147-159 SampleTag renders the literal words "Sample data" / "Live", not a coloured dot — the single most safety-critical label in the product satisfies the HF spec's colour-never-alone rule by construction. The surrounding honesty envelope (liveGet never throws, fixtures always carry sample:true) is well-designed.
- Landmarks are named and correct where they exist: Shell.tsx:136 `<nav className="rail" aria-label="Primary">`, Shell.tsx:470 `<aside className="ana" aria-label="AnA assistant">`, Shell.tsx:461 names the collapsed seam separately, and V2App.tsx:252 provides exactly one `<main>`.
- Shell.tsx:114-115 `railVisible` hides a flag-gated rail entry entirely rather than rendering it disabled — the comment at :109-113 argues the case correctly. A visible affordance for a capability the deployment lacks is worse than no affordance, and the code follows through.
- HUMAN_FACTORS_AND_USABILITY_SPEC_2026-06-15.md is a genuinely rigorous document — §124 ("Color is never the only channel") and the §187 checklist item are correctly scoped, name the specific controls at risk (confidence bands, status pills, draft-vs-official eSTAR), and tie back to the critical-task table. The spec is not the problem; enforcement is.
- Shell.tsx:437-447 and 1057 contain unusually honest engineering comments that document a removed fetch against a nonexistent endpoint and refuse to reinstate it. This culture of writing down what was wrong is why the codebase is auditable at all.

#### `P0` A11Y-001 — C2CForm — the governed data-entry drawer used by 113 call sites — has no label association and announces validation failure to nobody

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/C2CForm.tsx:171` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** Line 171-174 renders `<label className="de-label">{f.label}{f.required && <span className="req">*</span>}</label>` with no `htmlFor`, and line 176 renders the control `{renderField(f)}` as a *sibling* inside `.de-field`, not nested in the label. journey-v2.css:412 confirms `.de-label{display:block}` — there is no wrapping. No field gets `id`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-required` or `aria-invalid`. Line 189 renders the validation error as `{err && <div className="de-err">{err}</div>}` — a plain div with no `role="alert"`, no `aria-live`, and no `aria-describedby` link from any field. Line 157 sets `role="dialog"` with no `aria-modal`, no focus trap and no initial focus (contrast GovernedActionSignoff.tsx:62-67 and :83-105, same repo, same governed-action concern). Line 128-144 renders the `seg` control as bare buttons with `data-on` only — no role="radiogroup"/"radio", no aria-checked. Line 5-8 states 15 kit surfaces mount this; `grep -c C2CForm client/src/concept2cure/v2/surfaces/*.tsx` returns 113 references across AgencyMeetings, CmcModule, DesignControls, HumanFactors, IdentityConsole, Labeling, NdaCockpit, Nonclinical, QmpWorkspace and more.

**Failure scenario.** A Regulatory Affairs associate who uses JAWS opens the CMC change-control drawer to record a governed manufacturing change (CmcModule.tsx mounts C2CForm). Tabbing through, every field announces as "edit, blank" or at best reads the placeholder — she cannot tell the batch-number field from the reason-for-change field, and the required-field asterisk at line 173 is never announced because the label is not associated. She fills what she can infer and presses Save. `missing()` at line 72 catches an empty required field, `setErr` fires at line 77, and the message renders into a non-live div at line 189 — her screen reader says nothing, the dialog does not move focus, and no field is marked aria-invalid. From her perspective the button is simply dead. She presses it again. She cannot complete a 21 CFR §11 audited record entry, and because the form is modal-by-appearance but has no focus trap, tabbing past the last button walks her invisibly into the page behind the drawer.

**Fix.** Rewrite C2CForm's field renderer against the pattern already proven in GovernedActionSignoff.tsx: generate a `useId()` per field, put `htmlFor` on the label and `id` on the control, set `aria-required` from `f.required` and `aria-invalid` on fields returned by `missing()`, and point `aria-describedby` at both `f.desc` and the error node. Give the error div `role="alert"` and move focus to the first invalid control in `submit()` (line 74-86). Add `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-labelledby` on the title, initial focus and a Tab trap to the line 157 dialog. Convert the `seg` control at line 126-146 to `role="radiogroup"` with `role="radio"`/`aria-checked`. Because there are 113 call sites, fixing the primitive fixes all of them at once — this is the single highest-leverage change in the review.

#### `P0` A11Y-002 — The entire AnA transcript is a live region, so every streamed token re-announces the whole accumulated reply

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:490` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** Line 490: `<div className="ana-body" aria-live="polite">`. This container holds everything from line 491 to 657 — the first-run welcome block, the full context card (module name, `ac.here`, program, focus, stage, the readiness percentage at :549-554, evidence chips, recent activity), the empty-state greeting, and the entire message list. V2App.tsx:85 shows the streaming message body is rebuilt on every chunk (`body: m.text || (m.streaming ? m.statusPhase : '')`), and useAnaChat.ts documents a `text` event that "appended to the streaming message" per chunk. There is no `aria-atomic`, no `role="log"`, and no separate status node. The correct pattern exists twice in this same repo: components/ana/Composer.tsx:169 (`srOnly` + aria-live + role=status carrying only the status string) and ProjectHome.tsx:402. A grep for aria-live across the whole v2 tree returns exactly 2 hits — this one and ProjectHome's.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory writer using NVDA asks AnA to draft a Module 2.5 clinical overview section. The reply streams roughly 600 words. Each SSE `text` chunk mutates the text node inside the live region, so NVDA re-reads the entire accumulated reply from the beginning — dozens of times, each read longer than the last, queued and uninterruptible without silencing speech entirely. She never hears the end of the answer. Separately, when she navigates from the CMC surface to the eCTD surface, `getAnaContext` returns a different object at line 448, the whole context card at :521-589 re-renders inside the live region, and her screen reader spontaneously reads out "Working in… Sample data… Stage… Readiness 62 percent…" over whatever she was doing. She turns the AnA rail off, losing the product's central feature.

**Fix.** Remove `aria-live` from line 490. Add a dedicated visually-hidden status node the way Composer.tsx:169 does — one `<div className="sr-only" aria-live="polite" role="status">` that carries only short state transitions ("AnA is responding", "Response complete", the statusPhase). Give the message list `role="log"` with `aria-live="polite"` and `aria-relevant="additions"` so a *completed* turn is announced once, and suppress announcement while `m.streaming` is true by rendering the in-flight text outside the log until the `post_done` event lands. Move the context card at :521-589 out of any live region entirely.

#### `P0` A11Y-003 — Focus indicator stripped from every input and textarea shell-wide, and the one focus colour fails the 3:1 non-text contrast floor

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/styles/app-v2.css:61` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** Line 61: `.c2c-v2 input, .c2c-v2 textarea, .c2c-v2 select { … outline: 0; }`. Line 60 restores an indicator for `button:focus-visible` only — there is no `input:focus-visible` or `textarea:focus-visible` rule anywhere in the 26 v2 stylesheets. Consequences by control: the ⌘K palette input (Shell.tsx:1064) is styled only by app-v2.css:1431 `.cmdk-in input{flex:1;font-size:15px;}` and `.cmdk-in` has no `:focus-within` — it has literally zero focus indicator. The AnA composer textarea (Shell.tsx:692) relies on app-v2.css:1105 `.ana-composer:focus-within{border-color:var(--accent-100);}` — a 1px colour-only border change on the parent. Where indicators do exist they replace the outline with a border tint: app-v2.css:2614 `.esign-field input:focus{border-color:var(--accent-100);}` and :2227 `.gov-textarea:focus{outline:none;border-color:var(--accent-200);}` — the Part 11 e-signature field and the governed-action reason box. Measured: --accent-100 #d97757 against --bg-000 #faf9f5 = 2.96:1, against the white card #ffffff = 3.12:1, and against the --bg-100 #f0eee6 rail = 2.69:1. WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.11 requires 3:1. The global button ring at line 60 therefore also fails on the rail, which is the primary navigation.

**Failure scenario.** A reviewer with low vision who navigates by keyboard opens ⌘K, tabs once from the search input to the Clear button and shift-tabs back — the caret is the only cue that focus returned, and on an empty query the Clear button is not rendered at all (Shell.tsx:1073), so focus silently lands nowhere visible. Worse, in the Part 11 signing flow the same user tabs into the e-signature password field: the only feedback is a 1px border shifting from grey to an orange measuring 2.96:1 against the field background — below the perceptibility floor and a colour-only change, so a user with protanopia sees no change at all. He types his credentials into what he believes is the reason-for-change box, and the mis-keyed signature is rejected or, worse, the reason text is entered as the signature meaning on an audited record.

**Fix.** Delete `outline: 0` from line 61 and add a global `.c2c-v2 :is(input, textarea, select):focus-visible` rule matching line 60. Replace the focus colour: --accent-100 #d97757 cannot carry a focus ring at any of the shell's background values. Use a two-tone ring (a 2px dark --text-100 outline with a 1px light offset, or a 3px --accent ring layered on a white halo) so the indicator clears 3:1 against every surface, and never signal focus with border-colour alone — add a thickness or shadow delta so the cue survives colour-vision deficiency. Audit the 20 `outline:none`/`outline:0` sites the grep found in app-v2.css, coverage-v2.css, ectd-v2.css and editor-core.css the same way.

#### `P1` A11Y-004 — Command palette has no focus trap, no focus restoration, and no listbox semantics — a regression against the older mdx palette in the same repo

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:1061` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Line 1061 declares `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` but nothing enforces it: the keydown handler at :1036-1055 handles Escape/Arrow/Enter and never intercepts Tab, so focus walks straight out of the palette into the page that aria-modal has just told the screen reader to ignore. On close (:1031 `onClose()`, wired to V2App.tsx:285 `setCmdkOpen(false)`) the input unmounts and focus falls to `document.body` — no reference to the trigger is captured, so nothing is restored. The selection model is visual only: `sel` drives `className={...sel === si ? ' on' : ''}` at :1102, while the list container at :1087 is a plain `<div className="cmdk-list">` and each row at :1099 is a plain `<button>`. No `role="listbox"`, no `role="option"`, no `aria-selected`, no `aria-activedescendant`, and the input at :1064 has no `role="combobox"`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` and no label beyond a placeholder. The same product already does this correctly: client/src/concept2cure/mdx/shell/CmdK.tsx:264 `<div className="cmdk-list" role="listbox">` and :269-270 `role="option" aria-selected={sel === i}`.

**Failure scenario.** A submission manager using VoiceOver hits ⌘K from the eCTD surface and types "validation". Sighted users see the highlight move as they arrow down; she hears nothing at all — arrow keys change `sel` but no ARIA property changes, so there is no announcement of which of the six results is active. She presses Enter blind and lands on the wrong surface. Recovering, she presses Escape; focus drops to `document.body`, and because she was deep in the eCTD tree she must now Tab past the five rail destinations, the account button, and seven top-bar controls to get back — roughly 25 keystrokes. If instead she Tabs while the palette is open, she is placed on controls behind an `aria-modal` dialog that VoiceOver refuses to describe, with no visible focus ring on them (see A11Y-003).

**Fix.** Port the semantics from mdx/shell/CmdK.tsx:264-270 onto the canonical palette — better, extract one shared palette component so the two cannot diverge again. Add `role="combobox" aria-expanded aria-controls aria-activedescendant` to the input, `role="listbox"` to :1087, `role="option" aria-selected id={...}` to :1099, give each item a stable id, capture `document.activeElement` when `open` flips true (:942-948) and restore it in the cleanup, and add Tab/Shift+Tab cycling to the :1036 handler using the same approach as GovernedActionSignoff.tsx:88-105.

#### `P1` A11Y-005 — Token set fails WCAG AA for text: --text-400 measures 3.37:1 and is the default colour for metadata across the shell

`client/src/concept2cure/design/claude-design.css:80` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Measured contrast against --bg-000 #faf9f5: --text-400 #8a8880 = 3.37:1 (3.55:1 on a white card); --text-500 #b0aea5 = 2.11:1; --idle #b0aea5 (app-v2.css:26) = 2.11:1. AA requires 4.5:1 for text below 24px / 18.66px-bold. --text-400 is the shell's default metadata colour at small sizes: app-v2.css:132 `.reg-sub{font-size:13px;color:var(--text-400)}` (the surface subtitle), :144 `.chg-row-id{font-size:10.5px}`, :149 `.chg-row-m{font-size:11px}`, :108 `.cmdk-empty{font-size:12px}`, :90 the empty-state body, :1430 the palette search icon. Line 258 of the same file re-declares `--text-400: #8a8880` unchanged for dark mode, where it measures 4.27:1 on #262624 — still short of 4.5:1. Separately, `.reg-ask` (app-v2.css:133) sets `background:var(--accent-100); color:#fff` = 3.12:1 for a primary call-to-action label.

**Failure scenario.** A 55-year-old regulatory director reviewing a submission on a laptop in a bright conference room cannot read the surface subtitle under any section heading, the change-record IDs in the change-assessment table, or the "No results for…" text in the command palette — all rendered at 10.5–13px in a grey that fails AA by a third. She reads the headings only, misses the qualifying subtitle that scopes what the surface covers, and asks for a live demo instead of completing evaluation. On the change-assessment table specifically, the record ID at 10.5px monospace in --text-400 is the field she needs to quote in a regulatory correspondence.

**Fix.** Darken --text-400 to at least #6f6d66 (≈4.5:1 on #faf9f5) and retire --text-500/--idle as text colours entirely, restricting them to borders and disabled fills where 3:1 non-text applies. Give dark mode its own --text-400 value rather than reusing the light one at line 258. For `.reg-ask` at app-v2.css:133, either darken the fill to --accent-main-200 #c96442 (still only 3.70:1 — insufficient) or use dark text on a light accent fill. Add a token-contrast assertion to CI so the ratio is enforced rather than eyeballed.

#### `P1` A11Y-006 — Segmented control signals selection by colour alone at 1.06:1 separation, violating the repo's own HF spec

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/styles/journey-v2.css:421` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Line 420 `.de-seg-opt{border:1px solid var(--border-strong);background:var(--bg-000);color:var(--text-300);font-weight:500;}` and line 421 `.de-seg-opt[data-on]{border-color:var(--accent-100);background:var(--accent-000);color:var(--accent-200);}`. All three deltas are pure colour — the font-weight, size, border-width and geometry are identical, and C2CForm.tsx:133-142 renders no check glyph. Measured luminance separation between selected and unselected: background #faf9f5 vs #faf0ec = 1.06:1; text #6b6963 vs #c96442 = 1.41:1; border #d1cfc0 vs #d97757 = 1.99:1. HUMAN_FACTORS_AND_USABILITY_SPEC_2026-06-15.md:124 states "Color is never the only channel… status pills… carry a text label and/or icon and shape, not just the earthy color," and line 187 makes it a release checklist item. The correct treatment already exists elsewhere in the shell — Shell.tsx:284 renders `{I.check}` next to the selected client-domain option.

**Failure scenario.** A CMC lead with deuteranopia opens the Design Controls drawer and must choose a change classification on a `seg` field — the value that determines whether the change is reportable. In greyscale the selected chip differs from its neighbours by 6% in background luminance and carries no checkmark, no bolder weight and no shape change. He cannot tell which classification is currently applied, picks the wrong one, and the drawer saves it as a governed record with an audit entry (C2CForm.tsx:185). Nothing in the flow tells him the value changed, because the confirmation surface uses the same encoding.

**Fix.** Add a non-colour channel to `.de-seg-opt[data-on]`: render `{I.check}` in the selected button in C2CForm.tsx:133-142, raise the selected weight to 650, and thicken the border to 2px with a compensating 1px inset so layout does not shift. Then run the same audit over every `[data-on]`/`.is-done`/`.ok`/`.err` rule in the 26 v2 stylesheets — the AnA executed-action chips (Shell.tsx:645-648) and the activity rows (:579-580) already pair colour with a distinct icon and are the model to copy.

#### `P1` A11Y-007 — Both dropdown menus in the shell declare role="menu" but implement none of the menu keyboard contract

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:205` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Account menu: the trigger at :189-201 has `data-open={acct}` but no `aria-haspopup` and no `aria-expanded`. The panel at :205 is `role="menu"` with nine `role="menuitem"` children (:217-233), but there is no arrow-key handler, no roving tabindex, no Escape handler, and no focus move into or out of the menu — dismissal is only via the mouse scrim at :204. Domain switcher: :313 declares `role="menu"` while its children rendered by `segOpt` (:268-286) are plain `<button className="tb-dom-opt">` with no `role="menuitem"` and no `aria-checked`, so the menu reports zero items to strict AT implementations; the current selection is conveyed by `data-on` plus a check glyph at :284 (the glyph is good; the missing aria-checked is not). Its trigger at :299-309 likewise lacks aria-expanded. Contrast GovernedActionSignoff.tsx:156-162, which gets radiogroup semantics right in the same codebase.

**Failure scenario.** An org admin using NVDA activates the account button to reach the Admin console (the only entry point — Shell.tsx:98 gates it here). NVDA announces "menu, 9 items" and switches to focus mode, where the documented gesture is Down-arrow. Down-arrow does nothing, because no handler exists. Escape does nothing, because there is none. He is now inside a construct his screen reader has described as a menu that responds to no menu key, and the only way out is to Tab through all nine items — which also fires no close, leaving the menu visually open over the page while focus is somewhere behind it. He cannot reach the admin console without a mouse.

**Fix.** Either implement the full APG menu-button pattern (aria-haspopup="true" + aria-expanded on both triggers, Down/Up/Home/End roving focus, Escape to close and return focus to the trigger, focus first item on open) or — simpler and preferable here — drop `role="menu"`/`role="menuitem"` entirely and let the panels be plain grouped buttons, which already behave correctly under Tab. Keep the check glyph at :284 and add `aria-checked` with `role="menuitemradio"` if the menu role is retained. Do not leave the roles declared and the behaviour absent — that is worse than no roles at all.

#### `P1` COH-001 — There is no single design system — five styling mechanisms coexist and the same primitives are reimplemented with divergent accessibility

`client/src/concept2cure/mdx/app.css:45` · blocks **G2** · ~weeks

**Evidence.** Five mechanisms are live in client/src: 51 global CSS files, 5 CSS Modules, Tailwind utilities (tailwind.config.ts:75 globs all of client/src), 1,403 inline `style={{…}}` props in v2/surfaces alone, and 76 shadcn components in client/src/components/ui. Duplication is measurable, not theoretical. (a) `sr-only` is reimplemented five times with identical declarations under five prefixes — risk/app.css:134 `.risk-sr-only`, design/zen.css:789 `.zen-sr-only`, labeling/app.css:63 `.lb-sr-only`, submission/app.css:127 `.sub-sr-only`, tasking/app.css:119 `.task-sr-only` — while ProjectHome.tsx:402 relies on Tailwind's. (b) Two command palettes: mdx/shell/CmdK.tsx:264-270 has role=listbox/option/aria-selected; the canonical v2 Shell.tsx:1087-1111 has none (see A11Y-004). (c) Two AnA composers: components/ana/Composer.tsx (298 lines, labelled, tested) and a from-scratch reimplementation at Shell.tsx:678-911 whose textarea at :692 has no accessible name. (d) The mdx sub-app leaks unscoped global resets while v2 scopes everything under `.c2c-v2`: mdx/app.css:45 restyles every `button` document-wide, :47 sets `outline:0` on every `input, textarea` document-wide, :40-44 forces `body{font-size:13px}`, and :51 defines a bare `.shell` colliding with the class V2App.tsx:239 puts on its root. These are permanent once the MDX route is visited, since Vite CSS imports never unload. (e) design-system/CLAUDE.md:22 declares "Any hex… that isn't reading from these tokens is a bug" — the v2 stylesheets contain 124 hardcoded six-digit hexes (app-v2.css:146 `#8a1f1f`, :147 `#3f5c1f`, :171 `#7a4a0f`). (f) The kit mirrors have drifted: ui_kits/mdx has 68 files, design-system/ui_kits/mdx has 78, and .github/workflows/sync-design-system.yml:26 confirms the reconciling job is `workflow_dispatch` only and its source secret may not even resolve.

**Failure scenario.** A designer files a bug that the AnA composer is unlabelled for screen readers. An engineer fixes components/ana/Composer.tsx, sees the passing a11y-names.test.tsx, and ships — but the shell rail users actually see is the Shell.tsx:678 reimplementation, which is untouched and untested. The bug persists through a procurement accessibility review. Separately, a customer navigates to the device/MDX workstream and then back to any other surface: mdx/app.css:47 has now globally stripped the focus outline from every input in the application, including the shadcn forms in client/src/components/ui that were previously fine, and mdx/app.css:40 has reset body font-size for the whole document. Neither regression is attributable to the surface where it manifests.

**Fix.** Pick one owner per concern and delete the rest. Scope mdx/app.css under a `.mdx-app` root exactly as app-v2.css scopes under `.c2c-v2`, and remove its `body`/`button`/`input`/`svg` element selectors. Collapse the five `*-sr-only` classes to one utility. Delete the Shell.tsx:678-911 composer and the Shell.tsx:926-1123 palette in favour of the already-tested components/ana/Composer.tsx and a single extracted palette. Convert the 124 hardcoded hexes to tokens or add them to the token file honestly. Either wire sync-design-system.yml to a real source and put it on a schedule, or delete design-system/ui_kits/ and treat ui_kits/ as canonical — two drifting mirrors of the same kit is worse than one stale one.

#### `P1` PROC-001 — No lint rule has ever inspected any of the 862 aria attributes — client/src is excluded and jsx-a11y is not installed

`eslint.config.js:38` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Line 38 lists `'client/src/**'` in the project-wide `ignores` array, with the comment at :30-32 explaining it is "currently excluded because the UI is going through a separate styling/refactor pass. Re-enable once that lands." Line 42 additionally excludes `design-system/**`. Independently, `grep -c jsx-a11y package.json` returns 0 — eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y is not a dependency, and eslint.config.js:162-165 registers only @typescript-eslint, react, react-hooks and security. So even lifting the client/src exclusion today would surface zero accessibility findings. The .github/workflows directory contains 19 workflows and none runs axe, pa11y, Lighthouse a11y or a contrast check; lighthouse-ci.json exists at the repo root but no workflow references it. The one accessibility test in the entire client tree is components/ana/__tests__/a11y-names.test.tsx, hand-written and covering two components.

**Failure scenario.** A team member fixes the C2CForm labelling defect (A11Y-001) correctly, then a later refactor of the field renderer drops `htmlFor` again while moving code. CI is fully green — lint does not read the file, no a11y test covers the form, and the 113 surfaces that mount it have no coverage either. The regression ships and is discovered by a customer's procurement accessibility audit, where a VPAT gap on a governed data-entry form is a contract blocker rather than a bug report. Every finding in this review can silently regress the same way.

**Fix.** Install eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y and enable it for client/src with the recommended ruleset. Do not lift the client/src exclusion in one step — that will produce thousands of findings and get reverted.
