Scope it: start with `client/src/concept2cure/v2/**` and `client/src/concept2cure/components/ana/**` at error level for the rules that map to the defects found here (label-has-associated-control, no-noninteractive-element-to-interactive-role, aria-role, role-has-required-aria-props, no-autofocus), leave the rest at warn, and widen the glob per surface family. Add an axe-core pass to the existing Playwright config over the shell, the palette and one C2CForm-mounting surface, plus a token-contrast unit test asserting the ratios in A11Y-005.

#### `P1` UX-001 — Six controls in the top bar and AnA header render as enabled buttons and do nothing when clicked

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/Shell.tsx:337` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Six `<button>` elements have no `onClick` and no `disabled`: :327 `.tb-org` (organization switcher, title "switcher lands with the auth flow phase"), :337 `.tb-task` ("New task — assign & track from this screen (lands with the tasks surface port)"), :345 collaborate/message, :348 notifications bell, :351 help, and :482 "New thread" in the AnA rail header. All six are keyboard-focusable, receive the hover and focus styling from app-v2.css:60, and expose no state to assistive technology indicating unavailability. The explanatory text lives only in the `title` attribute, which does not appear on keyboard focus. The repo demonstrates it knows better at Shell.tsx:114-115, where an unavailable rail destination is not rendered at all, with a comment arguing exactly this point: "A visible entry for a capability the deployment does not have is worse than no entry."

**Failure scenario.** A submission manager mid-compile notices the notifications bell and clicks it expecting the gateway acknowledgement she is waiting on. Nothing happens — no panel, no toast, no disabled cursor. She clicks twice more, assumes the app has hung during an eCTD compile, and reloads the page, abandoning unsaved composer state. A screen-reader user has it worse: the button announces as an ordinary enabled "Notifications, button", activates silently, and gives no indication the feature does not exist. In a regulatory context the notifications and task controls are precisely the affordances a user trusts to tell them an agency deadline or gateway receipt has arrived.

**Fix.** Apply the rule the codebase already states at Shell.tsx:109-115: do not render an affordance for a capability that does not exist. Remove the six buttons until their surfaces land. If any must stay for layout reasons, mark them `disabled` with `aria-disabled="true"` and put the explanation in visible text or a `aria-describedby` tooltip that appears on focus, not in a mouse-only `title`.

#### `P2` A11Y-008 — No skip link, and the global top bar is nested inside <main> so it forms no banner landmark

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:252` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Line 252-262 renders `<main className="main"><TopBar …/>…</main>`, and TopBar returns `<header className="topbar">` at Shell.tsx:288. Per HTML-AAM a `<header>` descended from `<main>` maps to `generic`, not `banner` — so the breadcrumb, domain switcher, org identity, ⌘K trigger and the four action buttons are unreachable by landmark navigation. A grep for `skip-link|Skip to|skipToContent` across the whole v2 tree returns nothing. The rail rendered at :244 contains up to 15 focusable controls (client categories plus RAIL_PRIMARY plus the account button) that precede the page body in DOM order on every single route.

**Failure scenario.** A screen-reader user lands on the eCTD compile surface. Pressing D (landmark navigation in NVDA) cycles navigation → main → complementary; the top bar is not among them, so to reach the ⌘K trigger or the org switcher she must Tab linearly. And on every route change she is returned to the top of the document with no skip link, forcing her through all 15 rail controls before reaching the content she navigated to — on a surface family where a user moves between 8–10 surfaces per submission review, that is several hundred wasted keystrokes per session.

**Fix.** Move `<TopBar>` out of `<main>` in V2App.tsx:252 so its `<header>` maps to `banner`, or give it `role="banner"` explicitly if the grid layout requires it stay nested. Add a visually-hidden-until-focused skip link as the first focusable element of the shell root at :238, targeting the `.page` container at :263, and give that container `tabIndex={-1}` plus an `id` so the link can move focus there.

#### `P2` A11Y-009 — A complete dark theme is built and shipped but has no toggle — prefs.dark is read and never written

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:239` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `dark` is declared in the Prefs interface at :46, defaulted to `false` at :56, and consumed at :239 `className={`c2c-v2 shell${prefs.dark ? ' dark' : ''}`}` — but a grep across the entire v2 tree for any write returns only those three lines. Nothing calls `set('dark', …)`, and there is no global toggler either: `grep -rn "documentElement.classList" client/src` returns nothing. Meanwhile a full dark token set exists at claude-design.css:240-276 (dark --bg-*, --text-*, --accent-main-200 lightened "for legibility", dark shadow scale) and app-v2.css:40-44 ships `.dark` overrides for --idle-muted and the elevation scale. The account menu at Shell.tsx:93-108 lists nine entries and appearance is not among them.

**Failure scenario.** A pharmacovigilance reviewer with light sensitivity, working long signal-detection sessions, cannot switch the platform out of a cream #faf9f5 background. He looks in the account menu (the only settings entry point), finds Usage, Billing, Plans, Workspace, Coverage, Help and Log out — no appearance control. The only route to a theme that was fully designed and shipped is hand-editing the `c2c-v2-prefs` key in localStorage. He requests an accommodation his vendor cannot grant through the product.

**Fix.** Add an appearance control to the ACCT_ITEMS list at Shell.tsx:93-108 wired to `set('dark', …)`, defaulting from `window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` on first load rather than hardcoding `false` at V2App.tsx:56. Then verify the dark token set actually holds up — claude-design.css:258 re-declares `--text-400: #8a8880` unchanged from light mode, where it measures 4.27:1 on the dark #262624 background and still fails AA (see A11Y-005).

#### `P2` UX-002 — The rail defaults to collapsed, so every first-run user meets five unlabelled icons

`client/src/concept2cure/v2/V2App.tsx:57` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Line 57 sets `railCollapsed: true` in DEFAULT_PREFS, and `loadPrefs()` at :64-72 returns those defaults whenever localStorage is empty — i.e. for every new user, every new browser and every incognito session. app-v2.css:872 then applies `.c2c-v2.shell[data-collapsed="true"] .nav-item .lbl { display: none; }`, and :862 hides the `.rail-section` group headings. The accessible name survives via the `title` at Shell.tsx:126 (which is why this is P2 and not P1), but `title` renders no tooltip on keyboard focus, so the labels are unavailable to sighted keyboard users and absent for everyone until they hover. The rail is the shell's only persistent navigation — Shell.tsx:178-184 documents that it was deliberately narrowed to five top-level destinations, with everything else demoted to ⌘K and deep links.

**Failure scenario.** A regulatory director opens the platform for the first time during a purchase evaluation. The left rail shows five unlabelled 16px lucide glyphs and a set of client-category icons above them. He has no idea what the product contains — the demotion at Shell.tsx:178-184 means the other ~15 destinations exist only behind ⌘K, which he has not been told about. He clicks two icons at random, concludes the product is thin, and the evaluation is shaped by an accidental default in a preferences object.

**Fix.** Change `railCollapsed` to `false` at V2App.tsx:57 so the first-run experience shows labels, and let the user's own collapse choice persist from there. If the collapsed rail must stay reachable, replace `title` on the nav items (Shell.tsx:126) with a real tooltip that also fires on `:focus-visible`, so keyboard users get the label the mouse users get.

### Authorization attack paths: role/permission gates, cross-org reach, token lifetime & revocation, PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST, SCIM, and .js/.ts shadow resolution

The cross-tenant admin surfaces are genuinely well defended: every router that can see across organizations (/api/admin/master, /api/admin/scim-tenants, /api/admin/scim-ip-allowlist, /api/admin/access-management) is gated by `requirePlatformAdmin`, which deliberately has NO org-admin bypass and fails closed on DB error, and /api/admin/audit/siem is org-pinned from the verified JWT regardless of its looser role list. `tenant-users.ts` re-authorizes against the TARGET org from the database rather than the JWT. I could not construct a path by which a tenant admin reads another tenant's dossiers through the role helpers. The 19-entry PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST audits clean: all three routers mounted under the /api/v1 carve-out carry their own auth (X-API-Key for public-api, per-route JWT for pyramid, the auth router itself), and /api/setup is self-closing on first user plus rate-limited plus tenant-admission-gated. SCIM's own bearer auth is above the industry bar (constant-time multi-tenant compare with no early break, SHA-256-hashed DB tokens, opt-in fail-closed per-org IP allowlist, dedicated rate limiter, Part 11 audit on every lifecycle event).

What is genuinely broken is the session lifecycle, not the role matrix. Three P1s: (1) `users.status` is never consulted on any authentication path, so SCIM deprovisioning and master-admin suspension do not revoke anything — an offboarded employee logs straight back in; (2) access-token revocation is write-only — `revokeToken` writes to Redis/DB/memory on logout but no request path ever reads it, and `isTokenBlacklisted` has zero callers, so a stolen 24h bearer token cannot be killed; (3) I empirically confirmed with the repo's own esbuild config that the production bundle binds ~26 route modules to the stale `server/middleware/auth.js`, which lacks both the MFA/token-class guard and the org-membership re-check — and one of those mounts (pyramid at /api/v1) sits on the boundary allowlist, so the stale twin is its only gate and an MFA-partial token authenticates it. Plus a real cross-tenant IDOR on Cortex atoms where `requireOrgAccess` is present but compares nothing.

On the specific question "what does an admin claim grant across org boundaries": today, nothing across orgs — but the claim is read from the JWT and never re-resolved, so a role change or demotion has zero effect until the 24h token expires.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- The platform trust boundary is correctly separated from the tenant one. server/middleware/requirePlatformAdmin.ts exists specifically because requireRole's org-admin override was unusable for cross-tenant surfaces (documented at lines 10-18), accepts only true platform roles (super_admin/platform_admin/support) or an env allowlist, falls back to a DB platform_role_grants lookup, and DENIES on any DB error (lines 63-76). It gates every cross-tenant router: master-admin.ts:37, access-management.ts:44, scim-tenants.ts:26, scim-ip-allowlist.ts:27. This is the single control that stops a tenant admin from becoming a platform admin, and it is implemented correctly.
- The default-deny /api boundary is mounted once, before any route registration (server/index.ts:123 -> startup/middleware.ts:113-119), and its allowlist matching is prefix-confusion-safe: isPublicApiPath (authBoundary.ts:111-117) requires `path === entry.path || path.startsWith(entry.path + '/')`, so /api/healthxyz cannot ride on /api/health. Every one of the 19 entries carries a written justification naming the alternative control, and I verified each of those alternatives actually exists (public-api.ts:272 requireApiKey; pyramid.routes.ts per-route authenticateToken; setup.ts:84-89 self-closing gate; startup/inline-endpoints.ts:128/143 requireMetricsAuth).
- No pre-boundary middleware populates req.user. I checked every assignment to req.user under server/middleware and server/startup: only auth.ts:145/298, auth.js:63 and tenantContext.ts:262 set it, and none of them is mounted ahead of createAuthBoundary. The boundary's `if (req.user) return next()` shortcut (authBoundary.ts:161) therefore cannot be primed by attacker-controlled headers.
- JWT handling is hardened beyond the norm: HS256 pinned inline at both verify call sites with a dedicated CI gate to keep it that way (server/utils/jwtVerify.ts:86/91, npm run ci:jwt-verify-pinned), a 32-character minimum secret enforced at config load (config/environment.ts:104-118), per-environment secret suffixes, and previous-secret fallback restricted to genuine signature mismatches (isSignatureMismatch, jwtVerify.ts:57-64) so expired tokens are never retried.
- Token-class discrimination (refresh / mfa_challenge / mfa_partial rejected on the access path) was deliberately factored into server/middleware/tokenType.ts, a module with no .js twin, precisely so the shadow-resolver hazard could not silently drop the control — the module docstring says so explicitly. The same reasoning produced server/middleware/orgMembership.ts. This is a team that understood the failure mode and engineered around it; the remaining gap (AUTHZ-03) is that auth.js itself was never retired.
- The org-membership re-check is well designed: positive and negative results cached ~60s, fail-closed on 'revoked', fail-open with a structured warning only on genuine DB unavailability, size-capped at 5000 entries, explicit invalidation hooks called from mutation sites, and — notably — a single shared cache reused by the collaboration WebSocket with a deliberately stricter policy on 'indeterminate' because an open socket has unmediated CRDT access (orgMembership.ts:236-258).
- SCIM bearer auth is genuinely strong: constant work per tenant with no early break so timing cannot reveal which tenant matched (scim.ts:236-256), DB tokens stored only as SHA-256 hashes, an opt-in per-org source-IP allowlist that fails closed on an unresolvable IP (scim.ts:188-196), a dedicated 60/min/IP rate limiter because SCIM sits outside the /api limiters, Part 11 audit rows on every provisioning and deprovisioning event, Groups constrained to four org roles so a SCIM token can never mint a platform role, and every /Users/:id operation scoped by an organization_users join.
- server/db/tenantAdmission.ts is an unusual and valuable control: it refuses to create organization #2 in any deployed environment while RLS_ENFORCE is not 'on', and it FAILS CLOSED when the organization count cannot be determined. It is wired into all the org-creation paths I checked (auth.ts signup:850, setup.ts:100, tenants.ts:176). This converts the inert-RLS posture from a silent risk into an enforced single-tenant constraint.
- tenant-users.ts authorizes against the TARGET organization named in the request, resolved from the database, not from the caller's JWT claim (authorizeOrgAccess, lines 41-67), with a documented note that the handlers previously trusted request-supplied organizationId. Role values are constrained by z.enum to admin/manager/member/viewer (lines 16, 30), which is what prevents a tenant admin from writing 'super_admin' into organization_users and thereby satisfying requirePlatformAdmin's role check.
- Login hardening is thorough: bcrypt cost 12, account lockout with audit trail, uniform 'Invalid credentials' responses with an explicit comment against leaking remaining-attempts or lock state, audit events recorded for unknown-email attempts with the literal attacker-supplied value for SOC correlation, separate rate limiters for login and signup, NIST 800-63B password policy, and explicit rejection of empty and legacy temp_-prefixed password hashes (server/auth.ts:284-301). Refresh-token rotation revokes the old token (routes/auth.ts:1130) and the refresh path IS the one place revocation is checked (1040).
- The repository ships a CI gate for the exact defect class in AUTHZ-03 (scripts/ci/check-js-ts-shadows.mjs) containing an empirically-measured, dated resolver matrix across tsx, esbuild and vitest, with every shadow pair either deleted, reduced to a re-export shim, or allowlisted with a challengeable one-line reason. Nine of the thirteen pairs are already safe shims. That is materially better hygiene than most codebases with this problem.

#### `P1` AUTHZ-01 — Account deactivation revokes nothing: users.status is never checked on any authentication path

`server/routes/auth.ts:369` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/login selects the user at server/routes/auth.ts:369 and proceeds through account lockout (391), password-hash presence (415), bcrypt compare (423) and org-membership resolution (462-480) to token issuance (715) without ever reading `users.status`. The same is true of both authentication middlewares: server/middleware/auth.ts::authenticateToken (111-163) and server/auth.ts::authMiddleware (74-159) never load or test the column, and server/middleware/orgMembership.ts::queryOrgMembership (116-126) selects only `organizationUsers.role` — it re-checks that the membership row EXISTS, not that the account is active. Meanwhile the deprovisioning writes are all status-only: server/routes/scim.ts:672 (`UPDATE users SET status = 'inactive' ... WHERE id = $1` on SCIM DELETE), scim.ts:607-635 (PATCH active=false), scim.ts:560 (PUT), and server/routes/admin/master-admin.ts:42 (USER_STATUSES = active/suspended/inactive). None of them touch organization_users, so enforceOrgMembership still returns 'member'. I searched the whole server tree for account-status enforcement: the ONLY consumer is server/services/part11ComplianceService.ts:568 (`if (user.status !== 'active') return false`), which gates electronic signature only.

**Failure scenario.** A customer's IdP offboards a departing regulatory director. Okta issues `DELETE /scim/v2/Users/42`; the handler at server/routes/scim.ts:672 sets users.status='inactive', writes a scim.user.deactivated audit event, and returns 204 — the tenant's compliance dashboard shows the user as deprovisioned. The ex-employee then sends `POST /api/auth/login {"email":"...","password":"<their still-valid password>"}` and receives HTTP 200 with a 24h access token (routes/auth.ts:715). Every subsequent request passes the /api boundary: authenticateToken verifies the signature, enforceOrgMembership finds the untouched organization_users row and returns 'member'. They retain full read and write access to the tenant's regulatory dossiers indefinitely, re-logging in each day. The only thing they cannot do is apply a 21 CFR Part 11 electronic signature. The same holds for a master-admin 'suspend user' action.

**Fix.** Reject non-active accounts at every credential-to-session boundary, not just at signing time: (a) in routes/auth.ts login (and the enterprise/SSO login paths) return 401 when `userData.status !== 'active'`; (b) extend server/middleware/orgMembership.ts::queryOrgMembership to join `users` and treat `status <> 'active'` as 'revoked' so live tokens die within the 60s cache TTL; (c) make SCIM DELETE/PATCH-inactive also delete the organization_users row and call invalidateOrgMembershipCache(userId, orgId) so revocation is immediate on the mutating instance.

#### `P1` AUTHZ-02 — Access-token revocation is write-only — logout does not invalidate the bearer token, and no session can be killed

`server/routes/auth.ts:958` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** POST /api/auth/logout (routes/auth.ts:968) calls `revokeToken(token)` at line 991 and `revokeToken(req.body.refreshToken)` at 996; server/services/token-revocation.ts writes the SHA-256 hash through to Redis, the revoked_tokens table and an in-memory Set. Nothing reads it back on the access path. Grepping every consumer of the service across server/: `isTokenRevoked` appears only at routes/auth.ts:1040 (the refresh endpoint) and `getRevocationHealth` at routes/concept2cure.ts:3820 (a metrics probe). `isTokenBlacklisted`, exported at routes/auth.ts:958 as the sync wrapper, has ZERO callers anywhere in the repo. Neither server/middleware/auth.ts::authenticateToken (111-163), nor server/auth.ts::authMiddleware (74-159), nor server/middleware/authBoundary.ts::createAuthBoundary (145-195) imports or consults the revocation service. Access token TTL is 24h (routes/auth.ts:96, JWT_EXPIRES_IN='24h'; server/config/environment.ts:308 default '1d'), refresh 7d.

**Failure scenario.** An attacker captures a user's access token (browser XSS, a proxy log, an unlocked laptop, a leaked HAR file). The user or the customer's SOC notices and hits logout. `POST /api/auth/logout` returns 200 and the token hash lands in Redis and revoked_tokens. The attacker replays the identical token against any /api endpoint — say `GET /api/tenant-export/...` or any dossier read — and it is accepted, because authenticateToken verifies only the HS256 signature, expiry and token class, then calls enforceOrgMembership (which passes: the victim is still a member). The attacker keeps full tenant access until the token's `exp`, up to 24 hours after the reported compromise. There is no operator action short of rotating JWT_SECRET (which logs out every user on the deployment) that ends a single compromised session.

**Fix.** Consult the existing revocation store on the access path: add an `await isTokenRevoked(token)` check inside server/middleware/auth.ts::authenticateToken (and server/auth.ts::authMiddleware) before setting req.user, returning 401 on a hit. The store is already three-tier and durable, so the fix is a read, not new infrastructure. Delete the unused isTokenBlacklisted export or repoint it. Consider also cutting the access-token TTL from 24h to 15-60 minutes now that a working refresh path with rotation exists (routes/auth.ts:1111-1130).

#### `P1` AUTHZ-03 — Production build binds ~26 route modules to the stale server/middleware/auth.js, which has no MFA/token-class guard and no org-membership re-check

`server/middleware/auth.js:27` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** I reproduced the resolution empirically with the repo's own production build configuration (scripts/build-server.mjs: esbuild, platform node, bundle, format esm, packages external, target node20). Bundling server/routes/pyramid.routes.ts and reading the metafile inputs returns exactly `['server/middleware/auth.js']` — the stale twin, not auth.ts. The repo documents this itself: scripts/ci/check-js-ts-shadows.mjs carries an empirically-derived resolver matrix (a .ts importer with a './x.js' specifier gets x.ts under tsx/dev but x.js under esbuild/prod) and allowlists `'server/middleware/auth.js'` with the note "diverged legacy middleware; prod bundles it for ~26 .ts routes importing '../middleware/auth.js' (ledger M-5)". 34 files use that specifier, including server/routes/pyramid.routes.ts:43, cortex-unified.ts:20, billing-dashboard.ts:27, knowledge-base.ts:23, defense-packet.ts:28, se-matrix.ts:19, qc.routes.ts:18, docx-factory.ts:19, global-compliance.ts:21, and the bootstrap mounts register-document-routes.ts:4, register-ind-lifecycle-routes.ts, register-clinical-intel-routes.ts:82. What auth.js:27-106 omits versus auth.ts:111-163: (1) no `nonAccessTokenReason` check (auth.ts:128, middleware/tokenType.ts) — it accepts ANY token signed with config.jwt.secret that carries an organizationId claim (auth.js:51); (2) no `enforceOrgMembership` call (auth.ts:157); (3) no subject-claim validation (auth.ts:136); (4) loose bearer extraction via `authHeader.split(' ')[1]` (auth.js:44) instead of the strict regex at auth.ts:27; (5) an `isPublicRoute(req.path)` bypass (auth.js:29, PUBLIC_ROUTES at 260-268) that skips auth entirely for any mount-relative sub-path under /health, /api/health, /api/public, /auth/login, /auth/register, /auth/forgot-password, /auth/reset-password. The MFA-partial token that makes this live is minted at server/routes/authEnterprise.ts:339-349 — signed with config.jwt.secret, carrying userId/email/organizationId and `role:'pending_mfa', mfaPending:true`, returned to the client after PASSWORD ONLY, before the second factor.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker has a victim's password but not their second factor. Step 1: `POST /api/auth/enterprise/login {email,password}` returns 200 with the 5-minute partialToken (authEnterprise.ts:339) — MFA not yet completed. Step 2: `GET /api/v1/pyramids/types -H 'Authorization: Bearer <partialToken>'`. /api/v1 is on PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST (server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:85), so the global default-deny boundary does NOT run; pyramid.routes.ts is mounted first at /api/v1 (startup/routes.ts:104 before :110), so public-api's requireApiKey never runs either. The only gate is pyramid.routes.ts:277's authenticateToken — which in dist/index.js IS auth.js. auth.js:29 finds '/pyramids/types' non-public, verifies the signature, sees user.organizationId present at :51, sets req.user and calls next(). Response: 200. The MFA guard has been bypassed, and the same request from a user whose organization_users row was deleted also succeeds because auth.js never re-checks membership. The data returned here is static regulatory pyramid metadata, so the immediate loss is small — but the identical stale middleware is the route-level gate on /api/cortex, /api/knowledge-base, /api/billing, /api/document-authoring, /api/ectd, /api/submissions and /api/ind-lifecycle, where today only the boundary's correct auth.ts stands between an MFA-partial token and tenant dossiers. Any future allowlist entry or mount reorder converts this from a demo into a breach. Corroborating evidence that the twin is live in prod: billing-dashboard.ts:764's `requireRole('admin','owner')` resolves to auth.js's single-argument requireRole, which reads `req.user.roles` — a field auth.js never populates (it assigns the raw JWT payload at :63, and no token-minting site emits a `roles` claim) — so PUT /api/billing/seats returns 403 to every user in production while working in dev.

**Fix.** Delete server/middleware/auth.js and repoint the 34 importers at auth.ts (the mechanical change is dropping the '.js' from the specifier, which esbuild already resolves to the .ts twin — verified). If deletion must be staged, replace auth.js's body with a pure re-export shim (`export * from './auth.ts'`), which the repo's own CI gate already recognises as the behaviourally-safe pattern for the nine other allowlisted shadows. Do NOT ship the current diverged pair: the boundary is a single mount away from being the only thing containing it.

#### `P2` AUTHZ-04 — Cross-tenant read/update/delete of Cortex knowledge atoms — requireOrgAccess is mounted but compares nothing

`server/services/cortexPrimeService.ts:308` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** cortexPrimeService.getAtom (308-314) is `SELECT * FROM cortex.atoms WHERE id = $1 AND is_active = TRUE` — no org_id predicate. updateAtom (319-363) builds `UPDATE cortex.atoms SET ... WHERE id = $N` and deleteAtom (365-371) is `UPDATE cortex.atoms SET is_active = FALSE WHERE id = $1`; neither takes an orgId parameter at all, even though the table has an org_id column (db/migrations/073_cortex_prime_unified_brain.sql:15-32) and createAtom (276-303) populates it. The route layer does not compensate: server/routes/cortexRoutes.ts:187 exposes `GET /atoms/:id` with NO guard beyond the router-wide requireAuth at line 131, and :199 (PATCH) / :214 (DELETE) attach `requireOrgAccess` — which at server/middleware/auth.ts:211-239 only compares `req.params.orgId`, `req.params.organizationId`, `req.body.organizationId` or `req.query.organizationId` against the caller's org. On `/atoms/:id` none of those keys exist, so line 232's condition is false and the middleware calls next() unconditionally. The router is reachable: server/routes/cortex-unified.ts:1226 mounts cortexRoutes at '/' and '/main', and register-document-routes.ts:500 mounts cortex-unified at /api/cortex. Contrast with the sibling POST /atoms (172) and POST /search (233), which correctly derive orgId from req.user.

**Failure scenario.** A user authenticated in tenant A holds a normal JWT (no admin role needed — requireAuth is the only real gate). They send `GET /api/cortex/atoms/<uuid>` for an atom belonging to tenant B and receive 200 with the atom's full `content` (free-text regulatory knowledge extracted from that tenant's dossiers) and `structuredData`. `DELETE /api/cortex/atoms/<uuid>` returns `{"success":true}` and soft-deletes tenant B's atom from their knowledge base. `PATCH /api/cortex/atoms/<uuid> {"content":"..."}` silently rewrites it — a data-integrity event in a GxP system with no per-atom audit trail on this path. Honest scope caveat: atom ids are v4 UUID primary keys and the only writer is this same API, so the attacker needs a leaked or logged atom id rather than being able to enumerate; the read is not a bulk dump. The write and delete are the sharper end — a single leaked id yields silent cross-tenant tampering.

**Fix.** Add an orgId parameter to getAtom/updateAtom/deleteAtom in cortexPrimeService.ts and append `AND org_id = $N` to all three statements, sourcing orgId from req.user.organizationId at cortexRoutes.ts:187/199/214 exactly as createAtom and search already do. Separately, stop using requireOrgAccess as a tenant guard on routes that carry no org identifier in params/body/query — it is a no-op there and reads to a reviewer as protection that does not exist.

#### `P2` AUTHZ-05 — The JWT role claim is authoritative for every requireRole/requirePermission/requireOrgAccess decision and is never re-resolved — role changes and demotions do not take effect for up to 24h

`server/middleware/auth.ts:149` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** authenticateToken sets `role: decoded.role || 'user'` and `roles: decoded.roles || [decoded.role || 'user']` straight from the token (server/middleware/auth.ts:149-150). The only post-issuance re-check, enforceOrgMembership (orgMembership.ts:160-209), verifies that the organization_users ROW EXISTS — queryOrgMembership selects `role` at line 117 but discards it and returns only 'member' | 'revoked' (line 126). No middleware ever compares the token's role against the current DB role. The three helpers then apply blanket admin overrides on top of that stale claim: requireRole at auth.ts:197 (`if (!hasRole && !userRoles?.includes('admin'))` — an admin passes EVERY role gate, including requireRole('super_admin', ...) as used at server/routes/admin/audit-siem.ts:42); requirePermission at auth.ts:267 (`if (!hasPermission && req.user.role !== 'admin')`); requireOrgAccess at auth.ts:221 (`if (req.user.role === 'admin' ...) return next()` — skips the org comparison entirely for any orgId in params/body/query). Every self-signup account is an org admin: routes/auth.ts:872-876 inserts organizationUsers role 'admin' and :906-916 mints the token with `role:'admin'`. The demotion route at server/routes/tenant-users.ts:596-602 updates organization_users.role and does not (and could not usefully) invalidate anything token-side.

**Failure scenario.** Tenant A's admin Alice is demoted for cause: another admin calls `PATCH /api/tenant-users/7/42 {"role":"viewer"}`, which succeeds (tenant-users.ts:602) and the UI shows her as a viewer. Alice keeps the bearer token she obtained that morning, which still carries `role:"admin"`. She sends `POST /api/ind-lifecycle/registers/submission/123/safety-reports` — gated by `requireRole(AUTHOR)` at server/routes/ind-lifecycle/registers.routes.ts:228, importing requireRole from '../../middleware/auth' (extensionless, so auth.ts in both dev and prod). userRoles resolves to ['admin'] from her stale claim, the override at auth.ts:197 fires, and the write succeeds. She retains full authoring rights on the tenant's IND safety-report register for the remaining lifetime of the token — up to 24 hours after the demotion, with no way for the org to shorten it (see AUTHZ-02: logout does not revoke). Note on cross-tenant reach: I could NOT construct a path from an 'admin' claim to another tenant's data. requireOrgAccess's override is currently latent because its only eleven call sites (cortexRoutes.ts:172-503) all derive orgId from req.user rather than from request input; the genuinely cross-tenant admin routers are gated by requirePlatformAdmin, which has no org-admin bypass; and audit-siem's loose role list is neutralised by requireAuthedOrgId. The exposure is a stale-privilege window, not a tenant-boundary break — today.

**Fix.** Re-resolve the role rather than trusting the claim: have enforceOrgMembership return the DB role from the row it already selects (orgMembership.ts:117) and overwrite req.user.role/roles with it, which makes demotion effective within the existing 60s cache TTL at zero extra query cost. Separately, remove the blanket admin overrides from requireRole (auth.ts:197) and requirePermission (auth.ts:267) and make each call site name 'admin' explicitly when it means it — the codebase already proves the override is a hazard, since requirePlatformAdmin.ts:11 and admin/scim-tenants.ts:24 both exist specifically to route around it. Delete requireOrgAccess or rewrite it to fail closed when no org identifier is present in the request.

#### `P2` AUTHZ-06 — A SCIM token scoped to one organization can mutate user rows belonging to another organization

`server/routes/scim.ts:503` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** POST /scim/v2/Users resolves the target account by a GLOBAL email lookup with no tenant predicate: `SELECT id FROM users WHERE lower(email) = $1` (scim.ts:495). When that hits a user who exists but is NOT a member of the caller's org, the uniqueness guard at 499-503 does not fire (it checks organization_users for the CALLER's org only), and execution falls through to `UPDATE users SET status = $1, updated_at = now() WHERE id = $2` at scim.ts:503-506 followed by `INSERT INTO organization_users (organization_id, user_id, role) VALUES ($1, $2, 'member')` at 515-520. Both writes land on a row the caller's token has no authority over. This is the one place in the SCIM router where tenant scoping is missing — every /Users/:id operation (GET 441-446, PUT 554-558, PATCH 592-596, DELETE 664-668) correctly constrains via a `JOIN organization_users ou ON ou.user_id = u.id AND ou.organization_id = $1` join, and Groups role assignment is constrained to VALID_ROLES (scim.ts:60) so it cannot mint platform roles.

**Failure scenario.** The holder of org A's SCIM bearer token (the customer's own IdP integrator, or anyone who obtains the token from the Okta/Entra configuration) sends `POST /scim/v2/Users` with `{"schemas":["urn:ietf:params:scim:schemas:core:2.0:User"],"userName":"ceo@org-b.example","active":false}`. The handler flips org B's CEO's global users.status to 'inactive' — which blocks that person from applying any 21 CFR Part 11 electronic signature (part11ComplianceService.ts:568), a direct disruption to org B's submission workflow — and silently grafts them in as a 'member' of org A, where they now appear in org A's user list and SCIM Groups. Neither org B nor the platform operator receives any signal; the only audit event written (scim.ts:531) is filed under org A. Reachability caveat, stated plainly: this needs a valid SCIM token, and those are minted only by a platform admin (server/routes/admin/scim-tenants.ts:26, requirePlatformAdmin) or set by an operator in SCIM_TENANTS/SCIM_BEARER_TOKEN. It is a boundary break between two configured SCIM tenants, not something a plain authenticated tenant user reaches.

**Fix.** In POST /scim/v2/Users, when the global email lookup finds an existing user with no membership in the caller's org, either return 409 (the account belongs to another tenant — provisioning must not silently claim it) or, if cross-org membership is an intended product behaviour, insert only the organization_users row and never touch the shared users.status column from a tenant-scoped credential. Scope the status write to accounts whose only membership is the caller's org.

#### `P3` AUTHZ-07 — /api/health/full and /api/metrics are each registered twice — the unguarded copies are suppressed only by mount ordering

`server/bootstrap/register-platform-routes.ts:33` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** server/startup/inline-endpoints.ts:128 registers `app.get('/api/health/full', requireMetricsAuth, ...)` and :143 `app.get('/api/metrics', requireMetricsAuth, ...)`, with a deliberate comment at 137-140 that exception messages from the health checker 'can carry DB DSN fragments, file paths, env var names — all leakage on a public endpoint', so the guarded version returns `{status:'error'}` only. server/bootstrap/register-platform-routes.ts:33 and :45 register the SAME two paths with no guard, and the 33-42 handler echoes `err?.message` verbatim in a 500 body. Both /api/health (prefix) and /api/metrics (exact) are on PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST (server/middleware/authBoundary.ts:75-76), so the global boundary does not apply to either. Express resolves duplicates by registration order, and today the guarded pair wins: mountDiagnosticEndpoints runs at server/index.ts:127, before registerPreStartRoutes at :139 which reaches registerPlatformRoutes via startup/routes.ts:90.

**Failure scenario.** Not exploitable as shipped — I verified the ordering, and the guarded handlers are registered first, so `GET /api/health/full` without a JWT or METRICS_TOKEN returns 401. The failure mode is a one-line refactor: any change that moves mountDiagnosticEndpoints after route registration, or that lazily registers diagnostics, silently promotes the unguarded copy. At that moment `GET /api/health/full` becomes an unauthenticated endpoint returning full dependency health plus raw exception text (DSN fragments, file paths, env var names, per the code's own comment), and `GET /api/metrics` exposes DB pool counts and process internals to the internet, with no test or CI gate detecting the change.

**Fix.** Delete the duplicate registrations at register-platform-routes.ts:29-58 (they are strictly redundant with the guarded ones in startup/inline-endpoints.ts), or attach requireMetricsAuth to them so the outcome no longer depends on which module loads first. Add a route-collision assertion to the existing smoke suite so a duplicate path registration fails CI rather than resolving by accident.

### IDOR and cross-tenant data access (server/routes/, server/api/, ON CONFLICT / Drizzle upsert surface)

I mounted routers exactly as production does, drove real HTTP requests with a valid org-A JWT through the real `authenticateToken`/auth-boundary chain, and captured the SQL each handler emitted. That turned up one unconditional P0 (`/api/clinical-operations/*` has no tenant boundary whatsoever — `getOrgId()` returns `null` on every request, proven by captured bind params `$2 = null`, and every row is written with `org_id = NULL`), one P0 IDOR-plus-mass-assignment on the concept2cure project-task family (`.set(req.body)` writes a caller-supplied `organizationId`, confirmed by the emitted `update "project_tasks" set "organization_id" = $1`), and one P1 write-side tenant-injection on `POST /api/documents`. Equally important for the buyer: I disproved several apparently-severe leads. The unscoped Part 11 signing router (`server/api/signing/routes.ts`, which takes signer identity from the request body) is shadowed by a 4-line `.js` stub and is absent from the production bundle — `grep -ao 'signing.signing_requests' dist/index.js` returns 0. The unscoped `submission_tasks` and `vault.drafting_sessions` surfaces reference tables that no applied migration or Drizzle schema creates, so they 500 rather than leak. The Drizzle `onConflictDoUpdate` audit the brief flagged as a blind spot came back essentially clean — the tenant-bearing upserts (`tmfArtifactFilings`, `organizationIndustryProfiles`) correctly include `organizationId` in the conflict target. The dominant idiom across the codebase is correct (`WHERE id = $1 AND organization_id = $2` across the whole mdx-* family; 50+ correct `and(eq(projects.id, x), eq(projects.organizationId, org))` in concept2cure.ts). The real findings are outliers against a mostly-sound pattern, and the two P0s are narrow, well-localized fixes.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- Default-deny /api auth boundary is genuinely well-built (server/middleware/authBoundary.ts). Mounted once before any route registration (server/index.ts:123), it reuses the canonical authenticateToken rather than forking it, and every entry on PUBLIC_API_ALLOWLIST carries an inline comment naming the alternative control that covers it (Stripe signature verification, requireMetricsAuth, X-API-Key scopes). The prefix matcher is written so /api/healthxyz cannot ride on /api/health. My probe confirmed a no-token request to a data endpoint returns 401 AUTH_001.
- Organization id is derived exclusively from the verified JWT and never from headers. tenantContext.ts:94-114 and enterprise-security.ts:504-576 both refuse header-supplied org ids, and a mismatched x-organization-id is logged, audited as tenant_impersonation_attempt, and forwarded to a security webhook. This closes the single easiest tenant-impersonation path.
- The documents family is properly hardened and regression-tested. Every id-scoped handler in server/routes/document-routes.ts gates on requireAuthedOrgId and passes the org into storage, so a foreign-tenant id returns 404 rather than 403 (existence is not enumerable), downloads are tenant-checked before bytes are served, and reads/downloads are audited for 21 CFR Part 11 §11.10(e). server/__tests__/security/tenant-isolation-documents.contract.test.ts locks the read/update/delete behaviour against regression.
- The Drizzle onConflictDoUpdate audit — flagged in the brief as invisible to the static CI gate — came back essentially clean. All 8 sites resolve correctly: tmfArtifactFilings targets [organizationId, trialId, artifactCode], organizationIndustryProfiles targets organizationId, projectMembers targets (projectId, userId) inside an org-scoped create flow, and reportTypeRegistry is a platform-global catalog with no tenant data. Of the 82 raw-SQL ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE sites, the 43 whose target omits a tenant column are keyed on already-org-scoped foreign keys or unguessable UUIDs.
- scripts/ci/check-unbacked-tables.mjs is an unusually thoughtful CI gate — it detects code referencing tables nothing creates, recognises all three real provisioning paths (SQL migrations, Drizzle pgTable, runtime CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS), pins a baseline table-to-file map so an existing entry cannot silently gain new call sites, and documents its own limits honestly. It is the reason several unscoped routers I investigated are inert rather than leaking.
- Dangerous prototype routers were neutralised rather than left mounted. server/api/signing/routes.ts takes signer identity and role from the request body and has no tenant scoping anywhere, but it is shadowed by a 4-line server/api/gcc/signing/routes.js stub exposing only /status; grep -ao 'signing.signing_requests' dist/index.js returns 0, confirming it is absent from the shipped artifact. The same is true for the gcc labeling, site-intel and vault prototypes, and gcc/index.ts documents why the vault prototype was deleted.
- enforceOrgMembership (server/middleware/orgMembership.ts) re-checks the organization_users row on every authenticated request behind a 60s TTL cache, so a revoked user loses access within a minute instead of the full 1d token lifetime. It is deliberately split into its own module to dodge a documented Vite .js-twin resolver hazard that would otherwise silently drop the control under test, and the WebSocket path consults the same cache so invalidation reaches live collaboration sessions.
- The dominant query idiom across the codebase is correct and consistent. The entire mdx-* family (mdx-qms, mdx-cdx, mdx-ivdr, mdx-ivd-performance, mdx-software, mdx-submission-gateway, mdx-ana-memory) uses unconditional WHERE id = $1 AND organization_id = $2, concept2cure.ts contains 50+ correct and(eq(projects.id, x), eq(projects.organizationId, org)) predicates, and cortex-unified.ts checks thread ownership before returning or deleting. The findings above are outliers against a mostly-sound baseline, which is why they are narrow fixes rather than an architectural rewrite.
- Part 11 immutability is enforced structurally, not by convention: startup/middleware.ts:144-180 refuses DELETE and bulk-delete against audit and e-signature prefixes at the middleware layer, and db/migrations/041 adds a database trigger preventing UPDATE or DELETE on signature manifests. Defence at both tiers.

#### `P0` IDOR-01 — /api/clinical-operations/* has no tenant boundary: getOrgId() returns null on every request, org predicate silently drops

`server/routes/clinical-operations-routes.ts:93` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** getOrgId() (line 93) resolves only `(req as any).tenantId || (req as any).tenantContext?.organizationId`. It is the ONLY getOrgId in the repo that omits `req.user?.organizationId` (I dumped all 40+ implementations; every other one reads req.user). The router is mounted with `authenticateToken` alone (server/bootstrap/register-regulatory-routes.ts:323). authenticateToken sets `req.user` but never `req.tenantId` or `req.tenantContext`; tenantContextMiddleware is NOT in the global stack (server/startup/middleware.ts applyCoreMiddleware) and the only global writer of req.tenantId is validateApiKey (enterprise-security.ts:716, X-API-Key path only). So getOrgId() returns null for every JWT request. I proved this at runtime: mounting the router exactly as production does and issuing GET /studies/:id with a valid JWT carrying organizationId=1 emitted `SELECT * FROM clinical_ops.studies WHERE id = $1 AND ($2::TEXT IS NULL OR org_id = $2)` with captured params `["...ff", null]` — the guard degenerates to TRUE. The UPDATE path is worse: line 421 `let whereClause = 'WHERE id = $1'` only appends `AND org_id = ...` inside `if (orgId)`, so the probe captured `UPDATE clinical_ops.studies SET status = $2, updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1 RETURNING *` with no org term at all. POST /studies (line 356) likewise binds `orgId`=null, so every row is stored with org_id = NULL. ensureTables() (line 114) self-provisions clinical_ops.* on first request, so the tables exist in any deployment that ever received traffic — unlike the other unscoped families I checked. `grep -ao 'clinical_ops.studies' dist/index.js` = 29 occurrences, confirming it ships.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker is a normal authenticated user of org A. `GET /api/clinical-operations/studies` with their own valid Bearer token returns the study portfolio of EVERY tenant — no ID guessing needed, because the list query at line 320 uses the same `($1::TEXT IS NULL OR org_id = $1)` idiom and $1 is null. The response includes competitor protocol numbers, phase, indication, sponsor name, therapeutic area, and enrollment. They then pivot: `GET /api/clinical-operations/studies/{victimStudyId}/deviations` yields another sponsor's GCP protocol-deviation log, and `/monitoring-visits` yields their monitor names and visit schedule. Write side: `PUT /api/clinical-operations/studies/{victimStudyId}` with body `{"status":"terminated"}` silently updates a competitor's trial record and returns the mutated row (my probe returned HTTP 200 with the foreign row). `POST /api/clinical-operations/sites` with a foreign `studyId` inserts a site into another sponsor's trial and increments their site counters via the unconditional `UPDATE ... WHERE id = $1` at line 490.

**Fix.** Change getOrgId (line 93) to fall back to `req.user?.organizationId` — the one-line change that matches every other getOrgId in the repo. Then make the predicate unconditional rather than fail-open: replace the `($n::TEXT IS NULL OR org_id = $n)` idiom (lines 232, 240, 249, 320, 379, 456, 549, 621, 677, 706, 764, 792, 838, 869, 875) and the `if (orgId) { whereClause += ... }` blocks (lines 421, 516) with a hard `AND org_id = $n`, and return 403 when getOrgId() yields null so the module fails closed. Add `org_id TEXT NOT NULL` to the ensureTables DDL and backfill; existing rows are all NULL and cannot be attributed to a tenant after the fact.

#### `P0` IDOR-02 — concept2cure project-task IDOR plus mass assignment: PUT writes a caller-supplied organizationId with no org predicate

`server/routes/concept2cure.ts:13251` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `PUT /api/concept2cure/projects/:projectId/tasks/:taskId` (handler at 13240) does `const updates = req.body;` then `.update(projectTasks).set(updates).where(eq(projectTasks.id, taskId))` (13251-13252) — no organization predicate, and no allowlist on the patch body. `projectTasks.organizationId` is a real `notNull()` column (shared/schema.ts:6818). I confirmed Drizzle does not filter unknown-but-valid keys: running `.set({organizationId: 1, status: 'done'})` against the real schema emitted `update "project_tasks" set "organization_id" = $1, "status" = $2 ... where "project_tasks"."id" = $4`. The sibling handlers share the defect: GET tasks (13168) filters on `eq(projectTasks.projectId, projectId)` only, and DELETE (13273) on `eq(projectTasks.id, taskId)` only. The router-level guards (13167-13170: authMiddleware, tenantContextMiddleware, requireOrganizationContext) do NOT mitigate this — requireOrganizationContext only asserts that an org context exists and matches the JWT, it never constrains which rows a handler may touch. This is an outlier: the same file gets it right in 50+ places with `and(eq(projects.id, numericId), eq(projects.organizationId, organizationId))` (e.g. 2418, 2478, 2527, 3167). `grep -ao 'project_tasks' dist/index.js` = 10, so it ships.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker in org A (org id 1) enumerates small integer project ids. `GET /api/concept2cure/projects/57/tasks` returns org B's regulatory task board — task names, module types, due dates, blocked reasons, assignees — for any project id that exists, since only projectId is matched. They then send `PUT /api/concept2cure/projects/57/tasks/9812` with body `{"organizationId": 1}`. Because .set() maps that straight to the column and .where() has no tenant term, org B's task row is reassigned into org A's organization and thereafter renders inside the attacker's own UI through the correctly-scoped read paths — a silent cross-tenant record theft that leaves the victim's board short a task with no audit signal. The same body could instead set `status`/`blockedReason` to corrupt a competitor's submission checklist, or `DELETE /api/concept2cure/projects/57/tasks/9812` to destroy it outright.

**Fix.** On all three handlers, resolve the caller's org via the existing `organizationId` helper already used throughout this file and add it to the predicate: `.where(and(eq(projectTasks.id, taskId), eq(projectTasks.organizationId, organizationId)))`, and for the list `.where(and(eq(projectTasks.projectId, projectId), eq(projectTasks.organizationId, organizationId)))`. Separately, stop passing req.body into .set() — parse it through a Zod schema that omits organizationId, projectId and id, mirroring the taskSchema already defined at 13186 for the POST path.

#### `P1` IDOR-03 — POST /api/documents accepts organizationId from the request body, letting an attacker plant documents inside another tenant's dossier

`server/routes/document-routes.ts:163` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Every other handler in this file is properly hardened and opens with `const guard = requireAuthedOrgId(req, res); if (!guard.ok) return;` — GET / (110), GET /:id (140), PATCH /:id (253), DELETE /:id (275), GET /:id/download (297), each then passing guard.orgId into storage. `POST /` (163) is the sole handler that omits it: it does `insertDocumentSchema.parse(req.body)` then `storage.createDocument(documentData)`. insertDocumentSchema (shared/schema.ts:2166) omits only id/createdAt/updatedAt, so organizationId and clientWorkspaceId remain caller-supplied. DatabaseStorage.createDocument passes the parsed object straight through as `.insert(schema.documents).values(input as any)` with no org override. documents.organizationId is `notNull().references(() => organizations.id)` (shared/schema.ts:1785). The repo's own tenant-isolation contract test (server/__tests__/security/tenant-isolation-documents.contract.test.ts) covers read, update and delete by id but has no create case — the gap is unguarded by the existing suite.

**Failure scenario.** Attacker in org A sends `POST /api/documents` with a valid Bearer token and body `{"organizationId": 22, "clientWorkspaceId": <victim workspace>, "documentCode": "M2.5-CLINICAL-OVERVIEW", "title": "Clinical Overview v3 FINAL", ...}` where 22 is org B. The row is inserted into org B's document library. Because every read path IS correctly org-scoped, org B's users see the attacker's document rendered as their own authentic content in their regulatory workspace, with no indication of foreign origin. In a submission context this is a content-integrity attack: a planted or subtly-altered Module 2 document can be picked up by an eCTD assembly flow, and it is also a high-credibility internal phishing lure (an attacker-controlled document appearing inside a trusted, access-controlled dossier).

**Fix.** Add the same guard the rest of the file uses — `const guard = requireAuthedOrgId(req, res); if (!guard.ok) return;` — and override the tenant fields server-side rather than trusting the body: `storage.createDocument({ ...documentData, organizationId: guard.orgId })`. Better, tighten insertDocumentSchema (or a create-specific variant) to omit organizationId and clientWorkspaceId so they can never arrive from the client. Extend tenant-isolation-documents.contract.test.ts with a create case asserting a body-supplied foreign organizationId is rejected or overridden.

#### `P1` IDOR-04 — Fail-open tenant predicate idiom ($n IS NULL OR org_id = $n) converts a missing org context into a full-table grant

`server/routes/clinical-operations-routes.ts:379` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** Reported separately from IDOR-01 because it is a design defect worth fixing repo-wide, not just in one file. Sixteen call sites use `($n::TEXT IS NULL OR org_id = $n)` (clinical-operations-routes.ts:232, 240, 249, 320, 379, 456, 549, 621, 677, 706, 764, 792, 838, 869, 875 and server/api/drafting/routes.ts:460), and two use the equivalent imperative form `if (orgId) { whereClause += ' AND org_id = ...' }` (421, 516). The security property is inverted: a null tenant context — the exact state produced by any auth path that does not populate req.tenantId — silently widens the query to every tenant instead of narrowing it to none. I captured this live: bind params came back `["...", null]` and the handler returned HTTP 200 with a foreign row. A correct fail-closed predicate (`AND org_id = $n` with a null org) returns zero rows; this idiom returns all of them. Note the contrast with the repo's own correct convention in the mdx-* family, which uses an unconditional `WHERE id = $1 AND organization_id = $2` (mdx-qms.ts:367, mdx-cdx.ts:127, mdx-ivdr.ts:136, mdx-submission-gateway.ts:168 and ~40 more).

**Failure scenario.** Any future refactor that changes how tenant context is attached — swapping a mount's middleware, adding a new auth path such as SSO or a service token, or reordering the middleware stack — turns every one of these eighteen queries into a silent full-table read with no error, no log line, and no test failure. This already happened once here: the module was written assuming requireTenantContext upstream, that assumption was never true at the mount, and the fail-open idiom converted the mistake from a visible 403 into an invisible cross-tenant disclosure. The same class of change on a fail-closed predicate would produce loudly empty results and be caught in minutes.

**Fix.** Make the tenant predicate unconditional everywhere and resolve the org once at the router boundary: add a small `requireOrgId(req, res)` guard (server/utils/authedOrgId.ts already provides exactly this as requireAuthedOrgId) that 403s on a null org, then bind a guaranteed-non-null value into a plain `AND org_id = $n`. Consider a lint or CI rule rejecting the `IS NULL OR <tenant_col> =` pattern in SQL string literals — it is mechanically greppable and the existing scripts/ci/ gates show the team already builds this kind of check well.

#### `P2` IDOR-05 — Unscoped drafting-session read and RAG retrieval (latent — backing tables are not provisioned by any applied migration)

`server/api/drafting/routes.ts:487` · blocks **G3** · ~days

**Evidence.** This router IS live and reachable — mounted at /api/gcc/drafting via server/api/gcc/index.ts:31 and server/bootstrap/register-document-routes.ts:26, present in the production bundle (`grep -ao 'vault.drafting_sessions' dist/index.js` = 4), and my probe with an org-A JWT got HTTP 200. It has no tenant scoping anywhere: `GET /sessions/:id` (487) is `SELECT * FROM vault.drafting_sessions WHERE id = $1`; `GET /sessions` (459) uses the fail-open `WHERE ($1::UUID IS NULL OR program_id = $1)`; hybridSearch (176-186) calls `vault.hybrid_search(...)` and `vault.table_priority_search(...)` with no org argument at all. It calls getRequestDbClient(req), which falls back to the shared pool because requireTenantContext is not on this mount, so no RLS session vars are set even if RLS were live. What holds it back today: vault.drafting_sessions has no organizationId column and is created only by db/migrations/_legacy/044_gcc_cognitive_fabric_rag.sql; scripts/db_migrate.sh:117-123 globs `-maxdepth 1` and the _legacy README states these must not be run. The table is absent from shared/ (no Drizzle definition) and from the canonical 044c_gcc_vault_schema.sql. So the queries currently fail with 42P01 and surface as HTTP 500.

**Failure scenario.** Not exploitable on a canonically-migrated database — the relation does not exist. It becomes a P0 the moment anyone provisions vault.drafting_sessions and vault.hybrid_search (restoring the _legacy migration, a drizzle-kit push against a schema that later adds them, or a manual DBA action to 'fix the 500s'). At that point an org-A user calling `GET /api/gcc/drafting/sessions` with no query params retrieves every tenant's AI drafting history — prompt text, generated draft text, and reasoning traces derived from their regulatory dossiers — and `POST /api/gcc/drafting/generate` performs retrieval-augmented generation across all tenants' evidence atoms, letting the attacker ask the model to summarise a competitor's clinical data and receive it as cited output. logDraftingSession (271) also takes the audit user_id from `req.body.userId`, so attribution is caller-forgeable.

**Fix.** Do not let the schema land before the scoping does. Add organizationId to vault.drafting_sessions, add `AND organization_id = $n` to lines 459/487/590, give vault.hybrid_search and vault.table_priority_search a p_org_id parameter (ai.hybrid_search in db/migrations/059_gcc_vector_embeddings.sql:417-458 already models this correctly and is the pattern to copy), take the audit user id from req.user rather than req.body, and mount the router behind requireTenantContext so getRequestDbClient returns a tenant-scoped client. If the feature is not shipping, delete the router — it is currently reachable code backed by nothing.

### Injection, cryptography, secrets, and path handling

The three areas split sharply. SQL injection: clean — I traced all 3 `sql.raw(` sites and every template-literal `query()` with interpolation (including committee-service.ts:170) and found no injectable path; the interpolants are compile-time constants, two-branch ternaries over literals, `$n` placeholder indices, or numerically clamped limits. Crypto: strong — a single algorithm-pinned JWT verifier with rotation, AES-256-GCM MFA secrets with per-record IVs and timing-safe TOTP compare, a textbook audit HMAC seal, bcrypt cost 12 everywhere, and an unusually rigorous boot-time secret posture that fails closed on weak/reused keys. Secrets in CI/IaC: clean — no `pull_request_target`, `sensitive = true` throughout, ECS secrets by ARN reference, no secret reachable in a workflow log line. Path handling is where the money is. The C2C-AI-003 workspace confinement (`assertWithinDocumentWorkspace`) exists and is correct, but it is wired into exactly one tool — `convert_docx_to_pdf`. Six sibling AnA handlers that take the same class of model-supplied path still call `fs.readFile` unconfined, and the guard module's own docblock asserts the opposite. Separately, `/api/document-understanding` accepts a raw `filePath` and confines it to shared roots (`uploads/`, `exports/`, `storage/`…) rather than to the caller's tenant subdirectory, which defeats the exact `uploads/org-{id}/` control the rest of the codebase built to enforce tenancy. Both give an authenticated user of one tenant the contents of another tenant's regulatory dossier.

**Genuine strengths in this unit**

- SQL injection surface is genuinely clean, and I looked hard. All three `sql.raw(` sites take server-controlled values: shared/constants/operating-system-vocab.ts:50 is a frozen predicate string, and server/services/cerGenerationService.ts:416/656 JSON.stringify only an ISO date, a SHA-256 hex checksum, two integers, and a `format` already narrowed by a switch that throws on default. committee-service.ts:170's `${table}` is a two-branch ternary over the literals 'iacuc_protocols'/'irb_submissions' — the input value never reaches the string.
- Every dynamic-clause builder I traced interpolates only placeholder indices, not data: qms.service.ts:44-48 and :137-139, decision-record-service.ts:203-232, pharmacovigilanceService.ts:471-502, submission-chat-proposal-store.ts:309-329, authoring-plan-generator.ts:995. Dynamic identifiers are allowlisted (AnaToolExecutor.ts:13902 column map, site-intel/routes.ts:56-63 sort-field whitelist plus direction ternary, cortexComplianceService.ts:937 `/^[a-zA-Z0-9_.]+$/`), and dynamic LIMITs are numerically clamped (ivd-assessments.service.ts:96 `Math.min(500, Math.max(1, …))`, proposal-store:331) or parseInt'd at the route (innovation-routes.ts:1096).
- JWT handling is consolidated and correct. server/utils/jwtVerify.ts is the single verifier for 30+ call sites, pins `algorithms:['HS256']` inline at both `jwt.verify` calls (l.87, l.92) specifically so a textual CI gate can check it, and retries the previous secret only on a signature mismatch — never on TokenExpiredError, which would be a rotation footgun. The one direct `jwt.verify` outside the helper (routes/auth.ts:1051) is the refresh path, uses its own secret, and pins the algorithm too.
- server/config/environment.ts is the strongest part of the codebase. It enforces a 32-char floor on the JWT secret, refuses to boot when the previous secret equals the current one (a no-op rotation), mandates a dedicated REFRESH_TOKEN_SECRET distinct from the JWT secret in staging and production, forces a dedicated MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEY in production via assertMfaKeyPosture (l.245-268), and asserts RLS and audit-seal posture — all firing on import so a misconfigured deploy dies at boot rather than degrading silently.
- MFA cryptography is right: AES-256-GCM with a fresh 12-byte IV per record (mfaService.ts:189-196), auth-tag-verified multi-candidate decryption so a key migration cannot lock users out without ever decrypting under the wrong key (l.150-176), and `crypto.timingSafeEqual` on the TOTP comparison with the length pre-check that keeps it from throwing (l.265-268).
- The audit HMAC seal (server/services/audit/audit-hmac-seal.ts) is textbook: a pinned canonical byte layout with newline separators to kill field-boundary collisions (l.85-91), the sequence number bound into the input so reordering is detectable, timingSafeEqual verification that fails closed on malformed input (l.120-138), and a hard refusal to seal under an absent key (l.69-78).
- Password handling is uniform and defensive: bcryptjs cost 12 at every hash site (auth.ts:860/1754, users.ts:787/899, setup.ts:111), and server/auth.ts:283-304 rejects empty hashes outright and refuses legacy `temp_`-prefixed plaintext values instead of comparing them.
- server/routes/cer-routes.ts:463-497 is a model of how to fix a path traversal: a strict id allowlist regex first, a per-tenant root `data/cer_reports/org-{orgId}`, a post-normalization containment check using `tenantRoot + path.sep`, a generic 400 that does not echo the rejected input, and a Part 11 `cer_report_view` audit event written before the content is served.
- server/services/ana/uploaded-file-access.ts is the correct tenancy primitive and enforces the rule symmetrically (l.87-99): a tenant caller gets only rows whose org column AND storage-path prefix name that org, and an unscoped caller gets only ownerless rows — explicitly closing the direction where 'unscoped' short-circuited the check for everyone.
- CI and IaC secret hygiene holds up. No workflow uses `pull_request_target`, so fork PRs never see secrets; terraform/modules/secrets/variables.tf:12 marks the whole secrets map `sensitive` and each per-secret variable is individually sensitive; production ECS receives secrets as Secrets Manager ARN references (terraform/environments/production/main.tf:135-141) rather than plaintext task-definition env; both tfvars.example files carry explicit do-not-commit warnings and contain no real values; and no workflow `run:` block echoes a secret.
- Sentry egress is scrubbed at the boundary — `sendDefaultPii:false` plus a `beforeSend` that runs the whole event through `redactSecretsAndPiiObject` (server/utils/sentry.ts:13-34), with a deep walker that scrubs string values while preserving structure and skipping non-plain objects.
- No archive is ever extracted to disk — templateExtractor.ts, validateZip.js and the Python docx runtimes all operate on in-memory buffers (JSZip/AdmZip/BytesIO). That removes the zip-slip and symlink-planting primitive that would otherwise turn the Node/Python `path.resolve` vs `Path.resolve` symlink asymmetry into an arbitrary write.

#### `P0` INJ-PATH-001 — Cross-tenant dossier read: /api/document-understanding confines filePath to shared roots, not to the caller's tenant

`server/routes/document-understanding.ts:463` · blocks **G1** · ~hours

**Evidence.** Three handlers (`POST /analyze` l.454-470, `POST /extract-tables` l.574-590, `POST /extract-form-fields` l.616-632) take `body.filePath` verbatim, do `path.resolve(body.filePath)` (l.462) and admit it if `ALLOWED_ROOTS.some(root => fullPath.startsWith(root))` (l.463), where ALLOWED_ROOTS = uploads/ exports/ generated_documents/ csrs/ ectd/ storage/ (l.454-461). It then `fs.readFileSync(fullPath,'utf-8')` (l.470). Every non-empty line of that file becomes a returned element (`content: trimmed`, l.283) and the whole structure is serialized back at l.560 `res.json({success:true,data:result})`; `outputFormat:'markdown'` returns near-verbatim text. grep for `organizationId|requireAuth|router.use` across this file returns nothing — there is no tenancy check at all. It is mounted at server/bootstrap/register-clinical-intel-routes.ts:99-103 with no middleware (app.use(cfg.path, router), l.116), so only the global boundary in server/middleware/authBoundary.ts applies, and that boundary authenticates without authorizing (l.166-171). Tenant storage layout is `uploads/org-{id}/{fileId}` — see server/routes/chat/upload.ts:155 and the prefix assertion in server/services/ana/uploaded-file-access.ts:97. Secondary defect: `startsWith(root)` has no separator guard, so `path.resolve('ectd')` = /app/ectd also admits the sibling directory /app/ectd-stubs which exists at repo root.

**Failure scenario.** An authenticated user of org 1 sends `POST /api/document-understanding/analyze` with their own valid bearer token and body {"filePath":"uploads/org-2/file_1753900000000_a1b2c3","options":{"outputFormat":"markdown"}}. The global boundary passes them (they are authenticated), the allowlist passes the path (it is under uploads/), and the response body is org 2's uploaded regulatory document rendered as markdown. Repeating against `uploads/org-N/...` walks the whole customer base. The 404-vs-200 difference from `fs.existsSync` (l.468) is also a file-existence oracle for enumerating ids.

**Fix.** Do not accept raw paths on this surface. Take a `file_id` and resolve it through `loadUploadedFileMetadata` / `readLocalUploadBuffer` in server/services/ana/uploaded-file-access.ts, which already enforce both the `organization_id` column and the `uploads/org-{id}/` prefix symmetrically. If a path parameter must stay, derive the root as `path.resolve('uploads', `org-${req.tenantId}`)` from the JWT-derived org (never body/query) and test containment as `resolved === root || resolved.startsWith(root + path.sep)`. Apply to all three handlers.

#### `P0` INJ-PATH-002 — C2C-AI-003 workspace confinement is wired into one tool; six sibling AnA handlers still read model-supplied paths unconfined

`server/services/ana/AnaToolExecutor.ts:6214` · blocks **G1** · ~days

**Evidence.** `assertWithinDocumentWorkspace` (server/services/ana/document-workspace.ts:106) is correct — it rejects `..` and NUL, resolves against `process.cwd()` (the same base the Python consumer uses), tests containment with `root + path.sep`, and returns the resolved path so the guard and the sink address the same file. But a repo-wide grep shows exactly two call sites: AnaToolExecutor.ts:6743 and :6747, both inside `convert_docx_to_pdf`. The siblings are unguarded: (1) `build_from_template` (registered l.6210 as `async (input: Record<string, unknown>)` — no ToolContext at all) passes `input.template_path` (l.6214) into `builder.buildFromTemplate` (l.6220), which does `await fs.readFile(options.templatePath)` at server/services/docx/masterDocumentBuilder.ts:165 with no confinement; (2) `generate_document` (l.6132, also no ctx) reaches the same sink via l.6141/6145; (3) `start_legacy_import` (l.13809) passes `input.source_path` (l.13811) to `detectArchive` (l.13836), which does `fs.stat` (server/services/legacy-importer/detector.ts:171) and `fs.readFile(archivePath)` (l.177); (4) `validate_docx` (l.7181) does `fs.readFile(inputDocxPath)` (l.7194); (5) `verify_docx_against_source` (l.7221) reads and text-extracts any path (l.7245); (6) `insert_document_content` (l.6874) reads any path and writes the edited copy into the workspace. The tool schema for start_legacy_import literally documents the parameter as "Absolute path to the uploaded zip / folder" (server/services/ana/legacy-import-tool-defs.ts:27). Note also that document-workspace.ts:14-17 asserts convert_docx_to_pdf was "the only tool in the document-surgery family with that gap" — build_from_template and generate_document at l.6210/6132 have the identical no-ctx, no-confinement shape.

**Failure scenario.** An org-1 user drives AnA in chat: "build a document from the template at uploads/org-2/file_1753900000000_a1b2c3, replacing 'ZZZ' with 'ZZZ'". `build_from_template` reads org 2's DOCX off disk, unzips it, applies the no-op replacement, repacks it into `tmp/docbuilder/<uuid>/…docx`, and returns `outputPath` in the tool result. The attacker then calls `convert_docx_to_pdf` on that tmp path — which the guard *permits*, because tmp/ is a workspace root — and downloads org 2's dossier section as a PDF. Full content exfiltration. The `start_legacy_import` variant is quieter: source_path="uploads/org-2/file_…" causes the detector to return every entry name, size and SHA-256 of another tenant's eCTD archive plus the detected sponsor, application id and sequence, and persists all of it into the attacker's own org via the inserts at l.13843-13855.

**Fix.** Route every model-supplied path through `assertWithinDocumentWorkspace` and use the returned resolved value (never the caller's string) at all six handlers; add `ctx.organizationId` requirements to `build_from_template` and `generate_document`, matching the document-surgery family. Confinement is not authorization: for anything under uploads/, additionally require that the resolved path sits under `uploads/org-${ctx.organizationId}/`. Add a lint/CI gate asserting that every handler reading `input.*_path` calls the guard, so the next tool added does not reopen this.

#### `P2` INJ-PATH-003 — Arbitrary-directory file write and cross-user tampering via unvalidated user_id in the digest-preference routes

`server/routes/notification_routes.ts:276` · blocks **G2** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `saveUserPreferences` builds `path.join(prefsDir, `user_${userId}_prefs.json`)` (l.276) and `fs.writeFileSync(prefsFile, JSON.stringify(preferences, null, 2))` (l.277); `getUserPreferences` mirrors it at l.291-300 and JSON.parses the result back to the caller. `userId` comes from `req.body.user_id` on `POST /api/user/save-digest-prefs` (l.131, l.141) and from `req.query.user_id` on `GET /api/user/preferences` (l.165, l.174) — never from the authenticated session. Both routes are live: registered via `notificationRoutes.default(app)` at server/bootstrap/register-advanced-platform-routes.ts:63-70, guarded only by `authMiddleware` (server/auth.ts:74), which authenticates and does not authorize. I verified the traversal in node: path.join('/app/data/user_preferences','user_../../../../../tmp/pwn_prefs.json') resolves to '/tmp/pwn_prefs.json' — the `user_` prefix becomes a consumable directory segment, so N+1 `../` escapes N levels.

**Failure scenario.** Any authenticated tenant user sends `POST /api/user/save-digest-prefs` with {"user_id":"../../../../../tmp/pwn","prefs":{"a":"b"}} and the server writes attacker-controlled JSON to /tmp/pwn_prefs.json (filename suffix is fixed at `_prefs.json`, the directory is not). The simpler and more damaging variant needs no traversal at all: {"user_id":"4417"} silently overwrites user 4417's notification preferences, and `GET /api/user/preferences?user_id=4417` reads them back — a plain IDOR over every user in every tenant.

**Fix.** Derive the subject from `req.user` and drop `user_id` from the request contract entirely on both routes (an admin-on-behalf-of path, if needed, should take an explicit role check plus a tenancy check). Independently, validate with `/^\d+$/` before it touches a path, and move these preferences into the database alongside the rest of the user record rather than a cwd-relative JSON file.

#### `P2` CRY-004 — 21 CFR Part 11 signed audit exports default to signing with JWT_SECRET, so the documented JWT rotation invalidates every archived export signature

`server/services/audit/signedAuditExport.ts:80` · blocks **G2** · ~days

**Evidence.** `getSigningKey()` resolves `process.env.AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY || process.env.JWT_SECRET_PROD || process.env.JWT_SECRET` (l.80-88). `hmacSign` (l.92-97) uses it to sign the export manifest (l.290), and `verifySignedAuditExport` (l.338-356) recomputes with the same `getSigningKey()` and has no previous-key fallback — contrast server/utils/jwtVerify.ts:88-97, which does maintain one. Meanwhile server/config/environment.ts:137-156 documents JWT secret rotation as a routine four-step operator procedure. The same config file refuses this exact key-reuse pattern elsewhere: for refresh tokens it fails the boot when REFRESH_TOKEN_SECRET equals the JWT secret (l.227-233, "a single disclosure forges both") and for MFA it forces a dedicated MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEY in production (assertMfaKeyPosture, l.245-268). The audit export key gets no equivalent posture assertion. Also, l.354 compares the hex signature with `!==` rather than `crypto.timingSafeEqual` — server/services/audit/audit-hmac-seal.ts:120-138 gets this right for the chain seal.

**Failure scenario.** An operator performs the rotation the codebase documents: set JWT_SECRET_PREVIOUS to the old value, JWT_SECRET to a fresh one, deploy. Sessions survive (jwtVerify falls back). But every Part 11 audit export generated before the rotation — the artifacts a customer archives for inspection — now fails `verifySignedAuditExport` with 'Manifest signature invalid — manifest or signing key has been altered'. An FDA inspector re-verifying an untampered archived export is told the records were tampered with, and there is no key-id in the manifest to explain why. The inverse also holds: whoever holds JWT_SECRET can mint both a valid session and a valid Part 11 export attestation.

**Fix.** Mirror assertMfaKeyPosture: require a dedicated AUDIT_EXPORT_SIGNING_KEY (>= 32 chars, distinct from JWT_SECRET) in production and refuse to boot otherwise; drop the JWT_SECRET fallback outside dev/test. Stamp a key id into the manifest and let verification try a configured list of historical keys so archived exports stay verifiable across rotations. Switch the comparison at l.354 to crypto.timingSafeEqual over equal-length buffers.

#### `P2` CRY-005 — Upload file ids are Math.random-derived, which is the only barrier left once a raw-path read exists

`server/routes/chat/upload.ts:87` · blocks **G3** · ~hours

**Evidence.** `const fileId = `file_${Date.now()}_${Math.random().toString(36).substr(2, 6)}`` (l.87) and the identical pattern in server/services/ana/uploaded-file-access.ts:221. The id is the entire filename under `uploads/org-{id}/` (l.155-156). The code comment at l.148-154 acknowledges this — "the file id, while timestamp-suffixed, is enumerable" — and states the compensating control is the org-prefixed path plus org-scoped lookup. That control holds for the sanctioned resolver (uploaded-file-access.ts:97 checks both the prefix and the organization_id column), which is why this is P2 rather than higher. But V8's Math.random is xorshift128+, whose internal state is recoverable from a handful of consecutive outputs, and an attacker generates those at will by uploading files in their own tenant.

**Failure scenario.** Chained with INJ-PATH-001 or INJ-PATH-002, which read by raw path and therefore bypass the org-scoped resolver, the fileId is the last thing standing between an attacker and another tenant's bytes. The attacker uploads five files to their own org, recovers the PRNG state from the returned ids, predicts the 6-character suffix for a target upload, and brute-forces only the millisecond component (~1000 candidates per second of upload window) against `POST /api/document-understanding/analyze`, using the 404-vs-200 response as the oracle. Without the raw-path endpoints this is not reachable; with them it turns a known-filename attack into an enumeration attack.

**Fix.** Replace both id generators with `crypto.randomUUID()` (or `crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex')`). It is a two-line change with no schema impact — the column is already text — and it removes the enumeration primitive independently of the path fixes.

#### `P3` SEC-006 — The console redaction bridge does not redact the case its own docblock says it fixes

`server/utils/consoleBridge.ts:55` · ~hours

**Evidence.** The module header (l.11-15) states the gap being closed is "a `console.error('[auth] login failed:', err)` where `err.message` contains `\"password=hunter2\"` writes that string verbatim to stdout". But `redactArgs` returns strings, numbers and booleans unchanged (l.53-56) and explicitly returns `Error` instances unchanged (l.57-63, "Pass them through").
